On symmetric simplicial (super)string backgrounds, (super-)WZW defect fusion and the Chern–Simons theory
Rafał R. Suszek

Abstract
. The super- σ \sigma -model of dynamics of the super-charged loop in an ambient supermanifold in the presence of worldsheet defects of arbitrary topology is formalised within Gawȩdzki’s higher-cohomological approach, drawing inspiration from the precursor Ref. [ RS09 ] . A distinguished class of the corresponding backgrounds (supertargets with additional bicategorial supergeometric data), organised into simplicial hierarchies, is considered. To these, configurational (super)symmetry of the bulk field theory is lifted coherently, whereby the notion of a maximally (super)symmetric background, and in particular that of a simplicial Lie background, arises as the target structure requisite for the definition of the super- σ \sigma -model with defects fully transmissive to the currents of the bulk (super)symmetry. The formal concepts are illustrated in two settings of physical relevance: that of the WZW σ \sigma -model of the bosonic string in a compact simple 1-connected Lie group and that of the GS super- σ \sigma -model of the superstring in the Minkowski super-space. In the former setting, the structure of the background is fixed through a combination of simplicial, symmetry(-reducibility) and cohomological arguments, and a novel link between fusion of the maximally symmetric WZW defects of Fuchs et al. and the 3 d d CS theory with timelike Wilson lines with fixed holonomy is established. Moreover, a purely geometric interpretation of the Verlinde fusion rules is proposed. In the latter setting, a multiplicative structure compatible with supersymmetry is shown to exist on the GS super-1-gerbe of hep-th/1706.05682, and subsequently used in a novel construction of a class of maximally (rigidly) supersymmetric bi-branes whose elementary fusion is also studied.



In memory of K
†

Introduction

Recent years have witnessed an essential rethinking and generalisation of the notion of a physical symmetry and the attendant upsurge of interest in its field-theoretic realisations furnished by currents flowing through codimension
\,>1\,
submanifolds of the physical spacetime (beside the usual codimension-1 spacelike slices),
cf.
,
e.g.
, Refs.
[
FMS07a
,
FMS07b
,
GKSW15
]
and the references within. The ensuing research has led to the emergence of a plethora of novel notions and mechanisms such as ‘higher-form’, ‘higher-group’ or ‘higher-categorial’ symmetries, and – upon an ingenious incorporation of the good old gauge principle – also ‘non-invertible’ symmetries,
cf.
Ref.
[
BBSNT22
]
. These are now collectively beginning to supplant the ‘ancient’ (sub-)concept of (0-form) symmetry in its rôle of an organising principle in the investigation and solving of a field theory. A common feature shared by (many of) these developments is the emergence of ‘defects’ of various dimensionality decorated by some ‘background’ data (often (higher-)cohomological or (higher-)categorial (or both) in nature), whose insertion in spacetime effects symmetry transformations (resp. ‘twisting’) on the degrees of freedom of the field theory of interest, with the quantum-mechanical correlators depending solely on the homotopy class of the insertion, whence the qualifier ‘topological’ given to these objects.

In low-dimensional field theories of a topological character (such as,
e.g.
, the 3
d
Chern–Simons theory of Refs.
[
Wit89b
,
Wit89a
]
) and in those modelling the geometrodynamics of extended distributions of charge (such as,
e.g.
, the 2
d

\sigma
-model of the bosonic string), the interplay between – on the one hand – ‘defects’ and – on the other hand – symmetries, both rigid and gauged, or even dualities between theories, has been known for a long time,
cf.
,
e.g.
, Refs.
[
DHVW85
,
DHVW86
,
PZ01
,
BdBDO02
,
GW04
,
FFRS04
,
BG04
,
FFRS07
,
ST08
,
FSW08
,
BR07
,
RS09
,
SS08
,
FFRS09
,
DKR11
,
Sus11a
,
Sus12
,
CM16
,
Sus13
,
NR20
,
RW20
]
, and oftentimes associated, in the classical lagrangean formulation, with the appearance of Cheeger–Simons(-type) differential characters (
cf.
Ref.
[
CS85
]
) localised on codimension
\,\geq 1\,
submanifolds of the spacetime of the field theory. In the present work, and in an upcoming one dedicated to the study of ‘non-invertible’ maximally symmetric defects in the WZW
\sigma
-model, we undertake the task of defending the claim that the higher-geometric and -cohomological approach to the 2
d

\sigma
-model of charged-loop dynamics in the presence of worldsheet defects, as formalised in all generality in Ref.
[
RS09
]
, still has much to offer by way of identification and elucidation of the nontrivial information on the underlying field theory encoded in its category of defects with fusion, and so also by way of inspiration and as a testing ground – structurally rich yet rigorously tractable – for the more general field-theoretic context. As such, the work can be regarded as a continuation of the line of research started in the series of papers
[
RS09
,
RS11
,
Sus11a
,
Sus12
,
Sus13
]
, and its extension to the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded setting motivated by the deep nature of the results obtained and anticipated in the un-graded one and based on the recent developments
[
Sus17
,
Sus19
,
Sus18a
,
Sus18b
,
Sus20
,
Sus21a
,
Sus21b
]
, both – the continuation and the extension – to be constructively inscribed herewith in the renaissance of the idea of defect-mediated state correspondences in field theories, invoked in the opening paragraph.

The basic idea that motivates interest in a rigorous formulation of the 2
d

\sigma
-model with the topological Wess–Zumino term in the presence of defects, and – à la fois – anchors such interest in the broader context indicated above, is illustrated in Fig.
1
: A circular (spacelike) defect line
\,\ell_{1,2}\,
separating the two ‘domains’
\,\Sigma_{A},\ A\in\{1,2\}\,
of the two-dimensional spacetime
\,\Sigma
, inhabited by the respective ‘phases’
\,[\Sigma_{A},M]\,
of the (bulk)
\sigma
-model with the target space
\,M\,
(potentially of the type
\,M=M_{1}\sqcup M_{2}\,
and with
\,\Sigma_{A}\,
constrained to map to
\,M_{A}
), sets in a natural correspondence those pairs of the limiting (classical) field configurations
\,\phi_{A}\,
in either phase, taken with the respective (kinetic-)momenta
\,{\rm p}_{A}\,
determining their ‘evolution’ into the interior of the domains, which jointly define an admissible field configuration
\,\phi\in[\Sigma,M\sqcup Q\,]
of the
\sigma
-model
in the presence of the defect line
, the latter being, in general, mapped by (the restriction of)
\,\phi\,
to another manifold
\,Q
, sometimes suggestively termed the correspondence space.

Figure 1.
A correspondence, engendered by a circular defect
\,\ell_{1,2}
, between the bulk states
\,(\phi_{1},{\rm p}_{1})\,
and
\,(\phi_{2},{\rm p}_{2})\,
from the respective phases
\,[\Sigma_{A},M_{A}],\ A\in\{1,2\}\,
of the field theory separated by
\,\ell_{1,2}
.

In a field theory whose quantum description is governed by Segal’s axioms of Refs.
[
Seg88
,
Seg04
]
, incorporation of line defects necessitates admission of their intersections (or junctions), coming with their own targets
\,\bigsqcup_{n\geq 3}\,T_{n}\equiv T\,
(labelled by the valence of the junctions), and so, altogether, we are led to enquire as to the data which ought to be pulled back to the defect lines and junctions of an arbitrary defect graph
\,\Gamma\,
embedded in
\,\Sigma\,
from the respective targets in order to ensure the well-definedness of the ensuing field theory on the
\Gamma
-decorated spacetime
\,\Sigma\,
once the phases of the bulk
\sigma
-model have been defined in terms of a metric on
\,M\,
and a geometrisation of an integral class
\,[{\rm H}]\in H^{3}(M,2\pi{\mathbb{Z}})\,
of the Kalb–Ramond (torsion) field
\,{\rm H}
, known as the gerbe,
cf.
Refs.
[
Gaw88
]
and
[
Mur96
]
for the original hypercohomological and higher-geometric descriptions, respectively. Drawing inspiration from the study of a class of circular defects and the associated targets reported in Ref.
[
FSW08
]
, the question was asked and answered in all generality in Ref.
[
RS09
]
: Consistency of the
\sigma
-model for
\,(\Sigma,\Gamma)\,
was demonstrated to call for the full bicategory
\,\mathfrak{BGrb}^{\nabla}(M\sqcup Q\sqcup T)\,
of gerbes with connective structure over the composite target space
\,M\sqcup Q\sqcup T
, whose distinguished
k
-cells are to be pulled back to the
(2-k)
-dimensional submanifolds
\,\Sigma^{(k)}\subset\Sigma\,
(with
\,k\in\{0,1,2\}
) into which the embedded defect graph
\,\Gamma\,
decomposes the spacetime
\,\Sigma\,
of the theory, as illustrated in Fig.
2
.

Figure 2.
A decomposition of the worldsheet
\,\Sigma\,
into submanifolds
\,\Sigma^{(k)}\subset\Sigma\,
of the respective dimensions
\,k\in\{0,1,2\}
, mapped by the field
\,\phi\in[\Sigma,M\sqcup Q\sqcup T]\,
of the
\sigma
-model into the respective components of the composite target
\,M\sqcup Q\sqcup T\,
endowed with distinguished 0-cells (
\mathcal{G}
), 1-cells (
\Phi
) and 2-cells (
\varphi
) of the bicategory
\,\mathfrak{BGrb}^{\nabla}(M\sqcup Q\sqcup T)
. The green color indicates boundaries of the submanifolds (dark green for boundaries of 2
d
domains and light green for those of the 1
d
components of the defect graph) at which correspondences arise that generalise, in an obvious manner to be formalised presently, that of Fig.
1
.

The results of the detailed worldsheet analysis presented
ibidem
helped to concretise and formalise the raw idea of a defect/symmetry correspondence in Ref.
[
Sus11a
]
: The gluing law for the kinetic momenta of the states in correspondence, enforced at the defect line
\,\ell_{1,2}\,
by the so-called Defect Gluing Condition (DGC), was shown to define an isotropic subspace in the product of the spaces of states of the two phases separated by
\,\ell_{1,2}\,
(taken with the difference of the pullbacks of the symplectic forms that the first-order formalism of Refs.
[
Gaw72
,
Kij73
,
Kij74
,
KS76
,
Szc76
,
KT79
]
associates with the bulk Dirac–Feynman amplitudes for the phases), and data of the 1-cell of
\,\mathfrak{BGrb}^{\nabla}(M\sqcup Q\sqcup T)\,
assigned to the defect line were found to give rise to a linear map between the prequantum bundles associated to the two phases
via
Gawȩdzki’s cohomological transgression of Ref.
[
Gaw88
]
. The study also shed light upon the nature of the generalisation of the ‘ancient’ concept of symmetry of the
\sigma
-model (or, more generally, of a duality between such field theories) furnished by defect lines decorated by the higher-categorial target-space data: The latter need not induce a bijective correspondence between the spaces of states of the two phases, let alone map the respective hamiltonians into one another – these are the distinctive features of the very special topological defects discussed at great length in the original study
[
RS09
]
(
cf.
with the general considerations in Ref.
[
BBSNT22
]
), whereas the capacity of the new construct is most vividly demonstrated by the boundary defects of Ref.
[
RS09
]
which separate our favourite
\sigma
-model from the empty one and in this manner model the insertion of an arbitrary worldsheet boundary. In fact, the story does not end there: Defect junctions decorated with data of the 2-cells of
\,\mathfrak{BGrb}^{\nabla}(M\sqcup Q\sqcup T)\,
naturally take the rôle of zero-dimensional defects (the ‘non-genuine’ ones in the language of Ref.
[
BBSNT22
]
), and with the help of the identity defect they can be made to act on the Chan–Paton(-type) degrees of freedom of the defect-twisted sector. Actually, one might even try to squeeze the framework for ‘genuine’ 1-form symmetries by considering defect junctions between defect lines carrying data of the 1-cells of
\,\mathfrak{BGrb}^{\nabla}(M\sqcup Q\sqcup T)\,
mapping to the identity automorphism of the prequantum bundle under transgression,
cf.
Fig.
3
.

(a)
(b)
(c)

Figure 3.
The zoo of gerbe-theoretic 2-cells and 0-defects. (a) A generic fusion 0-defect with a 2-cell
\,\varphi_{1,2}^{3}\ :\ (\pi_{2,3}^{(3)\,*}\Phi_{2}\otimes{\rm id})\circ\pi_{1,2}^{(3)\,*}\Phi_{1}{\mathrel{\to}^{\cong}}\pi_{1,3}^{(3)\,*}\Phi_{3}
. (b) A (1-)defect-changing 0-defect with a 2-cell
\,\varphi_{{\rm in}>{\rm out}}\ :\ \pi_{1,2}^{(3)\,*}\Phi_{\rm in}{\mathrel{\to}^{\cong}}\pi_{1,3}^{(3)\,*}\Phi_{\rm out}
. (Here,
\,\pi_{2,3}^{(3)}\,
maps to
\,M\subset Q
.) (c) A candidate ‘genuine’ 1-form defect with a 2-cell
\,\varphi_{1,2}^{3}\ :\ (\pi_{2,3}^{(3)\,*}{\rm K}_{2}\otimes{\rm id})\circ\pi_{1,2}^{(3)\,*}{\rm K}_{1}{\mathrel{\to}^{\cong}}\pi_{1,3}^{(3)\,*}{\rm K}_{3}\,
for some automorphisms
\,{\rm K}_{A},\ A\in\{1,2,3\}\,
of the bulk 0-cell which trivialise under transgression. (Here,
\,\pi_{1,2}^{(3)},\ \pi_{2,3}^{(3)}\,
and
\,\pi_{1,3}^{(3)}\,
map to
\,Q\subset T_{3}
.)

With the bicategorial content of the
\sigma
-model with defects firmly established, it became possible to actually
employ
, in Ref.
[
RS09
]
, data of the
Z_{\sigma}
-equivariant structures on the bulk gerbe for discrete-symmetry groups
\,Z_{\sigma}\,
of the bulk theory, requisite for the gauging of
\,Z_{\sigma}\,
(aka orbifolding resp. orientifolding) and established in Refs.
[
GR03
,
GSW08
,
GSW11
]
, in the construction of defect networks
implementing
the discrete symmetry on bulk states resp. giving rise to the so-called
Z_{\sigma}
-twisted sector of the
\sigma
-model with the orbifold
\,M//Z_{\sigma}\,
as the target space (in the spirit of Refs.
[
DHVW85
,
DHVW86
]
). The construction involved fusion (through defect junctions and the attendant bicategorial 2-cells) of the 1-cells attached to the inherently topological discrete-symmetry defect lines, from which the cohomological Moore–Seiberg data of Ref.
[
MS89
]
of the relevant simple-current sector were extracted in Ref.
[
RS09
]
in the setting of the Wess–Zumino–Witten
\sigma
-model of Ref.
[
Wit84
]
. An adaptation of the idea to the substantially more structured case of a continuous gauge symmetry (investigated in the bicategorial framework in Refs.
[
GSW10
,
GSW13
]
) subsequently led the Author, in a study reported in Refs.
[
Sus12
,
Sus13
]
, to a reinterpretation (and hence an independent justification) of a full-fledged
{\rm G}_{\sigma}
-equivariant structure on the cells of
\,\mathfrak{BGrb}^{\nabla}(M\sqcup Q\sqcup T)
, defining a
\sigma
-model with a continuous rigid symmetry
\,{\rm G}_{\sigma}\,
(and with
{\rm G}_{\sigma}
-symmetric defects), as data of a topological gauge-symmetry defect implementing the gauging of
\,{\rm G}_{\sigma}\,
in what may be viewed as a generalisation of the worldsheet-orbifold scheme of Refs.
[
DHVW85
,
DHVW86
]
. The bicategorial framework derived in Ref.
[
RS09
]
was also applied in the study of T-duality presented in Ref.
[
SS08
]
(
cf.
also Ref.
[
Sus12
]
), and – finally – in the analysis of elementary (
i.e.
, ternary) fusion of the maximally symmetric defects of the WZW
\sigma
-model with the target
\,{\rm SU}(2)\,
(
cf.
Ref.
[
FSW08
]
) reported in Ref.
[
RS11
]
, which pointed towards a novel geometric interpretation of the Verlinde fusion rules of the chiral fusion ring of the
\sigma
-model in terms of the relevant elementary-fusion 2-cells.

The above recapitulation sets the scene for the study documented herein, and delineates its broader context. The study proposes, in its Part
I
, a complete framework for the lagrangean description of the dynamics of the (Nambu–Goto–)Green–Schwarz type (
cf.
Refs.
[
dAL82
,
GS84b
,
GS84a
]
) of (super)charged loops in a supermanifold (or a disjoint union thereof) in the presence of an arbitrary defect graph embedded in the 2
d
spacetime. It subsequently examines at great length a particular (general) instantiation of the framework, inspired by the analysis and use of the so-called induction of 2-cell data associated with valence
\,>3\,
defect junctions of topological defect lines from those of elementary (ternary) 2-cells in the un-graded setting of Refs.
[
RS09
,
Sus12
,
Sus13
]
, to wit, simplicial hierarchies of targets and the attendant simplicial gerbes, in which much of the higher-geometric analysis localises neatly on the first three levels of the hierarchy (indexed, form level 3 upwards, by the valence of the corresponding defect junctions): the bulk level (0) with its 0-cell (a gerbe), the correspondence level (1) with its 1-cell (a gerbe bi-module), and the elementary-fusion level (3) with its 2-cell (an elementary fusion 2-isomorphism). The final act of the general discussion sees simplicial hierarchy enriched equivariantly by an action of a (Lie) (super)group of rigid symmetries of the dynamics under consideration – this places us in the context of (super)field theory with maximally symmetric defects,
i.e.
, those fully transmissive to the Noether currents flowing in the bulk. The ensuing 2
d
superfield theory with defects is to be realised in Freed’s (inner
{\rm Hom}-)
functorial scheme delineated in Ref.
[
Fre99
]
(
i.e.
, in particular, with the spacetime of the theory kept Graßmann-
even
, unlike in,
e.g.
, Refs.
[
NR20
,
RW20
]
, and in contrast to the superembedding scheme of Ref.
[
Sor00
]
), and constitutes a consistent transcription of the proposal of Ref.
[
RS09
]
into the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded setting of (higher) target supergeometry, which allows for a unified treatment of bosonic and fermionic degrees of freedom of the super-charged loop (or superstring) – propagating across defects or, dually, twisted by the defect – within the higher-cohomological paradigm introduced (in the un-graded setting) by Gawȩdzki in his seminal paper
[
Gaw88
]
, and subsequently rendered higher-geometric and -categorial by Murray in the foundational Ref.
[
Mur96
]
and by his followers. The transcription rests heavily – implicitly and, at later stages, explicitly – upon the recent
constructive
results in the theory of (supersymmetric) gerbes on supermanifolds, reported in Refs.
[
Sus17
,
Sus19
,
Sus18a
,
Sus18b
,
Sus20
,
Sus21a
,
Sus21b
]
, and upon an obvious bicategorial extension of the very general study of gerbes on supermanifolds in Ref.
[
Hue20
]
. The ultimate goal here is a systematic incorporation of supersymmetry present in the world-sheet theories of super-
p
-branes of the superstring theory in the lagrangean modelling of their dynamics in the presence of worldvolume defects preserving all or some of the bulk supersymmetry. When viewed from this perspective, the current analysis remains, admittedly, incomplete, in that it only deals, at later stages of its specialisation, with the
global
(left) supersymmetry of the super-
\sigma
-model with defects – indeed, this supersymmetry is consistently built into the description following the original idea of Ref.
[
Sus17
]
. Investigation of the fate, in the presence of the rigidly supersymmetric defects, of the ‘infinitesimal gauge’ supersymmetry of Refs.
[
dAL82
,
Sie84
,
Sie83
]
, responsible for the restoration of balance between the two species of degrees of freedom in the vacuum of the bulk theory (aka
\kappa
-symmetry) and recently placed in the higher-geometric context in Refs.
[
Sus20
,
Sus21a
,
Sus21b
]
, is, on the other hand, postponed to a separate future study. Upon the latter augmentation, the approach to the dynamics of extended distributions of super-charge advocated herein is expected to provide insights into the structure of the relevant superfield theories similar in their breadth and depth to those offered by its predecessor in the un-graded setting, as recapitulated succinctly,
e.g.
, in the review Ref.
[
Sus21a
]
, and so, in particular, to contribute significantly to the development of the modern study of generalised symmetries invoked in the opening paragraph. This expectation, further backed up by the novel findings on deep structural relations between the higher geometry behind the fusion of the maximally symmetric defects of the WZW
\sigma
-model and the non-perturbative content of the (chiral) bulk theory (as encoded in the associated 3
d
topological Chern–Simons field theory), which we present in Part
II
, becomes the rationale for the study, developed in Part
III
, of the very first – to the best of the Author’s knowledge – examples of higher-supergeometric 1- and 2-cells associated with maximally (rigid-)supersymmetric defects in the Green–Schwarz super-
\sigma
-model of superstring dynamics in the flat supertarget
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})
. Thus, in both the un-graded and the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded geometric (target) sceneries, we put some flesh on the bones of the formal constructions of Part
I
by considering explicit examples of (super)string backgrounds for which the general principles are seen or expected to work, to wit, those whose 1-cells implement the fundamental symmetry of the bulk target – the right regular translations in the target Lie (super)group.

Let us now be more specific as to the contents of the present paper. It starts with a systematic introduction, given in Sec.
1
, of all the geometric ingredients – topological, tensorial and, ultimately, also higher-geometric – of the definition of the super-
\sigma
-model of the Green–Schwarz type on the mapping supermanifold for – as the domain – a Graßmann-even closed 2
d
spacetime (the worldsheet) with an embedded defect graph
\,\Gamma\,
and – as the codomain – a disjoint union of supermanifolds (the target super-space) related by a family of supermanifold morphisms reflecting the simplicial decomposition of the worldsheet induced by
\,\Gamma
. The latter comes with a ditinguished family of 0-cells (gerbes), 1-cells (gerbe bi-modules) and 2-cells (gerbe 2-isomorphisms) of the bicategory of gerbes with connective structure over the target super-space. The introduction, culminating in Definition
24
, is augmented with a detailed discussion, initiated in Sec.
1.2
and continued through Sec.
1.4
, of a special example of the target structures going into the definition in which data associated with defect junctions of arbitrary valence are induced from those defining the elementary (ternary) junctions, as they often do, through relative homotopy moves of defects junctions (resolutions and fusions), in the motivating case of topological defects implementing symmetries studied in Ref.
[
RS09
,
Sus12
]
, and in which the existence of the trivial (identity) defect is neatly encoded by certain ‘upstream’ mappings (the so-called degeneracy maps). The special example is provided by the simplicial superstring backgrounds of Def.
23
whose face maps
induce
the maps
\,T\longrightarrow Q\,
filtering through
\,T_{3}
, whence the induction. Their relevance to the present context essentially hinges on their elementary (combinatorial) properties stated in Props.
8
and
11
. In the next Sec.
2
(global) supersymmetry is incorporated in the three-tier architecture of the super-
\sigma
-models with defects introduced previously (and in particular – lifted from the super-target to the higher-geometric structure over it, and thus essentially categorified) and the notion of a maximally (super)symmetric background is introduced. Furthermore, a special construction of a supersymmetric background with explicit (built-in) supersymmetry is presented (in Def.
27
), which is at the heart of a programme, developed by the Author in a series of papers
[
Sus17
,
Sus19
,
Sus18a
,
Sus18b
,
Sus20
,
Sus21a
,
Sus21b
]
, of (
constructive
) supergeometrisation of the classes in the Cartan–Eilenberg cohomology of supersymmetry Lie superalgebras defining super-
\sigma
-models for the so-called super-
p
-branes of superstring theory in homogeneous spaces
\,{\rm G}/{\rm H}\,
of supersymmetry Lie supergroups
\,{\rm G}\,
(as in Refs.
[
dAL82
,
GS84a
,
GS84b
,
AETW87
,
MT98
,
Zho99
,
AF08
,
GSW09
,
FG12
,
DFGT09
]
. The supergeometrisation uses stepwise application (inspired by the works of de Azcárraga
et al.
,
cf.
,
e.g.
, Ref.
[
CdAIPB00
]
) of the bijective correspondence between classes in the second cohomology group
\,H^{2}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{a})\,
of a Lie superalgebra
\,\mathfrak{g}\,
with values in its trivial supercommutative module
\,\mathfrak{a}\,
and equivalence classes of supercentral extensions of
\,\mathfrak{g}\,
through
\,\mathfrak{a}
, and can be understood – morally (and prior to descent from
\,{\rm G}\,
to
\,{\rm G}/{\rm H}\,

via

{\rm H}
-equivariantisation, as dictated by a straightforward adaptation the gauge principle of Refs.
[
GSW10
,
GSW13
,
Sus12
]
) – as internalisation of the geometric construction due to Murray
et al.
from Refs.
[
Mur96
,
MS00
,
Ste00
,
Ste04
,
CJM04
,
Joh02
,
CJM+05
]
in the category of Lie supergroups. The general Part
I
concludes with a harmoniuos marriage, staged in Sec.
3
, between the principle of simplicial hierarchy and the principle of (maximal) (super)symmetry, blessed with the principle of (full) reducibility, whereby the fomer discussion winds up over a disjoint union of full orbits of the (super)symmetry group embedded in a simplicial
{\rm G}
-supermanifold. Upon categorification, after Ref.
[
CJM+05
]
, of the binary operation on the (super)group (whose appearance in this context, alongside local trivialisations of the bulk gerbe over (reduced-)supersymmetry orbits within the bulk target super-space, is carefully explained
ibidem
) – generalised suitably in Def.
28
and rendered compatible with supersymmetry in Def.
29
, with hindsight, so as to allow for a unified treatment of the un-graded and
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded examples studied afterwards – this last construction acquires a natural model, given by the Lie backgrounds introduced in Sec.
3.2
.

The next two parts of the paper provide an extensive illustration of the general considerations of Part
I
. Thus, first, in Part
II
, the un-graded maximally symmetric WZW simplicial backround is discussed with the first two rungs of the simplicial hierarchy given by a compact simple 1-connected Lie group
\,{\rm G}\,
with (the Cartan–Killing metric and) the Cartan 3-form and the associated integer tensor power of the basic gerbe of Ref.
[
Mei03
]
(the bulk) and the disjoint sum of the maximally symmetric bi-branes of Ref.
[
FSW08
]
(the correspondence), respectively. The bulk tensorial data are fixed by the requirement of a non-anomalous conformal symmetry of the ensuing 2
d
bulk field theory with the given target,
cf.
Ref.
[
Wit84
]
, and those of the bi-brane (and of the associated brane) are shown to be determined uniquely by the demand that the ensuing DGC be preserved by the loop-group extension of the left-right regular translational (resp. vector,
i.e.
, adjoint for the brane) symmetry of the target (Prop.
32
resp.
30
). Next, a proposal for the component of the target associated with junctions of the maximally symmetric defects is given in Def.
41
, based on the previous work reported in Refs.
[
RS09
,
RS11
]
and on the long chain of observations and inferences laid out in Secs.
4.4
,
4.5
and
5.4
. A distinguished place among them is occupied by Thm.
37
, one of the main concrete results of the paper, which asserts that the necessary condition (tensorial) for the existence of the maximally symmetric fusion 2-cells is satisfied
precisely
on the disjoint union of orbits of an appropriate lift of the left-right regular action to the (simplicial) nerve of the right-regular action groupoid of
\,{\rm G}\,
on itself deduced from the mixed simpliciality/symmetry(/reducibility) analysis of Sec.
4.4
. The theorem exploits an original interpretation of the said condition (on
\,T_{n}
) in terms of a partially symplectically reduced – à la Alekseev and Malkin (
cf.
Ref.
[
AM95
]
) – presymplectic form of the 3
d
group-
{\rm G}
Chern–Simons theory on the (timelike) cylinder
\,{\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathbb{S}}^{2}\,
over the 2-sphere punctured by a collection of (
n
) vertical (timelike) Wilson lines with holonomies from the same conjugacy classes as the (incoming) ones entering the definition of the component maximally symmetric bi-branes under fusion (Prop.
36
). The remarkable new link between the 2
d
WZW
\sigma
-model and the 3
d
CS theory thus established extends nontrivially the hitherto evidence on the deep structural relationship between the two studied extensively in the past (
cf.
Refs.
[
Wit89b
,
Gaw89
,
Wit92
,
Gaw99
,
GTTNB04
]
) and forming the basis of the functorial quantisation scheme for the WZW RCFT advanced in Ref.
[
FFFS02
,
FRS02
]
. Furthermore, it harmonises nicely with the general findings of Ref.
[
Sus12
]
on the transgression (into symmetry intertwiners) of the fusion 2-cells in the presence of a continuous bulk symmetry preserved at the defect. All this, put in conjunction with the results of Ref.
[
FFRS07
]
on the relation between the so-called Verlinde fusion rules of the WZW
\sigma
-model and junctions of the maximally symmetric defects (in the TFT-based quantum-mechanical description of the WZW RCFT), leads – through a gerbe-theoretic analysis of Sec.
5
making ample use of the multiplicative structure on the basic gerbe – to a very natural conjectural identification of the (higher-)geometric structure encoding the said fusion rules (Conjecture
1
) and to a definition of a bicategorial fusing matrix of the maximally symmetric WZW background (Def.
45
), anticipated to be intimately related to the corresponding component of the Moore–Seiberg data of the RCFT (
cf.
Ref.
[
MS89
]
), as – indeed – it was proven to be in the simple-current sector in Ref.
[
RS09
]
.

Part
III
provides an account of a pioneering, and hence – in particular – largely non-systematic exploration of the bicategorial structure associated with the Green–Schwarz super-
\sigma
-model of dynamics of the superstring in
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
(where
\,D_{d,1}\,
is the dimension of the relevant Majorana-spinor representation of the Clifford algebra
\,{\rm Cliff}({\mathbb{R}}^{d,1})
) in the presence of (candidate) maximally symmetric defects. Drawing inspiration from the long-known structural affinity between the 2
d
superfield theory and the bosonic WZW
\sigma
-model, noted already in Ref.
[
HM85
]
and more recently elaborated in Ref.
[
Sus19
]
, we adapt the definition of the bicategory for the maximally symmetric defect from Part
II
. In so doing, we take as the starting point the
explicit
construction, proposed in Ref.
[
Sus17
]
, of the Green–Schwarz super-1-gerbe
\,\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\,
(recalled in Sec.
6
), which (super)geometrises, in the hands-on and manifestly supersymmetric procedure à la de Azcárraga mentioned above, the Cartan–Eilenberg super-3-cocycle on the bulk Lie supergroup
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})
. In the first step, made in Sec.
7
, we prove the existence and explicitly reconstruct – essentially as a corollary to the non-obvious Prop.
46
– a supersymmetric generalised multiplicative structure on
\,\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\,
(Thm.
47
), another important result of the present paper. Next, in Sec.
8
, we identify, in a semi-systematic manner (drawing heavily on the un-graded intuition and the findings of Ref.
[
Sus21b
]
, anticipated in Ref.
[
Sus21a
]
and concerning the behaviour of
\,\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\,
in restriction to the vacuum of the super-
\sigma
-model in the dual, purely topological Hughes–Polchinsky formulation of Ref.
[
HP86
]
,
cf.
also Refs.
[
GIT90
,
Sus20
]
) three classes of (rigidly) supersymmetric
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-branes (Props.
48
,
49
and
53
). Upon coupling to the multiplicative structure on
\,\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\,
derived previously, these yield – in a manner fully analogous to the one originally devised in Ref.
[
FSW08
]
– the corresponding three classes of maximally (rigidly) supersymmetric
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-bi-branes of Thm.
54
. The case study in the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded setting closes with a detailed analysis – carried out on all three levels: equivariantly geometric, tensorial and higher-geometric in Sec.
9
– of the elementary fusion of the newly found species of super-bi-brane, summarised in Thm.
55
.

The general considerations and constructions presented in Part
I
and the detailed discussion, in Parts
II
and
III
, of the highly symmetric field theories of the (super-)WZW type in which they are seen to be of relevance leave us with a number of interesting questions and open several avenues of prospective research, which we indicate in the closing section of (the main text of) the paper, and to which we hope to return in near future.

Part I
Simplicial
\sigma
-models with (super)symmetry – general structures

In this opening part, we give a formal definition of the two-dimensional lagrangean superfield theory describing the dynamics of a (super-)charged loop in (the body of) an ambient supermanifold in the presence of an arbitrary defect embedded in the two-dimensional spacetime. The definition is a fairly straightforward generalisation of the one derived by Runkel and the Author in the setting of the bosonic two-dimensional
\sigma
-model with the Wess–Zumino term in Ref.
[
RS09
]
. Upon setting up the general bicategorial structure over the configuration (target) space of the superfield theory, we discuss at some length a particularly natural instantiation of the abstract definition, inspired by the study of topological defects in Refs.
[
RS09
,
Sus11a
,
Sus12
]
, in which the inherently simplicial relations between elements of the decomposition of the spacetime of the theory induced by the embedded defect graph are faithfully encoded in a hierarchical structure of the target space and the higher-geometric object over it, leading to induction of the data for defect junctions of valence higher than 3 from those of the elementary (ternary) ones. Subsequent coherent incorporation of the rigid (super)symmetry of the bulk theory into the simplicial picture leads to the emergence of distinguished maximally symmetric backgrounds, the main subject of this study. Final specialisation to the most elementary highly symmetric setting of a (target) Lie-(super)group (super)geometry then leads us to a systematic construction of a large class of defects compatible with the (super)symmetry of the phases of the 2d theory separated by them. The construction employs a suitable categorification of the constitutive binary operation on the target (super)group, and provides novel insights into the original 2d (super)field theory which base on the ensuing igher-geometric realisation of its fundamental rigid symmetry induced by the binary operation – these we consider subsequently in the next two parts.

1.
From decorated (super)string worldsheets to target bicategories

As our aim here is to put the study of supersymmetric defects of superstring theory in the higher-geometric framework developed in Refs.
[
Sus17
,
Sus19
,
Sus18a
,
Sus18b
,
Sus20
,
Sus21a
,
Sus21b
]
, we begin by adapting the formalism laid out in Ref.
[
RS09
]
(
cf.
also Refs.
[
RS11
,
Sus11a
,
Sus12
]
, and, in particular, the pioneering Ref.
[
FSW08
]
) for the study of multi-phase bosonic two-dimensional
\sigma
-models to the supersymmetric
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded setting of interest.

1.1.
Super-
\sigma
-models with defects

In the absence of defects,
superfield configurations
of the super-
\sigma
-model over a two-dimensional (purely Graßmann-even) oriented closed manifold
\,\Sigma
, termed the
worldsheet
, that describes propagation of a (closed) superstring in (the body of) a supermanifold
\,M
, termed the (
super
)
target
, are defined to be ‘elements’ of the mapping supermanifold

\displaystyle[\Sigma,M]\equiv\underline{{\rm Hom}}{}_{\rm{\bf sMan}}(\Sigma,M):={\rm Hom}_{\rm{\bf sMan}}(\Sigma\times-,M)\ :\ {\rm{\bf sMan}}\longrightarrow{\rm{\bf Set}}\,,

evaluated on the family
\,\{{\mathbb{R}}^{0|N}\}^{N\in{\mathbb{N}}}\,
of Graßmann-odd hyperplanes
1
1

1

Accordingly, whenever we consider restrictions (or extensions) of superfield configurations in the present paper, they are to be understood ‘functorially’.
, as proposed and elucidated by Freed in Ref.
[
Fre99
]
. The (super)target is assumed to carry an even metric tensor (degenerate in the Graßmann-odd coordinate directions) and a de Rham-closed even super-3-form field. In the presence of defects, the worldsheet and the supertarget are equipped with more structure, and field configurations have to satisfy additional conditions. Let us start by defining a worldsheet with defects.

Definition 1
.

An
oriented worldsheet with defects
(to be referred to simply as a
worldsheet
henceforth) is a pair
\,(\Sigma,d)\,
composed of a closed oriented two-dimensional manifold
\,\Sigma\,
and a distinguished decomposition
\,d\ :\ \Sigma\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\Sigma^{(2)}\cup\Sigma^{(1)}\cup\Sigma^{(0)}\,
into submanifolds subject to the following conditions:

(1)

\,\Sigma^{(k)}\,
is an oriented
k
-dimensional submanifold of
\,\Sigma
, with, in particular, the orientation of
\,\Sigma^{(2)}\,
induced by that of
\,\Sigma
;

(2)

\,\Sigma^{(k)}\cap\Sigma^{(l)}=\emptyset\,
for
\,k\neq l
, and
\,\Sigma^{(0)}\cup\Sigma^{(1)}\equiv\Gamma\,
is a closed subset in
\,\Sigma\,
(sometimes called the
defect quiver
).

The connected components of the defect quiver of dimension 1 (
i.e.
, those from
\,\Sigma^{(1)}
) are called
defect lines
, and those of dimension 0 (
i.e.
, from
\,\Sigma^{(0)}
) are called
defect junctions
.

\diamond

Remark 2
.

Note that any open subset
\,U\subset\Sigma\,
of a worldsheet with defects
\,(\Sigma,d)\,
gives rise to a worldsheet with defects
\,(U,d\mathord{\restriction}_{U})
. Note also that condition (2) is nontrivial in that it ensures that the three unions:
\,\Sigma^{(0)}
,
\Sigma^{(0)}\cup\Sigma^{(1)}
, and
\,\Sigma^{(0)}\cup\Sigma^{(1)}\cup\Sigma^{(2)}\,
are closed in
\,\Sigma
, which, in turn, implies that
\,\overline{\Sigma^{(k)}}\setminus\Sigma^{(k)}\,
is contained in the union of lower dimensional components.

Although we shall not make much use of the extra structure in the remainder of the present paper, we add, for completeness, that a
morphism
between two worldsheets with defects:
\,(\Sigma_{1},d_{1})\,
and
\,(\Sigma_{2},d_{2})\,
is a smooth orientation-preserving map
\,f\ :\ \Sigma_{1}\longrightarrow\Sigma_{2}\,
which is a diffeomorphism onto its image and compatible with the respective decompositions. This means that for
\,k\in\{0,1,2\}
, we have
\,f(\Sigma^{(k)}_{1})\subset\Sigma_{2}^{(k)}\,
and
\,f\mathord{\restriction}_{\Sigma_{1}^{(k)}}\,
is orientation-preserving.

Remark 3
.

The definition of the super-
\sigma
-model with defects makes ample use of disjoint sums of supermanifolds of various superdimensions. For the sake of brevity, we shall call any such disjoint union a
super-space
in what follows.

The
target super-space
of a super-
\sigma
-model with defects over a worldsheet
\,(\Sigma,d)\,
is a super-space
\,\mathscr{T}\,
with a disjoint-sum decomposition
\,\mathscr{T}=M\sqcup Q\sqcup T\,
and further structure. A
superfield configuration
of the super-
\sigma
-model is an ‘element’ of the mapping super-space
\,[\Sigma,\mathscr{T}]\,
(with an obvious meaning generalising the standard notion of the mapping supermanifold), which we shall denote as
\,\xi
, subject to certain coherence conditions. We shall describe the additional structure on the target super-space and the conditions imposed on a superfield configuration in parallel below.

The super-space
\,M

(T1)
The
bulk target super-space

\,M\,
is a super-space equipped with a Graßmann-even metric tensor
\,{\rm g}\,
(typically degenerate in the Graßmann-odd coordinate directions) and a de Rham-closed even super-3-form
\,{\rm H}
,

(1.1)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{d}}{\rm H}=0\,.

Just to underline: We do not require that
\,M\,
be connected or that all connected components of
\,M\,
have the same superdimension. The same holds for the super-spaces
\,Q\,
and
\,T\,
to be defined below.

To describe the
extension property
of a superfield configuration, we need some notation to distinguish the ‘values’ of the superfield taken on a little patch to the ‘left’ and to the ‘right’ of a defect line. Thus, let
\,(\Sigma,d),\ d\ :\ \Sigma\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\Sigma^{(2)}\cup\Sigma^{(1)}\cup\Sigma^{(0)}\,
be a worldsheet and let
\,U\,
be an open set in
\,\Sigma
. We say that
\,U\,

is split by

\,\Sigma^{(1)}\,

with decomposition

\,U=U_{1}\cup U_{\rm def}\cup U_{2}\,
if the following conditions hold:

•

\,U_{1},U_{2}\,
are open and
\,U_{\rm def}=\Sigma^{(1)}\cap U\,
is non-empty. The three sets:
\,U_{1},\ U_{2}\,
and
\,U_{\rm def}\,
are mutually disjoint, and
\,U_{1}\cup U_{\rm def}\,
and
\,U_{2}\cup U_{\rm def}\,
are path-connected.

•

Each point
\,p\in U_{\rm def}\,
has a neighbourhood
\,V\,
which is diffeomorphic to an open subset of
\,{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\,
such that
\,U_{\rm def}\,
gets mapped to the axis
\,{\mathbb{R}}\times\{0\}\,
in such a manner that the orientation is preserved,
\,U_{1}\,
gets mapped to the upper half plane and
\,U_{2}\,
to the lower half plane, also preserving the orientation.
Cf.
Fig.
4
for an illustration.

Intuitively, looking along the defect line in the direction of its orientation,
\,U_{1}\,
lies to the left of the defect, and
\,U_{2}\,
to its right. The conditions above ensure that the decomposition
\,U_{1}\cup U_{\rm def}\cup U_{2}\,
is unique.

Figure 4.
The splitting of an open neighbourhood
\,U\subset\Sigma\,
by a defect line, with the attendant model furnished by a diffeomorphism
\,f
.

(F1)
The restriction of
\,\xi\,
to
\,\Sigma^{(2)}\,
is an ‘element’ of
\,[\Sigma^{(2)},M]
. For
\,p\in\Sigma^{(1)}
, let
\,U\,
be an open neighbourhood of
\,p\,
which is split by
\,\Sigma^{(1)}\,
with the decomposition
\,U=U_{1}\cup U_{\rm def}\cup U_{2}
. Then,
\,\xi\mathord{\restriction}_{U_{1}}\,
has an extension to
\,U_{1}\cup U_{\rm def}\,
(in
\,[U_{1}\cup U_{\rm def},M]
), and
\,\xi\mathord{\restriction}_{U_{2}}\,
has an extension to
\,U_{2}\cup U_{\rm def}\,
(in
\,[U_{2}\cup U_{\rm def},M]
).

Condition (F1) allows to compare the ‘value’ of
\,\xi\,
and of its differential on either side of a defect line by taking limits approaching a point on
\,\Sigma^{(1)}\,
from the corresponding side. Below, we shall write the extension of
\,\xi\mathord{\restriction}_{U_{A}}\,
to
\,U_{A}\cup U_{\rm def}\,
as
\,\xi_{|A}
,
A\in\{1,2\}
, and we shall usually do so without explicitly specifying a choice of
\,U
.

The super-space
\,Q

(T2)
The
bi-brane superworldvolume
(or the
correspondence super-space
)
\,Q\,
is a super-space equipped with an even super-2-form
\,\omega\,
and two maps
\,\iota_{1},\iota_{2}\ :\ Q\longrightarrow M\,
such that

(1.2)

\displaystyle\Delta_{Q}{\rm H}=-{\mathsf{d}}\omega\,,

where

(1.3)

\displaystyle\Delta_{Q}:=\iota_{2}^{*}-\iota_{1}^{*}\,.

(F2)
The restriction of
\,\xi\,
to
\,\Sigma^{(1)}\,
is an ‘element’ of
\,[\Sigma^{(1)},Q]
. It satisfies the conditions
2
2

2

It is to be understood that the conditions are satisfied identically for
all

\,N\in{\mathbb{N}}\,
labelling the Graßmann-odd hyperplane completion of
\,\Sigma
.

\displaystyle\xi_{|A}\mathord{\restriction}_{U_{\rm def}}=\iota_{A}\circ\xi\mathord{\restriction}_{U_{\rm def}}\,,\qquad A\in\{1,2\}\,.

Condition (F2) constrains the possible ‘jumps’ that the superfield
\,\xi\,
may have across a defect line. There is also a second condition which constrains the change in the derivative of the superfield normal to the defect line. For this, we need to introduce a linear map
\,N\,
(for ‘normal’) which rotates a tangent vector on
\,\Sigma\,
into one orthogonal to it with respect to the euclidean metric
\,\delta^{(2,0)}_{\rm E}=\delta_{ij}\,{\mathsf{d}}\sigma^{i}\otimes{\mathsf{d}}\sigma^{j}\,
(in local worldsheet coordinates
\,\{\sigma^{i}\}^{i\in\{1,2\}}
),

(1.4)

\displaystyle N\ :\ \Gamma({\mathsf{T}}\Sigma)\longrightarrow\Gamma({\mathsf{T}}\Sigma)\ :\ v(\cdot)=v^{i}(\cdot)\,\tfrac{\partial\ }{\partial\sigma^{i}}\equiv v^{i}(\cdot)\,\partial_{i}\longmapsto v^{i}(\cdot)\,\epsilon_{ij}\,\delta^{jk}\,\partial_{k}\,,

where
\,\epsilon^{ij}=\epsilon_{ij}=\epsilon_{[ij]}\,
is the antisymmetric tensor in two dimensions, with the normalisation
\,\epsilon_{12}=1
. The map
\,N\,
squares to
\,N\circ N=-{\rm id}_{\Gamma({\mathsf{T}}\Sigma)}\,
and satisfies
\,\delta^{(2,0)}_{\rm E}(v,Nv)=0\,
and
\,\delta^{(2,0)}_{\rm E}(Nv,Nv)=\delta^{(2,0)}_{\rm E}(v,v)
. Note also that the pair
\,(v,Nv)\,
defines the standard orientation
\,[(\partial_{1},\partial_{2})]\,
of the euclidean worldsheet for any non-zero vector
\,v\in{\mathsf{T}}_{p}\Sigma
.

(F3)
– The
Defect Gluing Condition
(DGC): Let
\,t\in\Gamma({\mathsf{T}}\Sigma^{(1)})\,
be the vector field tangent to
\,\Sigma^{(1)}
. Then,

(1.5)

\displaystyle{\rm p}_{|2}\circ\iota_{2*}-{\rm p}_{|1}\circ\iota_{1*}=\omega_{\xi}(\cdot,\xi_{*}t)\,,

with

\displaystyle{\rm p}_{|A}=\sqrt{{\rm det}\,(\xi_{|A}^{*}{\rm g})}\cdot{\rm g}_{\xi_{|2}}\bigl(\cdot,\xi_{|A\,*}(Nt)\bigr)\,,\qquad A\in\{1,2\}\,.

Here,
\,\xi_{|A\,*}(Nt)\,
is understood as the limit of
\,\xi_{*}(Nt)\,
in which
\,x\in\Sigma^{(2)}\,
approaches the defect line from the appropriate side (namely, inside
\,U_{A}\,
in the notation of condition (F1)). This condition was derived in Ref.
[
RS09
]
by varying the
\sigma
-model action functional
3
3

3

In Ref.
[
RS09
]
, the so-called Polyakov formulation of the
\sigma
-model was considered, but this only affects the metric (‘kinetic’) term and it is straightforward to adapt the calculation in Ref.
[
RS09
, App. A.2]
to the definition of the action functional given in Eq. (
1.95
).
and demanding that the ‘boundary’ terms of the variation, localised at
\,\Sigma^{(1)}\,
vanish. It was subsequently shown, in Ref.
[
Sus11a
]
, to define an isotropic subspace in the two-phase space of states of the
\sigma
-model (the
\,{\rm p}_{|A}\,
acquiring the interpretation of the limiting values of the component of the kinetic momentum normal to the defect line on either side of the latter – clearly, the momentum suffers a discontinuity controlled by
\,\omega
), from which an explicit correspondence between defects and dualities of the
\sigma
-model was inferred. The correspondence is at the core of the construction of the gauge-symmetry defect laid out in Ref.
[
Sus12
]
.

The super-space
\,T

The super-space
\,T\,
is associated with junctions of defect lines, that is, it serves as the (super)target for
\,\xi\,
restricted to
\,\Sigma^{(0)}
. We shall only consider junctions which have
\,n\,
defect lines oriented towards the junction and one line oriented away from it. For the general discussion with arbitrary orientations, we refer the reader to
4
4

4

In Ref.
[
RS09
]
, no orientation was assigned to defect junctions. From the point of view of this article, this means that all junctions in Ref.
[

]
implicitly carry orientation ‘
+
’.
Ref.
[
RS09
, Sect. 2.5]
. In the present restricted setting, each point
\,x\in\Sigma^{(0)}\,
has a neighbourhood which is diffeomorphic to the two worldsheets shown in Fig.
5
. The super-space
\,T\,
has components
\,T_{n,1},\ n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}\,
to which a junction point
\,x\,
with

for an illustration of the case
\,n=2

(resp.

inter-bi-brane superworldvolume
(resp. the
(n,1)
-valent component inter-bi-brane superworldvolume
).

(T3)
The super-space
\,T\,
has a disjoint-sum decomposition
\,T=\bigsqcup_{n=2}^{\infty}T_{n,1}
. Each component
\,T_{n,1}\,

\,\pi_{1,n+1}^{(n+1)}\,
and

from

to
\,Q
. These maps satisfy the compatibility conditions

3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\,.

(1.25)

The pullbacks of the 2-form
\,\omega\,
from
\,Q\,
satisfy
the
Defect-Junction Identity
(DJI)

(1.26)

\displaystyle\Delta_{T_{n,1}}\omega=0\,,

where

\displaystyle\Delta_{T_{n,1}}:=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\,\pi_{k,k+1}^{(n+1)\,*}-\pi_{1,n+1}^{(n+1)\,*}\,.

Condition (
1.26
) was derived in Ref.
[
RS09
, Sect. 2.7]
from the requirement of (higher) gauge invariance of the
\sigma
-model action functional in the presence of defect junctions combined with the variational principle for the action functional. The said requirement translates into the necessary condition for the existence of a certain 2-isomorphism between 1-isomorphisms of 1-gerbes – this will be restated in the supergemoetric setting (on the basis of Ref.
[
RS09
]
) in Sec.
1.4
.

Condition (
1.25
) merely encodes the compatibility of the different extensions of the super-
\sigma
-model superfield
\,\xi
, as we now describe. In a small neighbourhood
\,V\,
around a point
\,v\in\Sigma^{(0)}
, as shown in Fig.
5
, the restriction of
\,\xi\,
to each connected component
\,I\,
of
\,\Sigma^{(1)}\cap V\,
(an open interval) has an extension to
\,I\cup\{v\}
. We denote these extensions by
\,\xi_{|k,k+1}\,
and

\,\xi_{|1,n+1}
, with a labelling as shown in Fig.
5
. For a field configuration close to a junction point, we require that these extensions allow a consistent extension of
\,\xi\,
in each of the wedges
\,1,2,\ldots,n+1\,
in Fig.
5
to
\,v
.

Figure 5.
Extensions (marked in violet) of the (super)field configuration to the closures
\,\ell_{|k,k+1}\,
and
\,\ell_{|1,n+1}\,
of the defect lines converging at a given defect junction
\,v\,
of valence
\,n+1
.

(F4)
Let
\,v\in\Sigma^{(0)}\,
have a neighbourhood as shown in Fig.
5
. The mapping
\,\xi\,
satisfies
5
5

5

The seemingly artificial notation should be read along the lines of footnote
2
.

\,\xi\mathord{\restriction}_{\{v\}}\in[\{v\},T_{n,1}]
, and
\,\pi_{1,n+1}^{(n+1)}\circ\xi\mathord{\restriction}_{\{v\}}=\xi_{|1,n+1}\mathord{\restriction}_{\{v\}}\,
and

\,\pi_{k,k+1}^{(n+1)}\circ\xi\mathord{\restriction}_{\{v\}}=\xi_{|k,k+1}\mathord{\restriction}_{\{v\}},\ k\in\overline{1,n}
.

Consider for example the defect line labelled
\,\ell_{1,2}\,
in Fig.
5
. The restriction
\,\xi_{|1,2}\mathord{\restriction}_{\{v\}}
, mapping to
\,Q
, is obtained as the limit of
\,\xi\mathord{\restriction}_{\ell_{1,2}}\,
in which a point
\,q\in\ell_{1,2}\,
approaches
\,v
. Since
\,\iota_{2}\circ\xi\mathord{\restriction}_{\ell_{1,2}}=\xi_{|2}\mathord{\restriction}_{\ell_{1,2}}
, mapping to
\,M
, is, itself, the limit of
\,\xi\mathord{\restriction}_{p_{2}}
, restricted to the wedge labelled
\,p_{2}
, in which
\,p\in p_{2}\,
approaches
\,q
, we see that the limit
\,p\to v
, taken in the wedge
\,p_{2}
, gives
\,\iota_{2}\circ\xi_{|1,2}\mathord{\restriction}_{\{v\}}=\iota_{2}\circ\pi_{1,2}^{(3)}\circ\xi\mathord{\restriction}_{\{v\}}
. If we repeat this procedure with respect to the defect line labelled
\,\ell_{2,3}
, we obtain the limiting value
\,\iota_{1}\circ\pi_{2,3}^{(3)}\circ\xi\mathord{\restriction}_{\{v\}}
. These two values agree in virtue of condition (
1.25
), and this is the reason to impose the condition in the first place.

Let us package the above description in two definitions:

Definition 4
.

A
target super-space

\,\mathscr{T}\,

for a multi-phase super-
\sigma
-model with defects
(or a
target super-space
for brevity) is a super-space
\,\mathscr{T}\,
with a disjoint-sum decomposition
\,\mathscr{T}=M\sqcup Q\sqcup T\,
having the properties described in (T1), (T2) and (T3).

\diamond

and

Definition 5
.

A
superfield configuration over a worldsheet with defects

\,(\Sigma,d)\,

of the multi-phase super-
\sigma
-model with the target super-space

\,\mathscr{T}\,
(or a
superfield configuration
for brevity) is an ‘element’
\,\xi\in[\Sigma,\mathscr{T}]\,
such that
\,\xi\mathord{\restriction}_{\Sigma^{(2)}}\in[\Sigma^{(2)},M]
,
\xi\mathord{\restriction}_{\Sigma^{(1)}}\in[\Sigma^{(1)},Q]\,
and
\,\xi\mathord{\restriction}_{\Sigma^{(0)}}\in[\Sigma^{(0)},T]
, with the restrictions subject to the conditions (F1), (F2), (F3) and (F4). We shall collectively denote the entirety of superfield configurations over a worldsheet with defects
\,(\Sigma,d)\,
as
\,\mathcal{F}(\Sigma,d)
.

\diamond

We now come to the superfield equations (of evolution). The
Dirac–Feynman amplitude of the multi-phase super-
\sigma
-model over a worldsheet with defects

\,(\Sigma,d)\,
(to be given in Sec.
1.4
) is a ‘functional’
6
6

6

It makes sense to think of it as a (nested) family of functionals labelled by the set
\,{\mathbb{N}}\ni N\,
of superdimensions
\,(0,N)\,
of the Graßmann-odd hyperplanes
\,{\mathbb{R}}^{0|N}\,
mentioned earlier.

\,\mathcal{A}_{\rm DF}\equiv{\rm e}^{{\mathsf{i}}\,S}\,
on
\,\mathcal{F}(\Sigma,d)
, typically expressed in terms of an action ‘functional’
\,S
. The corresponding superfield equations are obtained by varying the DF amplitude
\,\mathcal{A}_{\rm DF}\,
along an arbitrary path in
\,\mathcal{F}(\Sigma,d)
; in the case of the multi-phase (super-)
\sigma
-model (in the Polyakov formulation) with a purely Graßmann-even target (super-)space, this calculation was carried out in Ref.
[
RS09
, App. A.2]
, and it is straightforward to repeat the computation in the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded setting (in the spirit of Ref.
[
Fre99
]
). The result is that
\,\xi\in\mathcal{F}(\Sigma,d)\,
is a critical ‘point’ of the ‘functional’
\,\mathcal{A}_{\rm DF}\,
iff the restriction of
\,\xi\,
to
\,\Sigma^{(2)}\,
satisfies the following ‘differential equation’
7
7

7

Once again, we are dealing with a (nested) family of differential equations.

(1.27)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\delta^{ij}\,\bigl[{\rm g}\bigl(\cdot,\partial_{i}\bigl(\sqrt{{\rm det}\,(\xi^{*}{\rm g})}\,(\xi_{*}\partial_{j})^{a}\bigr)\,\tfrac{\partial\ }{\partial\xi^{a}}\bigr)-\sqrt{{\rm det}\,(\xi^{*}{\rm g})}\,\Gamma\bigl(\cdot,\xi_{*}\partial_{i},\xi_{*}\partial_{j}\bigr)-{\rm H}\bigl(\cdot,\xi_{*}\partial_{i},\xi_{*}N\partial_{j}\bigr)\bigr]=0\,,

expressed in the local (super)coordinates
\,\xi^{a}\,
on
\,M
, and in terms of the Christoffel form
\,\Gamma\,
of
\,{\rm g}
, the latter evaluating on a triple
\,(\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathcal{V}_{2},\mathcal{V}_{3})\,
of (Graßmann-even) vector fields on
\,M\,
as

\displaystyle-2\Gamma(\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathcal{V}_{2},\mathcal{V}_{3})=\mathcal{V}_{1}^{a}\,\partial_{a}{\rm g}_{bc}\,\mathcal{V}_{2}^{b}\,\mathcal{V}_{3}^{c}-\mathcal{V}_{2}^{b}\,\partial_{b}{\rm g}_{ac}\,\mathcal{V}_{1}^{a}\,\mathcal{V}_{3}^{c}-\mathcal{V}_{3}^{c}\,\partial_{c}{\rm g}_{ba}\,\mathcal{V}_{2}^{b}\,\mathcal{V}_{1}^{a}\,.

World-sheets with boundary

We shall briefly describe, along the lines of Ref.
[
RS09
, Sect. 2.4]
, how worldsheets with boundary can be understood as a special case of worldsheets with defect lines. In the decomposition
\,\mathscr{T}=M\sqcup Q\,
(we have
\,T=\emptyset\,
in this case), choose
\,M=N\sqcup{\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}
. The two maps
\,\iota_{A}\ :\ Q\longrightarrow M\,
are such that

3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 62.10449pt\raise-22.24854pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0566pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\jmath_{{\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 79.99448pt\raise-5.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\,,

where
\,\jmath_{X},\ X\in\{N,{\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}\}\,
are the canonical inclusions, and the dotted arrow represents the unique mapping of
\,Q\,
to the terminal object in
\,{\rm{\bf sMan}}
. Thus, in a small neighbourhood
\,U\,
of a point
\,p\in\Sigma^{(1)}\,
with a decomposition
\,U=U_{1}\cup U_{\rm def}\cup U_{2}\,
as before, the restriction of the superfield
\,\xi\,
to
\,U_{2}\,
has
\,{\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}\,
as the codomain. If we delete all the regions of the worldsheet which are mapped to
\,{\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}
, we obtain a worldsheet with a boundary. Let us write
\,Q_{\partial}\,
instead of
\,Q\,
under such circumstances, also later on when describing boundary conditions. The super-2-form on
\,Q_{\partial}\,
will be denoted as
\,\omega_{\partial}
. It satisfies

(1.28)

\displaystyle\iota_{1}^{*}{\rm H}={\mathsf{d}}\omega_{\partial}\,.

The DGC (
1.5
) turns into

(1.29)

\displaystyle{\rm p}_{|1}\circ\iota_{1*}=-\omega_{\partial\,\xi}(\cdot,\xi_{*}t)\,,

which is imposed for all
\,t\,
tangent to the worldsheet boundary. The super-space
\,Q_{\partial}\,
is called the
D-brane worldvolume
, and
\,\omega_{\partial}\,
is referred to as the
D-brane curvature
.

Typically, one takes
\,Q\equiv Q_{\partial}\,
to be a sub-supermanifold of
\,N\,
in the boundary setting. When discussing boundaries in the following, this is the situation that we shall (often) look at. We shall not make explicit the component
\,{\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}\,
of the target super-space, nor shall we (always) spell out the embedding map
\,\iota_{1}
.

1.2.
Simplicial target super-spaces

Our definition of the target super-space does not presuppose the existence of mappings between components of the inter-bi-brane superworldvolume associated with defect junctions of different valence which would be consistent with the compatibility conditions (
1.25
) or which would imply the latter for components of higher valence once they are satisfied for components of lower valence, and, indeed, nothing forces us to impose such relations generically. Neither does it reflect (automatically) the physically quite natural possibility to enhance any pre-existing defect through adjunction of an arbitrary number of identity defect lines (either intersecting the defect or not) across which the superfield continues smoothly. On the other hand, inspection of the prototypical poly-phase scenario, based on the concept of the pair groupoid, reveals a natural alternative. Indeed, consider a bi-brane worldvolume (assumed un-graded for the sake of simplicity) in the form of a submanifold
\,Q\subset M^{\times 2}\,
in the cartesian square of the bulk target space
\,M\,
and equipped with the obvious bi-brane maps
\,\iota_{A}={\rm pr}_{A}\ :\ Q\longrightarrow M\,
given by (the restrictions of) the canonical projections (to
\,Q
). It is now completely natural to take as the worldvolume
\,T_{2,1}\,
of the ‘elementary’ inter-bi-brane the intersection
\,T_{2,1}=\bigcap_{i\in\{1,2,3\}}\,\widehat{\rm pr}{}_{i}^{(3)\,-1}(Q)\subset M^{\times 3}\,
of the preimages of the correspondence space
\,Q\,
under the maps
\,\widehat{\rm pr}{}_{i}^{(3)}\ :\ M^{\times 3}\longrightarrow M^{\times 2},\ i\in\{1,2,3\}\,
of which the
i
-th one projects
out
the
i
-th factor,
i.e.
,
\,(\widehat{\rm pr}{}_{1}^{(3)},\widehat{\rm pr}{}_{2}^{(3)},\widehat{\rm pr}{}_{3}^{(3)})(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})=((x_{2},x_{3}),(x_{1},x_{3}),(x_{1},x_{2}))
, with the said projections as the inter-bi-brane maps,
\,(\pi^{(3)}_{1,2},\pi^{(3)}_{2,3},\pi^{(3)}_{1,3})=(\widehat{\rm pr}{}_{3}^{(3)},\widehat{\rm pr}{}_{1}^{(3)},\widehat{\rm pr}{}_{2}^{(3)})
. Proceeding recursively, we may now take
\,T_{n+1,1}\subset M^{\times n+2}\,
in the form
\,T_{n+1,1}=\bigcap_{i\in\overline{1,n+2}}\,\widehat{\rm pr}{}_{i}^{(n+2)\,-1}(T_{n,1})\,
where
\,\widehat{\rm pr}{}_{i}^{(n+1)}\ :\ T_{n+1,1}\longrightarrow T_{n,1}\ :\ (x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{n+2})\longmapsto(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{i-1},x_{i+1},x_{i+2},\ldots,x_{n+2})
. In order to work out the inter-bi-brane maps, we first change the representation of inter-bi-brane worldvolumes conveniently by referring to the worldsheet-defect picture: The
(n+1)
-tuple
\,(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{n+1})\in T_{n,1}\,
encodes the raw data of the discontinuities of the
\sigma
-model field in the
\,n+1\,
phases meeting at the defect junction of valence
\,(n,1)
, and these data can be equivalently transcribed as data of the
n
consecutive jumps of the field at the
n
incoming defect lines written as
\,((x_{1},x_{2}),(x_{2},x_{3}),\ldots,(x_{n},x_{n+1}))
, which reflects the obvious identification
\,M^{\times n+1}\equiv M^{\times 2}\times_{M}M^{\times 2}\times_{M}\cdots\times_{M}M^{\times 2}\,
(
n
copies). This new representation enables us to view the maps
\,\widehat{\rm pr}{}_{i}^{(n+1)}\,
from a different angle as the fibred product is none other than the submanifold of
composable
morphisms in the
n
-fold cartesian power of the morphism manifold
\,M^{\times 2}\,
of the pair groupoid of
\,M
. In this picture, the map
\,\widehat{\rm pr}{}_{i}^{(n+1)}\,
corresponds to the (post-)composition of the
i
-th morphism with the
(i-1)
-st one,
\,((x_{1},x_{2}),(x_{2},x_{3}),\ldots,(x_{n},x_{n+1}))\longmapsto((x_{1},x_{2}),(x_{2},x_{3}),\ldots,(x_{i-2},x_{i-1}),(x_{i},x_{i+1})\circ(x_{i-1},x_{i}),(x_{i+1},x_{i+2}),(x_{i+2},x_{i+3}),\ldots,(x_{n},x_{n+1}))
, which models the process of obtaining the defect junction through ‘fusion’ of the pair
\,(\ell_{i},\ell_{i+1})\,
of defect lines at a trivalent defect junction an infinitesimal distance
\,\epsilon>0\,
away from the original one of valence
\,(n,1)\,
and subsequently passing to the limit
\,\epsilon\to 0
,
cf.
[Figure:Ind] – a most natural scenario in the setting of the 2d Conformal Field Theory (with the so-called topological defects). The inter-bi-brane maps are now simply the canonical projections to the
n
cartesian factors
\,M^{\times 2}
, augmented by the ‘total-composition’ map which yields the pair
\,(x_{1},x_{n+1})\equiv(x_{n},x_{n+1})\circ(x_{n-1},x_{n})\circ\cdots\circ(x_{1},x_{2})
, and it is easy to see how these can be recovered from various
equivalent
fusion ‘cascades’, modelled by concatenations of the descent maps
\,T_{k,1}\longrightarrow T_{k-1,1},\ k\in\overline{n,3}\,
and the
\,\iota_{A}
. Such an
induction scheme
was originally conceived in Ref.
[
RS09
]
and later elaborated in Ref.
[
Sus11a
]
. As for the identity-defect enhancement, it is readily achieved in this setting by augmentation of the target-space structure
\,\mathscr{T}\,
above
\,M\,
by the
M
-fibred powers of the bulk target space and by all possible (multiple) extensions of its components by the
M
-fibred product with the target space
\,M\,
(to be marked symbolically by tildas on the enhanced worldvolumes), which enables us to consider defect-line and defect-junction data of the special form
\,((x_{1},x_{2}),(x_{2},x_{3}),\ldots,(x_{k-1},x_{k}),(x_{k},x_{k}),(x_{k},x_{k+1}),(x_{k+1},x_{k+2}),\ldots,(x_{n},x_{n+1}))\,
lying in the image of obvious embeddings
\,M\longrightarrow\widetilde{Q},\ \widetilde{Q}\longrightarrow\widetilde{T}{}_{2,1}\,
and
\,\widetilde{T}{}_{k-1,k}\longrightarrow\widetilde{T}{}_{k,1}
. The ensuing two families of maps going ‘upwards’ and ’downwards’ in the (enhanced) hierarchy of manifolds
\,(M,\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{T}{}_{2,1},\widetilde{T}{}_{3,1},\ldots)\,
satisfy some obvious identities expressing equivalences of distinguished pairs of paths in the hierarchy traced by their superpositions. Below, we formalise the resultant structure with view to its physically important implementation outside the model setting just described.

A useful abstraction of the properties of the above model target super-space, and a point of departure for our subsequent considerations is specified in

Definition 6
.

Let
\,\mathcal{C}\,
be a category with the set of objects
\,{\rm Ob}(\mathcal{C})
. A
simplicial object

\,(X_{\bullet},d^{(\bullet)}_{\cdot},s^{(\bullet)}_{\cdot})\,

in

\,\mathcal{C}\,
is a collection of objects
\,X_{n}\in{\rm Ob}(\mathcal{C}),\ n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, together with distinguished morphisms: the
face maps

\,d^{(n)}_{i}\in{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(X_{n},X_{n-1})\,
and the
degeneracy maps

\,s^{(n)}_{i}\in{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(X_{n},X_{n+1})
, defined for all
\,0\leq i\leq n\,
and satisfying the
simplicial identities
:

\displaystyle d^{(n-1)}_{i}\circ d^{(n)}_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d^{(n-1)}_{j-1}\circ d^{(n)}_{i}\,,\quad i<j\,,

(1.31)

\displaystyle s^{(n+1)}_{i}\circ s^{(n)}_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle s^{(n+1)}_{j+1}\circ s^{(n)}_{i}\,,\quad i\leq j\,,

(1.33)

\displaystyle d^{(n+1)}_{i}\circ s^{(n)}_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}s^{(n-1)}_{j-1}\circ d^{(n)}_{i}&\text{if}\quad i<j\,,\\
{\rm id}_{X_{n}}&\text{if}\quad i=j~\text{or}~i=j+1\,,\\
s^{(n-1)}_{j}\circ d^{(n)}_{i-1}&\textrm{if}\quad i>j+1\,.\end{cases}

A simplicial object in the category
\,{\rm{\bf Set}}\,
(resp.
\,{\rm{\bf Top}},\ ({\rm{\bf s}}){\rm{\bf Man}}\,

etc.
) is termed a
simplicial set
(resp.
space
, (
super
)
manifold

etc.
).

\diamond

A fundamental class of examples, generalising straightforwardly the model target space just described and thus of immediate relevance to us, is provided by nerves of categories (
cf.
Ref.
[
Seg68
]
).

Definition 7
.

Let
\,\mathcal{C}\,
be a small category with the set of objects
\,\mathrm{Ob}(\mathcal{C})
, the set of morphisms

\,\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{C})\,
and structure maps:
\,s\ :\ \mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{C})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Ob}(\mathcal{C})\,
(the source map
8
8

8

One should not confuse the source map
\,s\,
with a degeneracy map
\,s_{i}
.
),
\,t\ :\ \mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{C})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Ob}(\mathcal{C})\,
(the target map) and
\,{\rm id}_{\cdot}\ :\ \mathrm{Ob}(\mathcal{C})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{C})\,
(the identity map). The
nerve of

\,\mathcal{C}\,
is the simplicial set
\,{\mathsf{N}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})\,
with the following data:
\,{\mathsf{N}}_{0}(\mathcal{C})=\mathrm{Ob}(\mathcal{C})\,
and, for
\,n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle{\mathsf{N}}_{n}(\mathcal{C})=\{\ (f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{n})\in\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{C})^{\times n}\quad|\quad t(f_{i})=s(f_{i+1})\ \}\,,

i.e.
,
\,{\mathsf{N}}_{n}(\mathcal{C})\,
is the set of all
n
-tuples of composable morphisms (note that in this ordering convention
\,f_{i}\,
and
\,f_{i+1}\,
are composable if
\,f_{i+1}\circ f_{i}\,
makes sense). The degeneracy maps are:
\,s_{0}(a)={\rm id}_{a}\,
for
\,a\in\mathrm{Ob}(\mathcal{C})
, and
\,s_{i}^{(n)}(f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{n})=(f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{i},{\rm id}_{t(f_{i})},f_{i+1},\ldots,f_{n})\,
for
\,n\geq 1
. The face maps are:
\,d_{0}(f)=t(f)\,
and
\,d_{1}(f)=s(f)\,
for
\,f\in\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{C})
, and, for
\,n\geq 2
,

\displaystyle d_{i}^{(n)}(f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{n})=\begin{cases}(f_{2},f_{3},\ldots,f_{n})&\text{for}\quad i=0\,,\\
(f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{i+1}\circ f_{i},\ldots,f_{n})&\text{for}\quad 0<i<n\,,\\
(f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{n-1})&\text{for}\quad i=n\,.\end{cases}\,,

\diamond

That simplicial super-spaces provide a physically interesting abstraction of the properties of the nerve of the pair groupoid of the bulk target space, discussed earlier, and thus become distinguished candidates for target super-spaces is a direct consequence of the following

Proposition 8
.

Adopt the notation of Def.
6
. Let
\,\mathcal{C}\,
be a category and let
\,(X_{\bullet},d^{(\bullet)}_{\cdot},s^{(\bullet)}_{\cdot})\,
be a simplicial object in
\,\mathcal{C}
. For any
\,n\geq 2\,
there are (generically, i.e., without any further relations between the face maps) precisely
\,n+1\,
inequivalent ways to obtain morphisms
\,X_{n}\longrightarrow X_{1}\,
through composition of the face maps
\,d^{(k)}_{i},\ i\in\overline{0,k},\ k\in\overline{2,n}\,
and the morphisms thus induced admit presentations

\displaystyle\pi^{(n+1)}_{k,k+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d^{(2)}_{2}\circ d^{(3)}_{2}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(n-k+1)}_{2}\circ d^{(n-k+2)}_{0}\circ d^{(n-k+3)}_{0}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(n-1)}_{0}\circ d^{(n)}_{0}\,,\qquad k\in\overline{1,n}\,,

\displaystyle\pi^{(n+1)}_{1,n+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d^{(2)}_{1}\circ d^{(3)}_{1}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(n)}_{1}\,.

These satisfy the compatibility conditions

\displaystyle\raisebox{26.5777pt}{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern and - for

and

\displaystyle\pi^{(3)}_{i,j}\circ s^{(1)}_{k}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\iota_{1}&\textrm{if}\quad(i,j,k)=(1,2,0)\\
\iota_{2}&\textrm{if}\quad(i,j,k)=(2,3,1)\\

(1.58)

(1.59)

\displaystyle\pi^{(n+2)}_{1,n+2}\circ s^{(n)}_{i}=\pi^{(n+1)}_{1,n+1}\,,\qquad\qquad\pi^{(n+2)}_{l,l+1}\circ s^{(n)}_{m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\pi^{(n+1)}_{l-1,l}&\textrm{if}\quad l\geq m+2\\

where
\,\iota_{A}=s^{(0)}_{0}\circ d^{(1)}_{2-A}\ :\ X_{1}\circlearrowleft\,,\ A\in\{1,2\}
.

Proof.

.
∎

Before we conclude that simplicial super-spaces may, indeed, consistently model target super-spaces of multi-phase super-
\sigma
-models, we need to discuss the interpretation and fate of the identities satisfied by the various tensors supported on
\,X_{0}\,
and
\,X_{1}
. To this end, we now set up a natural framework
9
9

9

The underlying idea is an extension of the standard concept of relative cohomology,
cf.
the discussion in Ref.
[
Sus12
]
in the present setting.
for the description of the de Rham cohomology of a simplicial super-space.

Definition 9
.

Let
\,(X_{\bullet},d^{(\bullet)}_{\cdot},s^{(\bullet)}_{\cdot})\,
be a simplicial super-space. The
simplicial de Rham cohomology of

\,(X_{\bullet},d^{(\bullet)}_{\cdot},s^{(\bullet)}_{\cdot})\,
is the total cohomology of the semi-bounded bicomplex

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.75pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.38857pt\raise-25.91328pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.82558pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\mathsf{d}}^{p-1}_{(X_{n})}}$}}}\kern 55.27031pt\raise-56.65706pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.88391pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Delta^{(p)}_{n}}$}}}\kern
