3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\,,\qquad p,n\in{\mathbb{N}}

with the de Rham exterior derivatives as the horizontal coboundary operators and with the vertical coboundary operators

\displaystyle\Delta^{(p)}_{n}\equiv\sum_{i=0}^{n+1}\,(-1)^{i}\,d^{(n+1)\,*}_{i}\ :\ \Omega^{p}(X_{n})\longrightarrow\Omega^{p}(X_{n+1})\,,\qquad(p,n)\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\times 2}\,,

i.e.
, the cohomology of the diagonal cochain complex (a.k.a. the
simplicial de Rham complex
)

\displaystyle\bigl(\mathcal{A}^{\bullet}(X_{\bullet}),\mathcal{D}^{\bullet}\bigr)\qquad:\qquad\mathcal{A}^{0}(X_{\bullet})\xrightarrow{\ \mathcal{D}_{0}\ }\mathcal{A}^{1}(X_{\bullet})\xrightarrow{\ \mathcal{D}_{1}\ }\cdots\xrightarrow{\ \mathcal{D}_{q-1}\ }\mathcal{A}^{q}(X_{\bullet})\xrightarrow{\ \mathcal{D}_{q}\ }\cdots

with the
q
-cochain groups

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{q}(X_{\bullet})=\bigoplus_{p=0}^{q}\,A^{p,q-p}(X_{\bullet})\,,\qquad\qquad A^{p,q-p}(X_{\bullet})\equiv\Omega^{p}(X_{q-p})\,,\qquad\qquad q\in{\mathbb{N}}

related by the couboundary operators

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{q}\ :\ \mathcal{A}^{q}(X_{\bullet})\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{q+1}(X_{\bullet})\,,\qquad\qquad\mathcal{D}^{q}\mathord{\restriction}_{A^{p,q-p}(X_{\bullet})}=(-1)^{q-p+1}\,{\mathsf{d}}^{p}_{(X_{q-p})}+\Delta^{(p)}_{q-p}\,.

\diamond

Remark 10
.

The definition is a standard one for a pair of families of commuting coboundary operators, and so the only nontrivial fact which renders it meaningful is the following elementary property of the weighted-pullback operators (written for arbitrary
\,p,n\in{\mathbb{N}}
):
\,\Delta^{(p)}_{n+1}\circ\Delta^{(p)}_{n}=0
, whose verification uses the simplicial identities. The de Rham cohomology of simplicial manifolds was originally considered by Dupont in Ref.
[
Dup76
]
(
cf.
also Ref.
[
Dup88
]
).

The naturality of the cohomological framework proposed is put on display by the simple observation: If we were to think – in conformity with Prop.
8
– of the bulk target super-space
\,M
, the bi-brane superworldvolume
\,Q\,
and the component inter-bi-brane superworldvolume
\,T_{2,1}\,
as the first three elements of a simplicial super-space
\,(X_{\bullet},d^{(\bullet)}_{\cdot},s^{(\bullet)}_{\cdot})
,
i.e.
, if we were to set
\,(X_{0},X_{1},X_{2})\equiv(M,Q,T_{2,1})\,
with the previously suggested identification of the corresponding face maps
\,(d^{(1)}_{0},d^{(1)}_{1})\equiv(\iota_{2},\iota_{1})\,
and
\,(d^{(2)}_{0},d^{(2)}_{1},d^{(2)}_{2})\equiv(\pi^{(3)}_{2,3},\pi^{(3)}_{1,3},\pi^{(3)}_{1,2})
, then the identities (
1.1
), (
1.2
) and (
1.26
) satisfied by the triple
\,({\rm H},\omega,0,0)\in\Omega^{3}(X_{0})\oplus\Omega^{2}(X_{1})\oplus\Omega^{1}(X_{2})\oplus\Omega^{0}(X_{3})\,
would make it a 3-cocycle in the relative cohomology of
\,(X_{\bullet},d^{(\bullet)}_{\cdot},s^{(\bullet)}_{\cdot})
. Of course, we should then still have to impose the DJI’s coming from defect junctions of valence
\,(n,1),\ n>2
, but it turns out that the simplicial identities would come to our rescue once more, as clarified in

Proposition 11
.

Let
\,(X_{\bullet},d^{(\bullet)}_{\cdot},s^{(\bullet)}_{\cdot})\,
be a simplicial super-space with the maps
\,\pi^{(n+1)}_{1,n+1},\pi^{(n+1)}_{k,k+1}\ :\ X_{n}\longrightarrow X_{1},\ k\in\overline{1,n},\ n\geq 2\,
as in Eq. (
), and let
\,\omega\in\Omega^{2}(X_{1})
. Denote
\,\Delta_{X_{n}}=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\,\pi^{(n+1)\,*}_{k,k+1}-\pi^{(n+1)\,*}_{1,n+1}
. The following implication holds true:

\displaystyle\omega\in{\rm ker}\,\Delta^{(2)}_{1}\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad\forall_{n\geq 2}\ :\ \Delta_{X_{n}}\omega=0\,.

Proof.

The proof proceeds by induction. Thus, first, we deal with the case
\,n=3
. Invoking the observations and results of (the proof of) Prop.
8
, in conjunction with the assumption and the simplicial identities, we compute

(1.66)

\displaystyle\bigl(\pi^{(4)\,*}_{1,2}+\pi^{(4)\,*}_{2,3}+\pi^{(4)\,*}_{3,4}-\pi^{(4)\,*}_{1,4}\bigr)\omega=d^{(3)\,*}_{3}\circ\bigl(\Delta^{(2)}_{1}+\pi^{(3)\,*}_{1,3}\bigr)\omega+\bigl(\pi^{(4)\,*}_{3,4}-\pi^{(4)\,*}_{1,4}\bigr)\omega

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d^{(3)\,*}_{3}\circ\pi^{(3)\,*}_{1,3}\omega+\bigl(\pi^{(4)\,*}_{3,4}-\pi^{(4)\,*}_{1,4}\bigr)\omega\equiv\bigl(d^{(2)}_{1}\circ d^{(3)}_{3}\bigr)^{*}\omega+\bigl(d^{(2)}_{0}\circ d^{(3)}_{1}-d^{(2)}_{1}\circ d^{(3)}_{1}\bigr)^{*}\omega=d^{(3)\,*}_{1}\circ\Delta^{(2)}_{1}\omega=0\,,

as desired.

Next, we posit the induction hypothesis for
\,n\equiv N>2
,
i.e.
, assume that
\,\Delta_{X_{N}}\omega=0
, and use it, alongside the original assumption, to prove the induction step. We obtain, upon repeated application of the simplicial identities to the aforementioned results,

(1.68)

\displaystyle\Delta_{X_{N+1}}\omega=d^{(N+1)\,*}_{N+1}\circ\bigl(\Delta_{X_{N}}+\pi^{(N+1)\,*}_{1,N+1}\bigr)\omega+\bigl(\pi^{(N+2)\,*}_{N+1,N+2}-\pi^{(N+2)\,*}_{1,N+2}\bigr)\omega

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigl(d^{(2)}_{2}\circ d^{(3)}_{1}\circ d^{(4)}_{1}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(N+1)}_{1}\bigr)^{*}\omega+\bigl(d^{(2)}_{0}\circ d^{(3)}_{1}\circ d^{(4)}_{1}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(N+1)}_{1}-d^{(2)}_{1}\circ d^{(3)}_{1}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(N+1)}_{1}\bigr)^{*}\omega

(1.70)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigl(d^{(3)}_{1}\circ d^{(4)}_{1}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(N+1)}_{1}\bigr)^{*}\circ\Delta^{(2)}_{1}\omega=0\,,

which concludes the proof.
∎

At this stage, there seems to be no imprint of the extra structure modelling the physically relevant identity defect other than the very natural coherence identity in Prop.
8
(involving the degeneracy maps). We read off the additional information from

Proposition 12
.

Let
\,(X_{\bullet},d^{(\bullet)}_{\cdot},s^{(\bullet)}_{\cdot})\,
be a simplicial super-space and let
\,(\mathcal{A}^{\bullet}(X_{\bullet}),\mathcal{D}^{\bullet})\,
be the corresponding simplicial de Rham complex of Def.
9
. The family of maps

\displaystyle\nabla_{r}\ :\ \mathcal{A}^{r}(X_{\bullet})\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{r-1}(X_{\bullet})\ :\ \bigl(\omega_{0},\omega_{1},\ldots,\omega_{r}\bigr)\longmapsto\bigl(\widetilde{\Delta}{}_{(0)}^{r-1}\omega_{0},\widetilde{\Delta}{}_{(1)}^{r-2}\omega_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\Delta}{}_{(r-1)}^{0}\omega_{r-1}\bigr)\,,\qquad r\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\times}

induced by the degeneracy maps
\,s^{(\bullet)}_{\cdot}\,
of the simplicial super-space as

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Delta}{}_{(p)}^{r-p-1}\ :\ \Omega^{p}(X_{r-p})\longrightarrow\Omega^{p}(X_{r-p-1})\ :\ \omega_{p}\longmapsto\sum_{k=0}^{r-p-1}\,(-1)^{k}\,s^{(r-p-1)\,*}_{k}\omega_{p}\,,\qquad p\in\overline{0,r-1}

defines a chain complex

\displaystyle\bigl(\mathcal{B}_{\bullet}(X_{\bullet}),\nabla_{\bullet}\bigr)\,,\qquad\mathcal{B}_{r}(X_{\bullet})\equiv\mathcal{A}^{r}(X_{\bullet})\,.

Its boundary operators canonically determine cohomology maps

\displaystyle[\nabla_{r}]\ :\ H^{r}\bigl(\mathcal{A}^{\bullet}(X_{\bullet}),\mathcal{D}^{\bullet}\bigr)\longrightarrow H^{r-1}\bigl(\mathcal{A}^{\bullet}(X_{\bullet}),\mathcal{D}^{\bullet}\bigr)\,.

In what follows, we shall refer to the image
\,[\nabla_{r}]([\Omega_{r}])\in H^{r-1}(\mathcal{A}^{\bullet}(X_{\bullet}),\mathcal{D}^{\bullet})\,
of the class
\,[\Omega_{r}]\,
of a simplicial de Rham
r
-cocycle
\,\Omega_{r}\in\mathcal{A}^{r}(X_{\bullet})\,
by the name of the
degeneracy shadow of

\,[\Omega_{r}]
.

Proof.

The proof of the first part of the statement hinges upon the identity:
\,\widetilde{\Delta}{}_{(p)}^{n}\circ\widetilde{\Delta}{}_{(p)}^{n+1}=0
, analogous to (and checked similarly as) that encountered in Rem.
10
.

The second part of the statement follows directly from the ‘anticommutation’ relations
\,\widetilde{\Delta}{}_{(p)}^{n+1}\circ\Delta^{(p)}_{n+1}=-\Delta^{(p)}_{n}\circ\widetilde{\Delta}{}_{(p)}^{n}\,,\ p,n\in{\mathbb{N}}\,
proven with the help of the identity
\,(d^{(n+2)}_{k+1}-d^{(n+2)}_{k})\circ s^{(n+1)}_{k}=0\,
which follows from the simplicial identities, and from the equality
\,\widetilde{\Delta}{}_{(p)}^{0}\circ\Delta^{(p)}_{0}=0
, implied by a specialisation of the latter identity. Jointly, these yield
\,\nabla_{r}\circ\mathcal{D}^{r-1}=-\mathcal{D}^{r-2}\circ\nabla_{r-1}
.
∎

In the setting of the super-
\sigma
-model with a simplicial target super-space
\,(X_{\bullet},d^{(\bullet)}_{\cdot},s^{(\bullet)}_{\cdot})
, the above gives us the degeneracy shadow
\,(s^{(0)\,*}_{0}\omega,0,0)\in\mathcal{A}^{2}(X_{\bullet})\,
of the previously considered simplicial de Rham 3-cocycle

(1.71)

\displaystyle\Omega\equiv({\rm H},\omega,0,0)\in\mathcal{A}^{3}(X_{\bullet})\,.

Altogether, motivated by our findings, and with some hindsight, we give

Definition 13
.

A
simplicial target super-space for a multi-phase super-
\sigma
-model with defects
(or a
simplicial target super-space
for brevity) is a target super-space
\,\mathscr{T}=M\sqcup Q\sqcup T\,
in the sense of Def.
4
for which there exists a simplicial super-space
\,(X_{\bullet},d^{(\bullet)}_{\cdot},s^{(\bullet)}_{\cdot})\,
such that each connected component of
\,M\,
is a sub-supermanifold of
\,X_{0}
, each connected component of
\,Q\,
is a sub-supermanifold of
\,X_{1}\equiv Q\,
and, for every
\,n\geq 2
, each connected component of
\,T_{n,1}\,
is a sub-supermanifold of
\,X_{n}
, and such that the structural maps are induced from the face maps of the simplicial super-space as
per

\displaystyle\bigl(\iota_{1},\iota_{2}\bigr):=\bigl(d^{(1)}_{1},d^{(1)}_{0}\bigr)\,,\qquad\qquad\bigl(\pi^{(3)}_{1,2},\pi^{(3)}_{2,3},\pi^{(3)}_{1,3}\bigr):=\bigl(d^{(2)}_{2},d^{(2)}_{0},d^{(2)}_{1}\bigr)

and – for
\,n>2\,
–

\displaystyle\pi^{(n+1)}_{k,k+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d^{(2)}_{2}\circ d^{(3)}_{2}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(n-k+1)}_{2}\circ d^{(n-k+2)}_{0}\circ d^{(n-k+3)}_{0}\circ d^{(n)}_{0}\,,\qquad k\in\overline{1,n}\,,

(1.73)

\displaystyle\pi^{(n+1)}_{1,n+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d^{(2)}_{1}\circ d^{(3)}_{1}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(n)}_{1}\,.

Its tensorial data then compose a simplicial de Rham 3-cocycle
\,\Omega\equiv({\rm H},\omega,0,0)
.

\diamond

Remark 14
.

The construction of the cohomological bicomplex that captures the skew-tensorial data of a consistent target super-space clearly requires less structure than is present in the context in hand, to wit, only that of an incomplete simplicial space (a family of spaces with face maps), or even a truncation thereof at some fixed level
\,n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 2}
. Any triple
\,(M,Q,T_{n,1})\,
with properties (T1), (T2) and (T3) specified in Sec.
1.1
gives rise to such a (semi-bounded) bicomplex owing to the compatibility conditions (
1.25
). The ensuing cohomology can be understood as the dual of the relative (in the sense of Ref.
[
BT82
, Sec. 7]
) homology of
\,(M,Q,T_{n,1})\,
(suggested by the homological decomposition of
\,X(\Sigma)\subset\mathcal{T}
). The latter was elaborated in Ref.
[
Sus12
]
and subsequently employed in a discussion of the algebroidal structure on the set of (infinitesimal) generators of global symmetries of the
\sigma
-model, whereas the former was instrumental in the cohomological classification of the corresponding gauge anomalies carried out in Refs.
[
GSW10
,
GSW13
]
.

Remark 15
.

For an elementary exposition on simplicial methods,
cf.
,
e.g.
, Ref.
[
Fri12
]
. Groupoids and their nerves are covered neatly in Refs.
[
Seg68
,
Moe02
]
.

Presently, we shall discuss at length an important example of a simplicial target super-space, but in the meantime we take a closer look at the higher-geometric structures requisite for a rigorous definition of the Wess–Zumino (topological) term of the super-
\sigma
-model, and subsequently incorporate (global) (super)symmetry into the picture, also in the simplicial setting.

1.3.
A handful of rudimentary facts about 1-gerbes

A natural mathematical framework for an in-depth study of the two-dimensional (super-)
\sigma
-model is the theory of 1-gerbes advanced by Murray and Stevenson in Refs.
[
Mur96
,
MS00
,
Ste00
]
(
cf.
also a more recent review
[
Mur10
]
) and providing us with a convenient geometrisation of the (hyper-)cohomological description pioneered by Alvarez in Ref.
[
Alv85
]
and, in particular, Gawȩdzki in Ref.
[
Gaw88
]
. Our use of the theory in the present article is of a twofold nature: For one thing, we need it to write out a consistent definition of the action ‘functional’ of the theory for a generic (defect-decorated) worldsheet, which – as it happens – opens a direct avenue to the quantum theory and hence gives rise to the expectation that some important results obtained in this framework in the classical régime carry over to the quantum régime. For another thing, we invoke certain specific constructs, such as 1-gerbe trivialisations, bi-modules, multiplicative structures
etc.
, some of which are distinctive of the Lie (super)group-theoretic setting of interest, in order to formulate and (partly) solve the existence problem for the geometric structures postulated to describe what we shall call, in analogy with the un-graded precedent, maximally supersymmetric defects. In both cases, one may take the general discussion all the way to its logical conclusion without making an explicit use of the underlying extensive differential-(super)geometric and cohomological formalism, developing it, instead, along the lines of an essentially categorial approach that features 1-gerbes as objects on which a number of natural operations (such as,
e.g.
, the tensor product and pullback) can be carried out, and which can be mapped into one another by means of morphisms, the latter being themselves related by higher-order morphisms. This approach finds its formalisation in the notion of the weak monoidal 2-category (with duality) of abelian bundle 1-gerbes with connection over a given base supermanifold, introduced, in the un-graded setting, in Ref.
[
Ste00
]
, further developed in Ref.
[
Wal07
]
, and finally reconstructed in the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded setting in Ref.
[
Hue20
]
(the author explicitly treats the
0
-cells, but it is clear how the reconstruction extends to 1- and 2-cells) after having been rather extensively used by the Author in the context of superstring theory. The weak 2-category (implicitly) organises the subsequent brief recapitulation of the relevant elements of the general theory. On the other hand, identification of the relevant gerbe-theoretic structures for the concrete tensorial data of the (Green–Schwarz) super-
\sigma
-model with the bulk target super-space given by the super-Minkowski space requires concretisation of the general categorial definitions and relations, for which we need geometrisations of the abstract concepts. These we recall in a concise manner below, urging the interested Reader to consult the rich literature on the subject (cited in the Introduction) for details. In fact, incorporating supersymmetry in the higher-(super)geometric picture, as required by the underlying physical model, calls for a substantial refinement of the general discussion reflected in the choice of the cohomology to be geometrised. This we relegate to a separate Sec.
2
, focusing on generic structures in the present one.

The basic notion of gerbe theory is that of an
abelian bundle 1-gerbe with connection

\,\mathcal{G}\,
(to be referred to as a
1-gerbe
for brevity) over a supermanifold
\,M
, termed the
base
of
\,\mathcal{G}
, which is to be understood as a geometric structure that realises a class in the cohomology group
\,H^{3}(|M|;2\pi{\mathbb{Z}})
, termed the
Dixmier–Douady class
of
\,\mathcal{G}\,
and represented by an even de Rham super-3-cocycle on
\,M\,
which we call the
curvature
of
\,\mathcal{G}\,
and denote by
\,{\rm curv}(\mathcal{G})
. As such, a 1-gerbe is represented by the class
\,[b]_{\rm D}\,
of a 2-cocycle of its local data in the (Beilinson–)Deligne hypercohomology group
\,{\mathbb{H}}^{2}(M,\mathcal{D}(2)^{\bullet})
, for the Deligne complex
\,\mathcal{D}(2)^{\bullet}\ :\ \mathcal{O}^{{\mathbb{C}}\,\times}_{M,0}\xrightarrow{\ -{\mathsf{i}}\,{\mathsf{d}}\log\ }\underline{\Omega^{1}}(M)^{\mathbb{C}}_{0}\xrightarrow{\ \ {\mathsf{d}}\ \ }\underline{\Omega^{2}}(M)^{\mathbb{C}}_{0}\,
of differential sheaves over
\,M
,
cf.
,
e.g.
, Refs.
[
Bry93
,
Mur96
,
Hue20
]
, with the following components: the subsheaf
\,\mathcal{O}^{{\mathbb{C}}\,\times}_{M,0}\,
of Graßmann-even invertible elements of the complexification
\,\mathcal{O}_{M}^{\mathbb{C}}=\mathcal{O}_{M}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}\,
of the structure sheaf
\,\mathcal{O}_{M}\,
of
\,M
; the subsheaves
\,\underline{\Omega^{k}}(M)^{\mathbb{C}}_{0}\,
of Graßmann-even elements of the complexifications
\,\underline{\Omega^{k}}(M)^{\mathbb{C}}=\underline{\Omega^{k}}(M)\otimes{\mathbb{C}}\,
of the respective sheaves
\,\underline{\Omega^{k}}(M)\,
of
k
-forms on
\,M
. The geometric realisation of a class
\,[b]_{\rm D}\,
with the curvature
\,{\rm H}\in\Omega^{3}(M)_{0}\,
takes the form of a septuple

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\doteq\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}M,\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}M},{\rm B},L,\pi_{L},\mathcal{A}_{L},\mu_{L}\bigr)

composed of a surjective submersion
\,\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}M}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}M\longrightarrow M\,
endowed with a Graßmann-even primitive (termed the
curving
)
\,{\rm B}\in\Omega^{2}({\mathsf{Y}}M)_{0}\,
such that
\,{\mathsf{d}}{\rm B}=\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}M}^{*}{\rm H}
, and of a principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-bundle
\,\pi_{L}\ :\ L\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}M\,
(
cf.
App.
A
) with a principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-connection super-1-form (termed the
connection
)
\,\mathcal{A}_{L}\in\Omega^{1}(L)_{0}\,
such that
\,{\mathsf{d}}\mathcal{A}_{L}=\pi_{L}^{*}({\rm pr}_{2}^{*}-{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}){\rm B}
, and a groupoid structure on its fibres,
i.e.
, a distinguished connection-preserving principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-bundle isomorphism
\,\mu_{L}\ :\ {\rm pr}_{1,2}^{*}L\otimes{\rm pr}_{2,3}^{*}L\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }{\rm pr}_{1,3}^{*}L\,
over
\,{\mathsf{Y}}^{[3]}M
, subject to an associativity constraint over
\,{\mathsf{Y}}^{[4]}M\,
(
cf.
,
e.g.
, Ref.
[
Wal07
]
). A distinguished class of gerbes is composed of those whose curvature admits a global primitive (on
\,M
). A member of the class with the primitive
\,\omega\in\Omega^{2}(M)_{0}\,
shall be denoted by
\,\mathcal{I}_{\omega}
, and we have
\,{\rm curv}(\mathcal{I}_{\omega})={\mathsf{d}}\omega
. For these, we may take
\,\mathcal{I}_{\omega}=(M,{\rm id}_{M},\omega,M\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times},{\rm pr}_{1},{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\vartheta,{\boldsymbol{1}})
, with
\,\vartheta\in\Omega^{1}({\mathbb{C}}^{\times})\,
the Maurer–Cartan 1-form on the Lie group
\,{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
, and with the trivial groupoid structure
\,{\boldsymbol{1}}\,
given by group multiplication in the fibre.

There are a number of natural operations on 1-gerbes, such as,
e.g.
, the (
tensor
)
product

\,\mathcal{G}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{G}_{2}\,
of any two 1-gerbes
\,\mathcal{G}_{A},\ A=1,2\,
over the same base
\,M
, and the
pullback

\,f^{*}\mathcal{G}\,
of a 1-gerbe
\,\mathcal{G}\,
over
\,M\,
along an arbitrary supermanifold map
\,f\ :\ N\longrightarrow M
. The latter is the 1-gerbe
\,f^{*}\mathcal{G}=(f^{*}{\mathsf{Y}}M,\pi_{f^{*}{\mathsf{Y}}M},\widehat{f}{}^{*}{\rm B},\widehat{f}{}^{[2]\,*}L,\pi_{\widehat{f}{}^{[2]\,*}L},\widehat{\widehat{f}}{}^{[2]\,*}\mathcal{A}_{L},\widehat{f}{}^{[3]\,*}\mu_{L})\,
defined in terms of pullback surjective submersions:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 34.1684pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-34.1684pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 91.0739pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-29.66603pt\raise-25.034pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.34099pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi_{f^{*}{\mathsf{Y}}M}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-42.73468pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 91.0739pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathsf{Y}}M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 102.80307pt\raise-25.034pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.9764pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}M}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 102.80307pt\raise-42.73468pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 46.0595pt\raise-56.1791pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{f}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 94.40724pt\raise-50.068pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 94.40724pt\raise-50.068pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\qquad\quad{\rm and}\qquad\quad\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 34.7876pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-16.56946pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 83.3083pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-34.7876pt\raise-26.029pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.97696pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi_{\widehat{f}{}^{[2]\,*}L}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-42.67911pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 83.3083pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 89.71109pt\raise-26.029pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi_{L}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 89.71109pt\raise-42.67911pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-15.40279pt\raise-52.05803pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathsf{Y}}_{f}^{[2]}N\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 35.11243pt\raise-58.74748pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.68947pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{f}{}^{[2]}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 73.47496pt\raise-52.05803pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 73.47496pt\raise-52.05803pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}M}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\,.

Under these operations, the curvature behaves as follows:
\,{\rm curv}(\mathcal{G}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{G}_{2})={\rm curv}(\mathcal{G}_{1})+{\rm curv}(\mathcal{G}_{2})\,
and
\,{\rm curv}\bigl(f^{*}\mathcal{G}\bigr)=f^{*}{\rm curv}(\mathcal{G})
, and for trivial gerbes over
\,M\,
we find the identities
\,\mathcal{I}_{\omega_{1}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\omega_{2}}=\mathcal{I}_{\omega_{1}+\omega_{2}}\,
and
\,f^{*}\mathcal{I}_{\omega}=\mathcal{I}_{f^{*}\omega}
.

The geometric realisation of cohomological equivalence of 1-gerbes
\,\mathcal{G}_{A},\ A=1,2\,
(of the same curvature) over the common base,
\,[b_{1}]_{\rm D}=[b_{2}]_{\rm D}
, goes under the name of a
1-isomorphism

\,\Phi\,
between
\,\mathcal{G}_{1}\,
and
\,\mathcal{G}_{2}\,
and is denoted as
\,\Phi\ :\ \mathcal{G}_{1}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\mathcal{G}_{2}
. Thus, a 1-isomorphism is represented by a 1-cochain
\,p\,
in the Deligne hypercohomology introduced previously. It is now easy to see that (
cf.
Refs.
[
Gaj97
,
Joh02
]
)

Proposition 16
.

The set of 1-isomorphism classes of 1-gerbes of a given curvature over
\,M\,
is a torsor under an action of the group
\,\mathcal{W}^{3}(M;{\rm H}=0)\cong H^{2}(M,{\rm U}(1))\,
of
flat gerbes
over
\,M\,
(i.e., those with a vanishing curvature).

The action referred to above as well as the binary operation in
\,\mathcal{W}^{3}(M;{\rm H}=0)\,
are induced by the tensor product as
\,\mathcal{W}^{3}(M;0)\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2}(M,\mathcal{D}(2)^{\bullet})\longrightarrow{\mathbb{H}}^{2}(M,\mathcal{D}(2)^{\bullet})\ :\ ([b_{0}]_{\rm D},[b]_{\rm D})\longmapsto[b_{0}]_{\rm D}\otimes[b]_{\rm D}
. Given geometrisations
\,\mathcal{G}_{A}=({\mathsf{Y}}_{A}M,\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}_{A}M},{\rm B}_{A},L_{A},\pi_{L_{A}},\mathcal{A}_{L_{A}},\mu_{L_{A}}),\ A\in\{1,2\}\,
of the two 1-gerbes related by the 1-isomorphism
\,\Phi
, we obtain the geometric realisation of the latter in the form of a sextuple

\displaystyle\Phi\doteq\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2}M,\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2}M},E,\pi_{E},\mathcal{A}_{E},\alpha_{E}\bigr)

composed of a surjective submersion
\,\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2}M}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2}M\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}_{1}M\times_{M}{\mathsf{Y}}_{2}M\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2}M\,
and of a principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-bundle
\,\pi_{E}\ :\ E\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2}M\,
over it, endowed with a principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-connection 1-form
\,\mathcal{A}_{E}\in\Omega^{1}(E)_{0}\,
such that
\,{\mathsf{d}}\mathcal{A}_{E}=\pi_{E}^{*}\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2}M}^{*}({\rm pr}_{2}^{*}{\rm B}_{2}-{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}{\rm B}_{1})
, and with a distinguished connection-preserving principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-bundle isomorphism
\,\alpha_{E}\ :\ \pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2}M}^{\times 2\,*}{\rm pr}_{1,3}^{*}L_{1}\otimes{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}E\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}E\otimes\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2}M}^{\times 2\,*}{\rm pr}_{2,4}^{*}L_{2}\,
over
\,{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2}M\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2}M\times_{M}{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2}M
, subject to a further coherence constraint involving the two groupoid structures
\,\mu_{L_{A}}
,
cf.
,
e.g.
, Ref.
[
Wal07
]
.

Amidst 1-isomorphisms between 1-gerbes over a given base
\,M
, there are special types which play an important rôle in what follows. These are
trivialisations
, of the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\omega}\ :\ \mathcal{G}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\,,

with
\,{\mathsf{d}}\omega={\rm curv}(\mathcal{G})
, and, for an arbitrary pair
\,(\mathcal{G}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{2})\,
of 1-gerbes, the associated
(\mathcal{G}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{2})
-bi-modules
, defined as

\displaystyle\Phi_{\omega}\ :\ \mathcal{G}_{1}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\mathcal{G}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\,,

with
\,{\mathsf{d}}\omega={\rm curv}(\mathcal{G}_{1})-{\rm curv}(\mathcal{G}_{2})
. Whenever the two 1-gerbes involved in the latter definition are pullbacks
\,\mathcal{G}_{A}=\iota_{A}^{*}\mathcal{G},\ A\in\{1,2\}\,
of a single 1-gerbe over a manifold
\,M\,
to another manifold
\,Q\,
along the respective smooth maps
\,\iota_{A}\ :\ Q\longrightarrow M
, we should think of the corresponding pair
\,(\mathcal{G},\Phi_{\omega})\,
as a
geometric realisation of the class
\,[{\rm curv}(\mathcal{G})\oplus\omega]\,
of the 3-cocycle
\,{\rm curv}(\mathcal{G})\oplus\omega\,
in the relative de Rham cohomology of the pair
\,(Q,M)
.

As in the case of 1-gerbes, we have several natural operations on 1-isomorphisms, including the
tensor product

\,\Phi_{1,3}\otimes\Phi_{2,4}\,
of any two 1-isomorphisms
\,\Phi_{A,B}\ :\ \mathcal{G}_{A}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\mathcal{G}_{B},\ (A,B)=(1,3),(2,4)
, with
\,\Phi_{1,3}\otimes\Phi_{2,4}\ :\ \mathcal{G}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{G}_{2}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\mathcal{G}_{3}\otimes\mathcal{G}_{4}
, the
composition

\,\Phi_{2,3}\circ\Phi_{1,2}\,
of any two 1-isomorphisms
\,\Phi_{A,B}\ :\ \mathcal{G}_{A}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\mathcal{G}_{B},\ (A,B)=(1,2),(2,3)
, with
\,\Phi_{2,3}\circ\Phi_{1,2}\ :\ \mathcal{G}_{1}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\mathcal{G}_{3}
, and the
pullback

\,f^{*}\Phi\,
of a 1-isomorphism
\,\Phi\ :\ \mathcal{G}_{1}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\mathcal{G}_{2}\,
between gerbes
\,\mathcal{G}_{A},\ A=1,2\,
over
\,M\,
along an arbitrary supermanifold map
\,f\ :\ N\longrightarrow M
, with
\,f^{*}\Phi\ :\ f^{*}\mathcal{G}_{1}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }f^{*}\mathcal{G}_{2}
.

Besides 1-
iso
morphisms, there also exist non-invertible 1-morphisms between 1-gerbes, with the underlying structure of a vector bundle of a rank strictly greater than 1. We shall not have a need for these in the present paper (but
cf.
,
e.g.
, Ref.
[
Gaw05
]
).

On the lowest rung of the cohomology ladder, we find geometric objects that realise cohomological equivalences between 1-isomorphisms, represented by 0-cochains in the Deligne hypercohomology. Thus, for any two 1-isomorphisms
\,\Phi_{1,2}^{A},\ A=1,2\,
between the same two gerbes
\,\mathcal{G}_{B},\ B=1,2
, with equivalent Deligne data
\,[p_{2}-p_{1}]_{\rm D}=0
, we have a
2-isomorphism

\,\varphi\,
between
\,\Phi_{1,2}^{1}\,
and
\,\Phi_{1,2}^{2}
, denoted as
\,\varphi\ :\ \Phi_{1,2}^{1}{\mathrel{\to}^{\ \cong\ }}\Phi_{1,2}^{2}
, or – in the extended notation – as

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.16667pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-9.16667pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 40.65273pt\raise 26.3062pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.13391pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Phi_{1,2}^{1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 93.16437pt\raise 6.26476pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 40.65273pt\raise-26.30618pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.13391pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Phi_{1,2}^{2}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 93.16437pt\raise-6.26476pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.53682pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\varphi}$}}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 51.16669pt\raise-14.92516pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1.5}\lx@xy@tip{-1.5}}}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\kern 33.16667pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 63.16667pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 93.16667pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{2}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\,.

Clearly,
\,\varphi\,
is represented by a 0-cochain
\,f\,
in the Deligne hypercohomology based on
\,\mathcal{D}(2)^{\bullet}
, and inequivalent 2-isomorphisms between given two 1-isomorphisms are labelled by classes
\,[f_{2}-f_{1}]_{\rm D}\,
of 0-cocycles in the Deligne hypercohomology group
\,{\mathbb{H}}^{0}(M,\mathcal{D}(2)^{\bullet})
. Given geometrisations
\,\Phi^{A}_{1,2}=({\mathsf{Y}}^{A}{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2}M,\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}^{A}{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2}M},E_{A},\pi_{E_{A}},

\mathcal{A}_{E_{A}},\alpha_{E_{A}}),\ A\in\{1,2\}\,
of the two 1-isomorphisms related by the 2-isomorphism
\,\varphi
, we obtain the geometric realisation of the latter in the form of a triple

\displaystyle\varphi\doteq\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}{\mathsf{Y}}^{1,2}{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2}M,\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathsf{Y}}^{1,2}{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2}M},\beta\bigr)

composed of a surjective submersion
\,\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathsf{Y}}^{1,2}{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2}M}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}{\mathsf{Y}}^{1,2}{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2}M\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}^{1}{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2}M\times_{{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2}M}{\mathsf{Y}}^{2}{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2}M\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}^{1,2}{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2}M
, and, over it, a distinguished connection-preserving principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-bundle isomorphism
\,\beta\ :\ \pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathsf{Y}}^{1,2}{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2}M}^{*}{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}E_{1}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathsf{Y}}^{1,2}{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2}M}^{*}{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}E_{2}
, subject to a further coherence constraint involving the two isomorphisms
\,\alpha_{E_{A}}
,
cf.
,
e.g.
, Ref.
[
Wal07
]
. We have the important (
cf.
Refs.
[
Gaj97
,
Joh02
]
)

Proposition 17
.

The set of 2-isomorphism classes of 1-isomorphisms between a given pair of 1-gerbes over
\,M\,
is a torsor under an action of the group
\,H^{1}(M,{\rm U}(1))
.

Once again, we can subject the geometric objects in hand to a variety of natural operations. There is,
e.g.
, the
tensor product

\,\varphi_{1,3}\otimes\varphi_{2,4}\,
of any two 2-isomorphisms
\,\varphi_{A,B}\ :\ \Phi_{A}{\mathrel{\to}^{\ \cong\ }}\Phi_{B},\ (A,B)\in\{(1,3),(2,4)\}
, with
\,\varphi_{1,3}\otimes\varphi_{2,4}\ :\ \Phi_{1}\otimes\Phi_{2}{\mathrel{\to}^{\ \cong\ }}\Phi_{3}\otimes\Phi_{4}
, the
horizontal composition

\,\varphi_{2,3}\circ\varphi_{1,2}\,
of any two 2-isomorphisms
\,\varphi_{A,B}\ :\ \Phi_{A,B}^{1}{\mathrel{\to}^{\ \cong\ }}\Phi_{A,B}^{2},\ (A,B)\in\{(1,2),(2,3)\}\,
between 1-isomorphisms
\,\Phi_{A,B}^{C}\ :\ \mathcal{G}_{A}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\mathcal{G}_{B},\ C\in\{1,2\}\,
between 1-gerbes
\,\mathcal{G}_{A}\,
over
\,M
, with

Prior to concluding this section, let us remark that 1-gerbes over a given supermanifold
\,M
, together with the attendant 1-morphisms and 2-isomorphisms, compose a weak monoidal 2-category (with duality), termed the
weak 2-category of abelian bundle gerbes with connection over

\,M\,
and denoted as
\,\mathfrak{BGrb}^{\nabla}(M)
,
cf.
Refs.
[
Ste00
,
Wal07
]
(the structure carries over to the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded setting).

1.4.
The gerbe theory for the multi-phase super-
\sigma
-model

The physical significance of the weak 2-category
\,\mathfrak{BGrb}^{\nabla}(\mathscr{T})\,
(understood in a natural manner) over the target super-space
\,\mathscr{T}=M\sqcup Q\sqcup T\,
of the super-
\sigma
-model on a generic worldsheet with defects
\,(\Sigma,d)\,
stems from the observation that 0-, 1- and 2-cells of the 2-category provide just the cohomological data needed to define the topological term in the action ‘functional’ of the super-
\sigma
-model in the presence of the
self-intersecting defect embedded by
\,d\,
in
\,\Sigma
. This was substantiated in all generality in Ref.
[
RS09
]
, which is also where the said action functional was derived from elementary worldsheet invariance analysis, and the findings reported in that paper retain their validity in the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded setting. In order to appreciate this observation, we need to recall the basic gerbe-theoretic structure behind the two-dimensional (super)field theory under
consideration. To this end, we introduce (adapting the nomenclature introduced in Refs.
[
FSW08
,
RS09
]
,
cf.
also Refs.
[
RS11
,
Sus11a
,
Sus12
,
GSW13
,
Sus13
]
)

Definition 18
.

A
superstring background
is a triple
\,\mathfrak{B}=(\mathcal{M},\mathcal{B},\mathcal{J})\,
composed of the following geometric structures:

•

the
target

\,\mathcal{M}=(M,{\rm g},\mathcal{G})\,
consisting of a super-space
\,M\,
(the
bulk target super-space
) with a Graßmann-even metric tensor
\,{\rm g}\,
(typically degenerate in the Graßmann-odd coordinate directions) and a bundle gerbe
\,\mathcal{G}\,
(with connection) of curvature
\,{\rm curv}(\mathcal{G})={\rm H}\in\Omega^{3}(M)_{0}
;

•

the
\mathcal{G}
-bi-brane

\,\mathcal{B}=(Q,\iota_{1},\iota_{2},\omega,\Phi)\,
consisting of a super-space
\,Q
, termed the
\mathcal{G}
-bi-brane worldvolume
, with a Graßmann-even super-2-form
\,\omega
, termed
the
\mathcal{G}
-bi-brane curvature
, and a pair of supermanifold maps
\,\iota_{A}\ :\ Q\longrightarrow M,\ A\in\{1,2\}
, and of a
(\iota_{1}^{*}\mathcal{G},\iota_{2}^{*}\mathcal{G})
-bi-module

\displaystyle\Phi\ :\ \iota_{1}^{*}\mathcal{G}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\iota_{2}^{*}\mathcal{G}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\,,

to be called the
correspondence bi-module
;

•

the
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{B})
-inter-bi-brane

(1.76)

The existence of the structure of a simplicial super-space underlying the target super-space of the super-
\sigma
-model, with the cohomological constraints (
1.25
) on the component inter-bi-brane super-worldvolumes
\,T_{n,1}\,
associated with defect junctions of higher valence
\,n+1>3\,
ensured by the ‘master’ DJI satisfied over the ‘elementary’ super-worldvolume
\,T_{2,1}
, immediately suggests a distinguished higher-geometric sub-structure over such a target super-space which we describe below. For that, we need

Definition 20
.

Fix
\,n\in{\mathbb{N}}\setminus\{0,1\}
. A
full binary tree with
\,n\,
leaves
is a connected acyclic (undirected) graph, with a distinguished node of degree 2 (termed the
root
), and all other nodes of degree either 3 (the internal nodes) or 1 (the external nodes, termed the
leaves
), there being
\,n\,
of the latter, and with a distinguished ordering of every pair of nodes that are connected to an arbitrary node of degree greater than 1 and lie farther from the root than the node – these nodes are termed the
children of the node
and the ordering distinguishes a
left child
from a
right child
. We shall denote the set of all such trees as

\displaystyle{\rm FBT}_{n}=\{\ \textrm{full binary trees with $\,n\,$ leaves}\ \}

and distinsuish, for later reference, two of its elements:

(1.77)

\displaystyle\tau_{n}^{\rm L}\quad\doteq\hskip-10.0pt\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern-68.63152pt\hbox{{\hbox{\kern 139.76303pt\raise 28.4526pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern \lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\,.

(1.78)

A
level of the
(
full binary
)
tree
is the subset of its nodes at a fixed distance from the root, measured by the number of edges on the path from the root to that node. In particular, the root is the unique element at level 0. The
height of the
(
full binary
)
tree
is the number of edges between the root and any node at the highest level.

To every full binary tree with
\,n\,
leaves
\,\tau\in{\rm FBT}_{n}\,
of height
\,H\,
we associate its
level-ordered presentation
given by an embedding of the graph
\,\tau\,
in
\,{\mathbb{R}}^{\times 2}\,
obtained in the following manner:

-

the root of
\,\tau\,
is placed at
\,(0,0)
;

-

the nodes of degree 3 at level
\,L>0\,
are placed on the horizontal line
\,{\mathbb{R}}\times\{-L\}\,
so that the first coordinate of the left child of a given node at level
\,L-1\,
is greater than that of the right child, and no node is embedded between them on that line;

-

the leaves of
\,\tau\,
are placed on the horizontal line
\,{\mathbb{R}}\times\{-H\}\,
in such a manner that for any pair of children of some node above them the first coordinate of the left child is greater than that of the right child, and no node is embedded between them on that line – this placement determines a numbering of the leaves in the direction of the increasing first coordinate, with the range
\,\overline{1,n}
;

-

a child of a node is linked to the latter by a segment of the straight line passing through the node and the child.

Finally, the
planting
of the level-ordered presentation of the given full binary tree with
\,n\,
leaves
\,\tau\in{\rm FBT}_{n}\,
is the graph
\,\widehat{\tau}\,
obtained from that presentation through attachment of a single vertical edge
\,\{0\}\times[0,1]\,
to the (embedded) root of
\,\tau
,
cf.
Fig.
6
.

\diamond

Figure 6.
The planting of a level-ordered presentation of a full binary tree
\,\tau_{*}\,
with 9 leaves (of height 5). The induced numbering of the leaves is shown. The dotted lines sweep the levels of the tree (indexed on the right). The nodes of degree 3 are labelled for later reference.

The link between full binary trees and simplicial target super-spaces employs the pictorial representation of the face maps of a simplicial object given in App.
B
and is established in

Definition 21
.

Let
\,\mathcal{C}\,
be a category and let
\,(X_{\bullet},d^{(\bullet)}_{\cdot},s^{(\bullet)}_{\cdot})\,
be a simplicial object in
\,\mathcal{C}
. Given a full binary tree with
\,n>2\,
leaves
\,\tau\in{\rm FBT}_{n}\,
of height
\,H(>1)
, the
simplicial descent pattern for

\,\tau\,
is the following collection
\,\mathscr{D}(\tau)\equiv\{\mathscr{D}(\tau)_{L}\}_{L\in\overline{0,H-1}}
, indexed by the set
\,\overline{0,H-1}\,
of root and branch levels, of the following sequences of morphisms: Let
\,N_{L}\,
be the number of nodes of degree 3 at level
\,L\in\overline{0,H}\,
in
\,\widehat{\tau}
, so that, in particular,
\,(N_{0},N_{H})=(1,0)
. These numbers determine the numbers
\,\Lambda_{L}=n-\sum_{l=L+1}^{H-1}\,N_{l}-2N_{L}\,
of free lines intersecting the respective horizontal lines
\,{\mathbb{R}}\times\{-L\}
in (the planting of) a level-ordered presentation of
\,\tau
, so that, in particular,
\,\Lambda_{0}=0
. The free lines at level
\,L\,
originate from the respective leaves with numbers
\,0\leq n^{(L)}_{1}<n^{(L)}_{2}<\cdots<n^{(L)}_{\Lambda_{L}}\leq n
. To every node of degree 3 we associate the corresponding superposition
\,d^{(n)}_{k_{3},k_{4},\ldots,k_{n}}\equiv d^{(3)}_{k_{3}}\circ d^{(4)}_{k_{4}}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(n)}_{k_{n}}\ :\ X_{n}\longrightarrow X_{2}\,
of the face maps of the simplicial object (with
\,0\leq k_{m}\leq m,\ m\in\overline{3,n}
) through application of the scheme of the recursive ternary resolution laid out in App.
B
(
cf.
, in particular, Fig.
10
). Thus, there arises, at a given level
\,L
, a collection of
\,N_{L}\,
morphisms
\,X_{n}\longrightarrow X_{2}\,
that we augment with the collection of morphisms
\,\pi^{(n+1)}_{n^{(L)}_{m},n^{(L)}_{m}+1}\ :\ X_{n}\longrightarrow X_{1},\ m\in\overline{1,\Lambda_{L}}\,
associated with the free lines at that level. The
\,N_{L}+\Lambda_{L}\,
morphisms obtained in this manner are arranged, at each level
\,L\in\overline{0,H-1}\,
, into a sequence in which the position of the morphism (from left to right) is determined by the order of appearance of the corresponding intersection as the line
\,{\mathbb{R}}\times\{-L\}\,
traverses the level-ordered presentation of
\,\tau\,
in the direction of the increasing first coordinate.

\diamond

The last two definitions enable us to finally formulate

Definition 23
.

A
simplicial superstring background
is a superstring background with a simplicial target super-space, as described in Def.
13
. We call it
descent-complete
if it has the following property: For every
\,n>2\,
and every full binary tree
\,\tau\in{\rm FBT}_{n}
, the component inter-bi-brane super-worldvolume
\,T_{n,1}\,
contains the common intersection
\,T_{n,1}^{\rm int}\,
of the preimages of the component inter-bi-brane super-worldvolume
\,T_{2,1}\,
under the maps
\,d^{(n)}_{k_{3},k_{4},\ldots,k_{n}}\ :\ X_{n}\longrightarrow X_{2}\,
of the simplicial descent pattern
\,\mathscr{D}(\tau)\,
for
\,\tau\,
composed of the face maps of the underlying simplicial super-space as in Def.
21
and the preimages of the bi-brane super-worldvolume
\,Q\,
under the maps
\,\pi^{(n+1)}_{n^{(L)}_{m},n^{(L)}_{m}+1}\,
of the same simplicial descent pattern, so that the trees determine, over the intersection, the corresponding component fusion 2-isomorphisms
\,\varphi_{n+1}[\tau]\,
in terms of the elementary fusion 2-isomorphism
\,\varphi_{3}\,
and the 1-gerbe 2-isomorphism

\displaystyle\varphi_{n+1}[\tau]\mathord{\restriction}_{T_{n,1}^{\rm int}}=\mathscr{D}(\tau)_{0}(\varphi_{3})\bullet\mathscr{D}(\tau)_{1}(\widetilde{\varphi}{}_{3})\bullet\mathscr{D}(\tau)_{2}(\widetilde{\varphi}{}_{3})\bullet\cdots\bullet\mathscr{D}(\tau)_{H-1}(\widetilde{\varphi}{}_{3})\,,

where

•

\,\mathscr{D}(\tau)_{0}(\varphi_{3})\,
is the pullback of
\,\varphi_{3}\,
by the unique element of
\,\mathscr{D}(\tau)_{0}
;

•

\,\mathscr{D}(\tau)_{L}(\widetilde{\varphi}{}_{3}),\ L\in\overline{1,H-1}\,
is the horizontal composition of the identity 2-isomorphisms on the pullbacks of
\,\Phi\,
along the maps
\,T_{n,1}\longrightarrow Q\,
belonging to
\,\mathscr{D}(\tau)_{L}\,
(the pullbacks being corrected, whenever necessary, by appropriate identity 1-isomorphisms on trivial 1-gerbes coming from the higher-level bi-modules) with the pullbacks of
\,\widetilde{\varphi}{}_{3}\,
along the maps
\,T_{n,1}\longrightarrow T_{2,1}\,
belonging to
\,\mathscr{D}(\tau)_{k}
, the horizontal compositions being performed in the order of the appearance of the relevant maps of the two types in the sequence
\,\mathscr{D}(\tau)_{L}
.

\diamond

We shall spend some time discussing an important example of a superstring background of the type introduced above in later sections. Meanwhile, we recall the (super)field-theoretic rôle of the higher-geometric structure and, in the next section, incorporate target super-space (super)symmetry in the higher-geometric picture. We begin with the long-expected

Definition 24
.

The
two-dimensional Green–Schwarz
(
-type
)
super-
\sigma
-model
(or the
GS super-
\sigma
-model
for brevity) with background
\,\mathfrak{B}\,
as in Def.
18
on a worldsheet with defects
\,(\Sigma,d)\,
as in Def.
1
is a theory of superfield configurations
\,\xi\,
from
\,\mathcal{F}(\Sigma,d)\,
determined by the principle of least action for the Dirac–Feynman amplitude with the (action) ‘functional’

(1.95)

\displaystyle S_{\sigma}[(\xi\,|\,\Gamma)]=\int_{\Sigma}\,\sqrt{{\rm det}\,\bigl(\xi^{*}{\rm g}\bigr)}+S_{\rm WZ}[(\xi\,|\,\Gamma)]\,,

where the so-called ‘topological’, or Wess–Zumino term

\displaystyle S_{\rm WZ}[(\xi\,|\,\Gamma)]=-{\mathsf{i}}\,\log{\rm Hol}_{\mathcal{G},\Phi,(\varphi_{\cdot})}(\xi\,|\,\Gamma)

is given by the
decorated-surface holonomy

\,{\rm Hol}_{\mathcal{G},\Phi,(\varphi_{\cdot})}(\xi\,|\,\Gamma)\,
for the superfield configuration
\,\xi
, derived in the un-graded setting in Ref.
[
RS09
, Sect. 2]
and transcribed
verbatim
into the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded setting.

\diamond

The rôle of the gerbe-theoretic structures in the modelling of the dynamics of linear distributions of energy and topological charge (resp. super-charge) does not end with the rigorous definition of the DF amplitude. As was demonstrated in detail in the un-graded mono-phase setting by Gawȩdzki in the seminal paper
[
Gaw88
]
, and subsequently worked out for the un-graded multi-phase setting by the Author in Refs.
[
Sus11a
,
Sus12
]
(
cf.
also Refs.
[
Sus11b
,
Sus13
]
), the 1-gerbe associated with a single ‘phase’ of the two-dimensional field theory canonically defines, through the so-called transgression, a pre-quantum bundle of the theory in that phase, whereas a bi-brane attached to a defect line between phases gives rise to a correspondence
11
11

11

The interpretation of the inter-bi-brane in this paradigm is a little more involved, and so shall be omitted here. The interested Reader is referred to the original papers
[
Sus11a
,
Sus12
]
.
between global sections of the mono-phase pre-quantum bundles (to be thought of as quantum state spaces upon choosing a polarisation) which, under certain circumstances (discussed at length in Ref.
[
Sus11b
]
), becomes an equivalence (or
duality
) of the quantised mono-phase theories (aka a
quantomorphism
). This coherent quantum-mechanical interpretation of the higher geometry behind the multi-phase
\sigma
-model (which is further enhanced in the presence of global (target-space) symmetries and, in particular, in the context of their gauging) provides the fundamental motivation for its further study in the un-graded setting and the discussion of its analogon in the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded environment. In either case, it is customary to assume the presence of the action of a supersymmetry Lie supergroup, and to demand invariance of the ensuing super-
\sigma
-model (or, more concretely, of its DF amplitude) under the induced action on the superfields. Here, supersymmetry features as a global (target super-space) symmetry of the superfield theory, and so its consistent incorporation in the higher-geometric framework calls for a discussion of a gerbe-theoretic rendering of global symmetries in general. It also raises the question of compatibility of the defect with the global symmetries of the bulk phases separated by it. These are the issues that we review in the next section.

2.
Enter global (super)symmetry

Global symmetries of the charged geometrodynamics of the (super-)
\sigma
-model are induced, in a natural manner, from those automorphisms of the target super-space which preserve the bulk metric tensor and lift to isomorphisms of the gerbe-theoretic structure. In the presence of a nontrivial Graßmann-odd component of the structure sheaf of the target super-space, used to model fermionic degrees of freedom (
e.g.
, in the effective field theory of excitations of the embedded worldsheet), it is customary to assume that the symmetries form a Lie supergroup with a nontrivial Graßmann-odd component of the tangent Lie superalgebra that generates supersymmetry transformations mapping into one another components of the superfield of different statistics. Accordingly, in what follows, we assume given a Lie supergroup
\,{\rm G}_{\rm s}=(|{\rm G}_{\rm s}|,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm s})
, to be thought of as a group object in
\,{\rm{\bf sMan}}\,
or, equivalently, after Kostant
12
12

12

Cf.
also Ref.
[
Kos82
]
and, in particular, Ref.
[
CCF11
]
for a very lucid introduction into the formalism.

[
Kos77
]
, as a super-Harish–Chandra pair composed of a (body) Lie group
\,|{\rm G}_{\rm s}|\,
and a Lie superalgebra
\,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm s}=\mathfrak{g}_{\rm s}^{(0)}\oplus\mathfrak{g}_{\rm s}^{(1)}\,
with the Graßmann-even Lie subalgebra given by the tangent Lie algebra of the body Lie group,
\,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm s}^{(0)}\equiv{\rm Lie}\,|{\rm G}_{\rm s}|
, and the latter group realised on
\,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm s}\,
through an extension of the standard adjoint action of
\,|{\rm G}_{\rm s}|\,
on
\,{\rm Lie}\,|{\rm G}_{\rm s}|
, together with a
coherent
action

\displaystyle\mathscr{T}\lambda\equiv T\lambda\sqcup Q\lambda\sqcup M\lambda\equiv\bigsqcup_{n=2}^{\infty}\,T_{n,1}\lambda\sqcup Q\lambda\sqcup M\lambda\ :\ {\rm G}_{\rm s}\times\mathscr{T}\longrightarrow\mathscr{T}\,,

by which we mean a (left) action in the sense of Ref.
[
CCF11
, Def. 8.1.2]
intertwined by the structure maps of the background,
i.e.
, such that

(2.1)

\displaystyle\iota_{A}\circ Q\lambda=M\lambda\circ({\rm id}_{{\rm G}_{\rm s}}\times\iota_{A})\,,\qquad A\in\{1,2\}\,,

(2.2)

(2.3)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\pi^{(n+1)}_{k,k+1}\circ T_{n,1}\lambda=Q\lambda\circ\bigl({\rm id}_{{\rm G}_{\rm s}}\times\pi^{(n+1)}_{k,k+1}\bigr)\,,\qquad k\in\overline{1,n}\\
\\
\pi^{(n+1)}_{1,n+1}\circ T_{n,1}\lambda=Q\lambda\circ\bigl({\rm id}_{{\rm G}_{\rm s}}\times\pi^{(n+1)}_{1,n+1}\bigr)\end{array}\right.\,,\qquad n\in{\mathbb{N}}_{\geq 2}\,.

Next, we consider invariance of the superstring background under
\,\mathscr{T}\lambda
. As we intend to give a description of rather special backgrounds, namely, those (maximally) compatible with the supersymmetry present (in the form of the above coherent action), we shall impose the constraints of invariance
uniformly
on
all
components of the background
at the same time
, rather than distinguishing the bulk component and then considering various reduction schemes for the bulk supersymmetry group. The absence of points (in the standard sense of the word) in a non-even supermanifold makes the issue of invariance slightly more involved than in the un-graded setting and leaves us with two convenient formulations that find a practical application in what follows. Below, we detail the first one, which is closest to the standard notion known from the un-graded setting. The discussion of the other one, making ample use of the notion of an
\mathcal{S}
-point in a supermanifold, is postponed to Sec.
6
in which circumstances arise that make its application natural and, indeed, convenient. Meanwhile, we restrict our thinking of ‘points’ in the relevant supermanifolds
\,{\rm G}_{\rm s}\,
and (those composing)
\,\mathscr{T}\,
to the respective bodies and, accordingly, split the description of the property of interest into a global one for the body (which may have an arbitrary topology) with the help of the induced
|{\rm G}_{\rm s}|
-action:

\displaystyle|\mathscr{T}\lambda|_{g}\equiv\mathscr{T}\lambda\circ\bigl(\widehat{g}\times{\rm id}_{\mathscr{T}}\bigr)\ :\ \mathscr{T}\equiv{\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}\times\mathscr{T}\longrightarrow{\rm G}_{\rm s}\times\mathscr{T}\longrightarrow\mathscr{T}\,,\qquad g\in|{\rm G}_{\rm s}|\,,

using the topological points
\,\{\widehat{g}\ :\ {\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}\longrightarrow{\rm G}_{\rm s}\}_{g\in|{\rm G}_{\rm s}|}\equiv|{\rm G}_{\rm s}|
, and an infinitesimal one for the contractible soul with the help of the Lie derivative along fundamental supervector fields induced by
\,\mathcal{T}\lambda\,
and associated with vectors in
\,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm s}
. As for the latter, it proves convenient
13
13

13

This mode of description becomes indispensable in the analysis of
gauge
symmetries,
cf.
Ref.
[
Sus20
, Sec. 7]
.
– as shall become clear later on – to work with Graßmann-even supervector fields only, and so we present the said vector fields as sections
\,\mathcal{K}_{\cdot}\equiv\mathcal{K}_{\cdot}^{M}\sqcup\mathcal{K}_{\cdot}^{Q}\sqcup\bigsqcup_{n=2}^{\infty}\,\mathcal{K}_{\cdot}^{T_{n,1}}\in\Gamma(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm s}}\otimes\mathcal{T}\mathscr{T})\equiv\Gamma(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm s}}\otimes{\rm sDer}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{T}}))\,
of a subsheaf
\,\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm s}}\otimes\mathcal{T}\mathscr{T}\,
of the tangent sheaf
\,\mathcal{T}(\mathfrak{g}_{\rm s}\times\mathscr{T})\equiv{\rm sDer}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm s}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{T}})\,
over the super-
space

\,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm s}\times\mathscr{T}\,
that are linear in the global generators
\,\{X^{a}\}^{a\in\overline{1,D}},\ D\equiv{\rm dim}\,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm s}\,
of the structure sheaf
\,\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\rm s}}
(supercoordinates) on
\,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm s}\,
associated with a Graßmann-homogeneous basis
\,\{t_{a}\}_{a\in\overline{1,D}}\,
of the Lie superalgebra
\,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm s}
, of the respective Graßmann parities
\,|X^{a}|=|t_{a}|
. Thus, we consider vector fields on
\,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm s}\times\mathscr{T}\,
from the integrable superdistribution
\,\mathcal{T}\mathscr{T}\,
within
\,\mathcal{T}(\mathfrak{g}_{\rm s}\times\mathscr{T})\,
(
i.e.
,‘along’ the second cartesian factor), of the form
\,\mathcal{K}_{X}\equiv X^{a}\,\mathcal{K}_{t_{a}}\equiv X^{a}\,\mathcal{K}_{a}
, expressed in terms of the ‘standard’ fundamental vector fields
14
14

14

We put an extra minus sign in the definition in order to ensure that
\,t_{a}\longmapsto\mathcal{K}_{a}\,
extends to a Lie-superalgebra morphism rather than
anti
-morphism.

\,\mathcal{K}_{a}=-(t_{a}\otimes{\rm id}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{T}}})\circ\mathscr{T}\lambda^{*}\in{\rm sDer}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{T}})
,
cf.
Ref.
[
CCF11
, Def. 8.2.1]
. In what follows, we use the shorthand notation
\,X\in\mathfrak{g}_{\rm s}
. When taking Lie derivatives of (local) covariant tensor fields on
\,\mathscr{T}
, viewed as a subclass of tensor fields on the product super-space
\,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm s}\times\mathscr{T}
, in the setting of
global
supersymmetry, the resulting (local) covariant tensor fields are to be evaluated on vector fields from the aforementioned superdistribution
\,\mathcal{T}\mathscr{T}\,
exclusively, which we shall not indicate explicitly for the sake of transparency of the notation. This may be understood formally as restricting to its integral leaves within
\,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm s}\times\mathscr{T}
, and practically boils down to treating the supercoordinate sections
\,X^{a}\,
as ‘constant’
15
15

15

This is to be contrasted with the standard treatment of gauge (super)symmetry,
e.g.
, in the construction of equivariant structures on the higher-geometric objects.
. After these preparations, necessitated by peculiarities of supergeometry, we finally come to the conditions of
{\rm G}_{\rm s}
-invariance. Thus, a covariant tensor
\,T\,
on
\,\mathscr{T}\,
is called
{\rm G}_{\rm s}
-invariant
if it satisfies the following conditions

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}|\mathscr{T}\lambda|_{g}^{*}T=T\,,\qquad g\in|{\rm G}_{\rm s}|\cr\cr\,{-\hskip-8.0pt\mathscr{L}}_{\mathcal{K}_{X}}\Omega=0\,,\qquad X\in\mathfrak{g}_{\rm s}\end{array}\right.\,.

In the superfield-theoretic setting in hand, the above conditions are imposed on the tensorial components of the background
\,\mathfrak{B}
. Thus, we presuppose that the metric tensor on the bulk target super-space and both curvatures: that of the bulk 1-gerbe and that of the attendant super-bi-brane, composing the simplicial de Rham 3-cocycle
\,\Omega\,
of Eq. (
1.71
), satisfy

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\bigl(|M\lambda|_{g}^{*}{\rm g},|\mathscr{T}\lambda|_{g}^{*}\Omega\bigr)=({\rm g},\Omega)\,,\qquad g\in|{\rm G}_{\rm s}|\cr\cr\bigl(\,{-\hskip-8.0pt\mathscr{L}}_{\mathcal{K}^{M}_{X}}{\rm g},\,{-\hskip-8.0pt\mathscr{L}}_{\mathcal{K}_{X}}\Omega\bigr)=(0,0)\,,\qquad X\in\mathfrak{g}_{\rm s}\end{array}\right.\,,

where – just to reemphasise – the bottom identities are understood to obtain upon restriction to (the integral foliation of) the superdistribution
\,\mathcal{T}\mathscr{T}\,
in
\,\mathcal{T}(\mathfrak{g}_{\rm s}\times\mathscr{T})
. In this way, we arrive at

Definition 25
.

Let
\,{\rm G}_{\rm s}\,
be a Lie supergroup, as described above. A target super-space
\,\mathscr{T}\,
for a multi-phase super-
\sigma
-model with defects, understood as in Def.
4
, is called a
target
{\rm G}_{\rm s}
-super-space for a multi-phase super-
\sigma
-model with defects
(or a
target
{\rm G}_{\rm s}
-super-space
for short) if it admits a coherent action
\,\mathscr{T}\lambda\,
of
\,{\rm G}_{\rm s}\,
which satisfies the equivariance conditions (
2.1
) and (
2
), and if the tensors
\,({\rm g},\Omega)\,
are
{\rm G}_{\rm s}
-invariant in the sense of Eq. (
2
).

\diamond

An obvious specialisation of the last definition of interest to us is given in

Definition 26
.

Let
\,{\rm G}_{\rm s}\,
be a Lie supergroup, as described above. A
simplicial target
{\rm G}_{\rm s}
-super-space for a multi-phase super-
\sigma
-model with defects
(or a
simplicial target
{\rm G}_{\rm s}
-super-space
for short) is a simplicial target super-space, as introduced in Def.
13
, whose underlying simplicial super-space is a simplicial object in the category of
{\rm G}_{\rm s}
-super-spaces, so that, in particular, its face maps and degeneracy maps are
{\rm G}_{\rm s}
-equivariant, and if the tensors
\,({\rm g},\Omega)\,
are
{\rm G}_{\rm s}
-invariant in the sense of Eq. (
2
).

\diamond

The assumed coherence of the action of the supersymmetry Lie supergroup
\,{\rm G}_{\rm s}\,
on the simplicial target super-space enables us to consistently restrict the associated simplicial de Rham complex of Def.
9
to
{\rm G}_{\rm s}
-invariant forms, whereupon the
{\rm G}_{\rm s}
-invariant simplicial de Rham complex
arises. The de Rham 3-cocycle
\,\Omega\,
from the above definitions is a cochain from this subcomplex. As was recalled in Secs.
1.3
and
1.4
, the 1-gerbe, the bi-brane and the inter-bi-brane of the super-
\sigma
-model provide a geometrisation of the class
\,[\Omega]\,
in the (integral) simplicial de Rham cohomology, and it is now natural to look for a refinement of their construction based on a class in the
{\rm G}_{\rm s}
-invariant simplicial de Rham cohomology
. Unlike in the case of compact Lie groups, the refinement is not trivial
a priori
as there is no counterpart of the Cartan–Eilenberg theorem in the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded setting.

Let us, consequently, lift
{\rm G}_{\rm s}
-invariance to the higher-geometric components of the background. For that, we need, first, to introduce another piece of notation: Given, for some
\,k\in\{0,1,2\}
, a
k
-cell
\,\Psi\,
in
\,\mathfrak{BGrb}^{\nabla}(\mathscr{T})\,
that geometrises (the class of) a
(2-k)
-cochain
\,\psi\,
in the Deligne hypercohomology of
\,\mathscr{T}
, we denote by
\,\,{-\hskip-8.0pt\mathscr{L}}_{\mathcal{K}_{X}}\Psi\,
the
k
-cell in the (weak) 2-category of abelian bundle gerbes with connection over the integral foliation of
\,\mathcal{T}\mathscr{T}\,
in
\,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm s}\times\mathscr{T}\,
that provides a geometric realisation of the
(2-k)
-cochain
\,\,{-\hskip-8.0pt\mathscr{L}}_{\mathcal{K}_{X}}\psi\,
in the Deligne hypercohomology of that foliation. It is now straightforward to spell out the
{\rm G}_{\rm s}
-invariance conditions. Thus, we call the bulk 1-gerbe
\,\mathcal{G}\,

supersymmetric
, or
{\rm G}_{\rm s}
-invariant
, if there exist two families of 1-gerbe 1-isomorphisms:

\displaystyle\Lambda_{g}\ :\ |M\lambda|_{g}^{*}\mathcal{G}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\mathcal{G}\,,\qquad g\in|{\rm G}_{\rm s}|\qquad\quad{\rm and}\qquad\quad K_{X}\ :\ \,{-\hskip-8.0pt\mathscr{L}}_{\mathcal{K}^{M}_{X}}\mathcal{G}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\mathcal{I}_{0}\,,\qquad X\in\mathfrak{g}_{\rm s}\,.

Given such a 1-gerbe
\,\mathcal{G}
, we call the
\mathcal{G}
-bi-brane
\,\mathcal{B}\,

if (condition (
2.1
) is satisfied and) there exist the corresponding families of 1-gerbe 2-isomorphisms:

Finally, for
\,\mathcal{G}\,
and
\,\mathcal{B}\,
as above, we call the inter-
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{B})
-bi-brane
\,\mathcal{J}\,

maximally supersymmetric
if (condition (
2
) is satisfied and) the following identities (with readily reconstructible domains of the identity 2-isomorphisms, left out for the sake of transparency) hold true for all
\,n\in{\mathbb{N}}_{\geq 2}
:

\displaystyle{\rm id}\circ|T_{n,1}\lambda|_{g}^{*}\varphi_{n+1}=\bigl(\varphi_{n+1}\circ{\rm id}\bigr)\bullet\bigl({\rm id}\circ\pi_{1,2}^{*}\lambda_{g}\bigr)\bullet\bigl({\rm id}\circ\pi_{2,3}^{*}\lambda_{g}\circ{\rm id}\bigr)\bullet\cdots\bullet\bigl({\rm id}\circ\pi_{n,n+1}^{*}\lambda_{g}\circ{\rm id}\bigr)\bullet\bigl(\pi_{1,n+1}^{*}\widetilde{\lambda}{}_{g}\circ{\rm id}\bigr)

and

\displaystyle{\rm id}\circ\,{-\hskip-8.0pt\mathscr{L}}_{\mathcal{K}^{T_{n,1}}_{X}}\varphi_{n+1}=\bigl({\rm id}\circ\pi_{1,2}^{*}\kappa_{X}\bigr)\bullet\bigl({\rm id}\circ\pi_{2,3}^{*}\kappa_{X}\circ{\rm id}\bigr)\bullet\cdots\bullet\bigl({\rm id}\circ\pi_{n,n+1}^{*}\kappa_{X}\circ{\rm id}\bigr)\bullet\bigl(\pi_{1,n+1}^{*}\widetilde{\kappa}{}_{X}\circ{\rm id}\bigr)\,.

Whenever a superstring background consists of a supersymmetric target equipped with a
{\rm G}_{\rm s}
-invariant metric tensor and a
{\rm G}_{\rm s}
-invariant 1-gerbe, and of a maximally supersymmetric bi-brane and of an inter-bi-brane with the same property, we speak of a
maximally supersymmetric superstring background
(or a
maximally supersymmetric background
for brevity).

Prior to concluding the present section in which the conceptual foundation of the paper is completed, we discuss special circumstances in which maximal supersymmetry of the superstring background is attained. This we do in all generality, in anticipation of the concrete findings of the subsequent investigation of a particular background with the bulk supergeometry
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})
. We begin with

Definition 27
.

Let
\,\mathfrak{B}\,
be a superstring background as in Def.
18
and assume that the underlying super-space
\,\mathscr{T}=M\sqcup Q\sqcup T\,
is endowed with a coherent action
\,\mathscr{T}\lambda\equiv T\lambda\sqcup Q\lambda\sqcup M\lambda\,
of a (supersymmetry) Lie supergroup
\,{\rm G}_{\rm s}\,
as described above. We call
\,\mathfrak{B}\,
a
superstring super-background
(or a
super-background
for brevity) if the following conditions are satisfied:

(sBM)

There exist lifts of the action
\,M\lambda
:
\,{\mathsf{Y}}M\lambda\,
to
\,{\mathsf{Y}}M\,
and, then, that of
\,{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}M\lambda\equiv({\mathsf{Y}}M\lambda\circ{\rm pr}_{1,2},{\mathsf{Y}}M\lambda\circ{\rm pr}_{1,3})
(
cf.
App.
A
) to
\,L
,

\diamond

As we shall argue, a super-background is an example of a maximally supersymmetric background defined previously. We give a sketch of a demonstration below, focusing on
{\rm G}_{\rm s}
-invariance under the induced action
\,|\mathscr{T}\lambda|\,
of the body Lie group
\,{\rm G}_{\rm s}\,
and leaving the tangential action of the Lie superalgebra
\,\mathfrak{g}_{\rm s}\,
as an exercise to the Reader. We start our analysis with the 1-gerbe
\,\mathcal{G}=({\mathsf{Y}}M,\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}M},{\rm B},L,\pi_{L},\mathcal{A}_{L},\mu_{L})\,
and systematically reconstruct the corresponding pullback 1-gerbe
\,|M\lambda|_{g}^{*}\mathcal{G}\,
for an arbitrary
\,g\in|{\rm G}_{\rm s}|
. The existence of
\,{\mathsf{Y}}M\lambda\,
enables us to choose the surjective submersion
\,|M\lambda|_{g}^{*}{\mathsf{Y}}M\,
of
\,|M\lambda|_{g}^{*}\mathcal{G}\,
as
16
16

16

The standard model of the pullback surjective submersion given by
\,M{}_{|M\lambda|_{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\times_{\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}M}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathsf{Y}}M\,
is surjectively submersed by our
\,|M\lambda|_{g}^{*}{\mathsf{Y}}M\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}M\,
in virtue of the universal property of the pullback.

3.
Simplicial (super)symmetric Lie backgrounds

Considerations of supersymmetry distinguish a class of superstring backgrounds, to wit, those whose target super-spaces are disjoint sums of complete orbits of the supersymmetry group, the criterion of distinction here being ‘reducibility’ (and ‘elementarity’) with respect to the action of the (super)group. We are thus led to focus our attention on (super)string geometrodynamics on homogeneous spaces of Lie supergroups (as bulk target super-spaces). As was shown convincingly in the works
[
GSW10
,
GSW13
,
Sus12
,
Sus11b
,
Sus13
]
of Gawȩdzki, Waldorf and the Author, the higher-geometric structure requisite for the formulation of the geometrodynamics on a homogeneous space
\,{\rm G}/{\rm H}\,
associated with a
nontrivial
subgroup
\,{\rm H}\subset{\rm G}\,
of the supersymmetry group
\,{\rm G}\,
arises through equivariantisation of the given higher-geometric structure over
\,{\rm G}\,
with respect to the action of
\,{\rm H}\,
on
\,{\rm G}
, and so it is well justified to focus further on superstring backgrounds with bulk targets given by Lie supergroups first, which is what we do in this paper. Thus, we fix a Lie supergroup
\,{\rm G}\,
for the remainder of our discussion, together with its binary operation

(3.1)

\displaystyle{\rm m}\ :\ {\rm G}\times{\rm G}\longrightarrow{\rm G}\,,

In this setting, the supersymmetry group
\,{\rm G}_{\rm s}\,
is either the product Lie supergroup
\,{\rm G}\times{\rm G}\,
(the un-graded case) with the (left) action

(3.2)

\displaystyle\ell\wp\equiv\ell\circ\bigl({\rm id}_{\rm G}\times\wp\bigr)\circ\bigl({\rm id}_{\rm G}\times\tau\circ\bigl({\rm Inv}\times{\rm id}_{\rm G}\bigr)\bigr)\ :\ {\rm G}^{\times 2}\times{\rm G}\longrightarrow{\rm G}

written in terms of the standard left (
\ell
, with the second cartesian factor acted upon) and right (
\wp
, with the first cartesian factor acted upon) regular actions
\,\ell\equiv{\rm m}\equiv\wp\,
and the canonical transposition
\,\tau\ :\ {\rm G}_{(1)}\times{\rm G}_{(2)}\longrightarrow{\rm G}_{(2)}\times{\rm G}_{(1)}\,
of the cartesian factors, or its left factor
\,{\rm G}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}\equiv{\rm G}\,
(the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded case
18
18

18

What makes the situation quite a bit more complicated in this case is the presence of an additional ‘infinitesimal’ right
local
supersymmetry, known as
\kappa
-symmetry,
cf.
the original Ref.
[
dAL82
]
for an account of its discovery, Ref.
[
Sie83
]
for that of its re-discovery, and Refs.
[
Sus20
,
Sus21a

Sus21b
]
for an in-depth explanation of its geometric nature. The symmetry is crucial for the supersymmetric balance in the vacuum of the theory, but we shall not deal with it in the present paper, postponing its treatment to a future study – hence the simplification.
) with the (left) action

(3.3)

\displaystyle\ell\equiv\ell\wp\circ\bigl({\rm id}_{\rm G}\times\widehat{e}\times{\rm id}_{\rm G}\bigr).\

The best studied (super-)
\sigma
-models of the corresponding dynamics are collectively referred to as the Wess–Zumino–Witten (WZW) (super-)
\sigma
-models in the literature.

3.1.
Group-homomorphicity categorified

The WZW bulk target super-space combines the structure of a supermanifold and that of a supergroup, the two being related by the supermanifold map (
3.1
), and so a class of superbackgrounds becomes distinguished, namely those whose associated defects implement the binary operation. Fix a 1-gerbe
\,\mathcal{G}\,
over
\,{\rm G}\,
that defines the bulk dynamics. Following the general philosophy recalled in Sec.
1.4
, we then, in particular, look for a sub-supermanifold
\,Q\subset{\rm G}\times{\rm G}\,
which supports a 1-gerbe 1-isomorphism
\,{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{G}\mathord{\restriction}_{Q}\cong{\rm m}^{*}\mathcal{G}\mathord{\restriction}_{Q}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\,
for some
\,\omega\in\Omega^{2}(Q)
, or, equivalently, one on which we have
\,{\rm m}^{*}\mathcal{G}\mathord{\restriction}_{Q}\cong\bigl({\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{G}\otimes{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{G}\bigr)\mathord{\restriction}_{Q}\otimes\bigl({\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{G}^{*}\mathord{\restriction}_{Q}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{-\omega}\bigr)
. Such a reformulation of the original problem emphasises the significance of the condition of compatibility of the higher-geometric structure
\,\mathcal{G}\,
with the binary operation on its base that generalises the condition of homomorphicity encountered in the study of functions on
\,{\rm G}
. Indeed, if we had
\,{\rm m}^{*}\mathcal{G}\cong{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{G}\otimes{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{G}
, a solution to the above problem could be chosen in the form
\,Q={\rm G}\times D\,
for an
arbitrary

\,D\subset{\rm G}\,
that supports a trivialisation
\,\mathcal{G}\mathord{\restriction}_{D}\cong\mathcal{I}_{\omega_{D}}
. It would then suffice to take
\,\omega=-{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\omega_{D}
. The rigidity of the former assumption, precluding the much desired application of the argument in the familiar setting of the WZW
\sigma
-model with
\,{\rm G}\,
compact and 1-connected (
cf.
below), is readily circumvented by allowing a trivial but not necessarily zero correction

(3.4)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\varrho}\cong{\rm m}^{*}\mathcal{G}^{*}\otimes{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{G}\otimes{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{G}\,,

which does not invalidate the conclusion qualitatively but merely calls for a quantitative redefinition
\,-{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\omega_{D}\longmapsto-{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\omega_{D}+\varrho\,
of the bi-brane curvature
\,\omega
. This, then, is the sort of additional structure on the bulk 1-gerbe
\,\mathcal{G}\,
over
\,{\rm G}\,
that we consider in some detail below, with view to subsequent applications in the the study of maximally (super)symmetric WZW defects.

The point of departure in our discussion is the
Polyakov–Wiegmann
(
–type
)
identity

(3.5)

\displaystyle{\rm m}^{*}{\rm H}={\rm pr}_{1}^{*}{\rm H}+{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}{\rm H}-{\mathsf{d}}\varrho

that has to be satisfied by the curvature
\,{\rm H}\,
of the 1-gerbe
\,\mathcal{G}
. Following Ref.
[
GW09
]
, we develop its geometrisation over the nerve of the category

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern opposite to the category canonically associated with the Lie supergroup

\,\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.92361pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.92361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.92361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 30.92361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\,
into the terminal supermanifold, the identity morphism is the topological unit in

reverse

\tau_{\rm G}\ :\ {\rm G}\times{\rm G}\circlearrowleft\,
is the transposition, with
\,{\rm pr}_{1}\circ\tau_{\rm G}={\rm pr}_{2},\ {\rm pr}_{2}\circ\tau_{\rm G}={\rm pr}_{1}

\,\mathcal{S}\,
and the associated
\mathcal{S}

\,{\rm Hom}_{\rm{\bf sMan}}(\mathcal{S},{\rm G})\equiv{\rm G}(\mathcal{S})
, denoting the mapping
\,{\rm Hom}_{\rm{\bf sMan}}(\mathcal{S},{\rm m})\ :\ {\rm G}(\mathcal{S})^{\times 2}\longrightarrow{\rm G}(\mathcal{S})\,
induced by
\,{\rm m}\,
simply by a dot. Thus, an
\mathcal{S}
-point in the morphism supermanifold is
\,g\in{\rm G}(\mathcal{S})
, and the source and target maps are both represented by
\,g\longmapsto\bullet
, whereas the identity morphism associates the unit
\,e\equiv e_{{\rm G}(\mathcal{S})}\in{\rm G}(\mathcal{S})\,
to the (unique)
\mathcal{S}
-point
\,\bullet\in{\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}(\mathcal{S})
. In this notation, composition of morphisms is simply given by
\,(g_{1},g_{2})\longmapsto g_{2}\cdot g_{1}
. Now, the nerve consists, in the
\mathcal{S}
-point picture, of spaces
\,{\mathsf{N}}_{n}{\rm G}^{\rm op}(\mathcal{S})={\rm G}^{\times n}(\mathcal{S})\,
with the face and degeneracy maps given (for any
\,n
) by

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle d^{(n)}_{i}(\mathcal{S})(g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{n})=\begin{cases}(g_{2},\ldots,g_{n})&\text{if}\quad i=0\cr(g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{i-1},g_{i}\cdot g_{i+1},g_{i+2},\ldots,g_{n})&\text{if}\quad 0<i<n\,,\cr(g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{n-1})&\text{if}\quad i=n\end{cases}\\[15.00002pt]
\\
s^{(n)}_{i}(\mathcal{S})(g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{n})=(g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{i},e,g_{i+1},\ldots,g_{n})\,,\end{array}

and the non-trivial simplicial identities reflect the group axioms.

At this stage, a specialisation of the construction of Def.
9
gives us the cohomology of relevance to our subsequent considerations. Thus, we obtain the cochain bicomplex (here,

with the vertical coboundary operators

\displaystyle\Delta_{{\rm G}}^{(n;p)}\equiv\sum_{i=0}^{n+1}\,(-1)^{i}\,d^{(n+1)\,*}_{i}\ :\ \Omega^{p}\bigl({\rm G}^{\times n}\bigr)\longrightarrow\Omega^{p}\bigl({\rm G}^{\times n+1}\bigr)\,,\qquad(n,p)\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\times 2}\,.

On its diagonal, we find the cochain complex

\displaystyle\bigl(\mathcal{A}^{\bullet}\bigl({\mathsf{N}}_{\bullet}{\rm G}^{\rm op}\bigr),\mathcal{D}^{(\bullet)}_{{\rm G}}\bigr)\qquad:\qquad\mathcal{A}^{0}\bigl({\mathsf{N}}_{\bullet}{\rm G}^{\rm op}\bigr)\xrightarrow{\ \mathcal{D}_{{\rm G}}^{(0)}\ }\mathcal{A}^{1}\bigl({\mathsf{N}}_{\bullet}{\rm G}^{\rm op}\bigr)\xrightarrow{\ \mathcal{D}^{(1)}_{{\rm G}}\ }\cdots\xrightarrow{\ \mathcal{D}^{(q-1)}_{{\rm G}}\ }\mathcal{A}^{q}\bigl({\mathsf{N}}_{\bullet}{\rm G}^{\rm op}\bigr)\xrightarrow{\ \mathcal{D}^{(q)}_{{\rm G}}\ }\cdots

with the
q
-cochain groups

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{q}\bigl({\mathsf{N}}_{\bullet}{\rm G}^{\rm op}\bigr)=\bigoplus_{p=0}^{q}\,A^{p,q-p}\,,\qquad\qquad A^{p,q-p}\equiv\Omega^{p}\bigl({\rm G}^{\times q-p}\bigr)\,,\qquad\qquad q\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\times}

related by the couboundary operators

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{{\rm G}}^{(q)}\ :\ \mathcal{A}^{q}\bigl({\mathsf{N}}_{\bullet}{\rm G}^{\rm op}\bigr)\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{q+1}\bigl({\mathsf{N}}_{\bullet}{\rm G}^{\rm op}\bigr)\,,\qquad\qquad\mathcal{D}_{{\rm G}}^{(q)}\mathord{\restriction}_{A^{p,q-p}}=(-1)^{q-p+1}\,{\mathsf{d}}^{(p;q-p)}_{\rm dR}+\,\Delta_{\rm G}^{(q-p;p)}\,,

and it is the ensuing total cohomology of the bicomplex that captures the structure of the Polyakov–Wiegmann identity and, as such, becomes the starting point of the geometrisation/categorification that we are after. Prior to giving higher-geometric flesh to the last statement, we fix one more

We are now finally ready to formulate

Definition 28
.

Let
\,{\rm G}\,
be a Lie supergroup, let
\,\mathcal{G}=({\mathsf{Y}}{\rm G},\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm G}},{\rm B},L,\pi_{L},{\rm A},\mu_{L})\,
be a 1-gerbe of curvature
\,{\rm H}\equiv{\rm curv}\,(\mathcal{G})\in Z^{3}_{\rm dR}({\rm G})
, and suppose there exist: a 2-form
\,\varrho\in\Omega^{2}({\rm G}^{\times 2})
, a 1-form
\,\vartheta\in\Omega^{1}({\rm G}^{\times 3})\,
and a 0-form
\,\varphi\in\Omega^{0}({\rm G}^{\times 4})\,
such that
\,\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{G}}\equiv(0,{\rm H},\varrho,\vartheta,\varphi)\in{\rm Ker}\,\mathcal{D}^{(4)}_{\rm G}
. A
generalised multiplicative structure on

\,\mathcal{G}\,
associated with the 4-cocycle
\,\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{G}}\,
is a pair
\,(\mathcal{M},\alpha)\,
that consists of a 1-gerbe 1-isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}\ :\ d_{2}^{(2)\,*}\mathcal{G}\otimes d_{0}^{(2)\,*}\mathcal{G}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }d_{1}^{(2)\,*}\mathcal{G}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\varrho}

and a 1-gerbe 2-isomorphism (
cf.
App.
A
)

(3.11)
