0.85764pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.51425pt\raise-0.85764pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern-0.51425pt\raise 0.85764pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\,,

of 1-gerbe 2-isomorphisms over
\,{\rm G}^{\times 4}
, written in terms of the trivial
(-1)
-gerbe

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{-\varphi}\ :\ \mathcal{J}_{-d_{1}^{(4)\,*}\vartheta-d_{3}^{(4)\,*}\vartheta}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\mathcal{J}_{-d_{4}^{(4)\,*}\vartheta-d_{2}^{(4)\,*}\vartheta-d^{(4)\,*}_{0}\vartheta}\,.

Whenever
\,\varphi\equiv 0
, we call the corresponding generalised multiplicative structure
0-flat
, and
if
\,(\vartheta,\varphi)=(0,0)
, we speak of a (
standard
)
multiplicative structure
.

A 1-gerbe
\,\mathcal{G}\,
that admits a generalised (resp. 0-flat, resp. standard) multiplicative structure shall be called
generalised-multiplicative
(resp.
0-flat generalised-multiplicative
, resp.
multiplicative
).

\diamond

3.2.
WZW (super)targets as
{\rm G}_{\rm s}
-orbits

The foregoing discussion identifies the nerve
\,(\underline{{\mathsf{N}}}{}_{\bullet}{\rm G}\equiv{\mathsf{N}}_{\bullet}({\rm G}\rtimes{\rm G}),\underline{d}{}^{(\bullet)}_{\cdot},\underline{s}{}^{(\bullet)}_{\cdot})\,
of the action groupoid

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 43.97224pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-43.97224pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Mor}\bigl({\rm G}\rtimes{\rm G}\bigr)\equiv{\rm G}\times{\rm G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 50.48076pt\raise 9.84224pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.68947pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{s={\rm pr}_{1}\equiv\underline{d}{}^{(1)}_{1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 115.10413pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.02437pt\raise-9.84222pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.68947pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{t=\wp\equiv\underline{d}{}^{(1)}_{0}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 115.10413pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 115.10413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm G}\equiv\mathrm{Ob}\bigl({\rm G}\rtimes{\rm G}\bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 40.96507pt\raise 31.68947pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.68947pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm Id}_{\cdot}={\rm id}_{\rm G}\times\widehat{e}\equiv\underline{s}{}^{(0)}_{0}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 16.57553pt\raise 9.49768pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\,,

as the simplicial supermanifold in which to look for the simplicial WZW target super-space whose associated higher-geometric data – containing, in particular, a 1-gerbe over
\,M={\rm G}\equiv\underline{{\mathsf{N}}}{}_{0}{\rm G}\,
and the composite 1-isomorphism
\,(\mathcal{M}\otimes{\rm id})\circ({\rm id}\otimes{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{D}^{-1}\otimes{\rm id})\ :\ {\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{G}\equiv{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{G}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\omega_{D}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{-{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\omega_{D}}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }{\rm m}^{*}\mathcal{G}\mathord{\restriction}_{{\rm G}\times D}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\omega}\,
over
\,Q={\rm G}\times D\subset{\rm G}^{\times 2}\equiv\underline{{\mathsf{N}}}{}_{1}{\rm G}\,
(for some disjoint union
\,D\,
of
{\rm G}\lambda
-orbits within
\,{\rm G}
) – determine a multi-phase super-
\sigma
-model with a defect realising the basic algebraic structure (
3.1
) on the bulk WZW target. The lowest rung of the nerve comes with one of the previously distinguished
{\rm G}_{\rm s}
-actions

\displaystyle({\rm G}_{\rm s},{\rm G}\lambda\}\in\bigl\{\bigl({\rm G}^{\times 2},\ell\wp\bigr),({\rm G},\ell)\bigr\}\,,

from which we readily induce the structure of a simplicial
{\rm G}_{\rm s}
-supermanifold on
\,\underline{{\mathsf{N}}}{}_{\bullet}{\rm G}\,
by declaring its face maps to be
{\rm G}_{\rm s}
-equivariant. Once this is achieved, we may decompose the latter into complete
{\rm G}_{\rm s}
-orbits and subsequently look for suitable higher-geometric structures compatible with the supersymmetry over these supergeometries. The first part of this task shall be completed below.

Let us, first, determine the equivariant lift of
\,{\rm G}\lambda\,
from
\,{\rm G}\equiv\underline{{\mathsf{N}}}{}_{0}{\rm G}\,
to the higher components
\,\underline{{\mathsf{N}}}{}_{n}{\rm G}\equiv{\mathsf{N}}_{n}({\rm G}\rtimes{\rm G})\equiv{\mathsf{N}}_{n+1}({\rm G}^{\rm op}),\ n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\times}\,
of the nerve
19
19

19

In order to convince ourselves that this is, in fact, the nerve of the said action groupoid, it suffices to pass, in the
\mathcal{S}
-point picture, from the standard representation
\,((g,h_{1}),(g\cdot h_{1},h_{2}),\ldots,(g\cdot h_{1}\cdot\cdots\cdot h_{n-1},h_{n}))\,
of an
n
-tuple of composable morphisms of
\,{\rm G}\rtimes{\rm G}\,
(with
\,g,h_{i}\in{\rm G},\ i\in\overline{1,n}
) to the equivalent one
\,(g,h_{1},h_{2},\ldots,h_{n})
.
along its face maps
\,\underline{d}{}^{(n)}_{i}\equiv d^{(n+1)}_{i+1}
, turning also the degeneracy maps
\,\underline{s}{}^{(n)}_{i}\equiv s^{(n+1)}_{i+1},\ i\in\overline{0,n}\,
into
{\rm G}_{\rm s}
-equivariant mappings. For this purpose, introduce the canonical diagonal embedding
\,\Delta\ :\ {\rm G}\longrightarrow{\rm G}\times{\rm G}\,
determined by the identities
\,{\rm pr}_{1}\circ\Delta={\rm id}_{\rm G}={\rm pr}_{2}\circ\Delta
, and subsequently use it to define the conjugation (the adjoint action)
\,{\rm Ad}\equiv\ell\wp\circ(\Delta\times{\rm id}_{\rm G})\ :\ {\rm G}\times{\rm G}\longrightarrow({\rm G}\times{\rm G})\times{\rm G}\longrightarrow{\rm G}
. We then readily establish, for
\,({\rm G}_{\rm s},{\rm G}\lambda)=({\rm G}\times{\rm G},\ell\wp)
, the following form of the
{\rm G}_{\rm s}
-action at level
\,n\geq 1
:

\displaystyle{\rm G}\lambda^{(n)}\equiv\ell\wp^{(n)}=\bigl(\ell\wp\circ{\rm pr}_{1,2,3},{\rm Ad}\circ{\rm pr}_{1,2,4},{\rm Ad}\circ{\rm pr}_{1,2,5},\ldots,{\rm Ad}\circ{\rm pr}_{1,2,n+3}\bigr)\ :\ \bigl({\rm G}\times{\rm G}\bigr)\times{\rm G}^{\times n+1}\longrightarrow{\rm G}^{\times n+1}\,.

For
\,({\rm G}_{\rm s},{\rm G}\lambda)=({\rm G},\ell)
, on the other hand, we obtain

(3.22)

\displaystyle{\rm G}\lambda^{(n)}\equiv\ell^{(n)}=\ell\times{\rm id}_{{\rm G}^{\times n}}\ :\ {\rm G}\times{\rm G}^{\times n+1}\longrightarrow{\rm G}^{\times n+1}\,,\qquad n\geq 1\,.

It is now natural to investigate the decomposition of the newly found simplicial supermanifold
\,\underline{{\mathsf{N}}}{}_{\bullet}{\rm G}\,
into ‘irreducible’ sub-
{\rm G}_{\rm s}
-supermanifolds,
i.e.
, orbits of the action
\,{\rm G}\lambda^{(\bullet)}
. In the case of
\,({\rm G}_{\rm s},{\rm G}\lambda)=({\rm G},\ell)
, of relevance,
e.g.
, to the study of the super-
\sigma
-model on the Lie supergroup
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})
, this investigation trivialises, but its fully fledged application in the superfield-theoretic setting calls, first, for the incorporation of the ‘infinitesimal gauged right’ supersymmetry known as the
\kappa
-symmetry into the picture, and so we leave it out of the present discussion. Instead, we take a closer look at orbits of the action
\,\ell\wp^{(\bullet)}\,
of
\,{\rm G}\times{\rm G}\,
on components of
\,\underline{{\mathsf{N}}}{}_{\bullet}{\rm G}\,
for a Lie group
\,{\rm G}
. We treat the spaces
\,\underline{{\mathsf{N}}}{}_{n}{\rm G}\,
for the first few values of
n
explicitly, and, thus prepared, eventually give the general structure.

\bullet

n=0
: An element
\,(x,y)\in{\rm G}\times{\rm G}\,
acts on
\,\underline{{\mathsf{N}}}{}_{0}{\rm G}\equiv{\rm G}\ni g\,
as
\,\ell\wp^{(0)}_{(x,y)}(g)=x\cdot g\cdot y^{-1}
, so that
\,\underline{{\mathsf{N}}}{}_{0}{\rm G}\,
is a single orbit.

\bullet

n=1
:
\,(x,y)\,
acts on
\,\underline{{\mathsf{N}}}{}_{1}{\rm G}\ni(g,h)\,
as
\,\ell\wp^{(1)}_{(x,y)}(g,h)=(x\cdot g\cdot y^{-1},{\rm Ad}_{y}(h))
, and so we find the decomposition into orbits

\displaystyle\underline{{\mathsf{N}}}{}_{1}{\rm G}=\bigsqcup_{\lambda\in{\mathsf{k}}\,\mathscr{A}_{\rm W}(\mathfrak{g})}\,{\rm G}\times\mathscr{C}_{\lambda}\,,\qquad\qquad\mathscr{C}_{\lambda}=\bigl\{\ {\rm Ad}_{x}({\rm e}_{\lambda})\ |\ x\in{\rm G}\ \bigr\}\,,\qquad{\rm e}_{\lambda}={\rm e}^{2\pi{\mathsf{i}}\frac{\lambda}{{\mathsf{k}}}}\,,

labelled – with hindsight – by vectors in the
{\mathsf{k}}
-rescaled Weyl alcove

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}_{W}(\mathfrak{g})=\bigl\{\ \lambda\in\mathfrak{t}\quad\big|\quad\langle\lambda,\theta\rangle\leq 1\quad\land\quad\langle\lambda,\alpha_{i}\rangle\geq 0\,,\ i=\overline{1,{\rm rank}\,\mathfrak{g}}\ \bigr\}\,,

the latter being defined in terms of the simple roots
\alpha_{i}\,
and of the longest root
\,\theta\,
of
\,\mathfrak{g}
, and in terms of the canonical pairing
\,\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle\,
between roots and weights – the scaling, inconsequential at this stage, prepares us for the subsequent use of the
{\mathsf{k}}
-rescaled Cartan–Killing metric on
\,{\rm G}\,
(affording,
i.a.
, the identification between
\,\mathfrak{g}\,
and its dual).

\bullet

n=2
:
\,(x,y)\,
acts on
\,\underline{{\mathsf{N}}}{}_{2}{\rm G}\ni(g,h_{1},h_{2})\,
as
\,\ell\wp^{(2)}_{(x,y)}(g,h_{1},h_{2})=(x\cdot g\cdot y^{-1},{\rm Ad}_{y}(h_{1}),{\rm Ad}_{y}(h_{2}))
. Fixing
\,y\,
and varying
\,x\,
over
\,{\rm G}
, we obtain, again, the entire
\,{\rm G}\,
as the first cartesian factor of a single orbit. Let
\,\lambda_{i}\in{\mathsf{k}}\,\mathscr{A}_{\rm W}(\mathfrak{g})
,
i=1,2\,
be such that
\,h_{i}\in\mathscr{C}_{\lambda_{i}}
, and choose some
\,x,w\in{\rm G}\,
so that
\,h_{1}={\rm Ad}_{x}({\rm e}_{\lambda_{1}})\,
and
\,h_{2}={\rm Ad}_{x\cdot w}({\rm e}_{\lambda_{2}})
. Then, the
{\rm G}\times{\rm G}
-orbit of
\,(g,h_{1},h_{2})\,
coincides with that of
\,(g,{\rm e}_{\lambda_{1}},{\rm Ad}_{w}({\rm e}_{\lambda_{2}}))
. Any two such orbits for
\,w,w^{\prime}\in{\rm G}\,
are equal iff
\,w^{\prime}=u\cdot w\cdot v\,
for some
\,u\in\mathcal{S}_{\lambda_{1}}\,
and
\,v\in\mathcal{S}_{\lambda_{2}}
, where

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\lambda_{i}}:=\{\ g\in{\rm G}\quad|\quad{\rm Ad}_{g}({\rm e}_{\lambda_{i}})={\rm e}_{\lambda_{i}}\ \}

is the
{\rm Ad}
-stabiliser of
\,{\rm e}_{\lambda_{i}}
. This relation defines the double coset
\,\mathcal{S}_{\lambda_{1}}\backslash{\rm G}/\mathcal{S}_{\lambda_{2}}
. Altogether, then, we obtain the decomposition

\displaystyle\underline{{\mathsf{N}}}{}_{2}{\rm G}=\bigsqcup_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\in{\mathsf{k}}\,\mathscr{A}_{\rm W}(\mathfrak{g})^{\times 2}}\,\bigsqcup_{[w]\in\mathcal{S}_{\lambda_{1}}\backslash{\rm G}/\mathcal{S}_{\lambda_{2}}}\,{\rm G}\times T_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}^{[w]}\,,

where

\displaystyle T_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}^{[w]}=\left\{\ \left({\rm Ad}_{y}({\rm e}_{\lambda_{1}}),{\rm Ad}_{y\cdot w}({\rm e}_{\lambda_{2}})\right)\quad\big|\quad y\in{\rm G}\ \right\}\subset\mathscr{C}_{\lambda_{1}}\times\mathscr{C}_{\lambda_{2}}\,.

Define a map

\displaystyle\rho_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}\ :\ \mathcal{S}_{\lambda_{1}}\backslash{\rm G}/\mathcal{S}_{\lambda_{2}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{k}}\,\mathscr{A}_{\rm W}(\mathfrak{g})

by taking
\,\rho_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}([w])\,
to be the unique element of the
{\mathsf{k}}
-rescaled Weyl alcove such that
\,{\rm e}_{\lambda_{1}}\cdot{\rm Ad}_{w}({\rm e}_{\lambda_{2}})\in\mathscr{C}_{\rho_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}([w])}
. Fixing
\,[w]\in\rho_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}^{-1}(\{\lambda\})\,
and applying the face maps
\,\underline{d}{}^{(2)}_{\cdot}\ :\ \underline{{\mathsf{N}}}{}_{2}{\rm G}\longrightarrow\underline{{\mathsf{N}}}{}_{1}{\rm G}

(
i.e.
, the inter-bi-brane maps for
\,T_{2,1}
), we find

(3.23)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 54.56024pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 111.46574pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 174.37125pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(g\cdot h_{1},h_{2})\in{\rm G}\times\mathscr{C}_{\lambda_{2}}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-54.56024pt\raise-37.1422pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm G}\times T_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}^{[w]}\ni(g,h_{1},h_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 98.44576pt\raise-10.88164pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.68947pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\underline{d}{}^{(2)}_{0}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 174.37125pt\raise-7.76903pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 100.15228pt\raise-29.45273pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.68947pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\underline{d}{}^{(2)}_{1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 177.43375pt\raise-37.1422pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 101.85881pt\raise-48.02382pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.68947pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\underline{d}{}^{(2)}_{2}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 183.10616pt\raise-68.0192pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 111.46574pt\raise-37.1422pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 177.43375pt\raise-37.1422pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(g,h_{1}\cdot h_{2})\in{\rm G}\times\mathscr{C}_{\lambda}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-74.2844pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 111.46574pt\raise-74.2844pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 183.10616pt\raise-74.2844pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(g,h_{1})\in{\rm G}\times\mathscr{C}_{\lambda_{1}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\,,

and so
\,T_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}^{[w]}\,
sits in the intersection

\displaystyle T_{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}^{\lambda}:={\rm pr}_{2,3}\bigl(\underline{d}{}^{(2)\,-1}_{2}\bigl({\rm G}\times\mathscr{C}_{\lambda_{1}}\bigr)\cap\underline{d}{}^{(2)\,-1}_{0}\bigl({\rm G}\times\mathscr{C}_{\lambda_{2}}\bigr)\cap\underline{d}{}^{(2)\,-1}_{1}\bigl({\rm G}\times\mathscr{C}_{\lambda}\bigr)\bigr)\equiv\bigl(\mathscr{C}_{\lambda_{1}}\times\mathscr{C}_{\lambda_{1}}\bigr)\cap{\rm m}^{-1}(\mathscr{C}_{\lambda})\supset T_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}^{[w]}\,.

With view to our subsequent analyses, we introduce two more maps on
\,{\rm G}^{\times 2}
, to wit,

(3.24)

\displaystyle[W]\ :\ {\rm G}^{\times 2}\longrightarrow\bigsqcup_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\in{\mathsf{k}}\,\mathscr{A}_{\rm W}(\mathfrak{g})^{\times 2}}\,\mathcal{S}_{\lambda_{1}}\backslash{\rm G}/\mathcal{S}_{\lambda_{2}}\,,\qquad\qquad\Lambda\ :\ {\rm G}^{\times 2}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{k}}\,\mathscr{A}_{\rm W}(\mathfrak{g})

with the defining properties

\displaystyle(g_{1},g_{2})\in\mathscr{C}_{\lambda_{1}}\times\mathscr{C}_{\lambda_{2}}\ \Longrightarrow\ \left(\ (g_{1},g_{2})\in T_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}^{[W](g_{1},g_{2})}\qquad\land\qquad g_{1}\cdot g_{2}\in\mathscr{C}_{\Lambda(g_{1},g_{2})}\ \right)\,.

We have the obvious identity

\displaystyle\rho_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}\circ[W]|_{\mathscr{C}_{\lambda_{1}}\times\mathscr{C}_{\lambda_{2}}}=\Lambda|_{\mathscr{C}_{\lambda_{1}}\times\mathscr{C}_{\lambda_{2}}}\,.

Note that for
\,{\rm SU}(2)\,
the class
\,[w]\,
is uniquely fixed by
\,\lambda=\rho_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}([w])
, so that
\,T_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}^{\lambda}\equiv T_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}^{[w]}\,
is a single orbit.

\bullet

n>2
: This case is treated analogously to that of
\,n=2
. For
\,\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\ldots,\lambda_{n}\in{\mathsf{k}}\,\mathscr{A}_{\rm W}(\mathfrak{g})\,
and
\,w_{2},w_{3},\ldots,w_{n}\in{\rm G}
, we define

(3.25)

\displaystyle T_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\ldots,\lambda_{n})}^{[w_{2},w_{3},\ldots,w_{n}]}=\left\{\ \left({\rm Ad}_{y}({\rm e}_{\lambda_{1}}),{\rm Ad}_{y\cdot w_{2}}({\rm e}_{\lambda_{2}}),\ldots,{\rm Ad}_{y\cdot w_{n}}({\rm e}_{\lambda_{n}})\right)\quad|\quad y\in{\rm G}\ \right\}\,,

where
\,[w_{2},w_{3},\ldots,w_{n}]\,
denotes an equivalence class of tuples
\,(w_{2},w_{3},\ldots,w_{n})\in{\rm G}^{\times n-1}
, with two tuples
\,(w_{2},w_{3},\ldots,w_{n})\,
and
\,(w_{2}^{\prime},w_{3}^{\prime},\ldots,w_{n}^{\prime})\,
considered equivalent iff
\,T_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\ldots,\lambda_{n})}^{[w_{2},w_{3},\ldots,w_{n}]}=T_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\ldots,\lambda_{n})}^{[w_{2}^{\prime},w_{3}^{\prime},\ldots,w_{n}^{\prime}]}
. In other words,
\,[w_{2},w_{3},\ldots,w_{n}]\in\mathcal{S}_{\lambda_{1}}\backslash\times_{i=2}^{n}\,\left({\rm G}/\mathcal{S}_{\lambda_{i}}\right)
, where the left action of
\,{\rm G}\,
(defining the left coset) is the diagonal left multiplication:
\bigl(\,g,\bigl(w_{2}\cdot\mathcal{S}_{\lambda_{2}},w_{3}\cdot\mathcal{S}_{\lambda_{3}},\ldots,w_{n}\cdot\mathcal{S}_{\lambda_{n}}\bigr)\bigr)\longmapsto\left(g\cdot w_{2}\cdot\mathcal{S}_{\lambda_{2}},g\cdot w_{3}\cdot\mathcal{S}_{\lambda_{3}},\ldots,g\cdot w_{n}\cdot\mathcal{S}_{\lambda_{n}}\right)
. The decomposition of
\,\underline{{\mathsf{N}}}{}_{n}{\rm G}\,
into orbits can now be written as

\displaystyle\underline{{\mathsf{N}}}{}_{n}{\rm G}=\bigsqcup_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}\in{\mathsf{k}}\,\mathscr{A}_{\rm W}(\mathfrak{g})^{\times n}}\,\bigsqcup_{[\overrightarrow{w}]\in\mathcal{S}_{\lambda_{1}}\backslash\times_{i=2}^{n}\,\left({\rm G}/\mathcal{S}_{\lambda_{i}}\right)}\,{\rm G}\times T_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{[\overrightarrow{w}]}\,.

By construction, for a given
\,\overrightarrow{\lambda}:=(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\ldots,\lambda_{n})\,
and every class
\,[\overrightarrow{w}]:=[w_{2},w_{3},\ldots,w_{n}]
, there exists a unique
\,\lambda\in{\mathsf{k}}\,\mathscr{A}_{\rm W}(\mathfrak{g})\,
such that the orbit
\,{\rm G}\times T_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{[\overrightarrow{w}]}\,
gets mapped to
\,{\rm G}\times\mathscr{C}_{\lambda}\,
on multiplication in the second factor. As previously, this gives rise to a map

\displaystyle\rho_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}\ :\ \mathcal{S}_{\lambda_{1}}\backslash\times_{i=2}^{n}\,\left({\rm G}/\mathcal{S}_{\lambda_{i}}\right)\longrightarrow{\mathsf{k}}\,\mathscr{A}_{\rm W}(\mathfrak{g})

with the defining property:

(3.26)

\displaystyle\lambda\equiv\rho_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}([\overrightarrow{w}])\quad\text{ is such that }\quad{\rm e}_{\lambda_{1}}\cdot{\rm Ad}_{w_{2}}({\rm e}_{\lambda_{2}})\cdot\cdots\cdot{\rm Ad}_{w_{n}}({\rm e}_{\lambda_{n}})\in\mathscr{C}_{\lambda}\,,

and we land in

(3.27)

\displaystyle T_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{\lambda}:=\times_{i=1}^{n}\,\mathscr{C}_{\lambda_{i}}\cap{\rm m}_{1,2,\ldots,n}^{-1}(\mathscr{C}_{\lambda})\supset T_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{[\overrightarrow{w}]}\,.

By way of a closing remark, we note that the orbits
\,{\rm G}\times T_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{[\overrightarrow{w}]}\subset\underline{{\mathsf{N}}}{}_{n}{\rm G}\,
for
\,n>2\,
come with a number of smooth (
{\rm G}
-equivariant) maps to the ‘elementary’ geometries
\,{\rm G}\times T_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}^{[w]}
. All these maps act as the identity
\,{\rm id}_{\rm G}\,
on the spectator factor
\,{\rm G}
, and so we focus on their nontrivial cartesian factors supported on the
\,T_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{[\overrightarrow{w}]}
. A map from the class that we have in mind is labelled by arbitrary integers
\,1\leq i\leq j<k\leq l\leq n\,
and takes the following general form

(3.28)

\displaystyle\pi_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{[\overrightarrow{w}]}\bigl(i,j|k,l\bigr)\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\ T_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{[\overrightarrow{w}]}\longrightarrow\bigsqcup_{(\lambda,\mu)\in P^{{\mathsf{k}}}_{+}(\mathfrak{g})^{\times 2}}\,\bigsqcup_{[w]\in\mathcal{S}_{\lambda}\backslash{\rm G}/\mathcal{S}_{\mu}}\,T_{(\lambda,\mu)}^{[w]}

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\ (h_{1},h_{2},\ldots,h_{n})\longmapsto(h_{i}\cdot h_{i+1}\cdot\cdots\cdot h_{j},h_{k}\cdot h_{k+1}\cdot\cdots\cdot h_{l})\,,

with the understanding that
\,\pi_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{[\overrightarrow{w}]}(i,i|k,l>k)\equiv({\rm pr}_{i},{\rm m}^{(n)}_{k\,k+1\ldots l}),\pi_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{[\overrightarrow{w}]}(i,j>i|k,k)\equiv({\rm m}^{(n)}_{i\,i+1\ldots j},{\rm pr}_{k})\,
and
\,\pi_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{[\overrightarrow{w}]}(i,i|j,j)\equiv({\rm pr}_{i},{\rm pr}_{j})
. The maps are restrictions (which we have not made explicit, for the sake of transparency) of the corresponding (bi-)multiplications
\,{\rm G}^{\times n}\longrightarrow{\rm G}^{\times 2}\,
which we denote, accordingly, as

(3.30)

\displaystyle{\rm m}^{(n)}\bigl(i,j|k,l\bigr)\ :\ {\rm G}^{\times n}\longrightarrow{\rm G}^{\times 2}\,,\qquad\qquad{\rm m}^{(n)}\bigl(i,j|k,l\bigr)\mathord{\restriction}_{T_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{[\overrightarrow{w}]}}\equiv\pi_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{[\overrightarrow{w}]}\bigl(i,j|k,l\bigr)\,.

Upon picking up an arbitrary
n
-tuple
\,(h_{1},h_{2},\ldots,h_{n})\in T_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{[\overrightarrow{w}]}
, define

\displaystyle[W]_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{[\overrightarrow{w}]}(i,j|k,l):=[W]\circ\pi_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{[\overrightarrow{w}]}\bigl(i,j|k,l\bigr)(h_{1},h_{2},\ldots,h_{n})

and

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{[\overrightarrow{w}]}(i,j):=\Lambda\circ\pi_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{[\overrightarrow{w}]}\bigl(i,j-1|j,j\bigr)(h_{1},h_{2},\ldots,h_{n})\,,

the two being manifestly independent of the choice of the
n
-tuple. We then readily see that

\displaystyle\pi_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{[\overrightarrow{w}]}(i,j|k,l)\left(T_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{[\overrightarrow{w}]}\right)=T_{(\Lambda_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{[\overrightarrow{w}]}(i,j),\Lambda_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{[\overrightarrow{w}]}(k,l))}^{[W]_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{[\overrightarrow{w}]}(i,j|k,l)}\,.

The last observation shall be used in Sec.
5.4
to induce cohomological structure on the
\,\mathcal{T}_{n}\,
from that existing on
\,\mathcal{T}_{2}\,
(under additional hypotheses).

The above structure shall be reflected, in its entirety, in the construction of higher-geometric objects attached to a maximally (super)symmetric defect. Hence, we shall have more to say about its naturality later on, when we come to discuss the gerbe-theoretic components of the simplicial WZW background in the presence of the defects. But before we get there, we conclude this section with a refinement of our former treatment of the geometrisation of the Polyakov–Wiegmann identity.

To this end, we employ
\,{\rm G}\lambda\,
to induce, in the previously considered manner, the (pointwise) action of the body Lie group
\,|{\rm G}_{\rm s}|\,
on
\,\underline{{\mathsf{N}}}{}_{\bullet}{\rm G}\,
by the automorphisms

\displaystyle|\ell\wp^{(n)}|_{(g,h)}\equiv\ell\wp^{(n)}\circ\bigl(\widehat{g}\times\widehat{h}\times{\rm id}_{\rm G}\bigr)\ :\ {\rm G}\equiv{\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}\times{\rm G}\longrightarrow\bigl({\rm G}\times{\rm G}\bigr)\times{\rm G}\longrightarrow{\rm G}\,,

labelled by
\,(g,h)\in|{\rm G}\times{\rm G}|\equiv|{\rm G}|\times|{\rm G}|
, and

\displaystyle|\ell^{(n)}|_{g}\equiv\ell^{(n)}\circ\bigl(\widehat{g}\times{\rm id}_{\rm G}\bigr)\ :\ {\rm G}\equiv{\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}\times{\rm G}\longrightarrow{\rm G}\times{\rm G}\longrightarrow{\rm G}\,,

labelled by
\,g\in|{\rm G}|
, respectively, alongside the standard action of the respective Lie superalgebras by superderivations. On the object supermanifold
\,{\rm G}\,
of
\,{\rm G}\rtimes{\rm G}
, the latter is implemented by the fundamental vector fields of
\,{\rm G}\lambda^{(0)}
,
i.e.
,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{g}\ni(X,Y)\longmapsto\mathcal{K}^{\rm G}_{(X,Y)}=X^{A}\,R_{A}-Y^{A}\,L_{A}

for
\,({\rm G}_{\rm s},{\rm G}\lambda)=({\rm G}^{\times 2},\ell\wp)\,
with
\,\mathfrak{g}_{s}=\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{g}
, and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}\ni X\longmapsto\mathcal{K}^{\rm G}_{X}=X^{A}\,R_{A}\,,

for
\,({\rm G}_{\rm s},{\rm G}\lambda)=({\rm G},\ell)\,
with
\,\mathfrak{g}_{s}=\mathfrak{g}
, where
\,L_{A}=({\rm id}_{\mathcal{O}_{\rm G}}\otimes t_{A})\circ{\rm m}^{*}\,
and
\,R_{A}=\bigl(t_{A}\otimes{\rm id}_{\mathcal{O}_{\rm G}}\bigr)\circ{\rm m}^{*}\,
are the basis right- and left-invariant vector fields, respectively. These lift to the higher rungs of the simplicial supermanifold (with
\,\mathcal{O}_{{\rm G}^{\times n+1}}\equiv\mathcal{O}_{\rm G}^{\otimes n+1}
) as

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}\oplus\mathfrak{g}\ni(X,Y)\longmapsto\mathcal{K}^{{\rm G}^{\times n+1}}_{(X,Y)}=\mathcal{K}^{\rm G}_{(X,Y)}\otimes{\rm id}_{\mathcal{O}_{\rm G}}^{\otimes n}+Y^{A}\,\sum_{k=1}^{n}\,{\rm id}_{\mathcal{O}_{\rm G}}^{\otimes k}\otimes\bigl(R_{A}-L_{A}\bigr)\otimes{\rm id}_{\mathcal{O}_{\rm G}}^{\otimes n-k}

and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}\ni X\longmapsto\mathcal{K}^{{\rm G}^{\times n+1}}_{X}=\mathcal{K}^{\rm G}_{X}\otimes{\rm id}_{\mathcal{O}_{\rm G}}^{\otimes n}\,,

respectively.

At this stage, we may meaningfully restrict each row of the bicomplex (
3.10
) that captures (the de Rham representative of) the characteristic class of the (generalised) multiplicative structure on the 1-gerbe to the subcomplex composed of
{\rm G}_{\rm s}
-invariant super-differential forms on the corresponding component supermanifold
\,{\mathsf{N}}_{n}{\rm G}^{\rm op}
,

\displaystyle\bigl(\Omega^{\bullet}\bigl({\mathsf{N}}_{n}{\rm G}^{\rm op}\bigr)^{{\rm G}\lambda^{(n-1)}},{\mathsf{d}}_{\rm dR}^{(\bullet)}\bigr)\qquad:\qquad\Omega^{0}\bigl({\rm G}^{\times n}\bigr)^{{\rm G}\lambda^{(n-1)}}\xrightarrow{\ {\mathsf{d}}\ }\Omega^{1}\bigl({\rm G}^{\times n}\bigr)^{{\rm G}\lambda^{(n-1)}}\xrightarrow{\ {\mathsf{d}}\ }\cdots\xrightarrow{\ {\mathsf{d}}\ }\Omega^{p}\bigl({\rm G}^{\times n}\bigr)^{{\rm G}\lambda^{(n-1)}}\xrightarrow{\ {\mathsf{d}}\ }\cdots\,.

Here,
\,{\rm G}\lambda^{(-1)}\equiv{\rm pr}_{2}\ :\ {\rm G}_{\rm s}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}\,
and
\,\Omega^{0}({\rm G})^{{\rm G}\lambda^{(0)}}\,
are just (locally) constant sections of the structure sheaf
\,\mathcal{O}_{\rm G}\,
of
\,{\rm G}
. Note that the cohomology of the subcomplex for
\,({\rm G}_{\rm s},{\rm G}\lambda)=({\rm G},\ell)\,
and
\,n=1\,
is the
Cartan–Eilenberg cohomology of

\,{\rm G}\,
that we shall stumble upon presently, and so denote as
\,{\rm CaE}^{\bullet}({\rm G})\equiv H^{\bullet}_{\rm dR}({\rm G},{\mathbb{R}})^{\ell}\equiv H^{\bullet}_{\rm dR}({\rm G},{\mathbb{R}})^{{\rm L}({\rm G})}
. The restriction cannot be extended to the entire bicomplex in a manner that defines a
{\rm G}_{\rm s}
-invariant refinement of the associated cohomology because of the presence of the extra structural (degeneracy and face) map at each level of
\,{\mathsf{N}}_{\bullet}{\rm G}^{\rm op}\,
(relative to
\,\underline{{\mathsf{N}}}{}_{\bullet}{\rm G}
) which is readily seen to be non-equivariant with respect to the
{\rm G}_{\rm s}
-actions present. Nevertheless, it makes perfect sense to consider

Definition 29
.

Adopt the hitherto notation. A generalised multiplicative structure
\,(\mathcal{M},\alpha)\,
on a Cartan–Eilenberg super-1-gerbe
\,\mathcal{G}=({\mathsf{Y}}{\rm G},\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm G}},{\rm B},L,\pi_{L},{\rm A},\mu_{L})\,
over a Lie supergroup
\,{\rm G}\,
associated with a 4-cocycle
\,\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{G}}\,
is termed
{\rm G}\lambda^{(\bullet)}-
supersymmetric
if the tensorial data
\,\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{G}}\,
are (
{\rm G}\lambda^{(\bullet)}-
)LI, the structural 1-isomorphism
\,\mathcal{M}\,
is a
{\rm G}\lambda^{(2)}
-supersymmetric 1-gerbe 1-isomorphism, and the 2-isomorphism
\,\alpha\,
is a
{\rm G}\lambda^{(3)}
-supersymmetric 1-gerbe 2-isomorphism.

\diamond

The physical rationale behind the requirement that the generalised multiplicative structure be
{\rm G}\lambda^{(\bullet)}
-supersymmetric shall become clear in the next section in which we turn to un-graded physics with view to developing an intuition as to how the categorification of the Lie supergroup structure discussed above can be employed in the construction of the multi-phase WZW super-
\sigma
-model with the maximally supersymmetric defect implementing the right action of the Lie supergroup
\,{\rm G}\,
on itself.

Part II
The maximally symmetric WZW defects and the CS theory

Below, we place the abstract considerations and constructions of the previous part in the concrete and well-studied context of the Wess–Zumino–Witten
\sigma
-model of Ref.
[
Wit84
]
, with the bulk target space given by a compact simple 1-connected Lie group. The conceptual framework set up previously paves the way to an elaboration of a programme, pioneered by Gawȩdzki in the seminal Ref.
[
Gaw88
]
and continued and extended by His students and collaborators, and in particular by Fuchs, Schweigert and Waldorf in Ref.
[
FSW08
]
, and by Runkel and the Author in Refs.
[
RS09
,
RS11
]
, of decoding the constitutive algebraic data of the underlying 2d (quantum) Rational Conformal Field Theory from the higher-geometric and -categorial gerbe-theoretic constructs which arise in a rigorous approach to the definition and application of the topological Wess–Zumino term in the model’s Dirac–Feynman amplitude, and to its extension to the situation in which defects compatible with the rich bi-chiral bulk symmetry enter the field-theoretic picture. In the course of the present study, we identify – conjecturally yet with a solid partial evidence to lean upon – new elements of the said higher-geometric ‘code’ through a detailed analysis, organised by the general considerations of Part
I
, of the geometric and gerbe-theoretic data of the maximally symmetric inter-bi-brane (and the associated fusion 2-isomorhisms), which, remarkably but understandably (from the point of view of the RCFT and its functorial quantisation), lands us in the setting of the 3d topological gauge field theory of Chern and Simons coupled to a Wilson line defect. It is also to be noted that structures discussed hereunder provide a very concrete realisation of the idea of representing symmetries of a field theory by defects, the symmetry in question being the configurational symmetry induced from the right regular action of the target Lie group on itself.

4.
The geometry of the un-graded maximally symmetric WZW defect

As emphasised at the beginning of the closing section of the general Part
I
, the category of Lie (super)groups and their homogeneous spaces is a rich source of examples of highly symmetric geometries. The corresponding bosonic two-dimensional field theories, with the said geometries as targets, have long been known under the name of bosonic (gauged) Wess–Zumino–Witten (WZW)
\sigma
-models and explored extensively since their inception in Refs.
[
Wit84
,
KZ84
,
GW86
]
(the pure Lie-group geometry) and Refs.
[
GKO85
,
GKO86
,
BRS88
,
GK89
]
. While the ones on homogeneous spaces are quite heavily structured as field theories (
cf.
, in particular, Ref.
[
GK89
]
), the models of loop dynamics in group manifolds admit a fairly straightforward description in terms of elementary constructs of Cartan’s differential calculus and their higher geometrisations. Among other things, the issue of (maximal) symmetry of the associated backgrounds has been investigated at great length over the years, and shall be recapitulated and elaborated below with view to illustrating the former abstract definitions and to developing intuitions to be invoked in the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded setting of interest.

Thus, in this section, we discuss the
{\rm G}\times{\rm G}
-invariant simplicial submanifold
\,\mathcal{T}_{\bullet}\,
of
\,\underline{{\mathsf{N}}}{}_{\bullet}{\rm G}\,
which can serve as the target space of the WZW
\sigma
-model with a maximally symmetric defect, starting with the target-space of an empty defect quiver
\,\Gamma=\emptyset\,
and then passing to the bi-brane worldvolume for the maximally symmetric defect line. This paves the way to a proposal for the worldvolume of the maximally symmetric inter-bi-brane associated with junctions of an arbitrary number of such defect lines, which we provide towards the end of the section. In its derivation, we invoke the three-dimensional Chern–Simons theory that has long been known to encode the structure of the topological sector of the WZW
\sigma
-model in the presence of the maximally symmetric boundary defect. Further concrete and circumstantial evidence for our proposal shall be provided in the next section in which we introduce the full-fledged higher-geometric structure for the defect.

Prior to launching the technical analysis of the reference field theory with defects, we note that its standard formulation, while being essentially equivalent to the Nambu–Goto one introduced in Def.
24
, differs from the latter in that it presupposes the existence of a metric
\,\gamma\,
on the worldsheet
\,\Sigma\,
and employs the metric term in the Polyakov form:

\displaystyle S_{\sigma,{\rm metr}}^{\rm P}[\xi]=-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\|{\mathsf{d}}\xi\|_{(\gamma,{\rm g})}^{2}\,,\qquad\qquad\|{\mathsf{d}}\xi\|_{(\gamma,{\rm g})}^{2}=\int_{\Sigma}\,\bigl(\wedge\otimes{\rm g}_{\xi}\bigr)\bigl({\mathsf{d}}\xi,\bigl(\star_{\gamma}\otimes{\rm id}_{\xi^{*}{\mathsf{T}}\xi(\Sigma)}\bigr)({\mathsf{d}}\xi)\bigr)

which computes the norm squared of the tangent operator
\,{\mathsf{d}}\xi={\mathsf{d}}\sigma^{i}\otimes\xi_{*}\partial_{i}\in\Gamma({\mathsf{T}}^{*}\Sigma\otimes_{\Sigma,{\mathbb{R}}}\xi^{*}{\mathsf{T}}\xi(\Sigma))\,
(thus, the latter has the
{\mathsf{T}}\xi(\Sigma)
-legs contracted with the help of the target-space metric and the
{\mathsf{T}}^{*}\Sigma
-legs, of which the second one has been transformed by the Hodge operator
\,\star_{\gamma}\,
for the worldsheet metric
\,\gamma
, wedged to yield a top-degree form on
\,\Sigma
). In what follows, we denote the counterpart of
\,\mathcal{F}(\Sigma,d)\,
for the
\sigma
-model in the Polyakov formulation as
\,\mathcal{F}(\Sigma,d;\gamma)
, and use the symbol
\,\mathcal{F}_{\rm eom}(\Sigma,d;\gamma)\,
to refer to those field configurations which satisfy the ensuing field equations.

The reformulation of the field theory entails a parallel restatement of the field-theoretic description of the defects. Thus, we now consider a linear map (written in the previously introduced notation)

\displaystyle\widetilde{N}\ :\ \Gamma({\mathsf{T}}\Sigma)\longrightarrow\Gamma({\mathsf{T}}\Sigma)\ :\ v(\cdot)=v^{i}(\cdot)\,\partial_{i}\longmapsto-\sqrt{|{\rm det}\,\gamma_{\cdot}|}\,v^{k}(\cdot)\,\bigl(\gamma_{\cdot}^{-1}\bigr)^{ij}\,\epsilon_{jk}\,\partial_{i}

which squares to
\,\widetilde{N}\circ\widetilde{N}={\rm id}_{\Gamma({\mathsf{T}}\Sigma)}\,
and satisfies
\,g\bigl(v,\widetilde{N}v\bigr)=0\,
and
\,\gamma\bigl(\widetilde{N}v,\widetilde{N}v\bigr)=-\gamma(v,v)
. We also have
\,v\righthalfcup\widetilde{N}v\righthalfcup\sqrt{|{\rm det}\,\gamma|}\,{\mathsf{d}}\sigma^{1}\wedge{\mathsf{d}}\sigma^{2}=\gamma(v,v)
, so that the pair
\,(\widetilde{N}v,\gamma(v,v)^{-1}\,v)\,
defines the orientation of the minkowskian worldsheet (equivalent to
\,(\partial_{1},\partial_{2})
) for any
\,v\,
which is not light-like. Given the above and for an arbitrary
\,p\in\Sigma^{(1)}
,
\,t\in{\mathsf{T}}_{p}\Sigma^{(1)}\,
and
\,V\in{\mathsf{T}}_{\xi(p)}Q
, the DGC takes the form

(4.1)

\displaystyle{\rm g}_{\xi_{|2}(p)}(\iota_{2*}V,\xi_{|2\,*}(p)(\widetilde{N}t))-{\rm g}_{\xi_{|1}(p)}(\iota_{1*}V,\xi_{|1\,*}(p)(\widetilde{N}t))=\omega_{\xi(p)}(V,\xi_{*}(p)t)\,,

with
\,\xi_{|A\,*}(p)(\widetilde{N}t)\,
understood as the limit of
\,\xi_{*}(x)(\widetilde{N}t)\,
as
\,x\in\Sigma^{(2)}\,
approaches
\,p\,
from the appropriate side (namely, inside
\,U_{A}\,
in the notation
of condition (F1)). This condition was derived in Ref.
[
RS09
]
by varying the
\sigma
-model action
functional
20
20

20

In Ref.
[
RS09
]
, a euclidean world-sheet metric was used, but this only affects the metric (‘kinetic’) term and it is straightforward to adapt the calculation in Ref.
[
RS09
, App. A.2]
to the present definition of the action functional.
.

4.1.
The geometry of the bulk WZW

\sigma
-model

The target space of the WZW
\sigma
-model is the group manifold of the compact simple 1-connected Lie group
\,{\rm G}
, on which we take, as tensorial data of the background, the Cartan–Killing metric

(4.2)

\displaystyle{\rm g}\equiv{\rm g}_{\rm CK}^{({\mathsf{k}})}=-\tfrac{{\mathsf{k}}}{4\pi}\,\bigl({\rm id}_{\Omega^{1}({\rm G})^{\otimes 2}}\otimes{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\bigr)\bigl(\theta_{\rm L}\otimes\theta_{\rm L}\bigr)=\tfrac{{\mathsf{k}}}{8\pi}\,\delta_{AB}\,\theta_{\rm L}^{A}\otimes\theta_{\rm L}^{B}\,,\qquad{\mathsf{k}}\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\,,

and the
{\mathsf{k}}
-fold multiple

\displaystyle{\rm H}^{({\mathsf{k}})}_{\rm C}={\mathsf{k}}\,{\rm H}_{\rm C}

of the representative

(4.3)

\displaystyle{\rm H}_{\rm C}=\tfrac{1}{12\pi}\,{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\bigl(\theta_{\rm L}\wedge\theta_{\rm L}\wedge\theta_{\rm L}\bigr)=-\tfrac{1}{48\pi}\,f_{ABC}\,\theta_{\rm L}^{A}\wedge\theta_{\rm L}^{B}\wedge\theta_{\rm L}^{C}

of the generating class in the cohomology group
\,H^{3}_{\rm dR}({\rm G},2\pi{\mathbb{Z}})\cong 2\pi{\mathbb{Z}}\,
of a compact simple 1-connected Lie group
\,{\rm G}
, known as the Cartan 3-form and written in terms of the standard left-invariant Maurer–Cartan 1-form
\,\theta_{\rm L}\equiv\theta_{\rm L}^{A}\otimes t_{A}\in\Omega^{1}({\rm G})\otimes\mathfrak{g}\,
on
\,{\rm G}\,
and the trace
21
21

21

Whenever we are dealing with a
\mathfrak{g}
-valued element of a quotient
\,\mathscr{I}\,
of the tensor algebra of
\,\Omega^{1}(X)\,
on a manifold
\,X
, we abuse the notation mildly and write
\,{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,
instead of
\,{\rm id}_{\mathscr{I}}\otimes{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,
in order to unburden our formulæ.

\,{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,
on
\,\mathfrak{g}\,
which we normalise so that the relation
\,{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}(t_{A}\,t_{B})=-\frac{1}{2}\,\delta_{AB}\,
obtains for the generators
\,t_{A}\,
of
\,\mathfrak{g}
, the latter satisfying the defining commutation relations
\,[t_{A},t_{B}]=f_{ABC}\,t_{C}
, with
\,f_{ABC}\,
the structure constants of
\,\mathfrak{g}\,
(this yields the standard matrix trace for,
e.g.
,
\,{\rm G}={\rm SU}(2)
). In fact, the two tensors admit an equivalent definition in terms of the right-invariant Maurer–Cartan 1-form:

(4.4)

\displaystyle{\rm g}_{\rm CK}^{({\mathsf{k}})}\equiv-\tfrac{{\mathsf{k}}}{4\pi}\,\bigl({\rm id}_{\Omega^{1}({\rm G})^{\otimes 2}}\otimes{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\bigr)\bigl(\theta_{\rm R}\otimes\theta_{\rm R}\bigr)\,,\qquad\qquad{\rm H}_{\rm C}^{({\mathsf{k}})}\equiv\tfrac{{\mathsf{k}}}{12\pi}\,{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\bigl(\theta_{\rm R}\wedge\theta_{\rm R}\wedge\theta_{\rm R}\bigr)\,,

and so we conclude that

The bulk target space of the WZW
\sigma
-model,

\displaystyle M=\mathcal{T}_{0}=\underline{{\mathsf{N}}}{}_{0}{\rm G}\,,

is a single orbit of the action

\displaystyle\ell\wp^{(0)}\ :\ ({\rm G}\times{\rm G})\times\underline{{\mathsf{N}}}{}_{0}{\rm G}\longrightarrow\underline{{\mathsf{N}}}{}_{0}{\rm G}\,,

endowed with
\ell\wp
-invariant tensorial data
\,({\rm g}_{\rm CK}^{({\mathsf{k}})},{\rm H}_{\rm C}^{({\mathsf{k}})})
,

\displaystyle\forall_{(x,y)\in{\rm G}\times{\rm G}}\ :\ \bigl(\ \ell\wp_{(x,y)}^{*}{\rm g}_{\rm CK}^{({\mathsf{k}})}={\rm g}_{\rm CK}^{({\mathsf{k}})}\quad\land\quad\ell\wp_{(x,y)}^{*}{\rm H}_{\rm C}^{({\mathsf{k}})}={\rm H}_{\rm C}^{({\mathsf{k}})}\ \bigr)\,.

The normalisation of the metric tensor and of the associated metric term of the action functional
relative to
the topological term, parametrised by the so-called
level

\,{\mathsf{k}}
, is determined by the requirement of a non-anomalous conformal symmetry of the quantised theory,
cf.
Ref.
[
Wit84
]
. The quantisation of the level in the latter term (
i.e.
, the restriction
\,{\mathsf{k}}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\times}\subset{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\,
of the domain of the level), on the other hand, follows from topological considerations (and so could be skipped at this stage): It ensures that the Cartan 3-form has periods in
\,2\pi{\mathbb{Z}}\,
and becomes a representative of the generator class in
\,H^{3}({\rm G},2\pi{\mathbb{Z}})\cong 2\pi{\mathbb{Z}}
. Its cohomological nontriviality, thus constrained, leads to the appearance of the associated gerbe in the lagrangean description of the
\sigma
-model,
cf.
Ref.
[
Gaw88
]
. The relevant differential-geometric structure realising the de Rham class
\,[{\rm H}_{\rm C}^{({\mathsf{k}})}]\,
shall be indicated in Sec.
5.1
. Here, we note that the normalisation of the Cartan–Killing metric fixes the size of the group manifold and confine ourselves to the ensuing field equations.

Let us put the WZW
\sigma
-model, with
\,M={\rm G}\,
and the tensorial data introduced above, on the cylinder
\,\Sigma\cong{\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathbb{S}}^{1}\,
with rectilinear coordinates
\,\{\sigma^{i}\}^{i\in\{1,2\}}\,
and the minkowskian metric
\,\gamma\equiv\eta=-{\mathsf{d}}\sigma^{1}\otimes{\mathsf{d}}\sigma^{1}+{\mathsf{d}}\sigma^{2}\otimes{\mathsf{d}}\sigma^{2}
, and write

(4.5)

\displaystyle g^{-1}\,\partial_{i}g:=\partial_{i}\righthalfcup g^{*}\theta_{\rm L}\,,\qquad\qquad\partial_{i}g\,g^{-1}:=\partial_{i}\righthalfcup g^{*}\theta_{\rm R}

for an arbitrary
\,g\in[\Sigma,{\rm G}]\,
in what follows. By varying the DF amplitude of the ensuing bulk WZW
\sigma
-model, we obtain the equations for the embedding field
\,\xi\equiv g\in[\Sigma,{\rm G}]\,
in the compact form
\,(\eta^{ij}+\epsilon^{ij})\,\partial_{i}\bigl(g^{-1}\,\partial_{j}g\bigr)=0
, or, using the light-cone coordinates
\,\sigma^{\pm}=\sigma^{2}\pm\sigma^{1}\,
and the corresponding derivatives
\,\partial_{\pm}=\tfrac{\partial\ }{\partial\sigma^{\pm}}
,

\displaystyle\partial_{+}\bigl(g^{-1}\,\partial_{-}g\bigr)=0\,.

A general solution to this equation factorises as

(4.6)

\displaystyle g(\sigma)=g_{L}(\sigma^{+})\cdot g_{R}(\sigma^{-})^{-1}\,,

with
\,g_{L}\,
and
\,g_{R}\,
arbitrary
{\rm G}
-valued maps on
\,{\mathbb{R}}\,
with equal monodromies,
cf.
Ref.
[
GTTNB04
]
.

In addition to the conformal symmetry, that is a symmetry engendered by arbitrary worldsheet coordinate transformations

(4.7)

\displaystyle\sigma^{\pm}\longmapsto f_{\pm}(\sigma^{\pm})

under orientation-preserving diffeomorphisms
\,f_{\pm}\,
of the circle (a remnant of the combined Weyl and diffeomorphism invariance of
\,\mathcal{F}_{\rm eom}(\Sigma,d;\gamma)\,
preserving the conformal class of the
minkowskian gauge
\,\gamma=\eta
), the bulk theory enjoys a level-
{\mathsf{k}}

\,\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathsf{k}}^{(L)}\oplus\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathsf{k}}^{(R)}\,
Kač–Moody symmetry, realised on fields through Poisson brackets with the chiral currents

\displaystyle J_{L}(\sigma)=\tfrac{{\mathsf{k}}}{2\pi}\,g(\sigma)\,\partial_{+}g(\sigma)^{-1}\,,\qquad\qquad J_{R}(\sigma)=\tfrac{{\mathsf{k}}}{2\pi}\,g(\sigma)^{-1}\,\partial_{-}g(\sigma)\,.

Note that the currents become functions of the respective light-cone coordinates
\,\sigma^{\pm}\,
upon using the equations of motion of the
\sigma
-model. The Kač–Moody symmetry is an infinitesimal counterpart of

Loop-group invariance:
In the WZW
\sigma
-model on an (oriented) worldsheet
\,\Sigma\,
without defects,
i.e.
, for
\,\Sigma^{(1)}\cup\Sigma^{(0)}=\emptyset\,
and
\,d={\rm id}_{\Sigma}
, the loop-group action

(4.8)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{L}}\ell\wp\ :\ ({\mathsf{L}}{\rm G}\times{\mathsf{L}}{\rm G})\times[\Sigma,{\rm G}]\longrightarrow[\Sigma,{\rm G}]\ :\ \left((h_{L},h_{R}),g\right)\longmapsto h_{L}^{+}\cdot g\cdot(h_{R}^{-})^{-1}\,,

(4.9)

defined in terms of the maps
\,h_{L/R}^{\pm}:=h_{L/R}\circ\pi_{\pm}\,
with
\,\pi_{\pm}(\sigma):=\sigma^{\pm}
, and lifting the geometric action
\,\ell\wp\,
of Eq. (
3.2
) to the mapping space
\,[\Sigma,{\rm G}]
, preserves
\,\mathcal{F}_{\rm eom}(\Sigma,d;\gamma)
.

4.2.
The (untwisted) maximally symmetric WZW boundary

One possible criterion of characterisation of boundary conditions, or more general defect conditions satisfied by a field configuration of the WZW
\sigma
-model consists in specifying the scheme of reduction of the symmetries of the bulk model that occurs in the presence of the defect. In this brief review, we shall be concerned with those conformal defects of the WZW
\sigma
-model that preserve the maximal amount of the bulk symmetry, the latter being encoded in Eqs. (
4.7
) and (
4.9
). Infinitesimally, the symmetry generators (currents) will span at least one copy of the Kač–Moody algebra
\,\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathsf{k}}\subset\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathsf{k}}^{(L)}\oplus\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathsf{k}}^{(R)}
. Since the chiral components of the stress-energy tensor of the WZW
\sigma
-model can be written as squares of the respective chiral Kač–Moody currents (up to a multiplicative constant), this ensures that a copy of the Virasoro algebra
\,{\rm Vir}\,
embedded diagonally in the bulk symmetry algebra
\,{\rm Vir}^{(L)}\oplus{\rm Vir}^{(R)}\,
and corresponding to worldsheet diffeomorphisms that leave the defect unchanged is preserved. In the boundary case, this leads us to consider the so-called maximally symmetric
22
22

22

Here, we consider solely the
untwisted
maximally symmetric D-brane, distinguished by the diagonal character of the embedding
\,\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathsf{k}}\subset\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathsf{k}}^{(L)}\oplus\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathsf{k}}^{(R)}
, which corresponds to the reduction of the isometry group
\,{\rm G}\times{\rm G}\,
of left- and right-regular translations in the target space
\,{\rm G}\,
to a single copy of
\,{\rm G}\,
acting by conjugation (the adjoint action) as the isometry group of the D-brane worldvolume. In general, one finds also one-sided translates of these in the group manifold, as well as orbits of the adjoint action twisted by an outer automorphism of the Lie algebra
\,\mathfrak{g}\,
as admissible maximally symmetric D-brane worldvolumes,
cf.
Ref.
[
RS98
]
.
WZW D-brane, with the worldvolume given by a sub-collection

(4.10)

\displaystyle\qquad Q_{\partial}=\bigsqcup_{\lambda\in P^{{\mathsf{k}}}_{+}(\mathfrak{g})}\,\mathscr{C}_{\lambda}\subset\bigsqcup_{\lambda\in{\mathsf{k}}\,\mathscr{A}_{\rm W}(\mathfrak{g})}\,\mathscr{C}_{\lambda}

with labels from a subset
\,P^{{\mathsf{k}}}_{+}(\mathfrak{g})\subset{\mathsf{k}}\,\mathscr{A}_{\rm W}(\mathfrak{g})
, to be (re-)established presently through a cohomological analysis. Each connected component of the D-brane worldvolume carries the structure of a homogeneous
{\rm G}
-space with respect to the left action

\displaystyle\ell\wp^{\partial}\equiv{\rm Ad}\ :\ {\rm G}\times\mathscr{C}_{\lambda}\longrightarrow\mathscr{C}_{\lambda}\ :\ (x,g)\longmapsto x\cdot g\cdot x^{-1}\equiv{\rm Ad}_{x}(g)\,,

which is an explicit geometric realisation of the bulk-boundary symmetry reduction
\,{\rm G}\times{\rm G}\searrow{\rm G}
, given by the restriction of
\,\ell\wp\,
to the diagonal,
\,\ell\wp^{\partial}_{x}(g)\equiv\ell\wp_{(x,x)}(g)
.

In order to fix the D-brane curvature, we demand that the latter reduction admit a lift to the configuration space of the boundary WZW
\sigma
-model.

Proposition 30
.

In the WZW
\sigma
-model on a worldsheet
\,\Sigma\cong_{d}\Sigma^{(2)}\cup\Sigma^{(1)}\,
with a (boundary-type) defect
\,\Sigma^{(1)}\neq\emptyset=\partial\Sigma^{(1)}\,
(without junctions) embedded in the worldvolume
\,Q_{\partial}\,
of the maximally symmetric WZW D-brane of curvature
\,\omega_{\partial}^{({\mathsf{k}})}
, the latter
assumed
\ell\wp^{\partial}
-invariant
, the sets
\,\mathcal{F}(\Sigma,d;\eta)\,
and
\,\mathcal{F}_{\rm eom}(\Sigma,d;\eta)\,
admit a left action of
\,{\mathsf{L}}{\rm G}\,
of the form

(4.11)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{L}}\ell\wp^{\partial}\ :\ {\mathsf{L}}{\rm G}\times\mathcal{F}_{({\rm eom})}(\Sigma,d;\eta)\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{({\rm eom})}(\Sigma,d;\eta)\ :\ (h,g)\longmapsto h^{+}\cdot g\cdot(h^{-})^{-1}=:{}^{\textrm{\tiny$h$}}\hskip-1.0ptg

(4.12)

and the chiral currents of the bulk Kač–Moody symmetry glue at the boundary as dictated by the identity

(4.13)

\displaystyle(J_{R}-J_{L})|_{\partial\Sigma}=0

iff the curvature restricts to the connected components of
\,Q_{\partial}\,
as

(4.14)

\displaystyle\omega_{\partial,\lambda}^{({\mathsf{k}})}\equiv\omega_{\partial}^{({\mathsf{k}})}|_{\mathscr{C}_{\lambda}}=\tfrac{{\mathsf{k}}}{8\pi}\,{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\left(\theta_{\rm L}\wedge\left(\tfrac{{\rm id}_{\mathfrak{g}}+{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{\cdot}}{{\rm id}_{\mathfrak{g}}-{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{\cdot}}\right)\circ\theta_{\rm L}\right)\,.

Proof.

Cf.
App.
C
. ∎

Remark 31
.

It deserves to be emphasised that Eq. (
1.28
) in conjunction with the condition that
\,\omega_{\partial}^{({\mathsf{k}})}\,
be
\ell\wp^{\partial}
-invariant does not fix the D-brane curvature uniquely, and so the additional requirement stated in the thesis of the proposition plays a nontrivial rôle. Indeed, the former two only determine
\,\omega_{\partial}^{({\mathsf{k}})}\,
up to an
\ell\wp^{\partial}
-invariant 2-cocycle on each connected component
\,\mathscr{C}_{\lambda}\,
of the D-brane worldvolume. Each such 2-cocycle defines a class in the second cohomology group
\,H^{2}_{\rm dR}(\mathscr{C}_{\lambda};{\mathbb{R}})^{{\rm Ad}_{\cdot}}\,
of the complex of
{\rm Ad}_{\cdot}
-invariant forms on the conjugacy class
\,\mathscr{C}_{\lambda}
. From the assumed compactness and connectedness of
\,{\rm G}
, we infer – upon invoking Theorem 2.3 of Ref.
[
CE48
]
(see also,
e.g.
, Theorem I of Ref.
[
GHV73
, Chap. IV, § 1, Sec. 4.3]
) – the existence of an isomorphism between the said cohomology group and the standard second de Rham cohomology group,
\,H^{2}_{\rm dR}(\mathscr{C}_{\lambda};{\mathbb{R}})^{{\rm Ad}_{\cdot}}\cong H^{2}_{\rm dR}(\mathscr{C}_{\lambda};{\mathbb{R}})
. The de Rham groups of (non-singular) conjugacy classes are not trivial in general, hence we have a whole wealth of cohomologically non-equivalent solutions to the problem in hand prior to imposing the requirement of the existence of a loop-group lift of the symmetry-reduction scheme.

4.3.
The maximally symmetric WZW defect

We continue our discussion of the maximally symmetric defects of the WZW
\sigma
-model, passing now to the non-boundary case and focusing on the defect implementing the group multiplication in the manner discussed earlier. The corresponding geometric structure was first identified in Ref.
[
FSW08
]
. Its existence follows directly from the old observation: The Cartan 3-form (
4.3
) satisfies the Polyakov--Wiegmann identity
23
23

23

The identity was first shown and used in Ref.
[
PW84
]
.

\,{\rm m}^{*}{\rm H}_{\rm C}={\rm pr}_{1}^{*}{\rm H}_{\rm C}+{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}{\rm H}_{\rm C}-{\mathsf{d}}\varrho\,
(
cf.
Eq. (
3.5
)) in which

(4.15)

\displaystyle\varrho=\tfrac{1}{4\pi}\,{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\bigl({\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\theta_{\rm L}\wedge{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\theta_{\rm R}\bigr)\in\Omega^{2}\bigl({\rm G}^{\times 2}\bigr)

is such that the familar identity

(4.16)

\displaystyle\Delta^{(2;2)}_{\rm G}\varrho=0

holds true. Accordingly, we may adduce the reasoning from the beginning of Sec.
3.1
in conjunction with the old results quoted in the previous section and tentatively write the worldvolume of the maximally symmetric WZW bi-brane in the form

(4.17)

\displaystyle Q={\rm G}\times Q_{\partial}\,,

postponing the discussion of the existence of the requisite higher-geometric structure to Sec.
5
. Note that each of its connected components,
\,Q_{\lambda}={\rm G}\times\mathscr{C}_{\lambda}
, is endowed with the structure of a homogeneous
{\rm G}\times{\rm G}
-space with respect to the left action

(4.18)

\displaystyle\ell\wp^{(1)}\ :\ ({\rm G}\times{\rm G})\times Q_{\lambda}\longrightarrow Q_{\lambda}\ :\ \bigl((x,y),(g,h)\bigr)\longmapsto\left(x\cdot g\cdot y^{-1},{\rm Ad}_{y}(h)\right)\,.

We encountered it previously in Sec.
3.2
. It is
{\rm G}\times{\rm G}
-equivariantly isomorphic with the biconjugacy class
\,\mathscr{B}_{\lambda}=\{\ \left(x\cdot{\rm Ad}_{y}({\rm e}_{\lambda}),x\right)\ |\ x,y\in{\rm G}\ \}
, originally postulated as a component worldvolume in Ref.
[
FSW08
]
. The isomorphism is explicitly given by the map
\,mu_{\lambda}\ :\ Q_{\lambda}\longrightarrow\mathscr{B}_{\lambda}\ :\ (g,h)\longmapsto(g\cdot h,g)
, and it is readily seen to intertwine the left
{\rm G}\times{\rm G}
-action
\,\Delta\ell\wp\ :\ ({\rm G}\times{\rm G})\times\mathscr{B}_{\lambda}\longrightarrow\mathscr{B}_{\lambda}\ :\ ((x,y)(g_{1},g_{2}))\longmapsto(x\cdot g_{1}\cdot y^{-1},x\cdot g_{2}\cdot y^{-1})\,
on
\,\mathscr{B}_{\lambda}\,
with
\,\ell\wp^{(1)}
,
i.e.
,
\,\mu_{\lambda}\circ\ell\wp^{(1)}_{(x,y)}=\Delta\ell\wp_{(x,y)}\circ\mu_{\lambda}
. The advantage of our model of the bi-brane worldvolume is the ability to incorporate it into the simplicial framework laid out in Sec.
1.2
and
3
, which neatly organises our subsequent search for a candidate structure to be associated with defect-line intersections.

The bi-brane maps for the bi-brane worldvolume of Eq. (
4.17
) are

(4.19)

\displaystyle\iota_{1}={\rm pr}_{1}\,,\qquad\qquad\iota_{2}={\rm m}\,,

i.e.
, restrictions of the degeneracy maps of the nerve
\,\underline{{\mathsf{N}}}{}_{\bullet}{\rm G}\,
at level 1. As discussed earlier, they intertwine the
{\rm G}\times{\rm G}
-action
\,\ell\wp^{(1)}\,
structure on
\,Q\,
with the action
\,\ell\wp\,
on
\,{\rm G}
,

(4.20)

\displaystyle\iota_{\alpha}\circ\ell\wp^{(1)}_{(x,y)}=\ell\wp_{(x,y)}\circ\iota_{\alpha}\,.

In our search for the bi-brane curvature, we are guided by an analogon of the symmetry(-lift) principle employed in the boundary setting. We thus arrive at

Proposition 32
.

In the WZW
\sigma
-model on a worldsheet
\,\Sigma\cong_{d}\Sigma^{(2)}\cup\Sigma^{(1)}\,
with a defect
\,\Sigma^{(1)}\neq\emptyset=\partial\Sigma^{(1)}\,
(without junctions) embedded in the worldvolume
\,Q\,
of the maximally symmetric WZW bi-brane of curvature
\,\omega^{({\mathsf{k}})}
, the latter
assumed
\ell\wp^{(1)}
-invariant
, the sets
\,\mathcal{F}(\Sigma,d;\eta)\,
and
\,\mathcal{F}_{\rm eom}(\Sigma,d;\eta)
, with elements represented by pairs
\,(\xi|_{\Sigma^{(2)}},\xi|_{\Sigma^{(1)}})\equiv\left(g_{\rm b},(g,h)\right)
, admit a left action of
\,{\mathsf{L}}{\rm G}\times{\mathsf{L}}{\rm G}\,
of the form

(4.21)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{L}}\ell\wp^{(1)}\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\ ({\mathsf{L}}{\rm G}\times{\mathsf{L}}{\rm G})\times\mathcal{F}_{({\rm eom})}(\Sigma,d;\eta)\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{({\rm eom})}(\Sigma,d;\eta)

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\ \left((h_{L},h_{R}),\left(g_{\rm b},(g,h)\right)\right)\longmapsto\left(h_{L}^{+}\cdot g_{\rm b}\cdot(h_{R}^{-})^{-1},\left(h_{L}^{+}\cdot g\cdot(h_{R}^{-})^{-1},{\rm Ad}_{h_{R}^{-}}(h)\right)\right)

and the chiral currents of the bulk Kač–Moody symmetry are continuous across the defect as expressed by the identities

(4.23)

\displaystyle(J_{L\,1}-J_{L\,2})|_{\Gamma}=0\,,\qquad\qquad(J_{R\,1}-J_{R\,2})|_{\Gamma}=0

written for

\displaystyle J_{L\,\alpha}(\sigma)=\tfrac{{\mathsf{k}}}{2\pi}\,g_{{\rm b}|\alpha}\,\partial_{+}g_{{\rm b}|\alpha}^{-1}\,,\qquad\qquad J_{R\,\alpha}(\sigma)=\tfrac{{\mathsf{k}}}{2\pi}\,g_{{\rm b}|\alpha}^{-1}\,\partial_{-}g_{{\rm b}|\alpha}

with

(4.24)

\displaystyle g_{{\rm b}|\alpha}|_{\Gamma}=\iota_{\alpha}\circ(g,h)\,,

iff the curvature restricts to the connected components of
\,Q\,
as

(4.25)

\displaystyle\omega_{\lambda}^{({\mathsf{k}})}\equiv\omega^{({\mathsf{k}})}|_{Q_{\lambda}}=\varrho^{({\mathsf{k}})}|_{Q_{\lambda}}-{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\omega_{\partial,\lambda}^{({\mathsf{k}})}\,,

where

(4.26)

\displaystyle\varrho^{({\mathsf{k}})}=\tfrac{{\mathsf{k}}}{4\pi}\,{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\bigl({\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\theta_{\rm L}\wedge{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\theta_{\rm R}\bigr)\,.

Proof.

Cf.
App.
D
. ∎

Remark 33
.

The nontriviality of the requirement of the existence of a loop-group lift in the thesis of the proposition can be demonstrated with the help of arguments similar to those adduced in Remark
31
. Indeed, the compact and connected group
\,{\rm G}\,
is now replaced by the product group
\,{\rm G}\times{\rm G}\,
with the same topological properties, and so we end up with the de Rham cohomology group
\,H^{2}_{\rm dR}({\rm G}\times\mathscr{C}_{\lambda};{\mathbb{R}})\,
as the structure that enumerates cohomologically inequivalent
\ell\wp^{(1)}
-invariant 2-forms that satisfy
condition (
1.2
). By the Künneth Theorem, and in virtue of the 1-connectedness of both
\,{\rm G}\,
and the
\,\mathscr{C}_{\lambda}
, we obtain the result
\,H^{2}_{\rm dR}({\rm G}\times\mathscr{C}_{\lambda};{\mathbb{R}})\cong H^{2}_{\rm dR}(\mathscr{C}_{\lambda};{\mathbb{R}})
, which puts us back in the topological context discussed previously.

In summary,

The worldvolume of the maximally symmetric WZW bi-brane,

\displaystyle Q=\mathcal{T}_{1}\equiv\bigsqcup_{\lambda\in P^{{\mathsf{k}}}_{+}(\mathfrak{g})}\,{\rm G}\times\mathscr{C}_{\lambda}\subseteq\bigsqcup_{\lambda\in{\mathsf{k}}\,\mathscr{A}_{\rm W}(\mathfrak{g})}\,{\rm G}\times\mathscr{C}_{\lambda}\subset\underline{{\mathsf{N}}}{}_{1}{\rm G}\,,

is a disjoint union of a family of orbits of the action

\displaystyle\ell\wp^{(1)}\ :\ ({\rm G}\times{\rm G})\times\underline{{\mathsf{N}}}{}_{1}{\rm G}\longrightarrow\underline{{\mathsf{N}}}{}_{1}{\rm G}\,,

endowed with an
\ell\wp^{(1)}
-invariant curvature
\,\omega^{({\mathsf{k}})}
,

\displaystyle\forall_{(x,y)\in{\rm G}\times{\rm G}}\ :\ \ell\wp_{(x,y)}^{(1)\,*}=\omega^{({\mathsf{k}})}\,,

and
{\rm G}\times{\rm G}
-equivariant bi-brane maps

\displaystyle\iota_{1}=\underline{d}{}^{(1)}_{1}\mathord{\restriction}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}}\,,\qquad\qquad\iota_{2}=\underline{d}{}^{(1)}_{0}\mathord{\restriction}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}}\,.

4.4.
Junctions of the maximally symmetric WZW defect

Our concrete choice of the boundary data made in Sec.
4.2
leads us to a
proposal
for the worldvolume of the maximally symmetric WZW inter-bi-brane based on the
tentative
identification of the WZW target space under reconstruction as a simplicial
{\rm G}\times{\rm G}
-space. Such identification enables us to turn the simplicial crank, worked out in all generality in Sec.
1.2
, and yields inter-bi-brane worldvolumes as
{\rm G}\times{\rm G}
-submanifolds (sub-families of
{\rm G}\times{\rm G}
-orbits)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{n}\equiv T_{n,1}\subset\bigsqcup_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}\in{\mathsf{k}}\,\mathscr{A}_{\rm W}(\mathfrak{g})^{\times n}}\,\bigsqcup_{[\overrightarrow{w}]\in\mathcal{S}_{\lambda_{1}}\backslash\times_{i=2}^{n}\,\left({\rm G}/\mathcal{S}_{\lambda_{i}}\right)}\,{\rm G}\times T_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{[\overrightarrow{w}]}

in the disjoint unions of
{\rm G}\times{\rm G}
-orbits found in Sec.
3.2
. These are to be equipped with the inter-bi-brane maps which we read off from Def.
13
and Eq. (
3.1
) in the form

(4.27)

\displaystyle\pi^{(n+1)}_{1,n+1}(g,h_{1},h_{2},\ldots,h_{n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(g,h_{1}\cdot h_{2}\cdot\cdots\cdot h_{n})\,,

\displaystyle\pi^{(n+1)}_{k,k+1}(g,h_{1},h_{2},\ldots,h_{n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(g\cdot h_{1}\cdot h_{2}\cdot\cdots\cdot h_{k-1},h_{k})\,,\qquad k\in\overline{1,n}\,.

Having the above map
\,\mathcal{T}_{n}\,
to the bi-brane worldvolume
\,Q\,
of Sec.
4.3
imposes further constraints upon the admissible inter-bi-brane worldvolumes. For example, in the case of
\,n=2
, we see from Eq. (
3.23
) that only those spaces
\,{\rm G}\times T_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}^{[w]}\,

can
appear in
\,\mathcal{T}_{2}\,
for which
\,\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\rho_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}([w])\in P^{{\mathsf{k}}}_{+}(\mathfrak{g})
. In order to describe
\,\mathcal{T}_{2}
, we introduce, for each triple
\,\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\lambda\in P^{{\mathsf{k}}}_{+}(\mathfrak{g})
, a subset

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}^{\lambda}\subset\mathcal{S}_{\lambda_{1}}\backslash{\rm G}/\mathcal{S}_{\lambda_{2}}

with the property

\displaystyle[w]\in\mathcal{F}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}^{\lambda}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\rho_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}([w])=\lambda\,.

We emphasise that the set
\,\mathcal{F}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}^{\lambda}\,
need
not
consist of all the
\,[w]\,
satisfying the above
condition, in fact it could even be empty. A precise definition of
\,\mathcal{F}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}^{\lambda}\,
will be stated in Section

5.4
in terms of the existence of a certain gerbe 2-isomorphism. Altogether,

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{2}={\rm G}\times\bigsqcup_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\lambda)\in P^{{\mathsf{k}}}_{+}(\mathfrak{g})^{\times 3}}\,\bigsqcup_{[w]\in\mathcal{F}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}^{\lambda}}\,T_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}^{[w]}\subset\underline{{\mathsf{N}}}{}_{2}{\rm G}\,.

The sets
\,\mathcal{F}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}^{\lambda}\,
admit an obvious generalisation relevant to the definition of defect junctions of arbitrary valence, to be denoted as

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{\lambda}\subset\mathcal{S}_{\lambda_{1}}\backslash\times_{i=2}^{n}\,\left({\rm G}/\mathcal{S}_{\lambda_{i}}\right)\,,

with the property

\displaystyle[\overrightarrow{w}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{\lambda}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\rho_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}([\overrightarrow{w}])=\lambda\,.

These enter the definition of the inter-bi-brane worldvolumes

(4.29)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{n}={\rm G}\times\bigsqcup_{(\overrightarrow{\lambda},\lambda)\in P^{{\mathsf{k}}}_{+}(\mathfrak{g})^{\times n+1}}\,\bigsqcup_{[\overrightarrow{w}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{\lambda}}\,T_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{[\overrightarrow{w}]}\subset\underline{{\mathsf{N}}}{}_{n}{\rm G}\,.

As in the case of the
\,\mathcal{F}_{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}^{\lambda}
, we assume that the set
\,\mathcal{F}_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{\lambda}\,
is non-empty if a certain gerbe 2-isomorphism exists over the manifold
\,{\rm G}\times T_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{[\overrightarrow{w}]}\,
for some
\,[\overrightarrow{w}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{\lambda}
. Let us remark that the
\,\mathcal{T}_{n}\,
can be (
{\rm G}\times{\rm G}
-equivariantly) equivalently described as a disjoint union

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{n}\cong\bigsqcup_{(\overrightarrow{\lambda},\lambda)\in P^{{\mathsf{k}}}_{+}(\mathfrak{g})^{\times n+1}}\,\bigsqcup_{[\overrightarrow{w}]\in\mathcal{F}_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{\lambda}}\,[Q_{\lambda_{1}}{\,}{}_{\underline{d}{}^{(1)}_{0}}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{\underline{d}{}^{(1)}_{1}}Q_{\lambda_{2}}{\,}{}_{\underline{d}{}^{(1)}_{0}}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{\underline{d}{}^{(1)}_{1}}\cdots{\,}_{\underline{d}{}^{(1)}_{0}}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{\underline{d}{}^{(1)}_{1}}Q_{\lambda_{n}}]_{(\lambda,[\overrightarrow{w}])}

of full
{\rm G}\times{\rm G}
-orbits (labelled by the classes
\,[\overrightarrow{w}]
) contained in the preimages with respect to the map
\,\widetilde{\pi}{}^{(n+1)}_{1,n+1}:=\pi^{(n+1)}_{1,n+1}\circ{\rm pr}_{1,2,4,\ldots 2n}\,
of the ‘outgoing’ component bi-brane worldvolume
\,Q_{\lambda}\,
within the product of the ‘incoming’ component bi-brane worldvolumes
\,Q_{\lambda_{i}},\ i\in\overline{1,n}\,
fibred over
\,M\equiv{\rm G}\,
relative to the bi-brane maps
\,\underline{d}{}^{(1)}_{i}\ :\ Q\longrightarrow M,\ i\in\{0,1\}
. The factors of the fibred product are ordered in the manner determined by the ordering of the corresponding defect lines converging at the defect junction.

In order to verify the above proposal, arisen at the conjunction of the simplicial ‘scheme’ and elementary symmetry considerations, we must check that it solves the constraints (
1.26
), understood here as an identity defining the tangent distribution of the connected component
\,{\rm G}\times T_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{[\overrightarrow{w}]}\,
of
\,\mathcal{T}_{n}\,
within the larger space
\,{\rm G}\times T_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{\lambda}\,
with
\,\lambda={\rho_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}([\overrightarrow{w}])}\,
(
cf.
Eq. (
3.27
)) as the kernel of the 2-form
\,\Delta_{\mathcal{T}_{n}}\omega^{({\mathsf{k}})}
. Just to remind the Reader: The identity is a necessary condition for the existence of the 1-gerbe 2-isomorphism
\,\varphi_{n+1}\,
of Def.
18
, to be pulled back, along the map
\,X\ :\ \Sigma^{(0)}\longrightarrow T
, to a junction
\,x_{n+1}\in\Sigma^{(0)}\,
of
\,n+1\,
lines of the maximally symmetric WZW defect. Hence, its satisfaction is crucial for the consistency of the multi-phase WZW
\sigma
-model. Taking into account the explicit form of the inter-bi-brane maps (
) in conjunction with the cohomological identity (
4.16
), we obtain the formula

(4.30)

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathcal{T}_{n}}\omega^{({\mathsf{k}})}={\rm pr}_{2,3,\ldots,n+1}^{*}\Omega_{n}^{({\mathsf{k}})}\,,

written in terms of the universal 2-form

(4.31)

\displaystyle\Omega_{n}^{({\mathsf{k}})}=\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\,{\rm m}^{(n)}\bigl(i,i|i+1,n\bigr)^{*}\varrho^{({\mathsf{k}})}+{\rm m}_{n}^{*}\omega_{\partial,\lambda}^{({\mathsf{k}})}-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\,{\rm pr}_{j}^{*}\omega_{\partial,\lambda_{j}}^{({\mathsf{k}})}\in\Omega^{2}\bigl(T_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{\lambda}\bigr)

on the space
\,T_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{\lambda}\,
of Eq. (
3.27
) with the help of the maps (
3.30
). We readily establish

Proposition 34
.

Let
\,T_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{[\overrightarrow{w}]}\subset T_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{\lambda}\,
be the manifolds defined in

3.25
) and Eq. (

), respectively, and let

). Then, for
