is the
matrix with entries given by the fusion phases
\,\Phi_{[w_{123}^{(2)}]\lambda_{23}[w_{23}],[w_{12}]\lambda_{12}[w_{123}^{(1)}]}^{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\lambda_{3})\ \lambda}[w_{2},w_{3}]\,
labelled by
\,\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\lambda_{3},\lambda\in P^{{\mathsf{k}}}_{+}(\mathfrak{g})\,
and
\,[w_{2},w_{3}]\in\mathcal{F}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\lambda_{3})}^{\lambda}\,

as well as objects
\,\lambda_{12},\lambda_{23},w_{12},w_{23},w_{123}^{(1)},w_{123}^{(2)}\,
defined in Prop.
44
.

\diamond

As a continuation of Conjectures

1
and
2
, we anticipate that the above inter-bi-brane fusing matrix is related directly to the standard fusing matrix
\,F_{[w_{123}^{(2)}]\lambda_{23}[w_{23}],[w_{12}]\lambda_{12}[w_{123}^{(1)}]}^{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\lambda_{3})\ \lambda}[w_{2},w_{3}]\,
of
the WZW
\sigma
-model (written in the notation of Ref.
[
FRS02
]
), the latter being regarded as a largely cohomological object,
cf.
Ref.
[
MS89
, App. E]
. We hope to return to the question of the precise nature of this relationship in a future study.

When taken in conjunction with the naturality of the gerbe-theoretic constructions employed (and the underlying relation between the maximally symmetric gerbe (bi)modules resp. defects and sectors of the bulk WZW model), the remarks made towards the end of Sec.
4.5
certainly render the above conjectures plausible and the propositions contingent on their veracity highly interesting. Let us also note in this context that a potential source of refinement – suggested by the rôle of the 3d Chern–Simons theory in the analysis of Sec.
4.5
– of the fundamental correspondence (implicitly posited in Conjecture
1
) between – on the one hand – the disjoint unions

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}\equiv\bigsqcup_{[\overrightarrow{w}]\in\mathcal{F}^{\lambda}_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}}\,T_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{[\overrightarrow{w}]}

of gerbe-theoretically admissible
{\rm G}
-orbits
\,T_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{[\overrightarrow{w}]}\,
within the ‘fusion manifolds’
\,T_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{\lambda}\,
and -- on the other hand -- the multiplicity spaces
30
30

30

Cf.
,
e.g.
, Ref.
[
Gaw99
, Eq. (7.13)]
.
in the decompositions of the relevant poly-boundary Hilbert spaces into integrable highest-weight modules of the preserved (diagonal) current sub-algebra
\,\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathsf{k}}\subset\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathsf{k}}^{(L)}\oplus\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathsf{k}}^{(R)}\,
is not expected to provide us with any additional constraints on the admissible ‘multiplicity labels’
\,[\overrightarrow{w}]\,
and thus to determine a proper subset of
\,\mathcal{F}^{\lambda}_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}
. Indeed, in the light of the canonical relation, established in Ref.
[
Gaw02
]
, between the 3d Chern–Simons theory with the Wilson lines (as before) and the
maximally
gauged WZW model on
\,{\rm G}\,
(with only
topological
degrees of freedom left), and upon taking into account the gerbe-theoretic implementation, worked out in Refs.
[
GSW10
,
GSW13
]
, of the gauging (of
\,{\rm Ad}({\rm G})
) in terms of an (
{\rm Ad}({\rm G})
-)equivariant structure on the gerbe and the attendant bi-modules and fusion 2-isomorphisms, such constraints might only come from a restriction to those fusion 2-isomorphisms which admit an
{\rm Ad}({\rm G})
-equivariant structure. However, in the latter of the two papers, it was demonstrated that (for
\,{\rm G}\,
connected) there is no obstruction to endowing a maximally symmetric inter-bi-brane with a
{\rm Ad}({\rm G})
-equivariant structure other than that coming from the bulk gerbe,
cf.
Ref.
[
GSW13
, Cor. 11.17]
, and so all inter-bi-branes predicted by our reasoning descend to the topological gauged WZW model. In other words, the
\,\mathcal{M}^{\lambda}_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}\,
seem to be the
unique
gerbe-theoretic candidates for the ‘multiplicity geometries’. But that is hardly the end of the story: The detailed analysis, carried out in Ref.
[
RS09
]
, of a class of maximally symmetric defects distinguished by the
simple
(0-1) fusion rules of the corresponding chiral sectors of the WZW model, namely, of the simple-current defects (associated with the degenerate conjugacy classes of central elements of the target Lie group and implementing worldsheet orbifolding of the mono-phase WZW model with respect to the action of the centre of the target Lie group), puts some (group-cohomological) flesh on them and corroborates them within the said class. Finally, the detailed case study, reported in Ref.
[
RS11
]
, of the gerbe-theoretic content of the WZW model with the target Lie group
\,{\rm SU}(2)\,
and the maximally symmetric (self-intersecting) defect demonstrated the validity of Conjecture
1
in this case. Thus motivated, we shall return to the task of verifying the Conjectures in a future work. And meanwhile, we leave the ungraded world and pass to the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded setting to witness the simplicial machinery at work there for the first time ever.

Part III
Candidate maximally supersymmetric defects in the flat GS model

In the present part, we put the conceptual scheme set up in Part
I
to work in the supergeometric setting of the much tractable Green–Schwarz super-
\sigma
-model of the superstring with the bulk target superspace
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})
, introduced in Ref.
[
GS84a
]
, which has long been known to share many structural features with the ‘bosonic’ WZW
\sigma
-model of Part
II
,
cf.
Ref.
[
HM85
]
. Accordingly, we employ the intuitions developed in the study of the un-graded
\sigma
-model towards a systematic reconstruction of a class of supersymmetric defects associated with the supersymmetric generalised multiplicative structure on the GS super-1-gerbe of Ref.
[
Sus17
]
, whose derivation is an independent important result of our investigation. The latter is the next logical step in the development of the programme, initiated in Ref.
[
Sus17
]
and subsequently elaborated in a variety of directions in Refs.
[
Sus19
,
Sus18a
,
Sus18b
,
Sus20
,
Sus21a
,
Sus21b
]
, of the (higher-super)geometrisation, in the spirit of Murray
et al.
, of the cohomological data of super-
p
-brane super-
\sigma
-models,
i.e.
, of the classes in the Cartan–Eilenberg cohomology of Lie supergroups distinguished by the string-theoretic considerations leading up to the so-called ‘brane scan’ of Ref.
[
AETW87
]
, and the curved super-
\sigma
-models related to them through a variant of the İnönü–Wigner contraction. A peculiarity of all these superfield theories is the presence of an infinitesimal right gauge supersymmetry, known as the
\kappa
-symmetry and discovered first by de Azcárraga and Lukierski in Ref.
[
dAL82
]
and subsequently (anew) by Siegel in Ref.
[
Sie83
]
, whose geometric nature and a higher geometric signature were elucidated in Refs.
[
Sus20
,
Sus21a
,
Sus21b
]
. As the latter hinges on a duality of the original GS super-
\sigma
-model with the so-called Hughes–Polchinski model of Ref.
[
HP86
]
, whose adaptation to the setting with (supersymmetric) defects would call for a separate study, we content ourselves in what follows with the preliminary construction of candidate maximally supersymmetric bi-branes and postpone the un-circumnavigable task of verifying their
\kappa
-symmetry as well as the subsequent completion of the bi-categorial scheme laid out in Sec.
1
to a future work. For similar reasons, we are wary of thinking of the structures encountered hereunder as defect realisations of (super)
symmetries
of the 2
d
superfield theory – indeed, while the instantonic bi-branes of Sec.
8.4
do correspond to the translational symmetry in the body, and the superstring-like ones can be expected to correspond to
\kappa
-symmetry, the issue certainly requires further scrutiny.

We close this introductory part with a
memento
: Owing to the topological triviality of the body of the supermanifolds encountered in the analysis to come (and in virtue of the theorem, due to Kostant (
cf.
Ref.
[
Kos77
]
), which equates the de Rham cohomology of the supermanifold and that of its body), the said analysis is actually nontrivial (
i.e.
, its results are not known
a priori
) on the tensorial level, whereas the subsequent geometrisations are mere reconstructions done for the sake of bookkeeping. This may well obscure the deeper meaning of the tensorial calculus carried out in the
supersymmetric refinement
of the standard de Rham cohomology, which was revealed by a beautiful but largely overlooked reasoning due to Rabin and Crane (
cf.
Refs.
[
RC85
,
Rab87
]
): The refinement encodes the nontrivial topology of the orbifold of the supergeometry with respect to the action of the discrete Rabin–Kostelecký supergroup of Ref.
[
KR84
]
(in the usual sense in which the de Rham cohomology encodes the topology (homology) of the manifold), and so what we are dealing with is, secretly, a ‘proper’ higher-geometric analysis over the orbifold, which happens to be remarkably compactly packaged in terms of supersymmetry-invariant differential forms. With the
added complexity
of the supersymmetric higher geometry thus isolated, we should think of the considerations that follow as a judicious first step towards the study of generic supergeometries (with supersymmetry), with a body of an arbitrary topology.

6.
The Green–Schwarz super-WZW-model and its super-1-gerbe

We shall now set the stage for our considerations in the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded setting, which we take to be that of the Green–Schwarz super-
\sigma
-model of superstring dynamics in the bulk target with the body given by the standard minkowskian spacetime
\,{\rm Mink}(d,1)
. This choice of the target supergeometry -- flat and topologically trivial (in the body) -- is dictated by the intention to separate the truly novel phenomena engendered in the presence of the Graßmann-odd ‘fibre’ over the riemannian ‘base’
31
31

31

The rigorous structure behind this heuristic picture is provided by the Gawȩdzki–Batchelor Theorem of Refs.
[
Gaw77
,
Bat79
]
.
from the usual cohomological effects of a nontrivial topology of (the body of) the target.

6.1.
The geometry of the bulk GS super-
\sigma
-model

The target superspace of the GS super-
\sigma
-model is the supermanifold

\displaystyle{\mathbb{T}}\equiv{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\equiv{\mathbb{R}}^{d,1|D_{d,1}}\,,

with its global generators of the structure sheaf
\,\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{T}}\,
(coordinates): the Graßmann-even ones
\,\{x^{a}\}^{a\in\overline{0,d}}\,
and the Graßmann-odd ones
\,\{\theta^{\alpha}\}^{\alpha\in\overline{1,D_{d,1}}}
, the latter carrying indices of a Majorana-spinor representation of the group
\,{\rm Spin}(d,1)
, the latter being realised, in the standard manner, within the Clifford algebra
\,{\rm Cliff}({\mathbb{R}}^{d,1})\,
with generators
\,\{\Gamma_{a}\}_{a\in\overline{0,d}}\,
subject to the anticommutation relations
\,\{\Gamma_{a},\Gamma_{b}\}=2\eta_{ab}\,{\boldsymbol{1}}
, written for
\,\eta\equiv(\eta_{ab})_{a,b\in\overline{0,d}}=\textrm{diag}(-,\underbrace{+,+,\ldots,+}_{d\ \textrm{times}})
. In the Majorana-spinor representation of interest, we have a skew charge-cojugation matrix
\,C=(C_{\alpha\beta})_{\alpha,\beta\in\overline{1,D_{d,1}}}\,
and we further assume that the following symmetry relations

\displaystyle\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}^{\rm T}=\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,,\qquad\qquad\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\equiv C\,\Gamma_{a}\,,

and the Fierz identity

(6.1)

\displaystyle\eta_{ab}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}_{\alpha(\beta}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{b}_{\gamma\delta)}=0

hold true. Now, the bulk target superspace
\,{\mathbb{T}}\,
is a Lie supergroup,
i.e.
, in particular, an orbit of the left regular action
\,\ell\equiv{\rm m}
. In the global coordinates, the binary operation
\,{\rm m}\ :\ {\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{T}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{T}}\,
reads

\displaystyle{\rm m}\bigl(\bigl(\theta_{1}^{\alpha},x_{1}^{a}\bigr),\bigl(\theta_{2}^{\beta},x_{2}^{b}\bigr)\bigr)\equiv\bigl(\theta_{1}^{\alpha},x_{1}^{a}\bigr)\cdot\bigl(\theta_{2}^{\beta},x_{2}^{b}\bigr)=\bigl(\theta_{1}^{\alpha}+\theta_{2}^{\alpha},x_{1}^{a}+x_{2}^{a}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\theta_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,\theta_{2}\bigr)\,.

According to the general scheme,
\,\ell\,
determines the left actions

(6.2)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad{\mathbb{T}}\lambda^{(n)}\equiv\ell^{(n)}\equiv\ell\times{\rm id}_{{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})^{\times n}}\ :\ {\mathbb{T}}\times{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})^{\times n+1}\longrightarrow{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})^{\times n+1}

that lift to the de Rham complexes
\,(\Omega^{\bullet}({\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})^{\times n+1}),{\mathsf{d}}_{\rm dR}^{(\bullet)})\,
over
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})^{\times n+1}
. Here, the existence of global generators of the structure sheaf
\,\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{T}}\,
of
\,{\mathbb{T}}\,
(the global coordinates introduced earlier) enables us to rewrite the conditions of left invariance in a uniform and concise manner as

\displaystyle\forall_{(\varepsilon,t)\in{\mathbb{T}}}\ :\ {\mathbb{T}}\lambda_{(\varepsilon,t)}^{(n)\,*}\omega=\omega\,,

where we effectively treat the
\,(\varepsilon,t)\,
as (if they were) constant – this is a convenient alternative to the more general approach to invariance described in Sec.
2
. In particular, these conditions distinguish the basis LI super-1-forms on
\,{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 1}
:

\displaystyle\sigma_{\rm L}^{\alpha}(\theta,x)={\mathsf{d}}\theta^{\alpha}\,,\qquad\alpha\in\overline{1,D_{d,1}}\,,\qquad\qquad\qquad e_{\rm L}^{a}(\theta,x)={\mathsf{d}}x^{a}+\tfrac{1}{2}\,\theta\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta\,,\qquad a\in\overline{0,d}\,,

written in terms of the matrices
\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\equiv\eta^{-1\,ab}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{b}
. We note parenthetically that the Lie supergroup admits an equivalent description, due to Kostant, as the super-Harish–Chandra pair
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})=({\rm Mink}(d,1),\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1}))
,
cf.
Ref.
[
Kos77
]
. Here,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{t}\equiv\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})=\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})^{(0)}\oplus\mathfrak{smink}(d,1|D_{d,1})^{(1)}\equiv\mathfrak{t}^{(0)}\oplus\mathfrak{t}^{(1)}\,,

(6.3)

(6.4)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{t}^{(0)}=\bigoplus_{a=0}^{d}\,\left\langle P_{a}\right\rangle\qquad\qquad\mathfrak{t}^{(1)}=\bigoplus_{\alpha=1}^{D_{d,1}}\,\left\langle Q_{\alpha}\right\rangle

is the (supersymmetry) Lie superalgebra of
\,{\mathbb{T}}
, on which the Lie group
\,{\rm Mink}(d,1)\equiv{\mathbb{R}}^{d,1}\,
acts trivially.

On the above-introduced supergeometry, we have the (odd-degenerate) ‘metric’

\displaystyle{\rm g}=\eta_{ab}\,e_{\rm L}^{a}\otimes e_{\rm L}^{b}\,,

and the LI Green–Schwarz super-3-cocycle

\displaystyle{\rm H}_{\rm GS}=\sigma_{\rm L}\wedge\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,\sigma_{\rm L}\wedge e_{\rm L}^{a}\in Z^{3}_{\rm dR}({\mathbb{T}})^{{\mathbb{T}}\lambda}_{0}

of Refs.
[
GS84a
,
GS84b
]
, which together compose the tensorial data of the superbackground. Note that the super-3-cocycle can be rewritten entirely in terms of the RI counterparts

\displaystyle\sigma_{\rm R}^{\alpha}(\theta,x)={\mathsf{d}}\theta^{\alpha}\,,\qquad\alpha\in\overline{1,D_{d,1}}\,,\qquad\qquad\qquad e_{\rm R}^{a}(\theta,x)={\mathsf{d}}x^{a}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\theta\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta\,,\qquad a\in\overline{0,d}\,,

of the previously introduced basis LI super-1-forms as

(6.5)

\displaystyle{\rm H}_{\rm GS}=\sigma_{\rm R}\wedge\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,\sigma_{\rm R}\wedge e_{\rm R}^{a}\,,

in analogy with the WZW 3-cocycle (
4.4
). In fact the analogy goes much further: The GS super-3-cocycle
is
the Cartan 3-form on the Lie supergroup
\,{\mathbb{T}}
, as noticed in Ref.
[
HM85
]
. It is not hard to verify that the GS super-3-cocycle does
not
possess an LI primitive (
cf.
Ref.
[
Sus17
]
), and so it defines a class
\,[{\rm H}_{\rm GS}]\in{\rm CaE}^{3}({\mathbb{T}})\,
whose
concrete
geometrisation was worked out in Ref.
[
Sus17
]
. The latter is the central object in the analysis that follows.

6.2.
The higher geometry of the bulk GS superbackground

The Lie supergroup
\,{\mathbb{T}}\,
carries a distinguished 1-gerbe with curvature
\,{\rm H}_{\rm GS}
, which was constructed in Ref.
[
Sus17
]
in what may be regarded as a gerbe-theoretic variant of the ingenious stepwise extension scheme, proposed by de Azcárraga
et al.
in Ref.
[
CdAIPB00
]
, for the supersymmetry Lie superalgebra of
\,{\mathbb{T}}\,
associated with the physically distinguished classes in higher (
p+2>2
) Cartan–Eilenberg cohomology groups of
\,{\mathbb{T}}
. In each step of the procedure, the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded analogon
32
32

32

Cf. Ref.
[
Sus17
, App. C]
.
of the classic one-to-one correspondence between equivalence classes of central extensions of a Lie algebra
\,\mathfrak{g}\,
and classes in the second group
\,{\rm CE}^{2}(\mathfrak{g})\,
of the Chevalley–Eilenberg cohomology of
\,\mathfrak{g}\,
is used, and for
\,\mathfrak{g}\equiv\mathfrak{t}\,
and
\,p=1
, we thus arrive at a
Cartan–Eilenberg super-1-gerbe
(in the sense of Ref.
[
Sus17
, Def. 5.11]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\equiv\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1}),\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}},{\rm B}_{\rm GS},L,\pi_{L},\mathcal{A}_{L},\mu_{L}\bigr)

of curvature
\,{\rm H}_{\rm GS}\equiv{\rm curv}\,(\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS})
, termed the
Green–Schwarz
(GS)
super-1-gerbe
in Ref.
[
Sus17
]
. This is an example of a 1-gerbe over a supermanifold – such structures were studied at length in all generality in Ref.
[
Hue20
]
. On the total space of its surjective submersion

\displaystyle\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}}\equiv{\rm pr}_{1}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}\equiv{\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{0|D_{d,1}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{T}}\,,

with its global Graßmann-odd coordinates
\,\{\xi_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in\overline{1,D_{d,1}}}\,
in the fibre (co-spinors of
\,{\rm Spin}(d,1)
), there exists the curving

\displaystyle{\rm B}_{\rm GS}=e^{(2)}_{\alpha}\wedge\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm G}}^{*}\sigma_{\rm L}^{\alpha}\in\Omega^{2}({\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}})^{{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}\lambda}_{0}\,,\qquad\qquad{\mathsf{d}}{\rm B}_{\rm GS}=\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}}^{*}{\rm H}_{\rm GS}\,,

defined in terms of the LI super-1-forms

(6.6)

\displaystyle e^{(2)}_{\alpha}(\theta,x,\xi)={\mathsf{d}}\xi_{\alpha}-\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a\,\alpha\beta}\,\theta^{\beta}\,\bigl({\mathsf{d}}x^{a}+\tfrac{1}{6}\,\theta\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta\bigr)

that are LI with respect to the left regular action of the Lie supergroup
\,{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}\,
on itself,

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}\lambda\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}\ell\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm m}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}\,,

determined by the binary operation
\,{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm m}\,
with the coordinate presentation

(6.8)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm m}\bigl(\bigl(\theta_{1}^{\alpha},x_{1}^{a},\xi_{1\,\beta}\bigr),\bigl(\theta_{2}^{\gamma},x_{2}^{b},\xi_{2\,\delta}\bigr)\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigl(\theta^{\alpha}_{1}+\theta^{\alpha}_{2},x^{a}_{1}+x^{a}_{2}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\theta_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,\theta_{2},\xi_{1\,\beta}+\xi_{2\,\beta}+\overline{\Gamma}{}_{b\,\beta\gamma}\,\theta_{1}^{\gamma}\,x_{2}^{b}-\tfrac{1}{6}\,\bigl(\theta_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{b}\,\theta_{2}\bigr)\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{b}_{\beta\gamma}\,\bigl(2\theta_{1}^{\gamma}+\theta_{2}^{\gamma}\bigr)\bigr)\,,

i.e.
, we have

(6.9)

\displaystyle\forall_{(\varepsilon,t,\zeta)\in{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}\ell_{(\varepsilon,t,\zeta)}^{*}{\rm B}_{\rm GS}={\rm B}_{\rm GS}\,.

It will be convenient to rewrite the curving in the form

(6.10)

\displaystyle{\rm B}_{\rm GS}(\theta,x,\xi)=\theta\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta\wedge{\mathsf{d}}x^{a}+{\mathsf{d}}\xi_{\alpha}\wedge{\mathsf{d}}\theta^{\alpha}\,.

Over the fibred square
\,{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}{\mathbb{T}}\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}{}_{\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}}}\hskip-1.0pt\times_{\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}\times_{\mathbb{T}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}
, we have the principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-bundle

\displaystyle\pi_{L}={\rm pr}_{1}\ :\ L\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}{\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}{\mathbb{T}}

with a principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-connection super-1-form
\,\mathcal{A}_{\rm GS}\,
given, in the coordinates
\,((\theta,x,\xi_{1}),(\theta,x,\xi_{2}),z)\equiv(y_{1},y_{2},z)\in{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}{\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
, by the formula

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{L}(y_{1},y_{2},z)=\vartheta(z)+{\rm A}_{L}(y_{1},y_{2})\,,

(6.11)

(6.12)

\displaystyle\vartheta(z)=\tfrac{{\mathsf{i}}\,{\mathsf{d}}z}{z}\,,\qquad\qquad{\rm A}_{L}(y_{1},y_{2})\equiv\theta^{\alpha}\,{\mathsf{d}}\xi_{21\,\alpha}\,,\qquad\xi_{21}\equiv\xi_{2}-\xi_{1}\,.

The above super-1-form is LI with respect to the left regular action of the Lie supergroup
\,L\,
on itself,

\displaystyle L\ell\equiv L{\rm m}\ :\ L\times L\longrightarrow L\,,

determined by the binary operation
\,L{\rm m}\,
with the coordinate presentation

\displaystyle L{\rm m}\bigl(\bigl(y^{1}_{\ 1},y^{1}_{\ 2},z_{1}\bigr),\bigl(y^{2}_{\ 1},y^{2}_{\ 2},z_{2}\bigr)\bigr)=\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}{\rm m}\bigl(y^{1}_{\ 1},y^{2}_{\ 1}\bigr),{\mathsf{Y}}{\rm m}\bigl(y^{1}_{\ 2},y^{2}_{\ 2}\bigr),{\rm e}^{{\mathsf{i}}\,\lambda(y^{1}_{\ 1},y^{1}_{\ 2},y^{2}_{\ 1},y^{2}_{\ 2})}\cdot z_{1}\cdot z_{2}\bigr)

written in the shorthand notation
\,y^{A}_{\ B}\equiv(\theta_{A},x_{A},\xi^{A}_{B}),\ A,B\in\{1,2\}\,
and in terms of the maps

\displaystyle\lambda\bigl(y^{1}_{\ 1},y^{1}_{\ 2},y^{2}_{\ 1},y^{2}_{\ 2}\bigr):=\theta_{1}^{\alpha}\,\bigl(\xi^{2}_{2}-\xi^{2}_{1}\bigr)_{\alpha}\,,

i.e.
, we have

\displaystyle\forall_{(\upsilon_{1},\upsilon_{2},\varsigma)\in{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}}\ :\ L\ell_{(\upsilon_{1},\upsilon_{2},\varsigma)}^{*}\mathcal{A}_{L}=\mathcal{A}_{L}\,.

Finally, over the fibred cube
\,{\mathsf{Y}}^{[3]}{\mathbb{T}}\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}\times_{\mathbb{T}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}\times_{\mathbb{T}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}
, we find the trivial groupoid structure
\,\mu_{L}\ :\ {\rm pr}_{1,2}^{*}L\otimes{\rm pr}_{2,3}^{*}L\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }{\rm pr}_{1,3}^{*}L\,
with the coordinate presentation (written for
\,y_{A}\equiv(\theta,x,\xi_{A}),\ A\in\{1,2,3\}
)

\displaystyle\mu_{L}\bigl((y_{1},y_{2},1)\otimes(y_{2},y_{3},z)\bigr)=(y_{1},y_{3},z)\,.

In what follows, we shall denote such trivial principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-bundle isomorphisms suggestively as

(6.13)

\displaystyle\mu_{L}\equiv{\boldsymbol{1}}\,.

The isomorphism lifts to the category of Lie supergroups to which its domain and codomain belong, being endowed with the binary operation induced, in a natural manner, from that on
\,L
.

Just to (re-)emphasise: The GS super-1-gerbe is an example of a super-1-gerbe in the sense of Def.
27
, and so it is maximally supersymmetric by construction,
cf.
Sec.
2
.

7.
A multiplicative structure on the Green–Schwarz super-1-gerbe

In the next step, we equip the GS super-1-gerbe with a supersymmetric multiplicative structure, with view to constructing a maximally supersymmetric
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-bi-brane in structural analogy with the un-graded case reviewed in Sec.
5.3
.

We start by checking the relevant identity (
3.5
) for the curvature of the GS super-1-gerbe, with the understanding that it now ought to be satisfied in the
supersymmetric
de Rham cohomology. Thus, we compute – employing the Fierz identity (
6.1
), and in the shorthand notation
\,m_{A}\equiv(\theta_{A},x_{A}),\ A\in\{1,2,3,4\}
, to be used throughout our analysis –

(7.2)

\displaystyle{\rm m}^{*}{\rm H}_{\rm GS}(m_{1},m_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}{\rm H}_{\rm GS}(m_{1},m_{2})+{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}{\rm H}_{\rm GS}(m_{1},m_{2})+\tfrac{1}{2}\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta_{2}\wedge\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta_{2}\wedge\theta_{2}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta_{1}

\displaystyle+{\mathsf{d}}\bigl[2\eta_{ab}\,e^{a}_{\rm L}(m_{1})\wedge e_{\rm R}^{b}(m_{2})+2\theta_{2}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta_{1}\wedge\bigl(e_{\rm L}^{a}(m_{1})+{\mathsf{d}}x_{2}^{a}\bigr)\bigr]\,.

Note that the identity
\,{\mathsf{d}}({\mathsf{d}}\theta_{2}\wedge\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta_{2}\,\theta_{2}^{\alpha}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}_{\alpha\beta})=0
, which follows directly from Eq. (
6.1
), implies – in view of the contractibility of
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
– the existence of a primitive of the 2-form in the last bracket. Using the homotopy retraction
\,(\tau,\theta_{2})\longmapsto\tau\,\theta_{2},\ \tau\in[0,1]
, we readily establish

(7.4)

\displaystyle{\rm m}^{*}{\rm H}_{\rm GS}(m_{1},m_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}{\rm H}_{\rm GS}(m_{1},m_{2})+{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}{\rm H}_{\rm GS}(m_{1},m_{2})

\displaystyle+{\mathsf{d}}\bigl[2\eta_{ab}\,e^{a}_{\rm L}(m_{1})\wedge e_{\rm R}^{b}(m_{2})+2\theta_{2}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta_{1}\wedge\bigl(e_{\rm L}^{a}(m_{1})+{\mathsf{d}}x_{2}^{a}-\tfrac{1}{6}\,\theta_{2}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta_{2}\bigr)\bigr]

so that, altogether,

\displaystyle\Delta^{(1;3)}_{\mathbb{T}}{\rm H}_{\rm GS}\equiv{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}{\rm H}_{\rm GS}-{\rm m}^{*}{\rm H}_{\rm GS}+{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}{\rm H}_{\rm GS}={\mathsf{d}}\varrho_{\rm GS}\,,

where the super-2-form
\,\varrho_{\rm GS}\in\Omega^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{\times 2})\,
is given by the formula

\displaystyle\varrho_{\rm GS}(m_{1},m_{2})=-2\eta_{ab}\,e^{a}_{\rm L}(m_{1})\wedge e_{\rm R}^{b}(m_{2})-2\theta_{2}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,\sigma_{\rm L}(m_{1})\wedge\bigl(e_{\rm L}^{a}(m_{1})+\omega^{a}(m_{2})\bigr)

in which

\displaystyle\omega^{a}(m_{2})={\mathsf{d}}x_{2}^{a}-\tfrac{1}{6}\,\theta_{2}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta_{2}\,.

The crucial property of the above super-2-form is its manifest invariance with respect to the formerly introduced action
\,\ell^{(2)}
,

(7.5)

\displaystyle\forall_{(\varepsilon,t)\in{\mathbb{T}}}\ :\ \ell_{(\varepsilon,t)}^{(2)\,*}\varrho_{\rm GS}=\varrho_{\rm GS}\,.

Next, we examine the multiplicative properties of
\,\varrho_{\rm GS}
. To this end, we write out – invoking the Fierz identity (
6.1
) again (thrice), and for arbitrary
\,m_{A}\equiv(\theta_{A},x_{A})\in{\mathbb{T}},\ A\in\{1,2,3\}\,
–

(7.7)

\displaystyle\varrho_{\rm GS}(m_{2},m_{3})-\varrho_{\rm GS}(m_{1}\cdot m_{2},m_{3})+\varrho_{\rm GS}(m_{1},m_{2}\cdot m_{3})-\varrho_{\rm GS}(m_{1},m_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathsf{d}}\bigl[-2\theta_{2}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,\theta_{3}\,\bigl(e^{a}_{\rm L}(m_{1})+\tfrac{2}{3}\,\theta_{2}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta_{1}+\tfrac{1}{3}\,\theta_{3}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta_{1}\bigr)\bigr]\,,

or, equivalently,

(7.8)

\displaystyle\Delta^{(2;2)}_{\mathbb{T}}\varrho_{\rm GS}={\mathsf{d}}\vartheta_{\rm GS}\,,

where the super-1-form
\,\vartheta_{\rm GS}\in\Omega^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3})\,
is given by the formula

\displaystyle\vartheta_{\rm GS}(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})=-\tfrac{2}{3}\,\theta_{2}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,\theta_{3}\,\bigl(3e^{a}_{\rm L}(m_{1})+2\theta_{2}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,\sigma_{\rm L}(m_{1})+\theta_{3}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,\sigma_{\rm L}(m_{1})\bigr)\,,

and so, in particular, it is LI,

\displaystyle\forall_{(\varepsilon,t)\in{\mathbb{T}}}\ :\ \ell_{(\varepsilon,t)}^{(3)\,*}\vartheta_{\rm GS}=\vartheta_{\rm GS}\,.

Finally, we establish –through another application of the Fierz identity – the equality

\displaystyle\Delta^{(2;2)}_{\mathbb{T}}\vartheta_{\rm GS}=0\,.

Thus, altogether, we arrive at

Proposition 46
.

The quintuple
\,\mathcal{Z}_{\rm GS}\equiv(0,{\rm H}_{\rm GS},\varrho_{\rm GS},\vartheta_{\rm GS},0)\,
with
\,{\rm H}_{\rm GS},\ \varrho_{\rm GS}\,
and
\,\vartheta_{\rm GS}\,
defined above is a simplicial de Rham 4-cocycle over
\,{\mathsf{N}}_{\bullet}{\mathbb{T}}^{\rm op}
, i.e., it belongs to
\,{\rm Ker}\,\mathcal{D}^{(4)}_{\mathbb{T}}
.

The last result constitutes the point of departure in a reconstruction of a full-fledged 0-flat supersymmetric generalised multiplicative structure on the GS super-1-gerbe. In fact, in the cohomologically trivial setting of
\,{\mathbb{T}}^{\bullet}
, the existence of
\,\mathcal{Z}_{\rm GS}\,

ensures
the existence of the full-fledged supersymmetric multiplicative structure, and so what remains at this stage is a tedious derivation, which we append to the following theorem as its constructive proof.

Theorem 47
.

There exists a
\ell^{(\bullet)}
-supersymmetric 0-flat generalised multiplicative structure
\,(\mathcal{M}_{\rm GS},\alpha_{\rm GS})\,
on the Green–Schwarz super-1-gerbe
\,\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\,
associated with the 4-cocycle
\,\mathcal{Z}_{\rm GS}\,
of Prop.
46
.

Proof.

Cf.
App.
G
.
∎

The multiplicative structure found in the present section can subsequently be employed in a construction of supersymmetric bi-branes of the GS super-
\sigma
-model of the super-Minkowskian superstring, carried out in a far-reaching analogy with its un-graded precursor. For that, however, we need one more ingredient, to wit,
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-branes. These we discuss in the next section.

8.
Some supersymmetric
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-(bi-)branes

Below, we present three classes of
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-branes and subsequently employ them in the construction of maximally supersymmetric
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-bi-branes mimicking the un-graded scheme and taking into account the specific form of the chiral (left) rigid supersymmetry of the GS super-
\sigma
-model indicated in Eq. (
3.3
). For concreteness, we fix the superdimensionality of the bulk target super-space
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})\,
as
\,(d+1|D_{d,1})=(10|32)
. Thus, we shall, first, be dealing with quintuples

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{D}=\bigl(D,\iota_{D},\bullet_{D},\omega_{D},\mathcal{T}_{D})

composed of a sub-supermanifold

\displaystyle\iota_{D}\ :\ D\hookrightarrow{\rm sMink}(9,1|32)

mapped canonically into the terminal supermanifold
\,{\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}\,
as
\,\bullet_{D}\ :\ D-->{\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}\,
and equipped with an action of a Lie sub-supergroup
\,{\mathbb{D}}\subset{\mathbb{T}}
, or
reduced supersymmetry group
, induced by the left action
\,\ell\,
of
\,{\mathbb{T}}\,
on itself, and of a trivialisation

(8.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{D}\ :\ \iota_{D}^{*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\mathcal{I}_{\omega_{D}}\,,

that is a super-1-gerbe 1-isomorphism between the restriction
\,\iota_{D}^{*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\equiv\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\mathord{\restriction}_{D}\,
to the worldvolume
\,D\,
and the trivial super-1-gerbe
\,\mathcal{I}_{\omega_{D}}\,
of a curving
\,\omega_{D}\in\Omega^{2}(D)^{\mathbb{D}}\,
supported over
\,D
, the curving satisfying the identity
\,{\mathsf{d}}\omega_{D}=\iota_{D}^{*}{\rm H}_{\rm GS}
, or, equivalently, providing a primitive for the trivialisation
\,[\iota_{D}^{*}{\rm H}_{\rm GS}]\equiv 0\in H^{3}(D;{\mathbb{R}})^{\mathbb{D}}
. These shall be combined, in the manner reviewed in Sec.
5.3
, with the generalised multiplicative structure on
\,\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\,
derived in Sec.
7
, whereby the sought-after maximally supersymmetric
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-bi-modules shall be formed.

We do not, as yet, know a systematic way of identifying the relevant
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-modules, and so the structures that we discuss arise at the intersection of a variety of observations and intuitions established in the un-graded setting of the WZW
\sigma
-model and in the in-depth study of
\kappa
-symmetry related in Refs.
[
Sus20
,
Sus21a
]
and in particular the case study of Ref.
[
Sus21b
]
. As the latter right gauge supersymmetry is
not
incorporated in our analysis (but
cf.
Sec.
8.2
), we do not elaborate the analysis to the level attained in the un-graded case, restricting ourselves to a preliminary investigation of fusion and leaving the completion of the effort thus initiated to a separate future work.

8.1.
The instantonic adjoint
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-brane

In the light of the structural affinity between the bosonic WZW
\sigma
-model and the GS super-
\sigma
-model (
cf.
Ref.
[
HM85
]
, and also Ref.
[
Sus19
]
), and of the bi-invariance of the GS super-3-cocycle
\,{\rm H}_{\rm GS}
, it seems natural to begin our search for
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-branes with
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded analogons of the maximally symmetric WZW branes of Sec.
5.2
. Given the peculiar nature of the adjoint action of the Lie supergroup
\,{\mathbb{T}}\ni(\varepsilon,y),(\theta,x)\,
on itself,
\,{\rm Ad}_{(\varepsilon,y)}(\theta,x)=(\theta,x-\varepsilon\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{\cdot}\,\theta)
, it is clear that the corresponding superworldvolumes are
(0|0)
-superdimensional and localise at the topological points

(8.2)

\displaystyle\widehat{x}{}_{*}\ :\ {\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}\hookrightarrow{\rm sMink}(9,1|32)\,,\qquad x_{*}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{9,1}

in the bulk target super-space
\,{\rm sMink}(9,1|32)
. We have
\,\widehat{x}{}_{*}^{*}{\rm H}_{\rm GS}=0
, and we readily prove

Proposition 48
.

Over every topological point
\,x_{*}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{9,1}\,
in
\,{\rm sMink}(9,1|32)
, embedded as in Eq. (
8.2
), there exists a flat
(0|0)
-superdimensional
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-module

\displaystyle\bigl({\mathbb{D}}_{x_{*}},\mathcal{T}_{-1}^{(x_{*})}\bigr)\,,\qquad\qquad{\mathbb{D}}_{x_{*}}\equiv{\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}

with the 1-isomorphisms

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{-1}^{(x_{*})}=\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}_{x_{*}}{\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}\times_{{\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}}{\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}_{x_{*}}{\mathbb{R}}^{0|0},{\rm id}_{{\mathsf{Y}}_{x_{*}}{\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}},{\mathsf{Y}}_{x_{*}}{\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times},{\rm pr}_{1},{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\vartheta,{\boldsymbol{1}}\bigr)

between the pullback 1-gerbe

(8.4)

\displaystyle\widehat{x}{}_{*}^{*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}_{x_{*}}{\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}\equiv\widehat{x}{}_{*}^{*}{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}:={\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}{}_{\widehat{x}{}_{*}}\hskip-2.0pt\times_{\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}}}\hskip-2.0pt{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}},\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}_{x_{*}}{\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}}\equiv{\rm pr}_{1},\widehat{\widehat{x}}{}_{*}^{*}{\rm B}_{\rm GS}=0,\widehat{\widehat{x}}{}_{*}^{[2]\,*}L={\mathsf{Y}}_{x_{*}}^{[2]}{\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}{}_{\widehat{\widehat{x}}{}_{*}^{[2]}}\hskip-2.0pt\times_{\pi_{L}}\hskip-2.0ptL,

\displaystyle\pi_{\widehat{\widehat{x}}{}_{*}^{[2]\,*}L}\equiv{\rm pr}_{1},\widehat{\widehat{x}}{}_{*}^{[2]\,*}\mathcal{A}_{L}\equiv{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\vartheta,{\boldsymbol{1}}\bigr)

and the trivial 1-gerbe

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{0}=\bigl({\mathbb{R}}^{0|0},{\rm id}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}},0,{\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times},{\rm pr}_{1},{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\vartheta,{\boldsymbol{1}}\bigr)\,.

Upon restriction to the topological point
\,x_{*}
, the adjoint action trivialises, and so the
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-module is (trivially) (
{\rm Ad}
-)supersymmetric.

Proof.

Obvious.
∎

8.2.
A bi-supersymmetric
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-brane

The derivation of the next example draws heavily upon the detailed investigation, anticipated in Ref.
[
Sus21a
]
and presented in Ref.
[
Sus21b
]
, of the consequences of the supergerbe-theoretic interpretation of
\kappa
-symmetry advanced in Ref.
[
Sus20
]
: As was demonstrated in the convenient dual (purely topological) Hughes–Polchinsky formulation of the GS super-
\sigma
-model (
cf.
Refs.
[
HP86
,
GIT90
]
) employed in Ref.
[
Sus21b
]
(after Ref.
[
Sus19
]
), the supergerbe-theoretic manifestation of the critical
33
33

33

The criticality is to be understood in the sense defined by the principle of least action for the action functor of the GS super-
\sigma
-model.
embedding of the worldsheet of the fundamental superstring in the supertarget
\,{\rm sMink}(9,1|32)\,
is the trivialisation of the
{\rm SO}(9,1)
-covariant lift of the GS super-1-gerbe
\,\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\,
from
\,{\rm sMink}(9,1|32)\,
to
\,{\rm sISO}(9,1|32)\,
over the embedded superstring worldsheet. The conclusion hinges on the symmetry analysis of the classical vacuum of the dual super-
\sigma
-model (or, in other words, of the critical embedding), from which the latter emerges as a torsor of the
\kappa
-symmetry group, itself a distinguished Lie sub-supergroup
\,{\rm sISO}(9,1|32)_{\rm vac}\,
of the bulk supersymmetry group
\,{\rm sISO}(9,1|32)
. Consequently, the critically embedded superstring worldsheet determines a Lie sub-superalgebra of the Lie superalgebra
\,\mathfrak{siso}(9,1|32)\,
(the tangent Lie superalgebra of the
\kappa
-symmetry group whose fundamental vector fields span the tangent sheaf of the vacuum). Below, we reverse the logic of the analysis of Ref.
[
Sus21b
]
and first look for Lie sub-superalgebras of
\,\mathfrak{smink}(9,1|32)\,
with a structre analogous to the one of the
\kappa
-symmetry algebra, in which the supersymmetric geometry of the critical superstring is neatly encoded. Only upon identifying all such Lie sub-superalgebras in the superdimension
\,(9+1|32)\,
do we address the question as to which of them determine trivialisations of the bulk super-1-gerbe
\,\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
.

The starting point of the construction carried out in Ref.
[
Sus21b
]
is a choice of a projector

\displaystyle{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}\in{\rm End}\bigl(\mathfrak{t}^{(1)}\bigr)\,,\qquad\qquad{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}\cdot{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}={\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}

of rank
\,0<R\equiv{\rm rk}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}<32
, defined on the Graßmann-odd subspace
\,\mathfrak{smink}(9,1|32)^{(1)}\,
of the Lie superalgebra
\,\mathfrak{smink}(9,1|32)
, with the property

(8.5)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)\,{\rm T}}\cdot\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\cdot{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}={\mathsf{P}}^{(0)\,a}_{\ \ \ \ \ b}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{b}\cdot{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}\,,

where

\displaystyle{\mathsf{P}}^{(0)}\in{\rm End}\bigl(\mathfrak{t}^{(0)}\bigr)\,,\qquad\qquad{\mathsf{P}}^{(0)}\cdot{\mathsf{P}}^{(0)}={\mathsf{P}}^{(0)}

is a projector acting on the Graßmann-even Lie subalgebra
\,\mathfrak{smink}(9,1|32)^{(0)}\equiv\mathfrak{mink}(9,1)\,
of the Lie superalgebra
\,\mathfrak{smink}(9,1|32)
. In the original analysis, the rank of
\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(0)}\,
was constrained to be 2, and its image was required to contain the time direction corresponding to the Lie-(super)algebra index
\,a=0
. Below, we keep the latter condition (after all, we aim to model the worldvolume of an extended object to which a connected component of the boundary of an embedded worldvolume of the fundamental object (
e.g.
, the superstring) is confined) while relaxing the former one. More specifically, we fix, just to keep things simple, a subset
\,\overline{0,p}\subset\overline{0,9}\,
such that

\displaystyle{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(0)\,{\rm T}}\equiv\bigoplus_{\underline{a}=0}^{p}\,\left\langle P_{\underline{a}}\right\rangle\,,\qquad\qquad{\rm ker}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(0)\,{\rm T}}\equiv\bigoplus_{\widehat{a}=p+1}^{9}\,\left\langle P_{\widehat{a}}\right\rangle\,,

and, basing on the findings of Ref.
[
Sus20
]
and of the works cited therein, assume
\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}\,
to be of the form

\displaystyle{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}=\tfrac{1}{2}\,\bigl({\boldsymbol{1}}_{32}+\Gamma^{0}\,\Gamma^{1}\,\cdots\,\Gamma^{p}\bigr)\,.

The above is a projector iff
\,p(p+1)\equiv 2\mod 4
, and so we end up with the limited spectrum of admissible ranks:

\displaystyle{\rm rk}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(0)}\equiv p+1\in\{2,3,6,7,10\}\,.

Under these circumstances, we find

\displaystyle{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)\,{\rm T}}=C\,\tfrac{1}{2}\,\bigl({\boldsymbol{1}}_{32}+(-1)^{p}\,\Gamma^{0}\,\Gamma^{1}\,\cdots\,\Gamma^{p}\bigr)\,C^{-1}\,,

and so

\displaystyle{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)\,{\rm T}}\cdot\overline{\Gamma}{}^{\underline{a}}\cdot{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}=\overline{\Gamma}{}^{\underline{a}}\cdot{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}\,,\qquad\qquad{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)\,{\rm T}}\cdot\overline{\Gamma}{}^{\widehat{a}}\cdot{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}={\boldsymbol{0}}_{32}\,,

whence the desired identity (
8.5
) ensues. Consequently, we find the Lie brackets

\displaystyle\{(Q\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)})_{\alpha},(Q\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)})_{\beta}\}=\bigl({\mathsf{P}}^{(1)\,{\rm T}}\cdot\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\cdot{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}\bigr)_{\alpha\beta}\,P_{a}=\bigl(\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\cdot{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}\bigr)_{\alpha\beta}\,(P\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(0)})_{a}\,,

(8.6)

(8.7)

\displaystyle[(P\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(0)})_{a},(P\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(0)})_{b}]=0\,,\qquad\qquad[(Q\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)})_{\alpha},(P\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(0)})_{a}]=0\,,

defining a Lie sub-superalgebra of
\,\mathfrak{smink}(9,1|32)
, and – indeed – a Lie sub-supergroup of
\,{\rm sMink}(9,1|32)\,
(in the Kostant presentation). We shall write

\displaystyle{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)\,{\rm T}}=\left\langle Q_{\beta}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)\,\beta}_{\ \ \ \ \alpha}\ |\ \alpha\in\overline{1,32}\right\rangle\equiv\bigoplus_{\underline{\alpha}=1}^{R}\,\left\langle\breve{Q}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}\right\rangle

and

\displaystyle{\rm ker}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)\,{\rm T}}=\bigoplus_{\widehat{\alpha}=R+1}^{32}\,\left\langle\widehat{Q}{}_{\widehat{\alpha}}\right\rangle\,.

With the above, we may associate sub-supermanifolds of the supertarget
\,{\rm sMink}(9,1|32)\,
defined as the leaves of the foliation determined, through the super-variant of the (Global) Frobenius Theorem (
cf.
Ref.
[
CCF11
, Thm. 6.2.1]
), with the involutive superdistribution

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}_{(p,1|R)}={\rm ker}\,\bigl({\rm id}_{\mathfrak{t}}-{\mathsf{P}}\bigr)\circ\theta_{\rm L}\,,\qquad\qquad{\mathsf{P}}={\mathsf{P}}^{(0)}\oplus{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}\,,

written in terms of the LI Maurer–Cartan super-1-form
\,\theta_{\rm L}\equiv e^{a}_{\rm L}\otimes P_{a}+\sigma_{\rm L}^{\alpha}\otimes Q_{\alpha}\in\Omega^{1}({\mathbb{T}})^{\mathbb{T}}\otimes\mathfrak{t}
. The superdistribution is spanned by the LI vector fields associated with the vectors from
\,{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}
. Such a definition is the most natural first step towards the discussion of the (
right
)
\kappa
-symmetry and BPS charge of the ensuing
\mathcal{G}
-brane supergeometry, which, however, we postpone to a future work.

Fix a point
\,\widehat{x}{}_{*}\equiv(\widehat{x}{}^{\widehat{a}}_{*})^{\widehat{a}\in\overline{p+1,9}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{9-p}\equiv\{0\}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{9-p}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{9,1}\,
and let

(8.8)

\displaystyle\epsilon_{(p,1|R)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\ :\ D_{(p,1|R)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\hookrightarrow{\rm sMink}(9,1|32)

be a leaf of the foliation of
\,\mathscr{D}_{(p,1|R)}\,
through
\,\widehat{x}{}_{*}
. Write

\displaystyle{\mathsf{P}}\circ\theta_{\rm L}\equiv e{}^{\underline{a}}_{\rm L}\otimes P_{\underline{a}}+\sigma{}^{\underline{\alpha}}_{\rm L}\otimes\breve{Q}_{\underline{a}}\,,

denoting, furthermore, the global coordinates on

\displaystyle D_{(p,1|R)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\cong{\rm sMink}(p,1|R)\equiv{\mathbb{D}}_{(p,1|R)}

induced by the flows of the tangent superdistribution as
\,\{\breve{\theta}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}\}^{\underline{\alpha}\in\overline{1,R}}\,
(the Graßmann-odd ones) and
\,\{\breve{x}{}^{\underline{a}}\}^{\underline{a}\in\overline{0,p}}\,
(the Graßmann-even ones), so that the embedding
\,\epsilon_{(p,1|R)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\,
acquires the coordinate form
\,\epsilon_{(p,1|R)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}(\breve{\theta}{}^{\underline{\alpha}},\breve{x}{}^{\underline{a}})=(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\alpha},\breve{x}{}^{\underline{a}},\widehat{x}{}^{\widehat{b}}_{*})
, written in terms of the spinor
\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}\,
with components
\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}=\breve{\theta}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}\,
and
\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\widehat{\alpha}}=0
, and we have the basis LI super-1-forms

\displaystyle\breve{e}{}^{\underline{a}}_{\rm L}\bigl(\breve{\theta},\breve{x}\bigr)\equiv\epsilon_{(p,1|R)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})\,*}e{}^{\underline{a}}_{\rm L}\bigl(\breve{\theta},\breve{x}\bigr)={\mathsf{d}}\breve{x}{}^{\underline{a}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\,\breve{\theta}\,\overline{\gamma}{}^{\underline{a}}\,{\mathsf{d}}\breve{\theta}\,,\qquad\qquad\breve{\sigma}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}_{\rm L}\bigl(\breve{\theta},\breve{x}\bigr)\equiv\epsilon_{(p,1|R)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})\,*}\sigma{}^{\underline{\alpha}}_{\rm L}\bigl(\breve{\theta},\breve{x}\bigr)={\mathsf{d}}\breve{\theta}^{\underline{\alpha}}\,,

where
34
34

34

Here, we regard each
\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{\underline{a}}\,
as the matrix of a symmetric bilinear form on the 32-dimensional Majorana spinor module. In view of the identity
\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{\underline{a}}\cdot{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}={\mathsf{P}}^{(1)\,{\rm T}}\cdot\overline{\Gamma}{}^{\underline{a}}
, the form splits into a direct sum of its restrictions to (the cartesian squares of) the subspaces
\,{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}\,
and
\,{\rm ker}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}
, and
\,\overline{\gamma}{}^{\underline{a}}\,
is the matrix of the former restriction. This is to be contrasted with the endomorphism
\,\Gamma{}^{\underline{a}}\,
of the said module which becomes block-
anti
diagonal with respect to the direct-sum decomposition of the module into
\,{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}\,
and
\,{\rm ker}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}\,
for odd
\,p
. There is, in particular, no contradiction in the case
\,p=1\,
between the equality
\,\overline{\gamma}{}^{0}=-\overline{\gamma}{}^{1}
, crucial to our reasoning, and the defining anticommutation relations of the underlying Clifford algebra (note that
\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}\,
does not commute with
\,C
).

\displaystyle\overline{\gamma}{}^{\underline{a}}\equiv\overline{\Gamma}{}^{\underline{a}}\mathord{\restriction}_{{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}}\,.

We shall also use the notation

\displaystyle\overline{\Gamma}{}^{\widehat{b}}_{\widehat{\gamma}\underline{\delta}}\equiv\widehat{\chi}{}^{\widehat{b}}_{\widehat{\gamma}\underline{\delta}}

in what follows. It is now obvious that

(8.9)

\displaystyle\epsilon_{(p,1|R)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\bigl(D_{(p,1|R)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\bigr)\equiv\epsilon_{(p,1|R)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}(0)\vartriangleleft{\mathbb{D}}_{(p,1|R)}

is the orbit of the free
right
action of the subgroup

\displaystyle\jmath_{(p,1|R)}\ :\ {\mathbb{D}}_{(p,1|R)}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{T}}

whose embedding in the supertarget Lie supergroup admits the simple presentation
\,\jmath_{(p,1|R)}(\Theta^{\underline{\alpha}},X^{\underline{a}})=(\Theta^{\underline{\alpha}},0,X^{\underline{a}},0)\,
in the global Graßmann-odd
\,\{\Theta^{\underline{\alpha}}\}^{\underline{\alpha}\in\overline{1,R}}\,
and Graßmann-even
\,\{X^{\underline{a}}\}^{\underline{a}\in\overline{0,p}}\,
coordinates on
\,{\mathbb{D}}_{(p,1|R)}
, and whose action is inherited (through the embedding
\,\jmath_{(p,1|R)}\,
and restriction) from the right action of
\,{\mathbb{T}}\,
on itself. This point of view, consistent with our choice of the tangent superdistribution
\,\mathscr{D}_{(p,1|R)}
, is expected to be central to a future analysis of the
\kappa
-symmetry present. It is not a unique action, though, and it is the alternative one that is of key relevance to our present considerations. The latter is founded on the simple observation: The leaf
\,\epsilon_{(p,1|R)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}(D_{(p,1|R)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})})\,
is preserved (indeed, even generated) by the very same subgroup
\,\jmath_{(p,1|R)}({\mathbb{D}}_{(p,1|R)})\subset{\mathbb{T}}\,
acting freely from the
left
(also through
\,\jmath_{(p,1|R)}\,
and restriction),

\displaystyle\epsilon_{(p,1|R)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\bigl(D_{(p,1|R)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\bigr)\equiv{\mathbb{D}}_{(p,1|R)}\vartriangleright\epsilon_{(p,1|R)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}(0)\,.

The embedding (
8.8
) is equivariant with respect to the
{\mathbb{D}}_{(p,1|R)}
-actions: the regular action
\,\breve{\ell}\equiv\breve{\rm m}\,
(resp.
\,\breve{\wp}\equiv\breve{\rm m}
) of
\,{\mathbb{D}}_{(p,1|R)}\,
on its domain, defined just like the left (resp. right) regular action of
\,{\mathbb{T}}\,
on itself, and the induced left (resp. right) action on its codomain, mediated by the embedding
\,\jmath_{(p,1|R)}
,
i.e.
, we have, in the coordinate presentation,

(8.10)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\ell_{\jmath_{(p,1|R)}(\breve{\theta},\breve{x})}\circ\epsilon_{(p,1|R)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}=\epsilon_{(p,1|R)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\circ\breve{\ell}{}_{(\breve{\theta},\breve{x})}\,,\qquad\qquad\wp_{\jmath_{(p,1|R)}(\breve{\theta},\breve{x})}\circ\epsilon_{(p,1|R)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}=\epsilon_{(p,1|R)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\circ\breve{\wp}{}_{(\breve{\theta},\breve{x})}\,,

and so the embedded subgroup
\,\jmath_{(p,1|R)}({\mathbb{D}}_{(p,1|R)})\cong{\mathbb{D}}_{(p,1|R)}\,
becomes a candidate for the residual (global-)supersymmetry group of the
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-brane. Finally, it is worth noting that for the specific choice
\,\widehat{x}{}_{*}=0
, and for that choice exclusively, the corresponding leaf

\displaystyle\epsilon_{(p,1|R)}^{(0)}\bigl(D_{(p,1|R)}^{(0)}\bigr)=\epsilon_{(p,1|R)}^{(0)}(0)\vartriangleleft{\mathbb{D}}_{(p,1|R)}={\mathbb{D}}_{(p,1|R)}\vartriangleright\epsilon_{(p,1|R)}^{(0)}(0)=\jmath_{(p,1|R)}\bigl({\mathbb{D}}_{(p,1|R)}\bigr)

is a Lie sub-supergroup of
\,{\mathbb{T}}
, the embedding
\,\epsilon_{(p,1|R)}^{(0)}\,
being now promoted to the rank of a Lie-supergroup monomorphism. With these simple observations in mind, we may now proceed with our efforts to trivialise the GS super-1-gerbe over
\,\epsilon_{(p,1|R)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}(D_{(p,1|R)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})})\,
in a
\jmath_{(p,1|R)}({\mathbb{D}}_{(p,1|R)})
-invariant manner, or, equivalently, the pullback gerbe
\,\epsilon_{(p,1|R)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})\,*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\,
over
\,D_{(p,1|R)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\,
in a (left-)
{\mathbb{D}}_{(p,1|R)}
-invariant manner.

Following the latter path, we note that the curvature of
\,\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\,
restricts to
\,D_{(p,1|R)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\,
as

\displaystyle\epsilon_{(p,1|R)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})\,*}{\rm H}_{\rm GS}=\breve{\sigma}_{\rm L}\wedge\overline{\gamma}{}_{\underline{a}}\,\breve{\sigma}_{\rm L}\wedge\breve{e}{}^{\underline{a}}_{\rm L}\,.

Thus, in the case
\,p=1
, in which
\,R_{(p=1)}=\frac{32}{2}=16
, we obtain

\displaystyle\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})\,*}{\rm H}_{\rm GS}=\breve{\sigma}_{\rm L}\wedge\overline{\gamma}{}_{0}\,\breve{\sigma}_{\rm L}\wedge\breve{e}{}^{0}_{\rm L}+\breve{\sigma}_{\rm L}\wedge\overline{\gamma}{}_{1}\,\breve{\sigma}_{\rm L}\wedge\breve{e}{}^{1}_{\rm L}\,.

However,

(8.11)

\displaystyle\overline{\gamma}{}_{0}=-\overline{\gamma}{}^{0}\equiv-\overline{\Gamma}{}^{0}\mathord{\restriction}_{{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}}=\overline{\Gamma}{}^{1}\mathord{\restriction}_{{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}}\equiv\overline{\gamma}{}^{1}=\overline{\gamma}{}_{1}\,,

and so

\displaystyle\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})\,*}{\rm H}_{\rm GS}={\mathsf{d}}\bigl(-2\breve{e}{}^{0}_{\rm L}\wedge\breve{e}{}^{1}_{\rm L}\bigr)\,,

which is none other than the desired trivialisation of the curvature, with the manifestly
{\mathbb{D}}_{(p,1|R)}
-invariant curving

(8.12)

\displaystyle\omega_{(1,1|16)}=-2\breve{e}{}^{0}_{\rm L}\wedge\breve{e}{}^{1}_{\rm L}\in\Omega^{2}\bigl(D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\bigr)^{{\mathbb{D}}_{(1,1|16)}}\,.

Upon decomposing the sought-after primitive of
\,\epsilon_{(p,1|R)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})\,*}{\rm H}_{\rm GS}\,
for
\,p>1\,
in the basis of LI super-2-forms on
\,{\mathbb{D}}_{(p,1|R)}
,
i.e.
, writing it out as an
{\mathbb{R}}
-linear combination of the super-2-forms
\,\breve{e}{}^{\underline{a}}_{\rm L}\wedge\breve{e}{}^{\underline{b}}_{\rm L},\breve{e}{}^{\underline{a}}_{\rm L}\wedge\breve{\sigma}_{\rm L}^{\underline{\alpha}}\,
and
\,\breve{\sigma}_{\rm L}^{\underline{\alpha}}\wedge\breve{\sigma}_{\rm L}^{\underline{\beta}}
, we easily convince ourselves that the very same mechanism as the one that ensured success in the previous case dooms us to failure this time (unless we impose additional constraints, which might further restrict the Majorana-spinor representation under consideration). That said, it is only natural to expect that the embedded supergeometries
\,D_{(p,1|R)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\,
for
\,p>1\,
support supersymmetric trivialisations of the higher GS super-
p
-gerbes, and so our analysis is certain to be of use in any future study of supersymmetric boundary (and non-boundary) defects in the GS super-
\sigma
-models for higher super-
p
-branes carried out along the lines drawn in the present paper.

Our hitherto analysis leaves us with the well-posed question of existence of a supersymmetric geometrisation of the tensorial trivialisation
\,\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})\,*}{\rm H}_{\rm GS}={\mathsf{d}}\omega_{(1,1|16)}
. The answer is given in

Proposition 49
.

Over every (topological) hyperbolic plane in
\,{\rm sMink}(9,1|32)\,
labelled by
\,\widehat{x}{}_{*}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{8}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{9,1}\,
(as above), there exists a
{\mathbb{D}}_{(1,1|16)}
-supersymmetric
(1+1|16)
-superdimensional
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-module

\displaystyle\bigl(D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})},\mathcal{T}_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\bigr)\,,\qquad\qquad D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\cong{\rm sMink}(1,1|16)

of curvature
\,\omega_{(1,1|16)}\,
as in Eq. (
8.12
), determined by a 1-isomorphism

(8.13)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\ :\ \epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})\,*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\mathcal{I}_{\omega_{(1,1|16)}}

between the restricted GS super-1-gerbe
\,\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\,
and the trivial super-1-gerbe

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\omega_{(1,1|16)}}=\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}_{0}D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\equiv D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})},{\rm id}_{D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}},\omega_{(1,1|16)},D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times},{\rm pr}_{1},{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\vartheta,{\boldsymbol{1}}\bigr)\,.

Proof.

Cf.
App.
H
.
∎

The existence of the structure (
8.9
) of an orbit of a
right
action of the residual supersymmetry group
\,{\mathbb{D}}_{(1,1|16)}\,
on the worldvolume
\,D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\,
of the
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-brane investigated in the previous section provokes a natural question as to the existence of a lift of the right-handed supersymmetry to the restricted GS super-1-gerbe
\,\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})\,*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\,
and – if the answer is positive – as to the compatibility of its trivialisation
\,\mathcal{T}_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\,
with this extended supersymmetry. We inspect both issues below.

Our analysis begins with the verification of the
right
invariance of the curvature of
\,\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})\,*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
, the latter taking the coordinate form (in the previously introduced notation)
\,\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})\,*}{\rm H}_{\rm GS}(\breve{m})={\mathsf{d}}\breve{\theta}\wedge\overline{\gamma}{}_{0}\,{\mathsf{d}}\breve{\theta}\wedge{\mathsf{d}}\breve{x}{}_{+}
. Given the coordinate transformations

(8.14)

\displaystyle\breve{\theta}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}\longmapsto\breve{\theta}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}+\breve{\varepsilon}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}\,,\qquad\qquad\breve{x}{}_{+}\longmapsto\breve{x}{}_{+}+\breve{t}{}_{+}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\breve{\theta}\,\bigl(\overline{\gamma}{}^{0}+\overline{\gamma}{}^{1}\bigr)\,\breve{\varepsilon}=\breve{x}{}_{+}+\breve{t}{}_{+}

under a right translation by
\,\breve{c}=(\breve{\varepsilon},\breve{t})\in{\mathbb{D}}_{(1,1|16)}
, we immediately infer the anticipated property of the curvature super-3-form,

(8.15)

\displaystyle\breve{\wp}{}_{\breve{c}}^{*}\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})\,*}{\rm H}_{\rm GS}=\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})\,*}{\rm H}_{\rm GS}\,.

The higher-geometric lift of the above tensorial identity is given in

Proposition 50
.

The restriction of the Green–Schwarz super-1-gerbe to the super-worldvolume of the
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-brane of Prop.
49
is
right
-
{\mathbb{D}}_{(1,1|16)}
-invariant, i.e., there exists a family of 1-isomorphisms

\displaystyle\Phi_{\breve{c}}\ :\ \wp_{\breve{c}}^{*}\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})\,*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})\,*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\,,\qquad\qquad\breve{c}\in{\mathbb{D}}_{(1,1|16)}\,.

Proof.

Cf.
App.
I
.
∎

right

of the trivialisation established in Prop.

with the above

.

There exists a
{\mathbb{D}}_{(1,1|16)}
-indexed family of 1-gerbe 2-isomorphisms

(8.16)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern Remark 52
.

In the light of the findings of Refs.
[
Sus20
,
Sus21b
]
, the present study of the
right
extension of global supersymmetry seems to be the first step on a path towards giving a higher-geometric meaning to the
\kappa
-symmetry of the (left-)
{\mathbb{D}}_{(1,1|16)}
-supersymmetric
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-brane
\,(D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})},\mathcal{T}_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})})
. As such, it merits a separate investigation, involving, in particular, a verification of its compatibility with the left supersymmetry. We intend to return to these issues in a future work.

8.3.
An odd superpoint-supersymmetric
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-brane

The basic idea underlying the identification and reconstruction of
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-modules and the associated
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-branes that we pursue in this paper is the reduction of the bulk supersymmetry upon restriction to an embedded sub-supermanifold of the target supermanifold – itself an orbit of a free action of the reduced supersymmetry group whose Lie superalgebra spans the tangent sheaf of the orbit. In this picture, the bulk supersymmetry has so far been induced from (the adjoint (left) action of
\,{\mathbb{T}}\,
on itself or) a
chiral
(
left
) action of the mother supersymmetry group on itself, and so it is
left
actions of subgroups of the latter that we consider. As it turns out, they may and – as demonstrated in the previous section – sometimes do carry an additional
right
supersymmetry that lifts to the restricted super-1-gerbe. Thus, we end up with a product Lie sub-supergroup
\,{\mathbb{D}}_{(p,1|R)}^{({\rm L})}\times{\mathbb{D}}_{(p,1|R)}^{({\rm R})},\ {\mathbb{D}}_{(p,1|R)}^{({\rm L})}={\mathbb{D}}_{(p,1|R)}^{({\rm R})}\,
(resp. a direct-sum Lie sub-superalgebra
\,\mathfrak{d}_{(p,1|R)}^{({\rm L})}\oplus\mathfrak{d}_{(p,1|R)}^{({\rm R})},\ \mathfrak{d}_{(p,1|R)}^{({\rm L})}=\mathfrak{d}_{(p,1|R)}^{({\rm R})}
) of the
bi-chiral
supersymmetry group
\,{\mathbb{T}}^{({\rm L})}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{({\rm R})}\,
(resp. of the supersymmetry algebra
\,\mathfrak{t}^{({\rm L})}\oplus\mathfrak{t}^{({\rm R})}
) of the supertarget
\,{\mathbb{T}}\,
rather than that of its chiral (left) Lie sub-supergroup
\,{\mathbb{T}}^{({\rm L})}\,
(resp. Lie sub-superalgebra
\,\mathfrak{t}^{({\rm L})}
). The last observation leads us to contemplate a more general scenario in which we take the bi-chiral supersymmetry group as the point of departure and look for supersymmetric trivialisations of the GS super-
1
-gerbe over sub-supermanifolds of
\,{\mathbb{T}}\,
that arise as orbits of actions of its arbitrary Lie sub-supergroups,
\,{\mathbb{D}}\subset{\mathbb{T}}^{({\rm L})}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{({\rm R})}\,
with the corresponding Lie superalgebras
\,\mathfrak{d}\subset\mathfrak{t}^{({\rm L})}\oplus\mathfrak{t}^{({\rm R})}
, not necessarily having the structure of direct products (resp. sums) of chiral factors. In so doing, we take care to choose from among sub-supermanifolds of the supertarget
\,{\mathbb{T}}\,
with a non-vanishing Graßmann-odd component of the structure (and tangent) sheaf, and hence proper
super
symmetry which ensures that we do not wind up in the old category of bosonic branes. Below, we elaborate at some length a simple concrete example in which this scenario is seen to work out, thus arriving at a new species of
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-brane.

Our analysis begins with a choice of a subspace within the bi-chiral Lie superalgebra
\,\mathfrak{t}^{({\rm L})}\oplus\mathfrak{t}^{({\rm R})}\,
spanned by
\,N\leq 32\,
linearly independent vector combinations

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\underline{\alpha}}=\bigl(Q_{\underline{\alpha}},Q_{\underline{\alpha}}\bigr)\,,\qquad\underline{\alpha}\in\overline{1,N}

of the Graßmann-odd generators of the left (
(Q_{\underline{\alpha}},0)
) and right (
(0,Q_{\underline{\alpha}})
) summands, respectively, which we may – as we just have – choose to be the first
\,N\,
of the 32 generators of the chiral summands without any loss of generality. Geometrically, the
\,\mathcal{V}_{\underline{\alpha}}\,
are simply the coordinate differentations
\,\mathcal{V}_{\underline{\alpha}}(\theta,x)=\frac{\vec{\partial}\ }{\partial\theta^{\underline{\alpha}}}
, and so they span a purely Graßmann-odd superdistribution

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}_{(0|N)}=\bigoplus_{\underline{\alpha}=1}^{N}\,\left\langle\mathcal{V}_{\underline{\alpha}}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{T}}}

over
\,{\mathbb{T}}
, with the direct complement at
\,x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{9,1}\,
spanned by the remaining
\,\frac{\vec{\partial}\ }{\partial\theta^{\widehat{\alpha}}},\ \widehat{\alpha}\in\overline{N+1,32}\,
and the
\,\frac{\partial\ }{\partial x^{a}},\ a\in\overline{0,9}
. The superdistribution is manifestly involutive, and hence integrable in virtue of the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded variant of the Global Frobenius Theorem,
cf.
Ref.
[
CCF11
, Thm. 6.2.1]
. Its integral leaf

\displaystyle\delta_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\ :\ D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{T}}

through
\,x_{*}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{9,1}
, with the coordinate presentation
\,\delta_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}(\theta)=(\underline{\theta},x_{*})\,
written in terms of the spinor
\,\underline{\theta}\,
with components
\,\underline{\theta}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}=\theta^{\underline{\alpha}}\,
and
\,\underline{\theta}{}^{\widehat{\alpha}}=0
, is the orbit
\,\delta_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}(D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})})\equiv{\mathbb{D}}_{(0|N)}\vartriangleright(0,x_{*}^{a})\,
of that topological point under the (restricted) ‘twisted adjoint’ action

\displaystyle\widetilde{\rm Ad}\ :\ {\mathbb{D}}_{(0|N)}\times{\mathbb{T}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{T}}\,,

with the coordinate presentation

\displaystyle\widetilde{\rm Ad}{}_{\varepsilon}(\theta,x):=\bigl(\tfrac{1}{2}\,\underline{\varepsilon},0\bigr)\cdot(\theta,x)\cdot\bigl(\tfrac{1}{2}\,\underline{\varepsilon},0\bigr)=(\theta+\underline{\varepsilon},x)\,,

of the superpoint (Lie supergroup)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{D}}_{(0|N)}\equiv{\mathbb{R}}^{0|N}\,.

We have an analogon
\,\widetilde{\rm Ad}{}_{\varepsilon}\circ\delta_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}=\delta_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\circ\tau_{\varepsilon}\,
of formulæ (
8.10
), expressed in terms of the translational action

\displaystyle\tau\ :\ {\mathbb{D}}_{(0|N)}\times{\mathbb{D}}_{(0|N)}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{D}}_{(0|N)}

of
\,{\mathbb{D}}_{(0|N)}\,
on itself, with the coordinate presentation

\displaystyle\tau_{\varepsilon}(\theta)=\theta+\varepsilon\,,

and so the superpoint acquires the status of the reduced supersymmetry group of
\,D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}
.

Upon pullback to
\,D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}
, we find
\,\delta_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})\,*}\sigma^{\underline{\alpha}}_{\rm L}(\theta)={\mathsf{d}}\theta^{\underline{\alpha}},\ \delta_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})\,*}\sigma^{\widehat{\alpha}}_{\rm L}(\theta)=0\,
and
\,\delta_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})\,*}e^{a}_{\rm L}(\theta)=\tfrac{1}{2}\,\underline{\theta}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,{\mathsf{d}}\underline{\theta}
, and so the curvature of the GS super-1-gerbe trivialises as
\,\delta_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})\,*}{\rm H}_{\rm GS}=0\,
by the Fierz identity. Altogether, then, we anticipate a
{\mathbb{D}}_{(0|N)}
-supersymmetric structure

\displaystyle\Xi_{\varepsilon}\ :\ \tau_{\varepsilon}^{*}\delta_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})\,*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\delta_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})\,*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\,,\qquad\qquad\varepsilon\in{\mathbb{D}}_{(0|N)}\,,

and a trivialisation of
\,\delta_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})\,*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\ :\ \delta_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})\,*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\mathcal{I}_{0}\,,

compatible with the former. As the (zero) curving of the trivialisation is automatically
{\mathbb{D}}_{(0|N)}
-invariant, it makes sense to speak of a (left)
{\mathbb{D}}_{(0|N)}
–supersymmetric trivialisation here, which is the desirable circumstance. Still, there is no
a priori
reason to expect the tensorial data of the pullback super-1-gerbe
\,\delta_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})\,*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\,
to be
{\mathbb{D}}_{(0|N)}
-invariant. Instead, a generic
{\mathbb{D}}_{(0|N)}
-supersymmetric structure is likely to arise. These expectations are corroborated in (the proof of)

Proposition 53
.

Over every topological point
\,x_{*}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{9,1}\,
in
\,{\rm sMink}(9,1|32)
, embedded as
\,\widehat{x}{}_{*}\ :\ {\mathbb{R}}^{0|0}\hookrightarrow{\rm sMink}(9,1|32)
, and for every
\,N\in\overline{1,32}
, there exists a flat
{\mathbb{D}}_{(0|N)}
-supersymmetric
(0|N)
-superdimensional
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-module

\displaystyle\bigl(D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})},\mathcal{T}_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\bigr)\,,\qquad\qquad D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\cong{\mathbb{R}}^{0|N}

determined by a 1-isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\ :\ \delta_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})\,*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\mathcal{I}_{0}

between the restricted GS super-1-gerbe
\,\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\,
and the trivial super-1-gerbe over
\,D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}
.

Proof.

Cf.
App.
K
.
∎

8.4.
The multiplicative
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-bi-branes

With the three families of supersymmetric
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-branes on our hands, we may complete the construction of the associated maximally supersymmetric
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-bi-branes. Indeed, given a
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-brane (
8.1
), we define, over

\displaystyle Q_{D}\equiv{\mathbb{T}}\times D

and for

\displaystyle{\rm m}_{D}\equiv{\rm m}\circ\bigl({\rm id}_{\mathbb{T}}\times\iota_{D}\bigr)\,,\qquad\qquad\mathcal{M}_{D}\equiv\bigl({\rm id}_{\mathbb{T}}\times\iota_{D}\bigr)^{*}\mathcal{M}\,,\qquad\qquad\varrho_{{\rm GS},D}\equiv\bigl({\rm id}_{\mathbb{T}}\times\iota_{D}\bigr)^{*}\varrho_{\rm GS}\,,

the corresponding (multiplicative)
\ell^{(1)}
-supersymmetric 1-isomorphism

(8.18)

\displaystyle\Phi_{D}\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\ {\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\equiv{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\otimes{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{I}_{\omega_{D}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{-{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\omega_{D}}\xrightarrow{\ \ {\rm id}_{{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}}\otimes{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{D}^{-1}\otimes{\rm id}_{\mathcal{I}_{-{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\omega_{D}}}\ \ }{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\otimes{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\iota_{D}^{*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{-{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\omega_{D}}

\displaystyle\equiv\bigl({\rm id}_{\mathbb{T}}\times\iota_{D}\bigr)^{*}\bigl({\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\otimes{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\bigr)\otimes\mathcal{I}_{-{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\omega_{D}}\xrightarrow{\ \ \mathcal{M}_{D}\otimes{\rm id}_{\mathcal{I}_{-{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\omega_{D}}}\ \ }{\rm m}_{D}^{*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\omega_{Q_{D}}}\,,

with

\displaystyle\omega_{Q_{D}}\equiv\varrho_{{\rm GS},D}-{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\omega_{D}\in\Omega^{2}(Q_{D})^{\mathbb{T}}\,.

The above data give rise to a
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-bi-brane

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{D}=\bigl(Q_{D},{\rm pr}_{1},{\rm m}_{D},\omega_{Q_{D}},\Phi_{D}\bigr)\,.

Thus, upon substituting for
\,\mathcal{D}_{D}\,
(and
\,{\mathbb{D}}
) the data from the previous three sections, we obtain

Theorem 54
.

There exist three species of the
maximally supersymmetric multiplicative
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-bi-module
/
-brane
:

(1)

of
type
\,(0|0)\,
(
instantonic
):

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{x_{*}}\equiv\bigl({\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{0|0},{\rm pr}_{1},{\rm m}\circ\bigl({\rm id}_{\mathbb{T}}\times\widehat{x}_{*}\bigr),0,\Phi_{x_{*}}\bigr)\,,

with

\displaystyle\Phi_{x_{*}}=\bigl({\rm id}_{\mathbb{T}}\times\widehat{x}_{*}\bigr)^{*}\mathcal{M}\circ\bigl({\rm id}_{{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}}\otimes{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{-1}^{(x_{*})\,-1}\bigr)\,;

(2)

of
type
\,(1,1|16)\,
(
superstring-like
):

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{\widehat{x}{}_{*}}_{(1,1|16)}\equiv\bigl({\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1,1|16},{\rm pr}_{1},{\rm m}\circ\bigl({\rm id}_{\mathbb{T}}\times\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\bigr),\bigl({\rm id}_{\mathbb{T}}\times\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\bigr)^{*}\varrho_{\rm GS}-{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\omega_{1,1|16},\Phi^{\widehat{x}{}_{*}}_{(1,1|16)}\bigr)\,,

with

\displaystyle\Phi^{\widehat{x}{}_{*}}_{(1,1|16)}=\bigl(\bigl({\rm id}_{\mathbb{T}}\times\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\bigr)^{*}\mathcal{M}\otimes{\rm id}_{\mathcal{I}_{-{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\omega_{1,1|16}}}\bigr)\circ\bigl({\rm id}_{{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}}\otimes{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{T}^{\widehat{x}{}_{*}\,-1}_{(1,1|16)}\otimes{\rm id}_{\mathcal{I}_{-{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\omega_{1,1|16}}}\bigr)\,;

(3)

of
type
\,(0|N)\,
(
superpoint-like
):

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{x_{*}}_{(0|N)}\equiv\bigl({\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{0|N},{\rm pr}_{1},{\rm m}\circ\bigl({\rm id}_{\mathbb{T}}\times\delta_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\bigr),\bigl({\rm id}_{\mathbb{T}}\times\iota_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\bigr)^{*}\varrho_{\rm GS},\Phi^{x_{*}}_{(0|N)}\bigr)\,,

with

\displaystyle\Phi^{x_{*}}_{(0|N)}=\bigl({\rm id}_{\mathbb{T}}\times\delta_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\bigr)^{*}\mathcal{M}\circ\bigl({\rm id}_{{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}}\otimes{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{T}^{(x_{*})\,-1}_{(0|N)}\bigr)\,.

Proof.

The statement follows straightforwardly from Props.
48
,
49
and
53
taken in conjunction with the general considerations of Sec.
3.1
.
∎

9.
Supersymmetric
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-bi-brane fusion and elementary
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-inter-bi-branes

In this closing section, we undertake the task of fusing pairs
\,(\mathcal{B}_{D_{1}},\mathcal{B}_{D_{2}})\,
of the supersymmetric
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-bi-branes found previously. Given the incompleteness, emphasised earlier, of our symmetry analysis in which
\kappa
-symmetry has not been taken into account, we restrict our considerations to the most elementary structure in the inter-bi-brane hierarchy described in Sec.
1.4
, namely, the ternary inter-bi-brane and the relevant elementary fusion 2-isomorphism of Remark
19
. Accordingly, we shall look for sub-supermanifolds

\displaystyle\iota_{T_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}}\equiv{\rm id}_{\mathbb{T}}\times\iota_{\mathcal{F}_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}}\ :\ T_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}\equiv{\mathbb{T}}\times\mathcal{F}_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{T}}\times D_{1}\times D_{2}\,,\qquad\qquad\iota_{\mathcal{F}_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}}\ :\ \mathcal{F}_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}\hookrightarrow D_{1}\times D_{2}\,,

further embedded in
\,{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3}\,
by the mappings

\displaystyle\jmath_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}\equiv\jmath_{0,1,2}\circ\iota_{T_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}}\ :\ T_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3}\,,\qquad\quad\jmath_{0,1,2}\equiv{\rm id}_{\mathbb{T}}\times\iota_{D_{1},D_{2}}\equiv{\rm id}_{\mathbb{T}}\times\iota_{D_{1}}\times\iota_{D_{2}}\ :\ {\mathbb{T}}\times D_{1}\times D_{2}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3}\,,

with the property

\displaystyle{\rm m}^{D_{3}}_{D_{1},D_{2}}\bigl(\mathcal{F}_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}\bigr)=\iota_{D_{3}}(D_{3})\,,\qquad\qquad{\rm m}^{D_{3}}_{D_{1},D_{2}}\equiv{\rm m}\circ\bigl(\iota_{D_{1}}\times\iota_{D_{2}}\bigr)\circ\iota_{\mathcal{F}_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}}\equiv{\rm m}_{D_{1},D_{2}}\circ\iota_{\mathcal{F}_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}}\,,

so that it makest sense to write

\displaystyle f_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}\equiv\iota_{D_{3}}^{-1}\circ{\rm m}^{D_{3}}_{D_{1},D_{2}}\equiv\mu^{D_{3}}_{D_{1},D_{2}}\circ\iota_{\mathcal{F}_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}}\ :\ \mathcal{F}_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}\longrightarrow D_{3}\,,

and such that there exists, over
\,T_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}\,
and for

\displaystyle p_{A}\ :\ T_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}\longrightarrow D_{A}\,,\qquad A\in\{1,2,3\}\,,

(9.1)

(9.2)

\displaystyle p_{1}:={\rm pr}_{2}\circ\iota_{T_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}}\,,\qquad\qquad p_{2}:={\rm pr}_{3}\circ\iota_{T_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}}\,,\qquad\qquad p_{3}:=f_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}\circ{\rm pr}_{2}

and

\displaystyle p_{0,1},p_{01,2},p_{0,12}\ :\ T_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 2}\,,

(9.3)

(9.4)

\displaystyle p_{0,1}:=\bigl({\rm id}_{\mathbb{T}}\times\iota_{D_{1}}\bigr)\circ{\rm pr}_{1,2}\circ\iota_{T_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}}\equiv{\rm pr}_{1,2}\circ\jmath_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}\,,

(9.5)

(9.6)

\displaystyle p_{01,2}:=\bigl({\rm m}\times{\rm id}_{\mathbb{T}}\bigr)\circ\jmath_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}\,,\qquad\qquad p_{0,12}:=\bigl({\rm id}_{\mathbb{T}}\times{\rm m}\bigr)\circ\jmath_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}\,,

as well as for

\displaystyle\pi^{(3)}_{1,2}\equiv{\rm pr}_{1,2}\circ\iota_{T_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}}\,,\qquad\qquad\pi^{(3)}_{2,3}=\bigl({\rm m}\circ\bigl({\rm id}_{\mathbb{T}}\times\iota_{D_{1}}\bigr)\times{\rm id}_{D_{2}}\bigr)\circ\iota_{T_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}}\,,\qquad\qquad\pi^{(3)}_{1,3}\equiv{\rm id}_{\mathbb{T}}\times f_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}\,,

a (
\ell^{(2)}
-supersymmetric) 1-gerbe 2-isomorphism (we omit the obvious subscripts on the identity 1-isomorphisms for the sake of transparency and explicitly mark the dependence of the 2-isomorphism on the choice of the inequivalent ways of obtaining
\,D_{3}\,
from
\,D_{1}\,
and
\,D_{2}\,
through multiplication)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 97.7161pt\hbox{{\hbox{\kern 68.63152pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.22223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bullet}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-72.88727pt\raise 17.07156pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\textrm{\scriptsize$\begin{array}[]{c}\jmath_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}\,*}\bigl({\rm pr}_{1,2}^{*}{\rm m}^{*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\otimes{\rm pr}_{3}^{*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\bigr)\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\pi_{1,2}^{(3)\,*}\omega_{Q_{D_{1}}}-p_{2}^{*}\omega_{D_{2}}}\end{array}$}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 205.20403pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.22223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bullet}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 208.03062pt\raise 17.07156pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\textrm{\scriptsize$\begin{array}[]{c}\jmath_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}\,*}d_{1}^{(3)\,*}{\rm m}^{*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\pi_{1,2}^{(3)\,*}\omega_{Q_{D_{1}}}+\pi_{2,3}^{(3)\,*}\omega_{Q_{D_{2}}}}\\
