187.15083pt\raise-65.32216pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{I}_{p_{3}^{*}\omega_{D_{3}}+\jmath_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}\,*}({\rm pr}_{2,3}^{*}\varrho_{\rm GS}-{\mathsf{d}}\vartheta_{\rm GS})}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}

Once the latter has been found, we arrive at the elementary (trivalent)
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-inter-bi-brane

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{2,1}[\mathcal{F}_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}]=\bigl(T_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}},\pi_{1,2}^{(3)},\pi_{2,3}^{(3)},\pi_{1,3}^{(3)},\varphi_{3}[\mathcal{F}_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}]\bigr)\,.

We shall now derive the structures indicated above in the case of the two non-instantonic types (
(1,1|16)\,
and
\,(0|N)
) of
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-bi-branes of Sec.
8.4
.

9.1.
Supergeometric fusion of the supersymmetry orbits

Following the logic laid out in Refs.
[
FSW08
]
and
[
RS11
]
and recalled in Sec.
4
, we first look for (examples of) sub-supermanifolds

\displaystyle\Delta^{(x_{*\,1}/x_{*\,2})}_{(p_{1}/p_{2},q_{1}/q_{2}|N_{1}/N_{2})}\hookrightarrow D_{1}\times D_{2}\,,\qquad\qquad D_{A}\equiv D_{(p_{A},q_{A}|N_{A})}^{(x_{*\,A})}\in\{D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,A})},D_{(0|N_{A})}^{(x_{*\,A})}\}\,,\qquad A\in\{1,2\}

within the products of the (same-type) supersymmetric
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-brane worldvolumes introduced in Secs.
8.2
and
8.3
, whose embeddings

\displaystyle(\iota_{1}\times\iota_{2})\bigl(\Delta^{(x_{*\,1}/x_{*\,2})}_{(p_{1}/p_{2},q_{1}/q_{2}|N_{1}/N_{2})}\bigr)\,,\qquad\qquad\iota_{A}\equiv\iota_{(p_{A},q_{A}|N_{A})}^{(x_{*\,A})}\in\{\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,A})},\delta_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*\,A})}\}

in the cartesian square of the mother supersymmetry group
\,{\mathbb{T}}\,
produce worldvolumes of new (embedded) supersymmetric
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-branes under multiplication,

\displaystyle{\rm m}\circ(\iota_{1}\times\iota_{2})\bigl(\Delta^{(x_{*\,1}/x_{*\,2})}_{(p_{1}/p_{2},q_{1}/q_{2}|N_{1}/N_{2})}\bigr)\supset\iota_{3}(D_{3})\,,

with
\,D_{3}\equiv D_{(p_{3},q_{3}|N_{3})}^{(x_{*\,3})}\,
(embedded by
\,\iota_{3}\equiv\iota_{(p_{3},q_{3}|N_{3})}^{(x_{*\,3})}
) of the same type as the
\,D_{A},A\in\{1,2\}
.

In what follows, we switch freely between the sheaf-theoretic and
\mathcal{S}
-point picture in the description of the relevant supermanifolds.

9.1.1.
Superaligned bi-supersymmetric orbits

Consider, first, two embeddings
\,\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,A})}\ :\ D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,A})}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{T}},\ A\in\{1,2\}\,
which are
superaligned
in the sense expressed by the identities

\displaystyle\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,A})}\bigl(\breve{\theta}{}^{\underline{\alpha}},\breve{x}{}^{0},\breve{x}{}^{1}\bigr)=\bigl(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\alpha},\breve{x}{}^{0},\breve{x}{}^{1},\widehat{x}{}^{\widehat{a}}_{*\,A}\bigr)\,,\qquad\qquad\breve{\underline{\theta}}\in{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{01}\,,\qquad{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{01}\equiv\tfrac{1}{2}\,\bigl({\boldsymbol{1}}_{32}+\Gamma^{0}\,\Gamma^{1}\bigr)\,.

Take the respective (
\mathcal{S}
-)points
\,(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{A}^{\alpha},\breve{x}{}_{A}^{0},\breve{x}{}_{A}^{1},\widehat{x}{}_{*\,A}^{\widehat{a}})\in\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,A})}(D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,A})})\,
and compute their product

(9.24)

\displaystyle\bigl(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}^{\alpha},\breve{x}{}_{1}^{0},\breve{x}{}_{1}^{1},\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1}^{\widehat{a}}\bigr)\cdot\bigl(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}^{\alpha},\breve{x}{}_{2}^{0},\breve{x}{}_{2}^{1},\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2}^{\widehat{a}}\bigr)=\bigl(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}^{\alpha}+\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}^{\alpha},\breve{x}{}_{1}^{0}+\breve{x}{}_{2}^{0}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{0}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2},\breve{x}{}_{1}^{1}+\breve{x}{}_{2}^{1}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{1}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2},\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1}^{\widehat{a}}+\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2}^{\widehat{a}}\bigr)\,,

(9.25)

in
\,{\mathbb{T}}(\mathcal{S})
, using the equality
\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{\widehat{a}}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}=0\,
along the way. Clearly,
\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}+\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}\in{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{01}
, and so we conclude that

\displaystyle{\rm m}\circ\bigl(\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1})}\times\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}\bigr)\bigl(D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1})}\times D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}\bigr)=\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1}+\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}\bigl(D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1}+\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}\bigr)\,.

We thus obtain a partial localisation of the trivalent-fusion supermanifold:

\displaystyle\Delta_{(1/1,1/1|16/16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1}/\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}\bigl(1/1(1),1/1|16/16(16)\bigr)\equiv D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1})}\times D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}\,.

Of course, the result does
not
depend on the choice of the embeddings within the class under consideration,
i.e.
, we may vary,
e.g.
, the second Lorentz index of the embedding as in

\displaystyle\epsilon_{(1,1|16);i}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\ :\ D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{T}}\,,\qquad\qquad i\in\overline{1,9}\,,

(9.26)

(9.27)

(9.28)

\displaystyle\epsilon_{(1,1|16);i}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\bigl(\breve{\theta}{}^{\underline{\alpha}},\breve{x}{}^{0},\breve{x}{}^{1}\bigr)=\bigl(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\alpha},\breve{x}{}^{0},\widehat{x}{}^{1}_{*},\widehat{x}{}^{2}_{*},\ldots,\widehat{x}{}^{i-1}_{*},\breve{x}{}^{1},\widehat{x}{}^{i+1}_{*},\widehat{x}{}^{i+2}_{*},\ldots,\widehat{x}{}^{9}_{*}\bigr)\,,

(9.29)

(9.30)

\displaystyle\breve{\underline{\theta}}\in{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0i}\,,\qquad{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0i}\equiv\tfrac{1}{2}\,\bigl({\boldsymbol{1}}_{32}+\Gamma^{0}\,\Gamma^{i}\bigr)

(or take linear combinations of the space-like directions
etc.
), as long as we keep the two embeddings under ‘fusion’ superaligned as above.

9.1.2.
Spatially transverse bi-supersymmetric orbits

Next, we take a look at two embeddings
\,\epsilon_{(1(i_{A}),1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,A})}\ :\ D_{(1(i_{A}),1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,A})}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{T}},\ A\in\{1,2\}\,
that are
spatially transverse
in the sense expressed by the identities

\displaystyle\epsilon_{(1(i_{A}),1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,A})}\bigl(\breve{\theta}{}^{\alpha},\breve{x}{}^{0},\breve{x}{}^{1}\bigr)=\bigl(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\alpha}_{A},\breve{x}{}^{0},\widehat{x}{}^{1}_{*\,A},\widehat{x}{}^{2}_{*\,A},\ldots,\widehat{x}{}^{i_{A}-1}_{*\,A},\breve{x}{}^{1},\widehat{x}{}^{i_{A}+1}_{*\,A},\widehat{x}{}^{i_{A}+2}_{*\,A},\ldots,\widehat{x}{}^{9}_{*\,A}\bigr)\,,

(9.31)

(9.32)

\displaystyle\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{A}\in{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0i_{A}}\,,\qquad{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{0i_{A}}\equiv\tfrac{1}{2}\,\bigl({\boldsymbol{1}}_{32}+\Gamma^{0}\,\Gamma^{i_{A}}\bigr)\,,\qquad\qquad i_{1}\neq i_{2}\in\overline{1,9}\,.

Without any loss of generality, we shall assume that
\,i_{1}=1=i_{2}-1\,
from now onwards. Given arbitrary (
\mathcal{S}
-)points
\,(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\alpha}_{1},\breve{x}{}^{0}_{1},\breve{x}{}^{1}_{1},\widehat{x}{}^{2}_{*\,1},\widehat{x}{}^{3}_{*\,1},\ldots,\widehat{x}{}^{9}_{*\,1})\in\epsilon_{(1(1),1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1})}(D_{(1(1),1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1})})\,
and
\,(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\alpha}_{2},\breve{x}{}^{0}_{2},\widehat{x}{}^{1}_{*\,2},\breve{x}{}^{2}_{2},\widehat{x}{}^{3}_{*\,2},\widehat{x}{}^{4}_{*\,2},\ldots,\widehat{x}{}^{9}_{*\,2})\in\epsilon_{(1(2),1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}(D_{(1(2),1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})})
, we read off from their product

(9.36)

\displaystyle\bigl(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\alpha}_{1},\breve{x}{}^{0}_{1},\breve{x}{}^{1}_{1},\widehat{x}{}^{2}_{*\,1},\widehat{x}{}^{3}_{*\,1},\ldots,\widehat{x}{}^{9}_{*\,1}\bigr)\cdot\bigl(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\alpha}_{2},\breve{x}{}^{0}_{2},\widehat{x}{}^{1}_{*\,2},\breve{x}{}^{2}_{2},\widehat{x}{}^{3}_{*\,2},\widehat{x}{}^{4}_{*\,2},\ldots,\widehat{x}{}^{9}_{*\,2}\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigl(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\alpha}_{1}+\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\alpha}_{2},\breve{x}{}^{0}_{1}+\breve{x}{}^{0}_{2}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{0}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2},\breve{x}{}^{1}_{1}+\widehat{x}{}^{1}_{*\,2}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{1}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2},\widehat{x}{}^{2}_{*\,1}+\breve{x}{}^{2}_{2}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{2}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2},\widehat{x}{}^{3}_{*\,1}+\widehat{x}{}^{3}_{*\,2}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{3}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2},

\displaystyle\widehat{x}{}^{4}_{*\,1}+\widehat{x}{}^{4}_{*\,2}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{4}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2},\ldots,\widehat{x}{}^{9}_{*\,1}+\widehat{x}{}^{9}_{*\,2}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{9}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}\bigr)

that unless we impose the constraints

\displaystyle\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{\widehat{\widehat{a}}}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{=}}0\,,\qquad\qquad\widehat{\widehat{a}}\in\overline{3,9}\,,

the Graßmann-even coordinates with the Lorentz indices
\,\widehat{\widehat{a}}\,
all cease to be constant, but rather than being shifted arbitrarily in the body
and
the soul as in the (
\mathcal{S}
-point) definition of the orbit
\,\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}(D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})})\equiv{\mathbb{D}}_{1,1|16}\vartriangleright(0,\widehat{x}{}_{*})
, they are shifted in the soul exclusively, so that there is no chance to recover an orbit extending in the direction(s) labelled by (the)
\,\widehat{\widehat{a}}
. The seemingly most natural way to ensure that the constraints are obeyed is to assume either of the following conditions to hold true:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\breve{\underline{\theta}}_{1}\in{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{01}\\
\breve{\underline{\theta}}_{2}\in{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{02}\cap{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{01}\end{array}\right.\qquad\qquad\lor\qquad\qquad\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\breve{\underline{\theta}}_{1}\in{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{01}\cap{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{02}\\
\breve{\underline{\theta}}_{2}\in{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{02}\end{array}\right.\,.

Indeed, we then obtain the desired equalities
\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{\widehat{\widehat{a}}}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}=0=\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{\widehat{\widehat{a}}}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}
, respectively. In order to convince oneself that the space of solutions to the above conditions is non-trivial, note that
\,[{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{01},{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{02}]=\tfrac{1}{2}\,\Gamma^{1}\,\Gamma^{2}\neq 0\,
(in fact the right-hand side is even invertible), and so the decomposition of the 32-dimensional Majorana-spinor module of
\,{\rm Cliff}({\mathbb{R}}^{9,1})\,
that we work with all along into the two 16-dimensional eigenspaces of
\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{02}\,
(associated with the eigenvalues
0
and
1
, respectively) is
different
from the one induced by
\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{01}
. We shall denote the intersection of the two eigenspaces associated with the common eigenvalue
1
as

\displaystyle\Delta_{12}:={\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{01}\cap{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{02}\,,\qquad\qquad D_{12}\equiv{\rm dim}\,\Delta_{12}\neq 0\,.

Once either of the above conditions is satisfied, further reductions take place. Indeed, for a pair
\,(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1},\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2})\in{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{01}\times\Delta_{12}
, we have
\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{2}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}\equiv\bigl({\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{01}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}\bigr)\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{2}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}=\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{2}\,\bigl(\bigl({\boldsymbol{1}}_{32}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{01}\bigr)\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}\bigr)=0
, and, similarly, for a pair
\,(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1},\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2})\in\Delta_{12}\times{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{02}
, we find
\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{1}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}\equiv\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{1}\,\bigl({\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{02}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}\bigr)=\bigl(\bigl({\boldsymbol{1}}_{32}-{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{02}\bigr)\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}\bigr)\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{1}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}=0
. Consequently, in the former case, we arrive at

(9.43)

\displaystyle\bigl(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\alpha}_{1},\breve{x}{}^{0}_{1},\breve{x}{}^{1}_{1},\widehat{x}{}^{2}_{*\,1},\widehat{x}{}^{3}_{*\,1},\ldots,\widehat{x}{}^{9}_{*\,1}\bigr)\cdot\bigl(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\alpha}_{2},\breve{x}{}^{0}_{2},\widehat{x}{}^{1}_{*\,2},\breve{x}{}^{2}_{2},\widehat{x}{}^{3}_{*\,2},\widehat{x}{}^{4}_{*\,2},\ldots,\widehat{x}{}^{9}_{*\,2}\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigl(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\alpha}_{1}+\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\alpha}_{2},\breve{x}{}^{0}_{1}+\breve{x}{}^{0}_{2}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{0}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2},\breve{x}{}^{1}_{1}+\widehat{x}{}^{1}_{*\,2}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{1}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2},\widehat{x}{}^{2}_{*\,1}+\breve{x}{}^{2}_{2},\widehat{x}{}^{3}_{*\,1}+\widehat{x}{}^{3}_{*\,2},\widehat{x}{}^{4}_{*\,1}+\widehat{x}{}^{4}_{*\,2},\ldots,\widehat{x}{}^{9}_{*\,1}+\widehat{x}{}^{9}_{*\,2}\bigr)\,,

whereas in the latter case, we get

(9.45)

\displaystyle\bigl(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\alpha}_{1},\breve{x}{}^{0}_{1},\breve{x}{}^{1}_{1},\widehat{x}{}^{2}_{*\,1},\widehat{x}{}^{3}_{*\,1},\ldots,\widehat{x}{}^{9}_{*\,1}\bigr)\cdot\bigl(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\alpha}_{2},\breve{x}{}^{0}_{2},\widehat{x}{}^{1}_{*\,2},\breve{x}{}^{2}_{2},\widehat{x}{}^{3}_{*\,2},\widehat{x}{}^{4}_{*\,2},\ldots,\widehat{x}{}^{9}_{*\,2}\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigl(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\alpha}_{1}+\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\alpha}_{2},\breve{x}{}^{0}_{1}+\breve{x}{}^{0}_{2}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{0}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2},\breve{x}{}^{1}_{1}+\widehat{x}{}^{1}_{*\,2},\widehat{x}{}^{2}_{*\,1}+\breve{x}{}^{2}_{2}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{2}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2},\widehat{x}{}^{3}_{*\,1}+\widehat{x}{}^{3}_{*\,2},\widehat{x}{}^{4}_{*\,1}+\widehat{x}{}^{4}_{*\,2},\ldots,\widehat{x}{}^{9}_{*\,1}+\widehat{x}{}^{9}_{*\,2}\bigr)\,.

It is now clear that upon freezing the Graßmann-even coordinates
\,\breve{x}{}^{2}_{2}\,
and
\,\breve{x}{}^{1}_{1}
, respectively, at constant (pure-body) values:
\,\breve{x}{}^{2}_{2}\equiv\widehat{x}{}^{2}_{*\,2}\,
and
\,\breve{x}{}^{1}_{1}\equiv\widehat{x}{}^{1}_{*\,1}
, we recover the embedded orbits (other scenarios are possible
a priori
)

\displaystyle\epsilon_{(1(1),1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1}+\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}\bigl(D_{(1(1),1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1}+\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}\bigr)\quad{\rm with}\quad\widehat{x}{}_{*\,A}\equiv(0,0,\widehat{x}{}^{2}_{*\,A},\widehat{x}{}^{3}_{*\,A},\ldots,\widehat{x}{}^{9}_{*\,A})\quad{\rm for}\quad\bigl(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1},\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}\bigr)\in{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{01}\times\Delta_{12}\,,

(9.46)

(9.47)

\displaystyle\epsilon_{(1(2),1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1}+\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}\bigl(D_{(1(2),1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1}+\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}\bigr)\quad{\rm with}\quad\widehat{x}{}_{*\,A}\equiv(0,\widehat{x}{}^{1}_{*\,A},0,\widehat{x}{}^{3}_{*\,A},\widehat{x}{}^{4}_{*\,A},\ldots,\widehat{x}{}^{9}_{*\,A})\quad{\rm for}\quad\bigl(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1},\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}\bigr)\in\Delta_{12}\times{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{02}\,.

Denote the relevant sub-supermanifolds determined by the projection and the freeze-out as

\displaystyle D_{(0(2),1|D_{12})}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\equiv D_{(1(2),1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\mathord{\restriction}_{\breve{\underline{\theta}}\in\Delta_{12},\ \breve{x}{}^{2}\equiv\widehat{x}{}^{2}_{*}}\subset D_{(1(2),1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}

and

\displaystyle D_{(0(1),1|D_{12})}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\equiv D_{(1(1),1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\mathord{\restriction}_{\breve{\underline{\theta}}\in\Delta_{12},\ \breve{x}{}^{1}\equiv\widehat{x}{}^{1}_{*}}\subset D_{(1(1),1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}

to be able to partially localise the trivalent-fusion supermanifolds as

\displaystyle\Delta_{(1/1,1/1|16/16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1}/\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}\bigl(1(1)/0(2),1/1|16/D_{12}(16)\bigr)\equiv D_{(1(1),1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1})}\times D_{(0(2),1|D_{12})}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}

and

\displaystyle\Delta_{(1/1,1/1|16/16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1}/\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}\bigl(0(1)/1(2),1/1|D_{12}/16(16)\bigr)\equiv D_{(0(1),1|D_{12})}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1})}\times D_{(1(2),1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}\,.

9.1.3.
Superpoints of opposite chirality

Finally, we consider the fusion of a pair of superpoints
\,\delta_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*\,A})}\ :\ D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*\,A})}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{T}},\ A\in\{1,2\}
. The product of the respective (
\mathcal{S}
-)points
\,(\underline{\theta}{}_{A},x_{*\,A})\in\delta_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*\,A})}(D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*\,A})})\,
reads

\displaystyle\bigl(\underline{\theta}{}_{1},x_{*\,1}\bigr)\cdot\bigl(\underline{\theta}{}_{2},x_{*\,2}\bigr)=\bigl(\underline{\theta}{}_{1}+\underline{\theta}{}_{2},x_{*\,1}+x_{*\,2}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\underline{\theta}{}_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{\cdot}\,\underline{\theta}{}_{2}\bigr)\,,

and so we are led to impose the constraints

(9.48)

\displaystyle\underline{\theta}{}_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,\underline{\theta}{}_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{=}}0\,,\qquad a\in\overline{0,9}

on the Graßmann-odd coordinates of the superpoints under fusion in order to end up with another superpoint. There is a natural way to satisfy the above constraints in dimension
\,9+1\,
while recovering the full Majorana-spinor module in the product, to wit, it suffices to take the two spinors
\,\underline{\theta}{}_{1}\,
and
\,\underline{\theta}{}_{2}\,
of opposite chiralities. Indeed, the canonical (volume) element
\,\Gamma_{11}\equiv\Gamma^{0}\cdot\Gamma^{1}\cdot\cdots\cdot\Gamma^{9}\in{\rm Cliff}({\mathbb{R}}^{9,1})\,
belongs to the anti-centre,
\,\{\Gamma_{11},\Gamma^{a}\}=0,\ a\in\overline{0,9}
, and has the properties
\,C\cdot\Gamma_{11}=-\Gamma_{11}^{\rm T}\cdot C\,
and
\,\Gamma_{11}^{2}={\boldsymbol{1}}_{32}
, therefore eigenvectors
\,\theta_{\pm}\in{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}_{\pm}\equiv{\rm ker}\,{\mathsf{P}}_{\mp}\,
of the associated projectors
\,{\mathsf{P}}_{\pm}=\tfrac{1}{2}\,({\boldsymbol{1}}_{32}\pm\Gamma_{11})\,
satisfy
\,\underline{\theta}{}_{+}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,\underline{\theta}{}_{-}=0
, and any Majorana spinor can be represented as the sum of spinors of opposite chiralities. Denote the relevant sub-supermanifolds determined by the projection as

\displaystyle\Delta_{(0|16\pm)}^{(x_{*})}\equiv D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\mathord{\restriction}_{\underline{\theta}\in{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}_{\pm}}\,.

Altogether, we have the two natural ways to localise the trivalent-fusion supermanifolds:

\displaystyle\Delta_{(0/0|32/32)}^{(x_{*\,1}/x_{*\,2})}\bigl(0/0(0)|16+/16-(32)\bigr)=D_{(0|16+)}^{(x_{*\,1})}\times D_{(0|16-)}^{(x_{*\,2})}

and

\displaystyle\Delta_{(0/0|32/32)}^{(x_{*\,1}/x_{*\,2})}\bigl(0/0(0)|16-/16+(32)\bigr)=D_{(0|16-)}^{(x_{*\,1})}\times D_{(0|16+)}^{(x_{*\,2})}\,.

9.2.
Further constraints from the Defect-Junction Identity

The hitherto (super)algebraic considerations lead to a partial localisation of the
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-inter-bi-brane worldvolume within
\,{\mathbb{T}}\times D_{1}\times D_{2}
. Below, we take into account further constraints, of a tensorial nature, encoded by the DJI, which can now, upon invoking Eq. (
7.8
), be rewritten as

(9.49)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\iota_{\mathcal{F}_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}}^{*}\bigl(\bigl(\iota_{D_{3}}^{-1}\circ{\rm m}_{D_{1},D_{2}}\bigr)^{*}\omega_{D_{3}}-{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\omega_{D_{1}}-{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\omega_{D_{2}}\bigr)=-{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\iota_{\mathcal{F}_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}}^{*}\iota_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{*}\varrho_{\rm GS}+\jmath_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}\,*}{\mathsf{d}}\vartheta_{\rm GS}\,,

with

\displaystyle\omega_{D_{3}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\omega_{(1,1|16)}&\textrm{if}\quad D_{3}=D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,3})}\cr\cr 0&\textrm{if}\quad D_{3}=D_{(0|N_{3})}^{(x_{*\,3})}\end{array}\right.\,.

This is to be satisfied on the (sub-)supermanifold
\,\jmath_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}\ :\ T_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}\equiv{\mathbb{T}}\times\mathcal{F}_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3}\,
introduced in the opening paragraph of the present section. Just as in the un-graded setting, the logic of our approach is to use the above DJI to
identify
the admissible sub-supermanifolds
\,\mathcal{F}_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}
.

9.2.1.
Superaligned bi-supersymmetric worldvolumes

Taking into account the explicit form (
9.25
) of the product of (
\mathcal{S}
-)points in a pair of superaligned
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-branes, we readily calculate the left-hand side of (
9.49
) over
\,{\mathbb{T}}\times\Delta_{(1/1,1/1|16/16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1}/\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}(1/1(1),1|16/16(16))\ni((\theta,x),(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}^{\alpha},\breve{x}{}_{1}^{0},\breve{x}{}_{1}^{1},\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1}^{\widehat{a}}),(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}^{\alpha},\breve{x}{}_{2}^{0},\breve{x}{}_{2}^{1},\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2}^{\widehat{a}}))\equiv(m,\breve{m}{}_{1},\breve{m}{}_{2})\,
as

(9.55)

\displaystyle\omega_{D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1}+\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}}\bigl(\breve{m}{}_{1}\cdot\breve{m}{}_{2}\bigr)-\omega_{D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1})}}\bigl(\breve{m}{}_{1}\bigr)-\omega_{D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}}\bigl(\breve{m}{}_{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigl(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}+2\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}\bigr)\,\overline{\gamma}{}_{0}\,{\mathsf{d}}\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}\wedge{\mathsf{d}}\breve{x}{}_{+\,2}+\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}\,\overline{\gamma}{}_{0}\,{\mathsf{d}}\bigl(2\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}+\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}\bigr)\wedge{\mathsf{d}}\breve{x}{}_{+\,1}+{\mathsf{d}}\breve{x}{}_{+\,1}\wedge{\mathsf{d}}\breve{x}{}_{-\,2}-{\mathsf{d}}\breve{x}{}_{-\,1}\wedge{\mathsf{d}}\breve{x}{}_{+\,2}\,.

Upon comparison with the right-hand side,

(9.59)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{d}}\vartheta_{\rm GS}\bigl(m,\breve{m}{}_{1},\breve{m}{}_{2}\bigr)-\varrho_{\rm GS}\bigl(\breve{m}{}_{1},\breve{m}{}_{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigl(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}+2\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}\bigr)\,\overline{\gamma}{}_{0}\,{\mathsf{d}}\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}\wedge{\mathsf{d}}\breve{x}{}_{+\,2}+\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}\,\overline{\gamma}{}_{0}\,{\mathsf{d}}\bigl(2\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}+\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}\bigr)\wedge{\mathsf{d}}\breve{x}{}_{+\,1}+{\mathsf{d}}\breve{x}{}_{+\,2}\wedge{\mathsf{d}}\breve{x}{}_{-\,1}+{\mathsf{d}}\breve{x}{}_{-\,2}\wedge{\mathsf{d}}\breve{x}{}_{+\,1}

\displaystyle+{\mathsf{d}}\bigl(-\tfrac{2}{3}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{\underline{a}}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}\,\bigl(3e^{\underline{a}}_{\rm L}(m)+2\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{\underline{a}}\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta+\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{\underline{a}}\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta\bigr)\bigr)\,,

this yields the condition

\displaystyle 3{\mathsf{d}}\breve{x}{}_{-\,2}\wedge{\mathsf{d}}\breve{x}{}_{+\,1}-{\mathsf{d}}\bigl(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{\underline{a}}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}\,\bigl(3e^{\underline{a}}_{\rm L}(m)+2\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{\underline{a}}\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta+\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{\underline{a}}\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta\bigr)\bigr)=0

to be satisfied identically on the sub-supermanifold sought after. In view of the arbitrariness of
\,m
, we are thus led to the pair of constraints

(9.60)

\displaystyle\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}\,\overline{\gamma}{}_{0}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{=}}0\qquad\land\qquad{\mathsf{d}}\breve{x}{}_{+\,1}\wedge{\mathsf{d}}\breve{x}{}_{-\,2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{=}}0\,.

These can be solved naturally by taking a pair

\displaystyle[a_{1}:a_{2}:b_{1,2}]\in{\mathbb{R}}{\mathbb{P}}^{2}\setminus\{[0:0:1]\}\,,\qquad\qquad[c_{1}:c_{2}]\in{\mathbb{R}}{\mathbb{P}}^{1}

and setting

\displaystyle a_{1}\,\breve{x}{}_{+\,1}+a_{2}\,\breve{x}{}_{-\,2}=b_{1,2}\qquad\land\qquad c_{1}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}+c_{2}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}=0\,,

which defines a sub-supermanifold within
\,\Delta_{(1/1,1/1|16/16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1}/\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}(1/1(1),1|16/16(16))
. As we want to recover the entire
{\mathbb{D}}_{1,1|16}
-orbit through multiplication of (
\mathcal{S}
-)points of the latter, we have to assume

\displaystyle[a_{1}:a_{2}:b_{1,2}]\in{\mathbb{R}}{\mathbb{P}}^{2}\setminus\{[0:0:1]\}\,,\qquad\qquad[c_{1}:c_{2}]\in{\mathbb{R}}{\mathbb{P}}^{1}\setminus\{[1:1]\}\,.

We shall denote the sub-supermanifold thus determined as

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{D^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1})}_{(1,1|16)},D^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}_{(1,1|16)}}^{D^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1}+\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}_{(1,1|16)}}\bigl([a_{1}:a_{2}:b_{1,2}]\,|\,[c_{1}:c_{2}]\bigr)\hookrightarrow\Delta_{(1/1,1/1|16/16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1}/\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}\bigl(1/1(1),1|16/16(16)\bigr)\,,

and define the ensuing inter-
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-bi-brane worldvolume

\displaystyle T_{D^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1})}_{(1,1|16)},D^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}_{(1,1|16)}}^{D^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1}+\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}_{(1,1|16)}}\bigl([a_{1}:a_{2}:b_{1,2}]\,|\,[c_{1}:c_{2}]\bigr):={\mathbb{T}}\times\mathcal{F}_{D^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1})}_{(1,1|16)},D^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}_{(1,1|16)}}^{D^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1}+\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}_{(1,1|16)}}\bigl([a_{1}:a_{2}:b_{1,2}]\,|\,[c_{1}:c_{2}]\bigr)\,.

9.2.2.
Spatially transverse bi-supersymmetric worldvolumes

The reasoning and calculations are completely analogous to those from the previous paragraph, and lead us to the constraints

\displaystyle\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}\,\overline{\gamma}{}_{0}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{=}}0\qquad\land\qquad{\mathsf{d}}\bigl(\breve{x}{}^{0}_{1}+\breve{x}{}^{1}_{1}\bigr)\wedge{\mathsf{d}}\breve{x}{}^{0}_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{=}}0

for
\,(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1},\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2})\in{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{01}\times\Delta_{12}
, and to

\displaystyle\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}\,\overline{\gamma}{}_{0}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{=}}0\qquad\land\qquad{\mathsf{d}}\breve{x}{}^{0}_{1}\wedge{\mathsf{d}}\bigl(\breve{x}{}^{0}_{2}-\breve{x}{}^{2}_{2}\bigr)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{=}}0

for
\,(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1},\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2})\in\Delta_{12}\times{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}^{(1)}_{02}
, and as before, the solution can be taken in the form

\displaystyle a_{1}\,\bigl(\breve{x}{}^{0}_{1}+\breve{x}{}^{1}_{1}\bigr)+a_{2}\,\breve{x}{}^{0}_{2}=b_{1,2}\qquad\land\qquad c_{1}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}+c_{2}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}=0

or

\displaystyle a_{1}\,\breve{x}^{0}_{1}+a_{2}\,\bigl(\breve{x}{}^{0}_{2}-\breve{x}{}^{2}_{2}\bigr)=b_{1,2}\qquad\land\qquad c_{1}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}+c_{2}\,\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}=0\,,

respectively, with the previous restrictions for the constant coefficients. There is one subtle difference with respect to the previous case, though. Indeed, whenever
\,[c_{1}:c_{2}]\neq[0:1]\,
(resp.
\,[c_{1}:c_{2}]\neq[1:0]
), we have
\,(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1},\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2})\in\Delta_{12}\times\Delta_{12}
, and so we do
not
retrieve the entire orbit through multiplication as the odd components both come from the subspace
\,\Delta_{12}\,
(and therefore so does their sum). Consequently, we must require

\displaystyle[a_{1}:a_{2}:b_{1,2}]\in{\mathbb{R}}{\mathbb{P}}^{2}\setminus\{[0:0:1]\}\,,\qquad\qquad[c_{1}:c_{2}]\in\{[1:0],[0:1]\}\,,

and, accordingly, arrive at a pair of sub-supermanifolds:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{D^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1})}_{(1(1),1|16)},D^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}_{(1(2),1|16)}}^{D^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1}+\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}_{(1(1),1|16)}}\bigl([a_{1}:a_{2}:b_{1,2}]\,|\,[0:1]\bigr)\hookrightarrow\Delta_{(1/1,1/1|16/16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1}/\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}\bigl(1(1)/0(2),1/1|16/D_{12}(16)\bigr)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{D^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1})}_{(1(1),1|16)},D^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}_{(1(2),1|16)}}^{D^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1}+\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}_{(1(2),1|16)}}\bigl([a_{1}:a_{2}:b_{1,2}]\,|\,[1:0]\bigr)\hookrightarrow\Delta_{(1/1,1/1|16/16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1}/\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}\bigl(1(1)/0(2),1/1|16/D_{12}(16)\bigr)\,,

respectively. The corresponding inter-
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-bi-brane worldvolumes shall be denoted as

\displaystyle T_{D^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1})}_{(1(1),1|16)},D^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}_{(1(2),1|16)}}^{D^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1}+\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}_{(1(1),1|16)}}\bigl([a_{1}:a_{2}:b_{1,2}]\,|\,[c_{1}:c_{2}]\bigr):={\mathbb{T}}\times\mathcal{F}_{D^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1})}_{(1(1),1|16)},D^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}_{(1(2),1|16)}}^{D^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1}+\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}_{(1(1),1|16)}}\bigl([a_{1}:a_{2}:b_{1,2}]\,|\,[c_{1}:c_{2}]\bigr)\,,

and

\displaystyle T_{D^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1})}_{(1(1),1|16)},D^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}_{(1(2),1|16)}}^{D^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1}+\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}_{(1(2),1|16)}}\bigl([a_{1}:a_{2}:b_{1,2}]\,|\,[c_{1}:c_{2}]\bigr):={\mathbb{T}}\times\mathcal{F}_{D^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1})}_{(1(1),1|16)},D^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}_{(1(2),1|16)}}^{D^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1}+\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}_{(1(2),1|16)}}\bigl([a_{1}:a_{2}:b_{1,2}]\,|\,[c_{1}:c_{2}]\bigr)\,,

with
\,[c_{1}:c_{2}]=[0:1]\,
(resp.
\,[c_{1}:c_{2}]=[1:0]
), respectively.

9.2.3.
Superpoint worldvolumes of opposite chirality

In the last example, the situation simplifies dramatically as
\,\omega_{D^{(x_{*})}_{(0|N)}}\equiv 0
, which means that we have to identify a sub-supermanifold of

\displaystyle{\mathbb{T}}\times\Delta_{(0/0|32/32)}^{(x_{*\,1}/x_{*\,2})}\bigl(0/0(0)|16\pm/16\mp(32)\bigr)\ni\bigl((\theta,x),\bigl(\underline{\theta}{}_{1},x_{*\,1}\bigr),\bigl(\underline{\theta}{}_{2},x_{*\,2}\bigr)\bigr)\equiv\bigl(m,\breve{m}{}_{1},\breve{m}{}_{2}\bigr)\,,

(9.61)

(9.62)

\displaystyle\bigl(\underline{\theta}{}_{1},\underline{\theta}{}_{2}\bigr)\in{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}_{\pm}\times{\rm im}\,{\mathsf{P}}_{\mp}

on which
\,\varrho_{\rm GS}(\breve{m}{}_{1},\breve{m}{}_{2})={\mathsf{d}}\vartheta_{\rm GS}(m,\breve{m}{}_{1},\breve{m}{}_{2})
. Using (
9.48
), we readily establish that the right-hand side vanishes,
\,{\mathsf{d}}\vartheta_{\rm GS}(m,\breve{m}{}_{1},\breve{m}{}_{2})=0
, and so it suffices to identify those
\,(\underline{\theta}{}_{1},\underline{\theta}{}_{2})\,
for which so does the left-hand side which reduces to the expression
\,\varrho_{\rm GS}\bigl(\breve{m}{}_{1},\breve{m}{}_{2}\bigr)=\tfrac{1}{2}\,\underline{\theta}{}_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,\underline{\theta}{}_{2}\cdot{\mathsf{d}}\underline{\theta}{}_{1}\wedge\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,{\mathsf{d}}\underline{\theta}{}_{2}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\underline{\theta}{}_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,{\mathsf{d}}\underline{\theta}{}_{2}\wedge\underline{\theta}{}_{2}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,{\mathsf{d}}\underline{\theta}{}_{1}\,
upon invoking the Fierz identity (
6.1
), and hence vanishes in consequence of Eq. (
9.48
). As a result of the above straightforward check, we obtain the supermanifolds

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{D^{(x_{*\,1})}_{(0|16\pm)},D^{(x_{*\,2})}_{(0|16\mp)}}^{D^{(x_{*\,1}+x_{*\,2})}_{(0|32)}}\equiv\Delta_{(0/0|32/32)}^{(x_{*\,1}/x_{*\,2})}\bigl(0/0(0)|16\pm/16\mp(32)\bigr)\,,

and the candidate corresponding inter-
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-bi-brane worldvolumes

\displaystyle T_{D^{(x_{*\,1})}_{(0|16\pm)},D^{(x_{*\,2})}_{(0|16\mp)}}^{D^{(x_{*\,1}+x_{*\,2})}_{(0|32)}}:={\mathbb{T}}\times\mathcal{F}_{D^{(x_{*\,1})}_{(0|16\pm)},D^{(x_{*\,2})}_{(0|16\mp)}}^{D^{(x_{*\,1}+x_{*\,2})}_{(0|32)}}\,.

9.3.
The fusion 2-isomorphism

The previous sections have provided us with candidate
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-inter-bi-brane worldvolumes
\,T_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}\equiv{\mathbb{T}}\times\mathcal{F}_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}\,
on which the corresponding DJI’s (
9.49
) hold identically. In general, an ultimate localisation of the worldvolume would result only form a construction of the relevant fusion 2-isomorphism (
9.23
), however, in the (de Rham-)cohomologically trivial environment of our current interest, no extra constraints arise as we look for sub-supermanifolds on which not only the DJI obtains but also the underlying higher-supergeometric structure exists. Accordingly, we merely have to decipher the data of the latter over the
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-inter-bi-brane worldvolumes found. Before we do that, let us note, in the passing, that in all the cases of elementary fusion considered above the tensorial constraints lead to the nullification of the contribution from the trivial 1-isomorphism
\,\mathcal{J}_{-\vartheta_{\rm GS}}
, and so we descend to the faithful image
\,D_{1,2}^{3}\equiv\iota_{\mathcal{F}_{1,2}^{3}}(\mathcal{F}_{1,2}^{3})\,
of
\,\mathcal{F}_{1,2}^{3}\,
in
\,D_{1}\times D_{2}
, with the associated mapping
\,\mu_{1,2}^{3}\ :\ D_{1,2}^{3}\longrightarrow D_{3}\,
and end up with the standard (non-generalised) fusion 2-isomorphism

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 65.1784pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-65.1784pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\textrm{$\begin{array}[]{c}\iota_{1,2}^{*}\bigl({\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\otimes{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\bigr)\\

\cong_{2}{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\iota_{1}^{*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\otimes{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\iota_{2}^{*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\end{array}$}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-54.91698pt\raise-34.8937pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.99155pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{1}\otimes{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{2}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-62.45412pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 85.21553pt\raise 6.6582pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.68599pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\iota_{1,2}^{*}\mathcal{M}_{\rm GS}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 150.53667pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 150.53667pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\textrm{$\begin{array}[]{c}\iota_{1,2}^{*}\bigl({\rm m}^{*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\varrho_{\rm GS}}\bigr)\\

GS}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\,,

written in the shorthand notation:
\,X_{A}\equiv X_{{D^{(x_{*\,A})}_{(p_{A},q_{A}|N_{A})}\,*}},\ X\in\{\mathcal{T},\omega,\iota\},\ A\in\{1,2,3\}\,
and
\,\iota_{1,2}\equiv\iota_{D_{1},D_{2}}\mathord{\restriction}_{D_{1,2}^{3}}
. Its predecessor was encountered previously in the non-graded setting in Ref.
[
RS11
]
(
cf.
also Ref.
[
RS09
]
). After these general remarks, we may summarise the detailed results in

Theorem 55
.

Over each of the supermanifolds
\,T_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}\equiv{\mathbb{T}}\times\mathcal{F}_{D_{1},D_{2}}^{D_{3}}\,
identified in Secs.
9.2.1
,
9.2.2
and
9.2.3
, there exists a corresponding fusion 2-isomorphism
\,\check{\phi}[\mathcal{F}_{1,2}^{3}]
.

Proof.

Cf.
App.
L
.
∎

This concludes our preliminary analysis of the elementary fusion of the maximally supersymmetric bi-branes.

Conclusions & Outlook

In the present paper, the general framework for the lagrangean description of
\sigma
-model-type loop dynamics with Wess–Zumino-type terms in the presence of self-intersecting worldsheet defects, originally formulated for un-graded target spaces in Ref.
[
RS09
]
, has been extended to the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded geometric category, with the two-dimensional spacetime of the ensuing superfield theory with defects kept Graßmann-even consistently with the functorial paradigm laid out in Ref.
[
Fre99
]
. The framework has two layers: the worldsheet-defect layer and the target (super)space layer, with the structure of the former, essentially homological in its nature, reflected faithfully in the quasi-simplicial relations between bicategorial components of the latter. The relations become particularly rich and prominent in the case of the so-called topological defects amenable to homotopy manipulations – this simple observation, implicit already in Ref.
[
RS09
]
, and soon afterwards formalised and emphasised in Ref.
[
Sus12
]
, has led us to consider a rather natural class of (super)targets, to wit, the simplicial target (super-)spaces, introduced in Def.
13
, in which target inter-bi-brane worldvolumes associated with defect junctions of arbitrary valence form simplicial hierarchies whose face maps furnish a realisation of the induction scheme for topological defects discussed in the papers cited. The (super)geometry is augmented with the data of a simplicial (1-)gerbe over it, altogether giving rise to the simplicial (super)string background of Def.
23
. Simpliciality has subsequently been coherently combined with the principle of (super)symmetry (or, indeed, that of full reducibility with respect to the action of a (super)symmetry group), in its categorified form suggested by the findings of Refs.
[
FSW08
,
RS09
,
RS11
]
and, in particular, those of Refs.
[
Sus11a
,
Sus12
]
, whereby the notion of a maximally supersymmetric background has been introduced in Sec.
2
. The abstract concept has been illustrated amply on the ‘canonical’ examples, both Graßmann-even and
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded, of the Lie background of Sec.
3
, defined over the disjoint union of those full orbits of the simplicial action of the (super)symmetry group on the nerve of the right-regular action groupoid of a target Lie (super)group on itself which support the bi-categorial data of the maximally (super)symmetric WZW defect of Refs.
[
FSW08
,
RS09
,
RS11
]
resp. its
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded counterpart. In the un-graded Lie-group setting, the combined simplicial/symmetry analysis has been used to demonstrate – upon a physically justified loop-group enrichment – uniqueness of the tensorial data (the curvature) of the maximally symmetric WZW brane and bi-brane of Ref.
[
FSW08
]
(
cf.
Props.
30
and
32
), and yielded a novel proposal, explicited in Def.
41
, for the target geometry carrying the data of the so-called fusion 2-isomorphisms (or inter-bi-brane) of Refs.
[
RS09
,
RS11
]
, based on and partially corroborated by the findings of the detailed case study of Ref.
[
RS11
]
. The latter derivation has revealed a deep structural relationship between the (un-graded) maximally symmetric WZW background and the three-dimensional Chern–Simons theory with timelike Wilson lines, consistent with and elaborating substantially that established in Refs.
[
Wit89b
,
Gaw89
,
Wit92
,
Gaw99
,
GTTNB04
]
and the findings of Ref.
[
Sus12
]
– the relationship is at the core of the fundamental Thm.
37
. Furthermore, it has motivated a conjectural identification, in Sec.
5.4
, of the (higher-)geometric structure that is anticipated to encode the Verlinde fusion rules and (a part of) the Moore–Seiberg data of the (chiral) bulk WZW
\sigma
-model, in an extension of and in conformity with the partial results of Refs.
[
RS09
,
RS11
]
. In the supergeometric setting of the Green–Schwarz super-
\sigma
-model of the superstring in
\,{\rm sMink}(d,1|D_{d,1})
, on the other hand, the analysis has produced three classes of maximally supersymmetric (bi-)branes (Sec.
8.4
) and partial results on their (elementary) fusion (Sec.
9
). In full analogy with the un-graded setting, central rôle in the analysis has been played by the categorification of the binary operation on the (supersymmetry) Lie supergroup known as the multiplicative structure (
cf.
Ref.
[
CJM+05
]
). The latter notion has been generalised suitably in Sec.
3.1
, and subsequently an instantiation of such a generalised multiplicative structure, manifestly compatible with the supersymmetry present, has been identified on the Green–Schwarz super-1-gerbe of Ref.
[
Sus17
]
in Sec.
7
. These results are, to the best of the Author’s knowledge, the first examples of maximally (super)symmetric bi-branes and inter-bi-branes studied in the literature.

The work reported in the present paper leaves several important open questions: The first of them concerns the validity of the WZW conjectures of Sec.
5.4
. It is worth pointing out that Conjecture
1
, preliminarily verified in the case of
\,{\rm G}={\rm SU}(2)\,
(with simple fusion rules) in Ref.
[
RS11
]
, could readily be tested in the largely tractable yet nontrivial case of
\,{\rm G}={\rm SU}(3)
,
cf.
,
e.g.
, Ref.
[
Hay99
]
, and Conjecture
2
together with the field-theoretic content of Def.
45
could be investigated for the simplest non-abelian Lie group
\,{\rm G}={\rm SU}(2)
. The second issue that ought to be explored at length is the intricate structural relationship between the WZW fusion 2-isomorphisms and the Chern–Simons theory – here, one might start by performing a symplectic analysis of the WZW model in the presence of a self-intersecting maximally symmetric defect with reference to the known canonical description of the topological field theory (in close analogy with the original works by Gawȩdzki) and subsequently analyse the transgression of the higher-categorial data carried by the defect to the phase space of the
\sigma
-model under consideration (in keeping with Refs.
[
Sus11a
,
Sus12
]
). In the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded setting, the most pending question is that of the status of
\kappa
-symmetry, as understood – also in the higher-geometric terms – in the dual Hughes–Polchinsky formulation of the super-
\sigma
-model of Ref.
[
HP86
]
in Refs.
[
Sus20
,
Sus21a
,
Sus21b
]
, in the presence of the maximally supersymmetric (bi-)branes studied in the present paper. The answer, in conjunction with a positive outcome of the verification of the WZW conjectures, might pave the way towards the extraction of non-perturbative data of the super-WZW-model of Green and Schwarz from the higher-geometric analysis performed. Of course, a more systematic approach to the construction of maximally supersymmetric branes and bi-branes would most certainly also be welcome, and it is clear that it might go
via
analysis of Lie-superalgebraic skeleta of would-be travialisations of
\,\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\,
resp.
\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
-bimodules akin to those considered in Ref.
[
Sus21b
]
. Another important direction in which the higher-supergeometric research initiated in the present paper might be developed is the study of maximally supersymmetric defects (boundary and non-boundary) in the curved supertargets reviewed recently in Refs.
[
Sus20
]
, with emphasis on the İnönü–Wigner contractibility of Refs.
[
Sus18a
,
Sus18b
]
as an additional organising principle (in the spirit of Fronsdal). Last but not least, and more fundamentally, it seems reasonable to think of the work reported hereabove as a natural springboard for a systematic investigation of cross-defect loop dynamics over nerves of (Lie) (super)groupoids of a more general kind than the ones encountered. We are hoping to return to all these issues in a future study.

Appendices

Appendix A
Conventions on mappings and pullbacks

Let
\,\mathcal{M}\,
and
\,\mathcal{N}_{A},\ A\in\{1,2\}\,
be supermanifolds, and let
\,f_{A}\ :\ \mathcal{M}\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}_{A}\,
be supermanifold mappings (morphisms). We then define a supermanifold mapping

\displaystyle(f_{1},f_{2})\ :\ \mathcal{M}\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}_{1}\times\mathcal{N}_{2}

as the unique one fixed by the conditions

\displaystyle{\rm pr}_{A}\circ(f_{1},f_{2})=f_{A}\,,\qquad A\in\{1,2\}\,,

written in terms of the canonical projections
\,{\rm pr}_{A}\ :\ \mathcal{N}_{1}\times\mathcal{N}_{2}\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}_{A}
.

Similarly, given supermanifolds
\,\mathcal{M}_{A},\ A\in\{1,2\}\,
and
\,\mathcal{N}_{B},\ B\in\{1,2\}\,
alongside supermanifold mappings
\,F_{C}\ :\ \mathcal{M}_{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}_{C},\ C\in\{1,2\}
, we define a supermanifold mapping

\displaystyle F_{1}\times F_{2}\ :\ \mathcal{M}_{1}\times\mathcal{M}_{2}\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}_{1}\times\mathcal{N}_{2}

as the unique one fixed by the conditions

\displaystyle{\rm pr}_{A}\circ(F_{1}\times F_{2})=F_{A}\circ{\rm pr}_{A}\,,\qquad A\in\{1,2\}\,.

Let, next,
\,\mathcal{M}_{A},\ A\in\{1,2\}\,
be supermanifolds, and let

\displaystyle\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}\mathcal{M}_{2}}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}\mathcal{M}_{2}\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{2}

be a surjective submersion. Given a map

\displaystyle f\ :\ \mathcal{M}_{1}\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{2}\,,

we shall employ, unless expressly stated otherwise, the model of the pullback of
\,{\mathsf{Y}}\mathcal{M}_{2}\,
over
\,\mathcal{M}_{1}\,
along
\,f\,
provided by the fibred product

\displaystyle f^{*}{\mathsf{Y}}\mathcal{M}_{2}=\mathcal{M}_{1}{}_{f}\hskip-1.0pt\times_{\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}\mathcal{M}_{2}}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathsf{Y}}\mathcal{M}_{2}

that is defined by the commutative diagram

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern \displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}_{1}\mathcal{M}\times_{\mathcal{M}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathsf{Y}}_{2}\mathcal{M}=\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}_{1}\mathcal{M}}^{*}{\mathsf{Y}}_{2}\mathcal{M}\,.

When fibring a given surjective submersion with itself, we shall use the by now standard notation

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}^{[n]}\mathcal{M}\equiv\underbrace{{\mathsf{Y}}\mathcal{M}\times_{\mathcal{M}}{\mathsf{Y}}\mathcal{M}\times_{\mathcal{M}}\cdots\times_{\mathcal{M}}{\mathsf{Y}}\mathcal{M}}_{n\ {\rm times}}\,.

8

We prove the first part of the thesis by induction with respect to the value
\,n\geq 3\,
of the family index. \,X_{n},\ n>1\,

incoming lines and a single outgoing line come together. The face maps

From these, we read off the expressions

\displaystyle\pi^{(N+1)}_{k,k+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d^{(2)}_{2}\circ d^{(3)}_{2}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(N-k+1)}_{2}\circ d^{(N-k+2)}_{0}\circ d^{(N-k+3)}_{0}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(N)}_{0}\,,\qquad k\in\overline{1,N}\,,

(B.2)

\displaystyle\pi^{(N+1)}_{1,N+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d^{(2)}_{1}\circ d^{(3)}_{1}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(N)}_{1}\,,

which coincide with those given in Eq. (
). They are instrumental in proving the induction step. Indeed, inspection of the face maps depicted in Fig.
10
for
\,n=N+1\,
in conjunction with the induction hypothesis reveals that there are precisely
\,N\,
ways to arrive at the
k
-th edge from the original graph of valence
\,(N+1,1)\,
with the help of the face maps
\,d^{(N+1)}_{i},\ i\in\overline{0,N+1}
, to wit through all possible fusions of adjacent edges which do
not
involve the
k
-th edge, each fusion being followed by the application of the
unique
map
\,X_{N}\longrightarrow X_{1}\,
that maps the graph with a single ‘virtual’ edge to its ‘real’ edge with index
\,k
. Thus, we obtain the morphisms
\,\pi^{(N+1)}_{k-1,k}\circ d^{(N+1)}_{l},\ l\in\overline{0,k-2}
, represented by fusion of edges with indices smaller than
\,k
, and the morphisms
\,\pi^{(N+1)}_{k,k+1}\circ d^{(N+1)}_{m},\ m\in\overline{k+1,N+1}
, represented by fusion of edges with indices greater than
\,k
. At this stage, it suffices to check that all these morphisms are pairwise equal, which follows straightforwardly from the simplicial identities of Def.
6
. Indeed, on the one hand,

(B.4)

\displaystyle\pi^{(N+1)}_{k-1,k}\circ d^{(N+1)}_{l}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle d^{(2)}_{2}\circ d^{(3)}_{2}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(N-(k-1)+1)}_{2}\circ d^{(N-(k-1)+2)}_{0}\circ d^{(N-(k-1)+3)}_{0}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(N)}_{0}\circ d^{(N+1)}_{l}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d^{(2)}_{2}\circ d^{(3)}_{2}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(N-k+2)}_{2}\circ d^{(N-k+3)}_{0}\circ d^{(N-k+4)}_{0}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(N)}_{0}\circ d^{(N+1)}_{0}

as
\,l-(N-(N-(k-1)+1))=l-(k-2)\leq 0
, and, on the other hand,

(B.6)

\displaystyle\pi^{(N+1)}_{k,k+1}\circ d^{(N+1)}_{m}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle d^{(2)}_{2}\circ d^{(3)}_{2}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(N-k+1)}_{2}\circ d^{(N-k+2)}_{0}\circ d^{(N-k+3)}_{0}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(N)}_{0}\circ d^{(N+1)}_{m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d^{(2)}_{2}\circ d^{(3)}_{2}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(N-k+1)}_{2}\circ d^{(N-k+2)}_{m-(k-1)}\circ d^{(N-k+3)}_{0}\circ d^{(N-k+4)}_{0}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(N+1)}_{0}

(B.8)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d^{(2)}_{2}\circ d^{(3)}_{2}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(N-k+2)}_{2}\circ d^{(N-k+3)}_{0}\circ d^{(N-k+4)}_{0}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(N)}_{0}\circ d^{(N+1)}_{0}

as
\,m-(k-1)-(N-k+1-1)=m+1-N\leq 2
. We treat the remaining composite morphisms that send
\,X_{N+1}\,
to
\,X_{1}\,
in a way represented by the assignment of the distinguished ‘out-going’ edge with the index
\,0\,
to the junction of valence
\,(N+1,1)\,
analogously: Inspecting Fig.
10
once more, we infer that the
\,N\,
paths to the edge begin with the moves representing the face maps
\,d^{(N+1)}_{n},\ n\in\overline{1,N}
, and so it remains to verify the equality of all morphisms of the form

\displaystyle\pi^{(N+1)}_{1,N+1}\circ d^{(N+1)}_{n}\equiv d^{(2)}_{1}\circ d^{(3)}_{1}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(N)}_{1}\circ d^{(N+1)}_{n}=d^{(2)}_{1}\circ d^{(3)}_{1}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(N+1)}_{1}\,,

which follows from
\,n-(N-1)\leq N-(N-1)=1
. This concludes the proof of the first part of the proposition.

The second part is proved through direct calculation using the simplicial identities for the face maps and the convenient presentation (
),

(B.10)

\displaystyle d^{(1)}_{0}\circ\pi^{(n+1)}_{k-1,k}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle d^{(1)}_{0}\circ d^{(2)}_{2}\circ d^{(3)}_{2}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(N-(k-1)+1)}_{2}\circ d^{(N-(k-1)+2)}_{0}\circ d^{(N-(k-1)+3)}_{0}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(N)}_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d^{(1)}_{1}\circ d^{(2)}_{0}\circ d^{(3)}_{2}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(N-(k-1)+1)}_{2}\circ d^{(N-(k-1)+2)}_{0}\circ d^{(N-(k-1)+3)}_{0}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(N)}_{0}

(B.12)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ldots=d^{(1)}_{1}\circ d^{(2)}_{1}\circ d^{(3)}_{1}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(N-(k-1))}_{1}\circ d^{(N-(k-1)+1)}_{0}\circ d^{(N-(k-1)+2)}_{0}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(N)}_{0}

(B.14)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d^{(1)}_{1}\circ d^{(2)}_{1}\circ d^{(3)}_{1}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(N-(k-1)-1)}_{1}\circ d^{(N-(k-1))}_{2}\circ d^{(N-(k-1)+1)}_{0}\circ d^{(N-(k-1)+2)}_{0}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(N)}_{0}

(B.16)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ldots=d^{(1)}_{1}\circ d^{(2)}_{2}\circ d^{(3)}_{2}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(N-(k-1))}_{2}\circ d^{(N-(k-1)+2)}_{0}\circ d^{(N-(k-1)+3)}_{0}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(N)}_{0}

(B.18)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle d^{(1)}_{1}\circ\pi^{(n+1)}_{k,k+1}\,,\qquad k\in\overline{2,n}\,,

(B.21)

\displaystyle d^{(1)}_{1}\circ\pi^{(n+1)}_{1,2}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle d^{(1)}_{1}\circ d^{(2)}_{2}\circ d^{(3)}_{2}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(n)}_{2}=d^{(1)}_{1}\circ d^{(2)}_{1}\circ d^{(3)}_{2}\circ d^{(4)}_{2}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(n)}_{2}=\ldots=d^{(1)}_{1}\circ d^{(2)}_{1}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(n)}_{1}

(B.23)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle d^{(1)}_{1}\circ\pi^{(n+1)}_{1,n+1}\,,

(B.26)

\displaystyle d^{(1)}_{0}\circ\pi^{(n+1)}_{1,n+1}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle d^{(1)}_{0}\circ d^{(2)}_{1}\circ d^{(3)}_{1}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(n)}_{1}=d^{(1)}_{0}\circ d^{(2)}_{0}\circ d^{(3)}_{1}\circ d^{(4)}_{1}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(n)}_{1}=\ldots=d^{(1)}_{0}\circ d^{(2)}_{0}\circ\cdots\circ d^{(n)}_{0}

(B.28)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle d^{(1)}_{0}\circ\pi^{(n+1)}_{n,n+1}\,.

The identities involving the degeneracy maps are proven similarly.
∎

Appendix C
A proof of Proposition
30

In this appendix, we prove the uniqueness of the
\ell\wp^{\partial}
-invariant curvature
\,\omega_{\partial}^{({\mathsf{k}})}\,
of the maximally symmetric WZW D-brane. In view of the
{\mathsf{L}}{\rm G}\times{\mathsf{L}}{\rm G}
-covariance of the equations of motion, it suffices to ensure that the DGC (
1.29
) written for the (component) D-brane worldvolume
\,\mathscr{C}_{\lambda}\,
(and a 2-form
\,\omega_{\partial,\lambda}^{({\mathsf{k}})}\equiv\omega_{\partial}^{({\mathsf{k}})}|_{\mathscr{C}_{\lambda}}\in\Omega^{2}(\mathscr{C}_{\lambda})
, the latter assumed
{\rm Ad}_{\cdot}
-invariant) is satisfied by the
{\mathsf{L}}{\rm G}
-translate
\,{}^{\textrm{\tiny$h$}}\hskip-1.0ptg\,
of an arbitrary field configuration
\,g\in\mathscr{F}(\Sigma,d;\eta)
. To this end, we cast the DGC in a manageable form. First of all, we readily establish – for
\,{\rm e}_{\lambda}={\rm e}^{\frac{2\pi{\mathsf{i}}\,\lambda}{{\mathsf{k}}}}\,
and an arbitrary element
\,x\in{\rm G}\,
– the identity

(C.1)

\displaystyle\theta_{\rm L}\bigl({\rm Ad}_{x}({\rm e}_{\lambda})\bigr)=\bigl({\rm id}_{\mathfrak{g}}-{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{{\rm Ad}_{x}({\rm e}_{\lambda})}\bigr)\circ{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{{\rm Ad}_{x}({\rm e}_{\lambda})^{-1}}\circ\theta_{\rm R}(x)\,,

written in terms of the right-invariant (
\mathfrak{g}
-valued) Maurer–Cartan 1-form
\,\theta_{\rm R}
, which shows that it is meaningful to invert the operator

\displaystyle O:={\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{\cdot}\circ{\rm Inv}-{\rm id}_{\mathfrak{g}}\in C^{\infty}\bigl({\rm G},{\rm End}\,\mathfrak{g}\bigr)

on the
\theta_{\rm L}
-image of
\,{\mathsf{T}}\mathscr{C}_{\lambda}
, and so also on all of
\,\Omega^{1}(\mathscr{C}_{\lambda})
. Accordingly, we may write the D-brane curvature in the form

\displaystyle\omega_{\partial,\lambda}^{({\mathsf{k}})}=\tfrac{{\mathsf{k}}}{8\pi}\,{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\bigl(\Delta\circ\theta_{\rm L}\wedge O^{-1}\circ\theta_{\rm L}\bigr)

for some
\,\Delta\in C^{\infty}(\mathscr{C}_{\lambda},{\rm End}\,\mathfrak{g})\,
to be established in what follows, and we may further assume that the operators satisfy the identity

(C.2)

\displaystyle\Delta_{g}^{\dagger}\circ O_{g}^{-1}=-O_{g^{-1}}^{-1}\circ\Delta_{g}\,,

written in terms of the conjugate
\,\Delta_{g}^{\dagger}\,
of
\,\Delta_{g}\,
with respect to the scalar product
\,(X|Y)={\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,(X\,Y)\,
on
\,\mathfrak{g}\,
and encoding the skew symmetry of
\,\omega_{\partial,\lambda}
. Consider the vector fields

(C.3)

\displaystyle V_{A}(\cdot)=R_{A}(\cdot)-L_{A}(\cdot)\equiv O_{\cdot\,A}^{\ \ B}\,L_{B}(\cdot)\,,\qquad A\in\overline{1,{\rm dim}\,\mathfrak{g}}\,,

written in terms of the left- (
L_{A}
) and right-invariant vector fields on
\,{\rm G}\,
dual to the left- and right-invariant component Maurer–Cartan 1-forms, respectively,
\,L_{A}\righthalfcup\theta_{\rm L}^{B}=\delta_{A}^{\ B}=R_{A}\righthalfcup\theta_{\rm R}^{B}
,. The above are the fundamental vector fields for the adjoint action of the group on
\,\mathscr{C}_{\lambda}
, and so they generate the tangent bundle over
\,\mathscr{C}_{\lambda}
. For the choice

(C.4)

\displaystyle t=\partial_{2}\,,\qquad\qquad\widetilde{N}t=\partial_{1}\,,

and in the notation (
4.5
), we now obtain the result (in which we have dropped the obvious dependence on
\,p\in\partial\Sigma
)

\displaystyle{\rm g}_{g}^{({\mathsf{k}})}\left(\iota_{1\,*}V_{A},g_{|1\,*}\widetilde{N}t\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\tfrac{{\mathsf{k}}}{4\pi}\,{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\left(t_{A}\,O_{g^{-1}}\left(g^{-1}\partial_{1}g\right)\right)\,,

(C.6)

\displaystyle\omega_{\partial,\lambda\,g}^{({\mathsf{k}})}\left(V_{A},g_{*}t\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\tfrac{{\mathsf{k}}}{4\pi}\,{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\left(t_{A}\,\Delta_{g}\left(g^{-1}\,\partial_{2}g\right)\right)\,.

Thus, in the end, the DGC reduces to

\displaystyle\Delta_{g}\left(g^{-1}\,\partial_{2}g\right)=-O_{g^{-1}}\left(g^{-1}\partial_{1}g\right)\,.

Next, we consider a loop-group transformation (
4.12
) of the
\sigma
-model field and restrict it to the boundary of
\,\Sigma
, that is to the locus of the equation
\,\sigma^{1}=0
. We have (in the obvious shorthand notation)

\displaystyle{}^{\textrm{\tiny$h$}}\hskip-2.0ptg^{-1}\partial_{1}{}^{\textrm{\tiny$h$}}\hskip-2.0ptg(0,\sigma^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{h}\left(g^{-1}\partial_{1}g\right)(\sigma^{2})+\left({\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{h\cdot g^{-1}}+{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{h}\right)\left(h^{-1}\,\partial_{2}h\right)(\sigma^{2})\,,

(C.8)

\displaystyle{}^{\textrm{\tiny$h$}}\hskip-2.0ptg^{-1}\partial_{2}{}^{\textrm{\tiny$h$}}\hskip-2.0ptg(0,\sigma^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{h}\left(g^{-1}\partial_{2}g\right)(\sigma^{2})+\left({\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{h\cdot g^{-1}}-{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{h}\right)\left(h^{-1}\,\partial_{2}h\right)(\sigma^{2})\,,

and so the DGC for the transformed field (whose validity we assume as a constraint on
\,\omega_{\partial,\lambda}
) reads

(C.10)

\displaystyle\Delta_{{}^{\textrm{\tiny$h$}}\hskip-2.0ptg}\circ{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{h}\left(g^{-1}\,\partial_{2}g\right)+\Delta_{{}^{\textrm{\tiny$h$}}\hskip-2.0ptg}\circ\left({\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{h\cdot g^{-1}}-{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{h}\right)\left(h^{-1}\,\partial_{2}h\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{h}\circ({\rm id}_{\mathfrak{g}}-{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{g})\left(g^{-1}\partial_{1}g\right)+\left({\rm id}_{\mathfrak{g}}-{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{{\rm Ad}_{h}(g)}\right)\circ\left({\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{h\cdot g^{-1}}+{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{h}\right)\left(h^{-1}\,\partial_{2}h\right)\,.

The first terms on either side of the above equation cancel out in virtue of the assumed
{\rm Ad}
-invariance of
\,\omega_{\partial,\lambda}
, taken in conjunction with the DGC for
\,g
. Indeed, for a
constant
loop
\,h
, the said
{\rm Ad}
-invariance yields the equality

\displaystyle\tfrac{{\mathsf{k}}}{8\pi}\,{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\bigl({\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{h^{-1}}\circ\Delta_{{}^{\textrm{\tiny$h$}}\hskip-2.0ptg}\circ{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{h}\circ\theta_{\rm L}(g)\wedge O^{-1}_{g}\circ\theta_{\rm L}(g)\bigr)\equiv\omega_{\partial,\lambda\,{}^{\textrm{\tiny$h$}}\hskip-2.0ptg}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{=}}\omega_{\partial,\lambda\,g}\equiv

\displaystyle\tfrac{{\mathsf{k}}}{8\pi}\,{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\bigl(\Delta_{g}\circ\theta_{\rm L}(g)\wedge O^{-1}_{g}\circ\theta_{\rm L}(g)\bigr)\,,

and so upon contracting both sides of the above with
\,V_{A}
, we end up with the condition

\displaystyle{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{h^{-1}}\circ\Delta_{{}^{\textrm{\tiny$h$}}\hskip-2.0ptg}\circ{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{h}\circ\theta_{\rm L}(g)=\Delta_{g}\circ\theta_{\rm L}(g)\,.

The said cancellation now follows from the last relation upon evaluating both sides on the vector
\,g(0,\cdot)_{*}\partial_{2}(\sigma^{2})
, tangent to
\,\mathscr{C}_{\lambda}\,
at
\,g(0,\sigma^{2})
.

Thus, we are left with the condition

\displaystyle\Delta_{{}^{\textrm{\tiny$h$}}\hskip-2.0ptg}\circ\left({\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{h\cdot g^{-1}}-{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{h}\right)\left(h^{-1}\,\partial_{2}h\right)=\left({\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{h\cdot g^{-1}}-{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{h\cdot g}\right)\left(h^{-1}\,\partial_{2}h\right)\,,

which can be further rewritten as

\displaystyle\Delta_{{}^{\textrm{\tiny$h$}}\hskip-2.0ptg}\left({}^{\textrm{\tiny$h$}}\hskip-2.0ptg^{-1}\,\partial_{2}{}^{\textrm{\tiny$h$}}\hskip-2.0ptg-{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{h}\left(g^{-1}\,\partial_{2}g\right)\right)={}^{\textrm{\tiny$h$}}\hskip-2.0ptg^{-1}\,\partial_{2}{}^{\textrm{\tiny$h$}}\hskip-2.0ptg+\left(\partial_{2}{}^{\textrm{\tiny$h$}}\hskip-2.0ptg\right)\,{}^{\textrm{\tiny$h$}}\hskip-2.0ptg^{-1}-{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{h}\left(g^{-1}\,\partial_{2}g+\left(\partial_{2}g\right)\,g^{-1}\right)\,.

In the last step of the argument, we restrict our attention to constant boundary loops
\,g(0,\cdot)=:g_{*}\in\mathscr{C}_{\lambda}
. These are, clearly, always admissible as boundary conditions for classical field configurations of the
\sigma
-model in hand
37
37

37

Take,
e.g.
, the constant map
\,g(\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2})=g_{*}\,
as a classical field configuration.
. For these, the last condition takes the simple form

\displaystyle\Delta_{{}^{\textrm{\tiny$h$}}\hskip-2.0ptg_{*}}\left({}^{\textrm{\tiny$h$}}\hskip-2.0ptg_{*}^{-1}\,\partial_{2}{}^{\textrm{\tiny$h$}}\hskip-2.0ptg_{*}\right)={}^{\textrm{\tiny$h$}}\hskip-2.0ptg_{*}^{-1}\,\partial_{2}{}^{\textrm{\tiny$h$}}\hskip-2.0ptg_{*}+\left(\partial_{2}{}^{\textrm{\tiny$h$}}\hskip-2.0ptg_{*}\right)\,{}^{\textrm{\tiny$h$}}\hskip-2.0ptg_{*}^{-1}\,.

In view of the arbitrariness of both
\,h\,
and
\,g_{*}
, we recognise in
\,{}^{\textrm{\tiny$h$}}\hskip-2.0ptg_{*}^{-1}\,\partial_{2}{}^{\textrm{\tiny$h$}}\hskip-2.0ptg_{*}\,
a generic vector tangent to the conjugacy class
\,\mathscr{C}_{\lambda}\,
at its arbitrary point
\,g_{*}
. Therefore, we may eventually cast the condition in the form

\displaystyle\Delta_{y}\left(y^{-1}\,\partial y\right)=y^{-1}\,\partial y+(\partial y)\,y^{-1}\,,

with, now, an arbitrary loop
\,y\in{\mathsf{L}}\mathscr{C}_{\lambda}
. We conclude that
\,\Delta\,
and, hence, also
\,\omega_{\partial,\lambda}^{({\mathsf{k}})}\,
are unique if they exist. The latter fact can be verified explicitly by checking that the 2-form
\,\omega_{\partial,\lambda}^{({\mathsf{k}})}\,
of Eq. (
4.14
) is
{\rm Ad}_{\cdot}
-invariant, satisfies (
C.2
) and the condition

(C.11)

\displaystyle{\rm H}_{\rm C}^{({\mathsf{k}})}|_{\mathscr{C}_{\lambda}}={\mathsf{d}}\omega_{\partial,\lambda}^{({\mathsf{k}})}\,.

Indeed, using Eq. (
C.1
) alongside the obvious identities
\,{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\bigl(\theta_{\rm L}\wedge\theta_{\rm L}\bigr)=0={\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\bigl(\theta_{\rm R}\wedge\theta_{\rm R}\bigr)\,
we readily verify the following chain of equalities:

(C.13)

\displaystyle{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\bigg(\theta_{\rm L}\bigl({\rm Ad}_{x}({\rm e}_{\lambda})\bigr)\wedge\tfrac{{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{{\rm Ad}_{x}({\rm e}_{\lambda})}}{{\rm id}_{\mathfrak{g}}-{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{{\rm Ad}_{x}({\rm e}_{\lambda})}}\circ\theta_{\rm L}\bigl({\rm Ad}_{x}({\rm e}_{\lambda})\bigr)\bigg)={\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\bigl(\theta_{\rm R}(x)\wedge{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{{\rm Ad}_{x}({\rm e}_{\lambda})}\circ\theta_{\rm R}(x)\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\bigg(\theta_{\rm L}\bigl({\rm Ad}_{x}({\rm e}_{\lambda})\bigr)\wedge\tfrac{{\rm id}_{\mathfrak{g}}}{{\rm id}_{\mathfrak{g}}-{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{{\rm Ad}_{x}({\rm e}_{\lambda})}}\circ\theta_{\rm L}\bigl({\rm Ad}_{x}({\rm e}_{\lambda})\bigr)\bigg)\,,

and so we find

(C.14)

\displaystyle\omega_{\lambda}\bigl({\rm Ad}_{x}({\rm e}_{\lambda})\bigr)=\tfrac{{\mathsf{k}}}{4\pi}\,{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\bigl(\theta_{\rm L}(x)\wedge{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{{\rm e}_{\lambda}}\circ\theta_{\rm L}(x)\bigr)\,.

The
{\rm Ad}_{\cdot}
-invariance of
\,\omega_{\partial,\lambda}\,
is now apparent as an immediate consequence of the invariance of the Maurer–Cartan 1-form under left translations.

More generally, we could write, for any fixed element
\,g_{0}\in\mathscr{C}_{\lambda}
,

(C.15)

\displaystyle\omega_{\partial,\lambda}^{({\mathsf{k}})}\bigl({\rm Ad}_{x}(g_{0})\bigr)=\tfrac{{\mathsf{k}}}{4\pi}\,{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\bigl(\theta_{\rm L}(x)\wedge{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{g_{0}}\circ\theta_{\rm L}(x)\bigr)\,.

Upon recalling the Maurer–Cartan equation
\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta_{\rm L}=-\theta_{\rm L}\wedge\theta_{\rm L}
, it becomes completely straightforward to check the identity

\displaystyle{\mathsf{d}}\omega_{\partial,\lambda}^{({\mathsf{k}})}\bigl({\rm Ad}_{x}(g_{0})\bigr)=\tfrac{{\mathsf{k}}}{4\pi}\,{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\bigl(\theta_{\rm L}(x)\wedge\theta_{\rm L}(x)\wedge\bigl({\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{g_{0}^{-1}}-{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{g_{0}}\bigr)\circ\theta_{\rm L}(x)\bigr)={\rm H}_{\rm C}^{({\mathsf{k}})}\bigl({\rm Ad}_{x}(g_{0})\bigr)\,,

which proves Eq. (
C.11
).

Finally, note that under the identification
\,\Delta={\rm id}_{\mathfrak{g}}+{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{\cdot}
, the DGC translates into the desired gluing condition (
4.13
) for the chiral Kač–Moody currents.

We have just proved that imposition of the symmetry constraints determines the D-brane curvature uniquely. The converse implication of the proposition has long been known to hold true,
cf.
,
e.g.
, Ref.
[
GTTNB04
]
.∎

Appendix D
A proof of Proposition
32

Reasoning in analogy with App.
C
, we verify the uniqueness of the curvature 2-form by exploiting the constraint that an
{\mathsf{L}}{\rm G}\times{\mathsf{L}}{\rm G}
-translate of (an extension of) an arbitrary element
\,\left(g_{\rm b},(g,h)\right)\in\mathcal{F}_{({\rm eom})}(\Sigma,d;\eta)\,
should satisfy the DGC (
4.1
) and that the chiral Kač–Moody currents should be continuous across the defect. We begin by decomposing, for the sake of the simplicity of the ensuing analysis, the restriction
\,\omega_{\lambda}^{({\mathsf{k}})}\equiv\omega^{({\mathsf{k}})}|_{Q_{\lambda}}\,
of the sought-after 2-form as

\displaystyle\omega_{\lambda}^{({\mathsf{k}})}=\tfrac{{\mathsf{k}}}{8\pi}\,{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\left(\widetilde{\omega}{}^{1}\circ{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\theta_{\rm R}\wedge{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\theta_{\rm R}+2\widetilde{\omega}{}^{2}\circ{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\theta_{\rm L}\wedge{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\theta_{\rm R}+\widetilde{\Delta}\circ{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\theta_{\rm L}\wedge{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\bigl(O^{-1}\circ\theta_{\rm L}\bigr)\right)

in terms of some linear operators
\,\widetilde{\Delta},\widetilde{\omega}{}^{k}\equiv\omega^{k}_{AB}\,\tau^{A}\otimes t_{B}\in C^{\infty}\bigl(Q_{\lambda},{\rm End}\,\mathfrak{g}\bigr),\ k\in\{1,2\}\,
to be determined (
\{\tau^{A}\}^{A\in\overline{1,{\rm dim}\,\mathfrak{g}}}\,
is the basis of
\,\mathfrak{g}\,
dual to
\,\{t_{A}\}_{A\in\overline{1,{\rm dim}\,\mathfrak{g}}}
), further assuming
\,\widetilde{\omega}{}_{(g,h)}^{1\,\dagger}=-\widetilde{\omega}{}_{(g,h)}^{1}\,
and
\,\widetilde{\Delta}{}_{(g,h)}^{\dagger}\circ O_{h}^{-1}=-O_{h^{-1}}^{-1}\circ\widetilde{\Delta}{}_{(g,h)}\,
(
cf.
App.
C
). In writing the last term, we implicitly invoke the same argument as in the boundary case. The tangent space of the
{\rm G}\times{\rm G}
-homogeneous space
\,Q_{\lambda}\equiv{\rm G}\times\mathscr{C}_{\lambda}\,
at a given point
\,(g,h)\,
is spanned by two types of vectors:

(D.1)

\displaystyle X_{A}(g,h)=R_{A}(g)\,,\qquad\qquad Y_{A}(g,h)=-L_{A}(g)+V_{A}(h)\,,

the latter being written in terms of the fundamental vector fields (
C.3
) and jointly generating the action
\,\ell\wp^{(1)}
. This results in the emergence of two independent (
\mathfrak{g}
-valued) DGCs that constrain the bi-brane curvature. Indeed, having made the choice (
C.4
) once more, we find the DGC:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\omega}{}^{1}_{(g,h)}\left((\partial_{2}g)\,g^{-1}\right)-{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{g}\circ\widetilde{\omega}{}^{2\,\dagger}_{(g,h)}\left((\partial_{2}h)\,h^{-1}\right)=\bigl(\partial_{1}(g\cdot h)\bigr)\,(g\cdot h)^{-1}-(\partial_{1}g)\,g^{-1}

for
\,V=X_{A}
, and another one:

(D.3)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{g^{-1}}\circ\widetilde{\omega}{}^{1}_{(g,h)}\left((\partial_{2}g)\,g^{-1}\right)-\widetilde{\omega}{}^{2\,\dagger}_{(g,h)}\left((\partial_{2}h)\,h^{-1}\right)+O_{h}\circ\widetilde{\omega}{}^{2}_{(g,h)}\left(g^{-1}\partial_{2}g\right)-\widetilde{\Delta}{}_{(g,h)}\left(h^{-1}\partial_{2}h\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(g\cdot h)^{-1}\partial_{1}(g\cdot h)-g^{-1}\,\partial_{1}g

for
\,V=Y_{A}
.

Let us, to begin with, consider loop-group transformations of the type
\,(g,h)(0,\sigma^{2})\longmapsto(x(\sigma^{+})\cdot g(0,\sigma^{2}),h(0,\sigma^{2}))|_{\sigma^{1}=0}
. Under these, the two DGCs map to

\displaystyle\widetilde{\omega}{}^{1}_{(x\cdot g,h)}\circ{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{x}\left((\partial_{2}g)\,g^{-1}\right)-{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{x\cdot g}\circ\widetilde{\omega}{}^{2\,\dagger}_{(x\cdot g,h)}\left((\partial_{2}h)\,h^{-1}\right)+\widetilde{\omega}{}^{1}_{(x\cdot g,h)}\left((\partial_{2}x)\,x^{-1}\right)={\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{x\cdot g}\bigl((\partial_{1}h)\,h^{-1}\bigr)

and

(D.5)

respectively. We can subsequently use the assumed

These, in conjunction with the original DGCs, enable us to drop all terms with derivatives of
\,g\,
and
\,h\,
from the transformed DGCs, whereby we obtain

\displaystyle\widetilde{\omega}{}^{1}_{(x\cdot g,h)}\left((\partial_{2}x)\,x^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0\,,

(D.11)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{(x\cdot g)^{-1}}\circ\widetilde{\omega}{}^{1}_{(x\cdot g,h)}\left((\partial_{2}x)\,x^{-1}\right)+O_{h}\circ\widetilde{\omega}{}^{2}_{(x\cdot g,h)}\circ{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{g^{-1}}\left(x^{-1}\,\partial_{2}x\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{h}\circ{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{g^{-1}}\bigl(x^{-1}\partial_{2}x\bigr)\,.

Let us focus on the first relation. Setting
\,{}^{\textrm{\tiny$x$}}\hskip-2.0ptg:=x\cdot g
, we rewrite it as

\displaystyle\widetilde{\omega}{}^{1}_{\left({}^{\textrm{\tiny$x$}}\hskip-2.0ptg,h\right)}\left(\left(\partial_{2}{}^{\textrm{\tiny$x$}}\hskip-2.0ptg\right)\,{}^{\textrm{\tiny$x$}}\hskip-2.0ptg^{-1}-{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{x}\left((\partial_{2}g)\,g^{-1}\right)\right)=0\,,

and so, for an arbitrary constant loop
\,g(0,\cdot)=:g_{*}\in{\rm G}
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\omega}{}^{1}_{\left({}^{\textrm{\tiny$x$}}\hskip-2.0ptg_{*},h\right)}\left(\left(\partial_{2}{}^{\textrm{\tiny$x$}}\hskip-2.0ptg_{*}\right)\,{}^{\textrm{\tiny$x$}}\hskip-2.0ptg_{*}^{-1}\right)=0\,,

from which we deduce

\displaystyle\widetilde{\omega}{}^{1}=0\,.

This simplifies the other relation, giving

\displaystyle O_{h}\circ\widetilde{\omega}{}^{2}_{(x\cdot g,h)}\circ{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{g^{-1}}\left(x^{-1}\,\partial_{2}x\right)=O_{h}\circ{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{g^{-1}}\bigl(x^{-1}\partial_{2}x\bigr)\,,

which we next subject to the same operations, to the following effect:

\displaystyle O_{h}\circ\left(\widetilde{\omega}{}^{2}_{\left({}^{\textrm{\tiny$x$}}\hskip-2.0ptg_{*},h\right)}-{\rm id}_{\mathfrak{g}}\right)\left({}^{\textrm{\tiny$x$}}\hskip-2.0ptg_{*}^{-1}\,\partial_{2}{}^{\textrm{\tiny$x$}}\hskip-2.0ptg_{*}\right)=0\,,

whence
38
38

38

Recall that the tangent space
\,{\mathsf{T}}_{h}\mathscr{C}_{\lambda}\,
is spanned by the vectors
\,V_{A}(h)
.
also

\displaystyle\omega_{\lambda}^{({\mathsf{k}})}=\tfrac{{\mathsf{k}}}{4\pi}\,{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\left({\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\theta_{\rm L}\wedge{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\theta_{\rm R}\right)+\tfrac{{\mathsf{k}}}{8\pi}\,{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\left(\widetilde{\Delta}\circ{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\theta_{\rm L}\wedge{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\bigl(O^{-1}\circ\theta_{\rm L}\bigr)\right)\,.

The DGCs reduce accordingly,

\displaystyle\bigl(\partial_{+}(g\cdot h)\bigr)\,(g\cdot h)^{-1}-(\partial_{+}g)\,g^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0\,,

(D.13)

\displaystyle(g\cdot h)^{-1}\partial_{-}(g\cdot h)-g^{-1}\,\partial_{-}g

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigl({\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{h}+{\rm id}_{\mathfrak{g}}\bigr)\bigl(h^{-1}\,\partial_{2}h\bigr)+\widetilde{\Delta}{}_{(g,h)}\left(h^{-1}\partial_{2}h\right)\,,

and we readily recognise the continuity equation for the left-handed chiral current in the first of them, and that for the right-handed current in the first summand on the left-hand side of the second one. Taking both into account, we end up with the single constraint

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Delta}{}_{(g,h)}\left(h^{-1}\partial_{-}h\right)=-\bigl({\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{h}+{\rm id}_{\mathfrak{g}}\bigr)\bigl(h^{-1}\,\partial_{-}h\bigr)\,.

In view of the arbitrariness of
\,h
, this determines
\,\widetilde{\Delta}
, and so also
\,\omega_{\lambda}^{({\mathsf{k}})}
.

Having fixed all components of
\,\omega
, we proceed to verify that no additional consequences arise from imposition of the requirement of invariance of the DGCs under loop-group transformations of the second type:
\,(g,h)(0,\sigma^{2})\longmapsto(g(0,\sigma^{2})\cdot y(\sigma^{-})^{-1},y(\sigma^{-})\cdot h(0,\sigma^{2})\cdot y(\sigma^{-})^{-1})|_{\sigma^{1}=0}
. This boils down to checking invariance of both continuity equations for the chiral Kač–Moody currents under these loop-group transformations, a task readily accomplished through direct calculation.

In the next step, we check that the 2-form
\,\omega_{\lambda}^{({\mathsf{k}})}\,
given in Eq. (
4.25
) (and derived above) has all the required properties. Thus, we first consider the identity
\,{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}{\rm H}_{\rm C}^{({\mathsf{k}})}-{\rm m}^{*}{\rm H}_{\rm C}^{({\mathsf{k}})}={\mathsf{d}}\omega^{({\mathsf{k}})}
. That it holds true is a simple corollary of the previous result (
C.11
) and of the relation
\,{\rm m}^{*}{\rm H}_{\rm C}^{({\mathsf{k}})}={\rm pr}_{1}^{*}{\rm H}_{\rm C}^{({\mathsf{k}})}+{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}{\rm H}_{\rm C}^{({\mathsf{k}})}-{\mathsf{d}}\varrho^{({\mathsf{k}})}\,
between 3-forms on
\,{\rm G}\times{\rm G}
. The last relation, known as the Polyakov–Wiegmann formula (
cf.
Ref.
[
PW84
]
), is easily checked in a direct computation.

Finally, we convince ourselves that
\,\omega_{\lambda}^{({\mathsf{k}})}\,
is also
\ell\wp^{(1)}
-invariant as claimed. As
\,\ell\wp^{(1)}\,
restricts to the adjoint action on the cartesian factor
\,\mathscr{C}_{\lambda}\,
of
\,Q_{\lambda}\,
from which the component
\,{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\omega_{\lambda}^{({\mathsf{k}})}\,
is pulled back, and the latter was proven
{\rm Ad}
-invariant in Appendix
C
, it remains to show that the 2-form
\,\varrho^{({\mathsf{k}})}\,
is
\ell\wp^{(1)}
-invariant. We leave this elementary exercise to the reader.∎

Appendix E
A proof of Proposition
34

We want to show that the 2-form

(E.1)

\displaystyle\Omega_{n}^{({\mathsf{k}})}=\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\,\bigl({\rm pr}_{i},{\rm m}_{i+1\,i+2\,\cdots\,n}^{(n)}\bigr)^{*}\varrho^{({\mathsf{k}})}+{\rm m}_{n}^{*}\omega_{\partial}^{({\mathsf{k}})}-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\,{\rm pr}_{j}^{*}\omega_{\partial}^{({\mathsf{k}})}\,,

is annihilated by vector fields on the manifold
\,\mathcal{T}_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{[\overrightarrow{w}]}\,
defined in
Eq. (
3.25
). Clearly, the tangent space
\,{\mathsf{T}}_{(h_{1},h_{2},\ldots,h_{n})}\mathcal{T}_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{[\overrightarrow{w}]}=\left\langle\mathscr{V}_{A}(h_{1},h_{2},\ldots,h_{n})\ |\ A\in{1,{\rm dim}\,{\rm G}}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{R}}\,
is spanned by the fundamental vector fields
\,\mathscr{V}_{A}(h_{1},h_{2},\ldots,h_{n})=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\,V_{A}(h_{j})\,
(
cf.
Eq. (
C.3
)) for the (diagonal) adjoint action of
\,{\rm G}\,
on
\,\mathcal{T}_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{[\overrightarrow{w}]}
, and so we have to verify the identities
\,\mathscr{V}_{A}\righthalfcup\Omega_{n}^{({\mathsf{k}})}(h_{1},h_{2},\ldots,h_{n})=0,\ A\in\overline{1,{\rm dim}\,{\rm G}}
. Denote
\,h_{k,k+1,\ldots,l}:=h_{k}\cdot h_{k+1}\cdots h_{l},\ 1\leq k\leq l\leq n
. Upon substituting the defining expressions for
\,\varrho^{({\mathsf{k}})}\,
and
\,\omega_{\partial}^{({\mathsf{k}})}
, we readily obtain

\displaystyle\tfrac{4\pi}{{\mathsf{k}}}\,\mathscr{V}_{A}\righthalfcup\Omega_{n}^{({\mathsf{k}})}(h_{1},h_{2},\ldots,h_{n})

(E.2)

(E.3)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\,\bigl(({\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{h_{i}}-{\rm id}_{\mathfrak{g}})\circ\theta_{\rm R}(h_{i+1,i+2,\ldots,n})+({\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{h_{i+1,i+2,\ldots,n}^{-1}}-{\rm id}_{\mathfrak{g}})\circ\theta_{\rm L}(h_{i})\bigr)

(E.4)

(E.5)

\displaystyle-({\rm id}_{\mathfrak{g}}+{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{h_{1,2,\ldots,n}})\circ\theta_{\rm L}(h_{1,2,\ldots,n})+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\,({\rm id}_{\mathfrak{g}}+{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{h_{j}})\circ\theta_{\rm L}(h_{j})

(E.6)

(E.7)
