The claim now follows from the identities

\displaystyle\theta_{\rm L}(h_{1,2,\ldots,n})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\,{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{h_{i+1,i+2,\ldots,n}^{-1}}\circ\theta_{\rm L}(h_{i})\,,\qquad\qquad\theta_{\rm R}(h_{1,2,\ldots,n})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\,{\mathsf{T}}_{e}{\rm Ad}_{h_{1,2,\ldots,i-1}}\circ\theta_{\rm R}(h_{i})\,,

easily checked through direct inspection.∎

Appendix F
A proof of Proposition
36

We shall be concerned with 2-forms on the manifold
\,\mathcal{T}_{\overrightarrow{\lambda}}^{\lambda}
. Let
\,\Omega_{n}^{({\mathsf{k}})}\,
and
\,\Omega_{\mathscr{F}_{0,n+1}}^{({\mathsf{k}})}\,
be the 2-forms defined in Eqs. (
4.31
) and (
4.42
), respectively.
Our goal in this appendix is to verify identity (
4.48
) for
\,\hbar\,
the isomorphism of Eq. (
4.47
). We begin by actually identifying the 2-form
\,\Omega_{\mathscr{F}_{0,n+1}}
, so far defined only implicitly. Using Eq. (
C.14
), we find

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathscr{F}_{0,n+1}}^{({\mathsf{k}})}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\,{\rm pr}_{i}^{*}\omega_{\partial}-{\rm pr}_{n+1}^{*}\omega_{\partial}+\hbar^{-1\,*}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\,\tfrac{{\mathsf{k}}}{4\pi}\,{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\bigl({\rm m}_{n+1-j\,n+2-j\,\cdots\,n}^{(n)\,*}\theta_{\rm L}\wedge{\rm m}_{n-j\,n+1-j\,\cdots\,n}^{(n)\,*}\theta_{\rm L}\bigr)\,,

and so, upon consulting Eqs. (
4.31
) and (
4.26
), we conclude that identity
(
4.48
) holds true iff

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\,{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\bigl({\rm m}_{n+1-j\,n+2-j\,\cdots\,n}^{(n)\,*}\theta_{\rm L}\wedge{\rm m}_{n-j\,n+1-j\,\cdots\,n}^{(n)\,*}\theta_{\rm L}\bigr)=-\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\,{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\bigl({\rm pr}_{j}^{*}\theta_{\rm L}\wedge{\rm m}_{j+1\,j+2\,\cdots\,n}^{(n)\,*}\theta_{\rm R}\bigr)\,.

The latter readily follows from the identity
\,{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}(\theta_{\rm L}\wedge\theta_{\rm L})=0\,
on noting that

\displaystyle{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\bigl({\rm m}_{n+1-j\,n+2-j\,\cdots\,n}^{(n)\,*}\theta_{\rm L}\wedge{\rm m}_{n-j\,n+1-j\,\cdots\,n}^{(n)\,*}\theta_{\rm L}\bigr)=-{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\bigl({\rm pr}_{n-j}^{*}\theta_{\rm L}\wedge{\rm m}_{n+1-j\,n+2-j\,\cdots\,n}^{*}\theta_{\rm R}\bigr)\,.

Indeed, we thus obtain

\displaystyle-\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\,{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\bigl({\rm pr}_{n-j}^{*}\theta_{\rm L}\wedge{\rm m}_{n+1-j\,n+2-j\,\cdots\,n}^{(n)\,*}\theta_{\rm R}\bigr)\equiv-\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\,{\rm tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\bigl({\rm pr}_{n-j}^{*}\theta_{\rm L}\wedge{\rm m}_{n+1-j\,n+2-j\,\cdots\,n}^{*}\theta_{\rm R}\bigr)\,,

which yields the desired result.∎

Appendix G
A proof of Theorem
47

Below, we provide an explicit geometrisation of the super-4-cocycle
\,\mathcal{Z}_{\rm GS}
. In so doing, we use the conventions of App.
A
. Thus, upon taking the surjective submersion of the pullback
\,f^{*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\,
of the GS super-1-gerbe along
\,f\ :\ {\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathbb{T}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{T}},\ f\in\{{\rm m},{\rm pr}_{1},{\rm pr}_{2}\}\,
in the form
\,{\mathsf{Y}}_{f}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 2}\equiv f^{*}{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}
, and with coordinates
\,(m_{1},m_{2},(f(m_{1},m_{2}),\xi))\in f^{*}{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}
, we readily obtain the surjective submersion of the tensor-product 1-gerbe
\,{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\otimes{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\,
given by
\,{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 2}={\rm pr}_{1}^{*}{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}\times_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 2}}\hskip-1.0pt{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}
, with the projection to the base
\,\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 2}}=\pi_{{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}}\circ{\rm pr}_{1}\equiv\pi_{{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}}\circ{\rm pr}_{2}\,
and coordinates (here, as before,
\,y_{A}\equiv(m_{A},\xi_{A})\equiv(\theta_{A},x_{A},\xi_{A}),\ A\in\{1,2\}
)
\,((m_{1},m_{2},y_{1}),(m_{1},m_{2},y_{2}))\equiv(\widetilde{y}{}_{1},\widetilde{y}{}_{2})\in{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 2}
. The tensor-product 1-gerbe
\,{\rm m}^{*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\varrho_{\rm GS}}
, on the other hand, has the surjective submersion
\,\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}_{\rm m}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 2}}\equiv\pi_{{\rm m}^{*}{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}_{\rm m}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 2}\equiv{\rm m}^{*}{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 2}
, and so the reconstruction of the 1-isomorphism
\,\mathcal{M}\,
starts with the erection of a principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-bundle
\,\pi_{E}\ :\ E\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2,{\rm m}}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 2}\,
over the fibred product
\,{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2,{\rm m}}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 2}=\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 2}}^{*}{\mathsf{Y}}_{\rm m}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 2}
, with coordinates
\,((m_{1},m_{2},y_{1}),(m_{1},m_{2},y_{2}),(m_{1},m_{2},(m_{1}\cdot m_{2},\xi_{3})))\equiv(\widetilde{y}{}_{1},\widetilde{y}{}_{2},\widetilde{y}{}_{\rm m})\equiv\widetilde{y}{}_{1,2,{\rm m}}\in{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2,{\rm m}}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 2}\,
and the surjective submersion
\,\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2,{\rm m}}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 2}}\equiv\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 2}}\circ{\rm pr}_{1}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2,{\rm m}}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 2}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 2}
. The principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-connection on that bundle is to have the curvature

\displaystyle{\rm F}_{E}(\widetilde{y}{}_{1,2,{\rm m}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm B}_{\rm GS}(\widetilde{y}{}_{\rm m})+\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}_{\rm m}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 2}}\varrho_{\rm GS}(\widetilde{y}{}_{\rm m})-{\rm B}_{\rm GS}(\widetilde{y}{}_{1})-{\rm B}_{\rm GS}(\widetilde{y}{}_{2})\,.

The above is de Rham-exact by construction, and so the cohomological triviality of the underlying supergeometry enables us to employ the homotopy retraction
\,(\tau,(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}))\longmapsto(\tau\,\theta_{1},\tau\,\theta_{2}),\ \tau\in[0,1]\,
of the Graßmann-odd fibre to recast it in the desired exact form

(G.2)

\displaystyle{\rm F}_{E}(\widetilde{y}{}_{1,2,{\rm m}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathsf{d}}\bigl[\theta_{1}^{\alpha}\,{\mathsf{d}}\xi^{31}_{\alpha}+\theta_{2}^{\alpha}\,{\mathsf{d}}\xi^{32}_{\alpha}-\eta_{ab}\,\bigl(x_{1}^{a}\,{\mathsf{d}}x_{2}^{b}-x_{2}^{a}\,{\mathsf{d}}x_{1}^{b}\bigr)

\displaystyle+\theta_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,\theta_{2}\,\bigl(e^{a}_{\rm L}(m_{1})+{\mathsf{d}}x_{2}^{a}+\tfrac{1}{6}\,\theta_{2}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,{\mathsf{d}}\theta_{2}+\tfrac{1}{2}\,\theta_{2}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,\sigma_{\rm L}(m_{1})\bigr)\bigr]\,.

Upon setting

\displaystyle E={\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2,{\rm m}}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 2}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}\ni(\widetilde{y}{}_{1,2,{\rm m}},z)\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\alpha_{E}\equiv{\boldsymbol{1}}\ :\ {\rm pr}_{1,4}^{*}\widehat{\rm pr}{}_{1}^{[2]*}L\otimes{\rm pr}_{2,5}^{*}\widehat{\rm pr}{}_{2}^{[2]*}L\otimes{\rm pr}_{4,5,6}^{*}E\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }{\rm pr}_{1,2,3}^{*}E\otimes{\rm pr}_{3,6}^{*}\widehat{\rm m}{}^{[2]*}L\,,

the isomorphism being manifestly coherent with the trivial groupoid structure

. We still need to check that it is also

-equivariant,

\displaystyle\bigl({\rm pr}_{1,2,3}^{*}E\ell^{(2)}_{(\varepsilon,t)}\otimes{\rm pr}_{3,6}^{*}\widehat{\rm m}{}^{[2]*}L\ell_{((\varepsilon,t,0),(\varepsilon,t,0),1)}\bigr)\circ\alpha_{E}

This follows directly from the identity

\displaystyle LE_{1,23}\ell^{(3)}_{(\varepsilon,t)}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}_{1,23}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}\circlearrowleft\ :\ \bigl(\widehat{y}{}_{1,23},z\bigr)\longmapsto\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}_{1,23}\ell_{(\varepsilon,t)}\bigl(\widehat{y}{}_{1,23}\bigr),{\rm e}^{{\mathsf{i}}\,\Phi^{1,23}_{(\varepsilon,t)}(\widehat{y}{}_{1,23})}\cdot z\bigr)

where

\displaystyle\Phi^{1,23}_{(\varepsilon,t)}\bigl(\widehat{y}{}_{1,23}\bigr)=\varepsilon^{\alpha}\,\xi_{71\,\alpha}-\eta_{ab}\,\bigl(t^{a}+\tfrac{1}{2}\,\varepsilon\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,\theta_{1}\bigr)\,\bigl(x_{2}^{b}+x_{3}^{b}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\theta_{2}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{b}\,\theta_{3}\bigr)+\tfrac{1}{6}\,\varepsilon\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,\bigl(2\theta_{1}+\theta_{2}+\theta_{3}\bigr)\cdot\theta_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,\bigl(\theta_{2}+\theta_{3}\bigr)

and

(G.20)

\displaystyle\Phi^{12,3}_{(\varepsilon,t)}\bigl(\widehat{y}{}_{12,3}\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\alpha}\,\xi_{71\,\alpha}-\eta_{ab}\,\bigl(t^{a}+\tfrac{1}{2}\,\varepsilon\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,\theta_{1}\bigr)\,x_{2}^{b}-\eta_{ab}\,\bigl(t^{a}+\tfrac{1}{2}\,\varepsilon\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,(\theta_{1}+\theta_{2})\bigr)\,x_{3}^{b}+\tfrac{1}{6}\,\varepsilon\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,\bigl(2\theta_{1}+\theta_{2}\bigr)\cdot\theta_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,\theta_{2}

\displaystyle+\tfrac{1}{6}\,\varepsilon\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,\bigl(2\theta_{1}+2\theta_{2}+\theta_{3}\bigr)\cdot(\theta_{1}+\theta_{2})\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,\theta_{3}\,.

Now, the point of departure in the construction of the 1-gerbe 2-isomorphism
\,\alpha\,
is the identification of the bases of
\,LE_{12,3}\,
and
\,LE_{1,23}\,
as total spaces of the respective surjective submersions:
\,{\rm pr}_{1,2,3,7}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}_{12,3}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3}\longrightarrow({\mathsf{Y}}_{1}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3}\times_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3}}{\mathsf{Y}}_{2}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3}\times_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3}}{\mathsf{Y}}_{3}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3})\times_{{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3}}{\mathsf{Y}}_{123}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3}\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2,3}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3}\,
and
\,{\rm pr}_{1,2,3,6}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}_{1,23}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2,3}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3}\,
over the surjective submersion
\,\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2,3}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3}}\equiv\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}_{123}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3}}\circ{\rm pr}_{4}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2,3}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3}
, the latter being a fibre product of the surjective submersions of the 1-gerbes
\,\mathcal{G}^{(3)}_{{\rm GS}\,1}\otimes\mathcal{G}^{(3)}_{{\rm GS}\,2}\otimes\mathcal{G}^{(3)}_{{\rm GS}\,3}\,
and
\,\mathcal{G}^{(3)}_{123}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{d_{3}^{(3)\,*}\varrho_{\rm GS}+d_{1}^{(3)\,*}\varrho_{\rm GS}}\,
related by the two 1-gerbe 1-isomorphisms that we consider above. Thus, in order to establish
\,\alpha
, we take the fibre product of the two surjective submersions over their common base,
\,{\mathsf{Y}}_{12,3\times 1,23}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3}={\mathsf{Y}}_{12,3}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3}\times_{{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,2,3}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathsf{Y}}_{1,23}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3}
, and, over it, look for an isomorphism of the pullback principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-bundles (with connection)
\,{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}LE_{12,3}={\rm pr}_{1}^{*}({\mathsf{Y}}_{12,3}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times})\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}_{12,3\times 1,23}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3}{}_{{\rm pr}_{1}}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{{\rm pr}_{1}}\hskip-1.0pt({\mathsf{Y}}_{12,3}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times})\,
and
\,{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}LE_{1,23}={\rm pr}_{2}^{*}({\mathsf{Y}}_{1,23}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times})\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}_{12,3\times 1,23}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3}{}_{{\rm pr}_{2}}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{{\rm pr}_{1}}\hskip-1.0pt({\mathsf{Y}}_{1,23}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times})
. In the light of the (de Rham-)cohomological triviality of the supergeometries involved, the existence of the isomorphism sought after is ensured by the vanishing of the exterior derivative of the difference of the base components of the two pullback connection super-1-forms,
\,{\mathsf{d}}({\mathsf{a}}_{12,3}-{\mathsf{a}}_{1,23})=0
. This we check in a direct computation using the definitions of the curvatures of
\,L\,
and
\,E\,
alongside that of
\,\vartheta_{\rm GS}
. At this stage, we merely have to extract the data of the isomorphism from the comparison between
\,{\mathsf{a}}_{1,23}\,
and
\,{\mathsf{a}}_{12,3}
. Assuming the isomorphism to have the form

(G.22)

\displaystyle\beta\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\ {\rm pr}_{1}^{*}LE_{12,3}\longrightarrow{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}LE_{1,23}

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\ \bigl(\bigl(\widehat{y}{}_{1},\widehat{y}{}_{2},\widehat{y}{}_{3},\widehat{y}{}_{123}^{2}\bigr),\bigl(\widehat{y}{}_{12,3},z\bigr)\bigr)\longmapsto(\bigl(\widehat{y}{}_{1},\widehat{y}{}_{2},\widehat{y}{}_{3},\widehat{y}{}_{123}^{2}\bigr),\bigl(\widehat{y}{}_{1,23},{\rm e}^{{\mathsf{i}}\,\Phi(\widehat{y}{}_{1},\widehat{y}{}_{2},\widehat{y}{}_{3},\widehat{y}{}_{12},\widehat{y}{}_{3}^{2},\widehat{y}{}_{123}^{2})}\cdot z\bigr)\bigr)\,,

we arrive at

\displaystyle{\mathsf{d}}\Phi\bigl(\widehat{y}{}_{1},\widehat{y}{}_{2},\widehat{y}{}_{3},\widehat{y}{}_{12},\widehat{y}{}_{3}^{2},\widehat{y}{}_{123}^{2}\bigr)=({\mathsf{a}}_{1,23}-{\mathsf{a}}_{12,3})(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})\equiv\bigl(\Delta^{(2;2)}_{\mathbb{T}}{\mathsf{a}}_{E}-\vartheta_{\rm GS}\bigr)(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})\,,

and so

\displaystyle\Phi\bigl(\widehat{y}{}_{1},\widehat{y}{}_{2},\widehat{y}{}_{3},\widehat{y}{}_{12},\widehat{y}{}_{3}^{2},\widehat{y}{}_{123}^{2}\bigr)\equiv\widetilde{\Phi}(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})\,,

with the latter derived with the help of the usual homotopy retraction
\,(\tau,(\theta_{1},\theta_{2},\theta_{3}))\longmapsto(\tau\,\theta_{1},\tau\,\theta_{2},\tau\,\theta_{3}),

\tau\in[0,1]\,
in the form

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})=\tfrac{1}{2}\,\bigl(x_{1}^{a}\,\theta_{2}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,\theta_{3}+x_{3}^{a}\,\theta_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,\theta_{2}\bigr)-\tfrac{1}{6}\,\theta_{1}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,\bigl(2\theta_{2}+\theta_{3}\bigr)\cdot\theta_{2}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,\theta_{3}\,.

Coherence of the thus reconstructed isomorphism
\,\beta\,
with the trivial isomorphism
\,\alpha_{E}\equiv{\boldsymbol{1}}\,
is a straightforward consequence of the dependence of the data
\,\Phi\,
of the former on the base coordinates
\,(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})\,
exclusively.

We are finally in a position to check the supersymmetry of the 1-gerbe 2-isomorphism
\,\alpha_{\rm GS}
. To this end, define the composite actions
\,{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}LE_{12,3}\ell^{(3)}_{(\varepsilon,t)}\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}_{1}\ell_{(\varepsilon,t)}\times{\mathsf{Y}}_{2}\ell_{(\varepsilon,t)}\times{\mathsf{Y}}_{3}\ell_{(\varepsilon,t)}\times{\mathsf{Y}}_{123}\ell_{(\varepsilon,t)}\times LE_{12,3}\ell^{(3)}_{(\varepsilon,t)}\ :\ {\rm pr}_{1}^{*}LE_{12,3}\circlearrowleft\,
and
\,{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}LE_{1,23}\ell^{(3)}_{(\varepsilon,t)}\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}_{1}\ell_{(\varepsilon,t)}\times{\mathsf{Y}}_{2}\ell_{(\varepsilon,t)}\times{\mathsf{Y}}_{3}\ell_{(\varepsilon,t)}\times{\mathsf{Y}}_{123}\ell_{(\varepsilon,t)}\times LE_{1,23}\ell^{(3)}_{(\varepsilon,t)}\ :\ {\rm pr}_{2}^{*}LE_{1,23}\circlearrowleft
. The requirement of supersymmetry(-equivariance) now reads

\displaystyle\beta\circ{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}LE_{12,3}\ell^{(3)}_{(\varepsilon,t)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{=}}{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}LE_{1,23}\ell^{(3)}_{(\varepsilon,t)}\circ\beta\,,

or, equivalently,

\displaystyle\Phi^{12,3}_{(\varepsilon,t)}\bigl(\widehat{y}{}_{12,3}\bigr)+\widetilde{\Phi}\bigl(\ell_{(\varepsilon,t)}(m_{1}),m_{2},m_{3}\bigr)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{=}}\widetilde{\Phi}(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})+\Phi^{1,23}_{(\varepsilon,t)}\bigl(\widehat{y}{}_{1,23}\bigr)\,,

and is readily verified to follow from the Fierz identity (
6.1
). Thus, the existence of the anticipated supersymmetric 1-gerbe 2-isomorphism

\displaystyle\alpha_{\rm GS}\equiv\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}_{12,3\times 1,23}{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3},{\rm pr}_{1,2,3,6}\circ{\rm pr}_{1},\beta\bigr)

is demonstrated.

We conclude the reconstruction of the supersymmetric multiplicative structure on the Green–Schwarz super-1-gerbe by convincing ourselves that the 2-isomorphism just found,
\,\alpha_{\rm GS}
, satisfies the coherence condition expressed by the commutativity of Diag. (
). As the data
\,\widetilde{\Phi}\,
of the 2-isomorphism depend only on the projection all the way to
\,{\mathbb{T}}^{\times 3}
, the latter condition takes the simple form
\,\Delta^{(3;0)}_{\mathbb{T}}\widetilde{\Phi}=0
, and is readily shown to be equivalent to the Fierz identity (
6.1
).

Appendix H
A proof of Proposition
49

In what follows, we construct a 1-gerbe 1-isomorphism (
8.13
) invariant, in the previously discussed sense, with respect to the left action of the residual supersymmetry group

\displaystyle{\mathbb{D}}_{(1,1|16)}\equiv{\rm sMink}(1,1|16)\,.

As usual, we begin by setting up a convenient hands-on description of the pullback super-1-gerbe
\,\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})\,*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\,
to the
(1+1|16)
-superdimensional worldvolume of a leaf
\,D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\,
‘through’ a fixed
\,(\widehat{x}{}^{\widehat{a}}),\ \widehat{a}\in\overline{2,9}
, with the structure sheaf generated by the global coordinates
\,(\breve{\theta}{}^{\underline{\alpha}},\breve{x}{}^{\underline{a}}),\ (\underline{\alpha},\underline{a})\in\overline{1,16}\times\{0,1\}
as introduced above. Its surjective submersion shall be taken in the form
\,\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}}\equiv{\rm pr}_{1}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}=\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})\,*}{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}\longrightarrow D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}
, with the coordinates
\,((\breve{\theta}{}^{\underline{\alpha}},\breve{x}{}^{\underline{a}}),(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}^{\beta},\breve{x}{}^{\underline{a}},\widehat{x}{}^{\widehat{b}}_{*},\breve{\xi}{}_{\underline{\delta}},\widehat{\xi}_{\widehat{\epsilon}}))\equiv(\breve{m},(\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}(\breve{m}),\xi))\equiv\breve{y}\in{\mathsf{Y}}D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}
. On it, we find the pullback curving

(H.2)

(H.3)

\displaystyle{\rm A}_{T_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}}\bigl(\breve{y}\bigr)\equiv-\breve{\theta}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}\,{\mathsf{d}}\breve{\xi}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\,\bigl(\breve{x}{}_{+}\,{\mathsf{d}}\breve{x}{}_{-}-\breve{x}{}_{-}\,{\mathsf{d}}\breve{x}{}_{+}\bigr)\equiv-\breve{\theta}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}\,{\mathsf{d}}\breve{\xi}{}_{\underline{\alpha}}+\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}}^{*}{\mathsf{a}}_{T_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}}\bigl(\breve{y}\bigr)\,.

Direct inspection of the variance of its base component

in which

leads to the definition of the desired lift
\,T\breve{\ell}{}^{(1,1|16)}\ :\ {\mathbb{D}}_{(1,1|16)}\times T_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\longrightarrow T_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\,
of the action
\,{\mathsf{Y}}\breve{\ell}{}^{(1,1|16)}\,
to the total space
\,T_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\,
of the principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-bundle,

with

\,\mathcal{A}_{T_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}}
.

At this stage, it remains to verify the existence, over

(H.7)

where on the pullback bundles

{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-bundle
\,\widehat{\epsilon}{}_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})\,[2]\,*}L\otimes{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}T_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\cong{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}\,

{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-connection super-1-form

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\widehat{\epsilon}{}_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})\,[2]\,*}L\otimes{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}T_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}}\bigl(\breve{y}{}_{1},\breve{y}{}_{2},z\bigr)\equiv\vartheta(z)+\bigl({\rm pr}_{2}^{*}{\rm A}_{T_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}}+{\rm A}_{\widehat{\epsilon}{}_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})\,[2]\,*}L}\bigr)\bigl(\breve{y}{}_{1},\breve{y}{}_{2}\bigr)\,.

, we consider the difference of the base components of the relevant principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-connection super-1-forms,
\,({\rm pr}_{1}^{*}{\rm A}_{T_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}}-{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}{\rm A}_{T_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}}-{\rm A}_{\widehat{\epsilon}{}_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})\,[2]\,*}L}\bigr)(\breve{y}{}_{1},\breve{y}{}_{2})=0
, and derive therefrom

(H.8)

\displaystyle\alpha_{T_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}}\equiv{\boldsymbol{1}}\,,

manifestly coherent with the trivial groupoid structure on (the pullback of)
\,L
. We complete our analysis with a check of the equivariance of
\,\alpha_{T_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}}
. The condition of equivariance, Eq. (
H.7
), rewrites as

\displaystyle\lambda\bigl(\bigl(\jmath_{(1,1|16)}\bigl(\breve{c}\bigr),0\bigr),\bigl(\jmath_{(1,1|16)}(\breve{c}\bigr),0\bigr),\bigl(\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\bigl(\breve{m}\bigr),\xi^{1}\bigr),\bigl(\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\bigl(\breve{m}\bigr),\xi^{2}\bigr)\bigr)+\Delta_{\breve{c}}^{(1,1|16)}\bigl(\breve{m},\breve{\xi}{}^{2}\bigr)=\Delta_{\breve{c}}^{(1,1|16)}\bigl(\breve{m},\breve{\xi}{}^{1}\bigr)\,,

and so holds true trivially.

Thus, we obtain a manifestly (left-)supersymmetric trivialisation

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}=\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})},\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}},T_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})},\pi_{T_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}},\mathcal{A}_{T_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}},\alpha_{T_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}}\bigr)

on the restricted super-1-gerbe
\,\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})\,*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
.

Appendix I
A proof of Proposition
50

We begin our constructive proof by lifting the maps
\,\breve{\wp}{}_{\breve{c}}\,
to
\,{\mathsf{Y}}D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\,
in an obvious manner,
i.e.
, as mappings
\,{\mathsf{Y}}\breve{\wp}{}_{\breve{c}}^{(1,1|16)}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\,
with the coordinate presentation
\,{\mathsf{Y}}\breve{\wp}{}_{\breve{c}}^{(1,1|16)}(\breve{y})\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}\breve{\wp}{}_{\breve{c}}^{(1,1|16)}(\breve{m},(\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}(\breve{m}),\xi))

=(\breve{\wp}{}_{\breve{c}}(\breve{m}),{\mathsf{Y}}\wp_{(\jmath_{(1,1|16)}(\breve{c}),0)}(\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}(\breve{m}),\xi))

\,{\mathsf{Y}}\wp_{y_{2}}(y_{1})={\mathsf{Y}}{\rm m}(y_{1},y_{2})\,
(for
\,(y_{1},y_{2})\in{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}\times{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}

as

on the restricted super-1-gerbe

Appendix J
A proof of Proposition
51

Having established the right supersymmetry of the restriction
\,\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})\,*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\,
of the GS super-1-gerbe in the proof of Prop.
50
, we may, now, proceed with the examination of its trivialisation
\,\mathcal{T}_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}
. The first step on this path consists in checking the behaviour of the curving
\,\omega_{(1,1|16)}\bigl(\breve{m}\bigr)={\mathsf{d}}\breve{x}{}_{+}\wedge{\mathsf{d}}\breve{x}{}_{-}+\breve{\theta}\,\overline{\gamma}{}_{0}\,{\mathsf{d}}\breve{\theta}\wedge{\mathsf{d}}\breve{x}{}_{+}\,
of the trivial super-1-gerbe
\,\mathcal{I}_{\omega_{(1,1|16)}}\,
under a right translation
\,\breve{\wp}{}_{\breve{c}}
. The latter is established in a direct calculation using the transformation formulæ (
8.14
) augmented with
\,\breve{x}{}_{-}\longmapsto\breve{x}{}_{-}+\breve{t}{}_{-}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\breve{\theta}\,(\overline{\gamma}{}^{0}-\overline{\gamma}{}^{1})\,\breve{\varepsilon}=\breve{x}{}_{-}+\breve{t}{}_{-}-\breve{\varepsilon}\,\overline{\gamma}{}_{0}\,\breve{\theta}
, which yields
\,\breve{\wp}{}_{\breve{c}}^{*}\omega_{(1,1|16)}(\breve{m})=\omega_{(1,1|16)}(\breve{m})+{\mathsf{d}}(2\breve{\varepsilon}\,\overline{\gamma}{}_{0}\,\breve{\theta}\,{\mathsf{d}}\breve{x}{}_{+})
. Thus, the
left
trivial super-1-gerbe
\,\mathcal{I}_{\omega_{(1,1|16)}}\,
is
not
a
right
trivial super-1-gerbe. Nevertheless, invariance of its curvature
\,{\mathsf{d}}\omega_{(1,1|16)}\,
under the right supersymmetry ensures its right supersymmetry as a 1-gerbe. Indeed, reasoning along the lines drawn previously for
\,\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})\,*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
, we arrive at the (trivial) principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-bundle

\displaystyle\pi_{J_{\breve{c}}}={\rm pr}_{1}\ :\ J_{\breve{c}}=D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}\longrightarrow D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}

with, in the global coordinates
\,(\breve{m},z)\in J_{\breve{c}}
, the principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-connection super-1-form

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{J_{\breve{c}}}\bigl(\breve{m},z\bigr)=\vartheta(z)-2\breve{\varepsilon}\,\overline{\gamma}{}_{0}\,\breve{\theta}\,{\mathsf{d}}\breve{x}{}_{+}\equiv\vartheta(z)+{\rm A}_{J_{\breve{c}}}(\breve{m})

and the trivial isomorphism

\displaystyle\alpha_{J_{\breve{c}}}\equiv{\boldsymbol{1}}\ :\ J_{\breve{c}}\circlearrowleft\,,

manifestly coherent with the trivial groupoid structure of
\,\mathcal{I}_{\omega_{(1,1|16)}}
. Hence, altogether, the right supersymmetry is implemented on the latter by the
{\mathbb{D}}_{(1,1|16)}
-indexed family of 1-gerbe 1-isomorphisms

\displaystyle\Psi_{\breve{c}}\equiv\bigl(D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})},{\rm id}_{D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}},J_{\breve{c}},\pi_{J_{\breve{c}}},\mathcal{A}_{J_{\breve{c}}},\alpha_{J_{\breve{c}}}\bigr)\ :\ \breve{\wp}{}_{\breve{c}}^{*}\mathcal{I}_{\omega_{(1,1|16)}}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\mathcal{I}_{\omega_{(1,1|16)}}\,,\qquad\qquad\breve{c}\in{\mathbb{D}}_{(1,1|16)}\,.

The next, and final, step towards geometrisation of the extended supersymmetry over
\,D_{(1,1|16)}\,
is the confirmation (or, indeed, description) of the compatibility of the trivialisation with the right-supersymmetric structures on the two super-1-gerbes involved:
\,\{\Phi_{\breve{c}}\}_{\breve{c}\in{\mathbb{D}}_{(1,1|16)}}\,
on
\,\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})\,*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\,
and
\,\{\Psi_{\breve{c}}\}_{\breve{c}\in{\mathbb{D}}_{(1,1|16)}}\,
on
\,\mathcal{I}_{\omega_{(1,1|16)}}
. This we achieve by identifying the family of 2-isomorphisms of Diag. (
8.16
). To this end, we consider the two surjective submersions:
\,{\rm pr}_{1}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}_{\breve{c},0}D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}_{\breve{c}}D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\,
and
\,{\rm id}_{{\mathsf{Y}}_{\breve{c}}D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}_{\breve{c}}D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}_{\breve{c}}D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\,
over the surjective submersion of either of the composite 1-isomorphisms
\,\wp_{\breve{c}}^{*}\epsilon_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})\,*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\mathcal{I}_{\omega_{(1,1|16)}}
, and take their product fibred over the common base, to the effect
\,{\mathsf{Y}}_{\breve{c},0}D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}_{\breve{c},0}D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\times_{{\mathsf{Y}}_{\breve{c}}D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathsf{Y}}_{\breve{c}}D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}
, whereupon we present the principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-bundles of the two composite 1-gerbe 1-isomorphisms over the latter conveniently as the trivial principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-bundles
\,{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}T_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\otimes P_{\breve{c}}\cong{\mathsf{Y}}_{\breve{c},0}D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}\,
and
\,\widehat{\breve{\wp}}{}^{*}_{\breve{c}}T_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\otimes(\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}_{\breve{c}}D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}}\circ{\rm pr}_{1})^{*}J_{\breve{c}}\cong{\mathsf{Y}}_{\breve{c},0}D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
. A direct comparison of the base components of the respective principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-connection super-1-forms ,

(J.2)

\displaystyle\bigl({\rm pr}_{1}^{*}{\mathsf{Y}}\breve{\wp}{}_{\breve{c}}^{(1,1|16)\,*}{\rm A}_{T_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}}+{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}_{\breve{c}}D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}}^{*}{\rm A}_{J_{\breve{c}}}-{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}{\rm A}_{T_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}}-{\rm A}_{P_{\breve{c}}}\bigr)\bigl(\breve{y}{}_{1},\breve{y}{}_{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathsf{d}}\bigl(\tfrac{1}{2}\,\bigl(\breve{t}{}_{+}\,\breve{x}{}_{-}-\breve{t}{}_{-}\,\breve{x}{}_{+}\bigr)-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\bigl(\breve{x}{}_{+}+3\breve{t}{}_{+}\bigr)\,\breve{\varepsilon}\,\overline{\gamma}{}_{0}\,\breve{\theta}-\breve{\varepsilon}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}\,\breve{\xi}{}^{1}_{\underline{\alpha}}\bigr)\,,

gives us the coordinate presentation of the isomorphism sought-after,

\displaystyle\beta_{\breve{c}}\ :\ {\rm pr}_{2}^{*}T_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\otimes P_{\breve{c}}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\widehat{\breve{\wp}}{}^{*}_{\breve{c}}T_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\otimes\bigl(\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}_{\breve{c}}D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}}\circ{\rm pr}_{1}\bigr)^{*}J_{\breve{c}}\,,

to wit

\displaystyle\beta_{\breve{c}}\bigl(\breve{y}{}_{1},\breve{y}{}_{2},z\bigr)=\bigl(\breve{y}{}_{1},\breve{y}{}_{2},{\rm e}^{{\mathsf{i}}\,\Lambda^{(1,1|16)}_{\breve{c}}(\breve{y}{}_{1},\breve{y}{}_{2})}\cdot z\bigr)\,,\qquad\quad\Lambda^{(1,1|16)}_{\breve{c}}\bigl(\breve{y}{}_{1},\breve{y}{}_{2}\bigr)=\tfrac{1}{2}\,\bigl(\breve{t}{}_{+}\,\breve{x}{}_{-}-\breve{t}{}_{-}\,\breve{x}{}_{+}\bigr)-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\bigl(\breve{x}{}_{+}+3\breve{t}{}_{+}\bigr)\,\breve{\varepsilon}\,\overline{\gamma}{}_{0}\,\breve{\theta}-\breve{\varepsilon}{}^{\underline{\alpha}}\,\breve{\xi}{}^{1}_{\underline{\alpha}}\,.

Coherence of the above isomorphism with those of the composite 1-isomorphisms over
\,{\mathsf{Y}}_{\breve{c},0}D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\times_{D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}}{\mathsf{Y}}_{\breve{c},0}D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\ni(\breve{y}{}_{1},\breve{y}{}_{2},\breve{y}{}_{3},\breve{y}{}_{4})\,
is neatly reflected by the identity

\displaystyle\Pi^{(1,1|16)}_{\breve{c}}\bigl(\breve{y}{}_{1},\breve{y}{}_{2},\breve{y}{}_{3},\breve{y}{}_{4}\bigr)+\Lambda^{(1,1|16)}_{\breve{c}}\bigl(\breve{y}{}_{1},\breve{y}{}_{2}\bigr)=\Lambda^{(1,1|16)}_{\breve{c}}\bigl(\breve{y}{}_{3},\breve{y}{}_{4}\bigr)\,.

Altogether, we obtain the desired family of 2-isomorphisms

\displaystyle\varphi_{\breve{c}}=\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}_{\breve{c},0}D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})},\beta_{\breve{c}}\bigr)\,,\qquad\qquad\breve{c}\in{\mathbb{D}}_{(1,1|16)}\,.

Appendix K
A proof of Proposition
53

The first step of the reconstruction of the trvialisation consists in defining the surjective submersion
\,{\mathsf{Y}}D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}=\delta_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})\,*}{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}\,
of the pullback 1-gerbe, with the coordinates
\,(\theta{}^{\underline{\alpha}},(\underline{\theta}^{\alpha},x_{*}^{a},\xi_{\beta}))\equiv(\theta,(\delta_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}(\theta),\xi))\equiv\widetilde{y}
. On it, we have
\,\widehat{\delta}{}_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})\,*}{\rm B}_{\rm GS}(\widetilde{y})={\mathsf{d}}(\underline{\theta}{}^{\alpha}\,{\mathsf{d}}\xi_{\alpha})
, and so we erect a trivial principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-bundle

\displaystyle\pi_{T_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}}={\rm pr}_{1}\ :\ T_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}={\mathsf{Y}}D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}

with coordinates
\,(\widetilde{y},z)
, and with the principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-connection super-1-form

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{T_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}}\bigl(\widetilde{y},z\bigr)=\vartheta(z)-\underline{\theta}{}^{\alpha}\,{\mathsf{d}}\xi_{\alpha}\equiv\vartheta(z)+{\rm A}_{T_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}}\bigl(\widetilde{y}\bigr)

as the principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-bundle of the trivialisation sought after. Next, on the
D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}
-fibred square

\,{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\times_{D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}}{\mathsf{Y}}D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\,
of the surjective submersion, with coordinates
\,((\theta,(\delta_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}(\theta),\xi^{1})),(\theta,(\delta_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}(\theta),

\xi^{2})))\equiv(\widetilde{y}{}_{1},\widetilde{y}{}_{2})
, we obtain – for
\,\widehat{\delta}{}_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})\,[2]}\equiv\widehat{\delta}{}_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\times\widehat{\delta}{}_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\,
– the identity
\,\widehat{\delta}{}_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})\,[2]\,*}{\rm A}_{L}(\widetilde{y}{}_{1},\widetilde{y}{}_{2})=\underline{\theta}{}^{\alpha}\,{\mathsf{d}}\xi^{21}_{\alpha}
, and so also
\,\widehat{\delta}{}_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})\,[2]\,*}{\rm A}_{L}+{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}{\rm A}_{T_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}}={\rm pr}_{1}^{*}{\rm A}_{T_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}}
, from which we read off, over
\,{\mathsf{Y}}^{(x_{*})\,[2]}D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}
, the trivial isomorphism

(K.1)

\displaystyle\alpha_{T_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}}\equiv{\boldsymbol{1}}\ :\ \widehat{\delta}{}_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})\,[2]\,*}L\otimes{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}T_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}T_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\,,

manifestly coherent with the trivial groupoid structures of
\,\delta_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})\,*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\,
and
\,\mathcal{I}_{0}
. All in all, we have the trivialisation

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\equiv\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})},\pi_{{\mathsf{Y}}D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}},T_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})},\pi_{T_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}},\mathcal{A}_{T_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}},\alpha_{T_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}}\bigr)\,.

At this stage, it remains to verify the (
{\mathbb{D}}_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}
-)supersymmetry of the trivialisation just derived. Proceeding as in the case of the right supersymmetry of the trivialisation
\,\mathcal{T}_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}
, we begin by lifting the maps
\,\tau_{\varepsilon}\,
to
\,{\mathsf{Y}}D_{(0|N)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\,
as mappings
\,{\mathsf{Y}}\tau_{\varepsilon}^{(0|N)}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\,
with the coordinate presentation
\,{\mathsf{Y}}\tau_{\varepsilon}^{(0|N)}(\widetilde{y})\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}\tau_{\varepsilon}^{(0|N)}(\theta,(\delta_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}(\theta),\xi))=(\tau_{\varepsilon}(\theta),{\mathsf{Y}}\widetilde{\rm Ad}{}_{\varepsilon}(\delta_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}(\theta),\xi))
, written in terms of the mappings
\,{\mathsf{Y}}\widetilde{\rm Ad}{}_{\varepsilon}(y)={\mathsf{Y}}\wp_{(\frac{1}{2}\,\underline{\varepsilon},0,0)}\circ{\mathsf{Y}}\ell_{(\frac{1}{2}\,\underline{\varepsilon},0,0)}(y)\,
(for
\,y\in{\mathsf{Y}}{\mathbb{T}}
), and subsequently employing them to induce mappings on the fibred product
\,{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\tau_{\varepsilon}^{(0|N)}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}D_{(0|N)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}D_{(0|N)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*})}\,
as
per

\,{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}\tau_{\varepsilon}^{(0|N)}(\widetilde{y}{}_{1},\widetilde{y}{}_{2})=({\mathsf{Y}}\tau_{\varepsilon}^{(0|N)}(\widetilde{y}{}_{1}),{\mathsf{Y}}\tau_{\varepsilon}^{(0|N)}(\widetilde{y}{}_{2}))
, whence the coordinate transformations

. These imply

\displaystyle{\mathsf{Y}}\tau_{\varepsilon}^{(0|N)\,*}\widehat{\delta}{}_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})\,*}{\rm B}_{\rm GS}\bigl(\widetilde{y}\bigr)=\widehat{\delta}{}_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})\,*}{\rm B}_{\rm GS}\bigl(\widetilde{y}\bigr)+{\mathsf{d}}\bigl(\tfrac{1}{12}\,\underline{\varepsilon}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,\underline{\theta}\cdot\underline{\theta}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,{\mathsf{d}}\underline{\theta}\bigr)\,,

\,{\rm pr}_{1,2}^{*}P_{\varepsilon}\otimes{\rm pr}_{2,4}^{*}\widehat{\delta}{}_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})\,[2]\,*}L\cong{\mathsf{Y}}^{[2]}_{\varepsilon,0}D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
, the coordinate presentation

\displaystyle\alpha_{Q_{\varepsilon}}\bigl(\widetilde{y}{}_{1},\widetilde{y}{}_{2},\widetilde{y}{}_{3},\widetilde{y}{}_{4},z\bigr)=\bigl(\widetilde{y}{}_{1},\widetilde{y}{}_{2},\widetilde{y}{}_{3},\widetilde{y}{}_{4},{\rm e}^{{\mathsf{i}}\,\Upsilon_{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{y}{}_{1},\widetilde{y}{}_{2},\widetilde{y}{}_{3},\widetilde{y}{}_{4})}\cdot z\bigr)\,,\qquad\qquad\Upsilon_{\varepsilon}\bigl(\widetilde{y}{}_{1},\widetilde{y}{}_{2},\widetilde{y}{}_{3},\widetilde{y}{}_{4}\bigr)=-\underline{\varepsilon}{}^{\alpha}\,\xi^{31}_{\alpha}\,.

Its compatibility with the trivial groupoid structure
\,\mu_{\widehat{\delta}{}_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})\,[2]\,*}L}={\boldsymbol{1}}\,
over
\,{\mathsf{Y}}_{\varepsilon,0}D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\times_{D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}}{\mathsf{Y}}_{\varepsilon,0}D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\times_{D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}}{\mathsf{Y}}_{\varepsilon,0}D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\ni(\widetilde{y}{}_{1},\widetilde{y}{}_{2},\widetilde{y}{}_{3},\widetilde{y}{}_{4},\widetilde{y}{}_{5},\widetilde{y}{}_{6})
, follows from the identity

\displaystyle\bigl({\rm pr}_{1,2,3,4}^{*}\Upsilon_{\varepsilon}+{\rm pr}_{3,4,5,6}^{*}\Upsilon_{\varepsilon}\bigr)\bigl(\widetilde{y}{}_{1},\widetilde{y}{}_{2},\widetilde{y}{}_{3},\widetilde{y}{}_{4},\widetilde{y}{}_{5},\widetilde{y}{}_{6}\bigr)={\rm pr}_{1,2,5,6}^{*}\Upsilon_{\varepsilon}\bigl(\widetilde{y}{}_{1},\widetilde{y}{}_{2},\widetilde{y}{}_{3},\widetilde{y}{}_{4},\widetilde{y}{}_{5},\widetilde{y}{}_{6}\bigr)\,.

We thus arrive at the
{\mathbb{D}}_{(0|N)}
-supersymmetric structure

\displaystyle\Xi_{\varepsilon}\equiv\bigl({\mathsf{Y}}_{\varepsilon,0}D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})},{\rm id}_{{\mathsf{Y}}_{\varepsilon,0}D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}},Q_{\varepsilon},\pi_{Q_{\varepsilon}},\mathcal{A}_{Q_{\varepsilon}},\alpha_{Q_{\varepsilon}}\bigr)\ :\ \tau_{\varepsilon}^{*}\delta_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})\,*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\delta_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})\,*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}\,,\qquad\qquad\varepsilon\in{\mathbb{D}}_{(0|N)}

on the restricted super-1-gerbe
\,\delta_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})\,*}\mathcal{G}_{\rm GS}
.

Once again, upon identifying the above, we pass to inspect the behaviour of the previously established trivialisation
\,\mathcal{T}_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\,
under (suitable lifts of) the maps
\,\tau_{\varepsilon}\,
in relation to the the
{\mathbb{D}}_{(0|N)}
-supersymmetric structure
\,\Xi_{\varepsilon}
. What simplifies the situation considerably is the manifest
{\mathbb{D}}_{(0|N)}
-supersymmetry of the trivial 1-gerbe
\,\mathcal{I}_{0}
, which enables us to pass directly to the implementation of the supersymmetry present on the trivialisation given by the
{\mathbb{D}}_{(0|N)}
-indexed family of 1-gerbe 2-isomorphisms

As before, we consider the two surjective submersions:
\,{\rm pr}_{1}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}_{\varepsilon,0}D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}_{\varepsilon}D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\,
and
\,{\rm id}_{{\mathsf{Y}}_{\varepsilon}D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}}\ :\ {\mathsf{Y}}_{\varepsilon}D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Y}}_{\varepsilon}D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\,
over the surjective submersion of the composite 1-isomorphisms,
\,\mathcal{T}_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\circ\Xi_{\varepsilon}\,
and
\,\tau_{\varepsilon}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}
, and take their product fibred over the common base, whereby we obtain
\,{\mathsf{Y}}_{\varepsilon,0}D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\equiv{\mathsf{Y}}_{\varepsilon,0}D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\times_{{\mathsf{Y}}_{\varepsilon}D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}}\hskip-1.0pt{\mathsf{Y}}_{\varepsilon}D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}
. Inspection of the difference of the base components of the relevant principal
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-bundles
\,{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}T_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\otimes Q_{\varepsilon}\cong{\mathsf{Y}}_{\varepsilon,0}D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}\,
and
\,\widehat{\tau}{}^{*}_{\varepsilon}T_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\cong{\mathsf{Y}}_{\varepsilon,0}D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}\,
now yields

\displaystyle\bigl({\rm pr}_{1}^{*}{\mathsf{Y}}\tau_{\varepsilon}^{(0|N)\,*}{\rm A}_{T_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}}-{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}{\rm A}_{T_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}}-{\rm A}_{Q_{\varepsilon}}\bigr)\bigl(\widetilde{y}{}_{1},\widetilde{y}{}_{2}\bigr)={\mathsf{d}}\bigl(-\underline{\varepsilon}{}^{\alpha}\,\xi^{1}_{\alpha}-\tfrac{1}{16}\,\underline{\varepsilon}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,\underline{\theta}\cdot\underline{\varepsilon}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,\underline{\theta}\bigr)\,,

whence the coordinate presentation of the isomorphism
\,\widetilde{\beta}{}_{\varepsilon}\ :\ {\rm pr}_{2}^{*}T_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\otimes Q_{\varepsilon}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\widehat{\tau}{}^{*}_{\varepsilon}T_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\,
given by
\,\widetilde{\beta}_{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{y}{}_{1},\widetilde{y}{}_{2},z)=(\widetilde{y}{}_{1},\widetilde{y}{}_{2},{\rm e}^{{\mathsf{i}}\,\widetilde{\Lambda}{}_{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{y}{}_{1},\widetilde{y}{}_{2})}\cdot z)
, where
\,\widetilde{\Lambda}{}_{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{y}{}_{1},\widetilde{y}{}_{2})=-\underline{\varepsilon}{}^{\alpha}\,\xi^{1}_{\alpha}-\tfrac{1}{16}\,\underline{\varepsilon}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}_{a}\,\underline{\theta}\cdot\underline{\varepsilon}\,\overline{\Gamma}{}^{a}\,\underline{\theta}
. Its coherence with the isomorphisms of the composite 1-isomorphisms over
\,{\mathsf{Y}}_{\varepsilon,0}D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\times_{D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}}{\mathsf{Y}}_{\varepsilon,0}D_{(0|N)}^{(x_{*})}\ni(\widetilde{y}{}_{1},\widetilde{y}{}_{2},\widetilde{y}{}_{3},\widetilde{y}{}_{4})\,
is ensured by the identity

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{\varepsilon}\bigl(\widetilde{y}{}_{1},\widetilde{y}{}_{2},\widetilde{y}{}_{3},\widetilde{y}{}_{4}\bigr)+\widetilde{\Lambda}{}_{\varepsilon}\bigl(\widetilde{y}{}_{1},\widetilde{y}{}_{2}\bigr)=\widetilde{\Lambda}{}_{\varepsilon}\bigl(\widetilde{y}{}_{3},\widetilde{y}{}_{4}\bigr)\,.

\displaystyle{\rm A}_{E}\bigl(\widehat{\iota}{}_{1,2}(y_{(1,2)}),\widehat{\iota}{}_{1,2}(y_{{\rm m}(1,2)})\bigr)+{\rm A}_{L}\bigl(\widehat{\rm m}\circ\widehat{\iota}{}_{1,2}(y_{{\rm m}(1,2)}),\widehat{\iota}{}_{3}\circ\widehat{\mu}{}_{1,2}^{3}(y_{3(1*2)})\bigr)+{\rm A}_{T_{3}}\bigl(\widehat{\mu}{}_{1,2}^{3}(y_{3(1*2)})\bigr)

\displaystyle-{\rm A}_{L}\bigl({\rm pr}_{1}\circ\widehat{\iota}{}_{1,2}(y_{(1,2)}),\widehat{\iota}{}_{1,2}\circ{\rm pr}_{1}(y_{1;2})\bigr)-{\rm A}_{L}\bigl({\rm pr}_{2}\circ\widehat{\iota}{}_{1,2}(y_{(1,2)}),\widehat{\iota}{}_{1,2}\circ{\rm pr}_{2}(y_{1;2})\bigr)

\displaystyle-{\rm A}_{L}\bigl(\widehat{\rm pr}{}_{1}\circ\widehat{\iota}{}_{1,2}\circ{\rm pr}_{1}(y_{1;2}),\widehat{\iota}{}_{1}\circ\widehat{\rm pr}{}_{1}\circ{\rm pr}_{1}(y_{1|2})\bigr)-{\rm A}_{L}\bigl(\widehat{\rm pr}{}_{2}\circ\widehat{\iota}{}_{1,2}\circ{\rm pr}_{2}(y_{1;2}),\widehat{\iota}{}_{2}\circ\widehat{\rm pr}{}_{2}\circ{\rm pr}_{2}(y_{1|2})\bigr)

\displaystyle-{\rm A}_{T_{1}}\bigl(\widehat{\rm pr}{}_{1}\circ{\rm pr}_{1}(y_{1|2})\bigr)-{\rm A}_{T_{2}}\bigl(\widehat{\rm pr}{}_{2}\circ{\rm pr}_{2}(y_{1|2})\bigr)\,,

the right-hand side being de Rham-exact due to (de Rham-)cohomological triviality of the supergeometries involved. In fact, it is easy to see that in all the cases considered the components of the above super-1-forms that depend on the coordinates in the fibre over the underlying product worldvolume
\,D_{1,2}^{3}\,
cancel out, and so by the end of the long day, we are left with

(L.46)

\displaystyle\nu_{1,2}^{3}\bigl(\widehat{y}{}_{1,2},\widehat{y}{}_{3}\bigr)\equiv\underline{\nu}{}_{1,2}^{3}\bigl(\breve{m}{}_{1},\breve{m}{}_{2}\bigr)\,,

where

\displaystyle{\mathsf{d}}\underline{\nu}{}_{1,2}^{3}\bigl(\breve{m}{}_{1},\breve{m}{}_{2}\bigr)={\mathsf{a}}_{E}\bigl(\breve{m}{}_{1},\breve{m}{}_{2},\breve{m}{}_{1}\cdot\breve{m}{}_{2}\bigr)+{\mathsf{a}}_{T_{3}}\bigl(\mu_{1,2}^{3}\bigl(\breve{m}{}_{1},\breve{m}{}_{2}\bigr)\bigr)-{\mathsf{a}}_{T_{1}}\bigl(\breve{m}{}_{1}\bigr)-{\mathsf{a}}_{T_{2}}\bigl(\breve{m}{}_{2}\bigr)\,,

with

\displaystyle{\mathsf{a}}_{T_{A}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}{\mathsf{a}}_{T_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,A})}}&\textrm{if}\quad D_{A}=D_{(1,1|16)}^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,A})}\cr\cr 0&\textrm{if}\quad D_{A}=D_{(0|N_{A})}^{(x_{*\,A})}\end{array}\right.\,.

Coherence of the thus established 2-isomorphism is a straightforward consequence of the triviality of
\,\alpha_{E}\,
(
cf.
Eq. (
G.5
)) and of the
\,\alpha_{T_{A}}\,
(
cf.
Eqs. (
H.8
) and (
K.1
)), as well as that of the multiplicative structure
\,\mu_{L}\,
of the GS super-1-gerbe (
cf.
Eq. (
6.13
)), and of the dependence of (the coordinate presentation of)
\,\beta_{1,2}^{3}\,
solely on the base coordinates (
cf.
Eq. (
L.46
)).

In the final step of our analysis, we inspect the various cases one by one.

L.0.1.
Fusion of superaligned bi-supersymmetric branes

Given
\,((\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{1}^{\alpha},\breve{x}{}_{1}^{0},\breve{x}{}_{1}^{1},\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1}^{\widehat{a}}),(\breve{\underline{\theta}}{}_{2}^{\alpha},\breve{x}{}_{2}^{0},\breve{x}{}_{2}^{1},\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2}^{\widehat{a}}))\equiv(\breve{m}{}_{1},\breve{m}{}_{2})\in\iota_{D^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1})}_{(1,1|16)},D^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}_{(1,1|16)}}(\mathcal{F}_{D^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1})}_{(1,1|16)},D^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}_{(1,1|16)}}^{D^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1}+\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}_{(1,1|16)}}([a_{1}:a_{2}:b_{1,2}]\,|\,[c_{1}:c_{2}]))
, we calculate

\displaystyle{\mathsf{a}}_{E}\bigl(\breve{m}{}_{1},\breve{m}{}_{2},\breve{m}{}_{1}\cdot\breve{m}{}_{2}\bigr)+{\mathsf{a}}_{T^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1}+\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}_{(1,1|16)}}\bigl(\mu_{1,2}^{3}\bigl(\breve{m}{}_{1},\breve{m}{}_{2}\bigr)\bigr)-{\mathsf{a}}_{T^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,1})}_{(1,1|16)}}\bigl(\breve{m}{}_{1}\bigr)-{\mathsf{a}}_{T^{(\widehat{x}{}_{*\,2})}_{(1,1|16)}}\bigl(\breve{m}{}_{2}\bigr)=\breve{x}{}_{+\,1}\,{\mathsf{d}}\breve{x}{}_{-\,2}-\breve{x}{}_{-\,2}\,{\mathsf{d}}\breve{x}{}_{+\,1}\,,

and the exactness of the result is ensured by the second constraints in Eq. (
9.60
). Indeed, whenever

\,a_{1}=0\,
and
\,\breve{x}{}_{-\,2}=d_{-\,2}\in{\mathbb{R}}
, we obtain
\,\underline{\nu}{}_{1,2}^{3}\bigl(\breve{m}{}_{1},\breve{m}{}_{2}\bigr)=-d_{-\,2}\,\breve{x}{}_{+\,1}
, whereas if
\,a_{2}=0\,
and
\,\breve{x}{}_{+\,1}=d_{+\,1}\in{\mathbb{R}}
, we have

\,\underline{\nu}{}_{1,2}^{3}\bigl(\breve{m}{}_{1},\breve{m}{}_{2}\bigr)=d_{+\,1}\,\breve{x}{}_{-\,2}
. Finally, for
\,a_{1},a_{2}\neq 0
, we find
\,\underline{\nu}{}_{1,2}^{3}(\breve{m}{}_{1},\breve{m}{}_{2})=-\tfrac{b_{1,2}}{a_{2}}\,\breve{x}{}_{+\,1}
.

L.0.2.
Fusion of spatially transverse bi-supersymmetric branes

Here, we have two subcases of Sec.
9.2.2
. In both subcases, we may simply adapt the results of the analysis carried out in the previous case.

L.0.3.
Fusion of superpoint branes of opposite chirality

Given
\,((\underline{\theta}{}_{1},x_{*\,1}),(\underline{\theta}{}_{2},x_{*\,2}))\equiv(\breve{m}{}_{1},\breve{m}{}_{2})\in\iota_{D^{(x_{*\,1})}_{(0|16\pm)},D^{(x_{*\,2})}_{(0|16\mp)}}(\mathcal{F}_{D^{(x_{*\,1})}_{(0|16\pm)},D^{(x_{*\,2})}_{(0|16\mp)}}^{D^{(x_{*\,1}+x_{*\,2})}_{(0|32)}})
, we readily establish

\displaystyle{\mathsf{a}}_{E}\bigl(\breve{m}{}_{1},\breve{m}{}_{2},\breve{m}{}_{1}\cdot\breve{m}{}_{2}\bigr)+{\mathsf{a}}_{T^{(x_{*\,1}+x_{*\,2})}_{(0|32)}}\bigl(\mu_{1,2}^{3}\bigl(\breve{m}{}_{1},\breve{m}{}_{2}\bigr)\bigr)-{\mathsf{a}}_{T^{(x_{*\,1})}_{(0|16\pm)}}\bigl(\breve{m}{}_{1}\bigr)-{\mathsf{a}}_{T^{(x_{*\,2})}_{(0|16\mp)}}\bigl(\breve{m}{}_{2}\bigr)=0\,,

and so we may consistently set
\,\underline{\nu}{}_{1,2}^{3}(\breve{m}{}_{1},\breve{m}{}_{2})=0
.
